# Historical AI development and validation notes

Archived development notes; not a claim of current verification. Original paths are shown per section.

## docs/ANDROID-CANDIDATE-COMPLETE-PROPOSAL-2026-11-16.md

# Android measured next-hop proposal preservation

The original candidate plan measures a target reached through a captured packet
prefix. Its proposal now replaces **prefix position + 1** in the original complete
path. It retains all other members, their private source references and policies,
the unchanged final node's DNS, and the exact Local/Server/Compare selection. It no
longer truncates the suffix, appends an extra imported node, or silently saves Local.

The proposal checks the original admitted result and generation, exact measured
candidate source, complete member/proof identity uniqueness, and the original
captured packet position. The existing complete ordinary or typed SOCKS/CONNECT
compiler remains final admission authority. Replacing a role with an incompatible
transport, putting an imported proxy at the final position, or duplicating the
retained final identity fails before any profile write. The final source compiler
readback retains every original member and independent identity. Server/Compare
previews use their existing family-specific constructor; no new parser is opened.

Saved rows keep private node references rather than copying credentials. Existing
joined candidate/outer graph authority, expiration, fresh pre-commit checks, normal
explicit Load and source-change-away/back refusal remain active. This change creates
no native session and does not widen the current temporary prefix admission to
remote execution. The future proposal source preserves the requested execution even
when a given native candidate path remains unavailable.

## Exact validation

The implementation was tested with the shipping Java sources, real Android JSON
library, real shared Go source constructors, and existing bounded native callback /
outer graph doubles. The metric in these tests is still fifty TCP-connect samples;
a fixture callback is not authentication, a SOCKS UDP association, CONNECT TLS,
packet routing, an APK/device run, or kernel acceptance.

The source helper built in **23.915 seconds / 120**; the actual shared fixture
generator passed in **65.380 seconds / 180**. The helper and generated private fixture
were retained with hashes and reused unchanged for the subsequent Java phases.
Every Java baseline and counterfactual retains its original **120-second** process
bound and each Java compile retains **60 seconds**.

| Required phase | Assertions | Compiled causal controls |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Detached Local proposal, original drain, explicit Save/Load | 117 | 5 |
| Four/eight-member Server and Compare source/Save preservation | 164 | 1 |
| Connected catalog, admitted result, source ownership and Save | 211 | 15 |
| SOCKS typed proposal, credentials, source/Load and retirement | 354 | 5 |
| HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT typed proposal, UoT prerequisite and retirement | 372 | 5 |
| Total distinct assertions / controls | **1,218** | **31** |

The ordinary/detached phases and their twenty-one controls finished in **212.526
seconds** overall, with each original child bound retained. The two typed families
and ten controls finished in **136.255 seconds** overall. Positive cases include
three-, four-, and eight-member original paths and non-first measured prefixes.
The typed tests now replace a valid penultimate slot in a complete path; their old
two-node append expectation was corrected. Final-slot, missing original UoT,
changed credentials, cancellation during compilation, exact typed close, stale
source, source change-away/back and failed cleanup assertions remain in place.
The added forced-Local production mutation fails specifically at the saved
requested-execution assertion.

## Source composition and retained failures

The primary helper/proof source base was
`51ad480d4a719ed49a4a84c89fc5129ef9e94bc8`, with the six code/test changes in this unit.
Publication composes those unchanged six files on
`1c5a0ab79322c7f8c8f3890f1830423c25a4411a`. It preserves the intervening Android
Hysteria2 admission, desktop changes, native gates, shared closed-prefix preparation
and input registrations. The changed Android dependencies add only the Hysteria2
final branch and help text; this unit's selected SS-final paths keep their existing
admission branches.

A further current-Java composition check compiled the new NodeStore and ordered
selection files together with both changed production files in **1.888 seconds**.
Its four/eight-member Server/Compare phase passed all **164 assertions** in **30.024
seconds**, using the retained actual helper. The subsequent optional Local replay
stopped before its first fixture could be created with **No space left on device**.
This environment failure is retained and is not called a completed replay. No
production code, assertions or deadlines changed in response. The complete original
1,218-assertion / 31-control proof above remains separately attributed.

An earlier local typed-driver attempt tried to prepare already-prepared test sources
and stopped at the exact fixture anchor assertion before compilation. The corrected
driver reused the original preparation once and then passed both complete typed
families. Neither failed driver attempt is included in the passing totals.

All source hashes, helper identity, generated fixture, positive/negative transcripts,
child timings and composition receipts remain in
`candidate-proposal-full-path-validation/{attempt1,attempt2,attempt3,attempt4,composition}`.
The final publication manifest records every changed file and its preimage.

## docs/ANDROID-CANDIDATE-PROPOSAL-ROWS-2026-11-16.md

# Android measured candidate rows and complete profile proposals — 2026-11-16

## Scope

This change keeps a completed TCP candidate measurement visible when a known complete-profile layout cannot be saved. Each row carries either an individually compiled complete proposal or a precise Save-unavailable reason. A valid proposal in another row remains available. The measurement remains TCP handshake timing through the captured prefix; it does not establish authentication, complete-path connectivity, or installed-device acceptance.

The complete proposal retains every original member outside the measured replacement position, its execution choice, and its final policy. The only row-level exception is a private checked exception constructed after a fresh original-result check for an explicit layout limitation: a retained duplicate identity, an imported replacement outside the penultimate position, another retained imported intermediate, an unsupported retained final transport for that import, or a measured nonpacket transport replacing a packet intermediate. Source mutation, expired or foreign results, cancellation, network or owner changes, and unexpected native/compiler failures still invalidate the batch.

## Ownership and UI

The preparation checks its original source and result before and after every proposal operation, including the narrow incompatibility catch. It publishes results only after the original temporary service, candidate worker, captured handles, and MTU hold have joined. Completed result authority remains bound to the captured source, network, Home epoch, settings, and original result object.

The Save picker contains only current, individually compiled proposals and indexes its filtered list. Incompatible rows remain visible with their measured value and explicit reason. If every row is incompatible, pressing Save retains those rows and reasons. Saving remains separate from explicit Load and Connect.

## Executable validation

All four required local gates passed. Their check counts describe individual executable assertions, not a requirements completion percentage.

The tested base is `a86c99f18ec58cbeadbd195c0eb049f7a3c13013`, including the published shared typed-prefix foundation, Android Hysteria2 final-layer adoption, and Android typed-prefix host. These are local source/component results; the generated native package and system-VPN acceptance belong to the corresponding release and device gates.

| Gate | Result | Retained controls |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `android/test_android_node_candidate_preparation_ui.py` | 58 shipping dialog/Product checks | All 10 compiled refusal controls |
| `android/test_android_node_candidate_profile.py` | 117 detached source/drain/Save checks; 164 complete execution checks; new 3/4/8-node row matrices of 32/16/10 checks | All six original and four additive compiled refusal controls |
| `android/test_android_node_candidate_preparation_owner.py` | 73 composed process-owner checks | All seven original and three additive compiled refusal controls |
| `android/test_android_node_candidate_prefix_owner.py` | 79 composed prefix-owner checks | All eight existing compiled refusal controls |

The source/profile gate executes actual Java stores and actual shared Go graph compilers. The new matrix includes a real valid Hysteria2 source and verifies that an imported penultimate replacement receives the precise unsupported-final reason without rewriting the retained final. It also proves that unknown compiler errors, source mutation, and canceled owners remain fatal. The original Server/Compare Save/Load execution tests remain intact.

The owner gate executes the shipping supervisor, session, runtime, service, result and MTU ownership code against explicitly modeled OS/native/source boundaries. Its controls refuse arbitrary-exception swallowing, reverting a known incompatibility to whole-batch failure, and dropping a measured incompatible row. Existing permission, cleanup, replacement-owner and post-DOWN guards remain required. The UI gate compiles the shipping dialog and extracted Product callbacks against widget/owner test boundaries; it places an incompatible row before a valid proposal and checks the exact saved result identity.

`focused-source-validation.yml` requires the exact ordered ten supervisor markers. The original seven remain unchanged; the three new row controls are additive. The existing Android multihop workflow continues to run all four full gates. No child deadline was increased.

The initial UI pass before forwarding is preserved separately, followed by the successful current-base UI rerun. A read-only fixture review found that importing the independent paired test candidate changes the selected node. The unstarted profile phase was deliberately stopped before compiler execution. The corrected fixture observes that real side effect and reselects the original control through the shipping store API. Production selection validation was unchanged. Subsequent source/profile checks passed on the corrected fixture.

## Evidence boundaries

The exact source, generated source hashes, receipts, and the precompiler stop are retained under `android-candidate-proposal-results-validation`. The UI's final rerun preceded the fixture-only selection correction; its actual production and UI inputs are unchanged. Each later phase records the corrected complete source inventory before and after execution.

Current native packaging, Android system-VPN behavior, physical networking, and exact-release acceptance remain separate requirements. A TCP measurement and an explicit Save reason do not close the underlying unsupported complete graph, prove authentication, or authorize a live connection.

## docs/ANDROID-CONNECT-COMPLETE-COUNT-COVERAGE-2026-11-16.md

# Android CONNECT complete-count coverage — 2026-11-16

This adds coverage to the Android CONNECT Server/Compare implementation already
published in `9ac3f3a6075b282f4ecc193d5bb016acfae78f43`. It does not replace that
implementation or its final-Start-Layer-Off product boundary. The tested base is
`a1a1bbea` and the two-test source snapshot is
`467a9791f6bf09fbf35638e9ac99e606c40c800b`.

The source/profile gate now exercises all total counts three through eight with
HTTP and HTTPS CONNECT. The added five-, six-, and seven-node cases keep the
original mixed WireGuard/AmneziaWG positions and independently selected prefix
layers. Every positive case checks the complete proof inventory: global position,
paired node/profile identity, original mode and Start Layer, and an empty private
Router identity only for the imported penultimate proxy. Existing profile
Add/Load/Update/Delete, original credential references, replacement invalidation,
member/identity readback, malformed inputs, final-layer refusal, and all nine
causal controls remain active.

The host fixture also exercises every supported count rather than only three,
four, and eight. Both HTTP and HTTPS fixtures run the shipping session, VPN
service, MTU bridge, cleanup, and retained-restoration code. The typed factory,
native-proof, and foreign-restore causal controls remain independent.

Validation of the frozen source snapshot
`467a9791f6bf09fbf35638e9ac99e606c40c800b`:

- Actual shared Go compiler plus shipping Java stores/editor/capture: **1,401
  checks and all nine causal controls passed**, 473.663 seconds total. Every
  original Go compile and individual Java process bound was retained.
- Shipping Java host/restoration: **1,570 checks and all three causal controls
  passed** (671 lifecycle plus 114 restore checks for each proxy transport).
- Android workflow contract: **30 tests passed**, 37.070 seconds.
- All 5,933 tracked files were unchanged during the source gate. The five
  production files `AndroidNodeChainCapture`, `AndroidNodeChainExecutionCapture`,
  `AndroidNodeStore`, `AndroidOrderedPathSelection`, and `ProductActivity` were
  independently compared byte-for-byte with published `9ac3f3a6` and matched.

The tests retain the existing 120-second Go helper build, 180-second Go fixture
generator, 60-second javac, and 120-second Java process limits. The helper compiler used one CPU; the immutable
helper runtime used two CPUs. Validation used Go 1.26.3 and JDK 17.

This is source/profile and shipping Java ownership evidence. The host fixture
explicitly doubles native handles and OS services. It is not APK, Android-device,
TUN, encrypted-traffic, native lease, or installed-kernel acceptance evidence.
The earlier unpublished Android draft is not used as proof for the published
implementation or this test-only change.

## docs/ANDROID-CONNECT-FIFTY-VALIDATION.md

# Android CONNECT ordered fifty caller

## November 14 current composition

This caller extension follows the published Android SOCKS fifty caller
(`373f768c`), CONNECT host (`d791f483`), and retained native CONNECT fifty
factory (`43a6f29c`). The current native component passed all six HTTP/HTTPS
and WG/AWG Fast/AWG Strong combinations and seven compiled causal controls.
Its mandatory 44-phase exact-SHA workflow separately refreshes the complete
original proof, reference-lifecycle and fifty-response inventories. These local
results do not certify a successful hosted release or installed device.

The Android caller retains its distinct schema-four CONNECT factory and the
same service, worker, history and cleanup ownership described below. The
complete Java caller was rerun on `43a6f29c`, including the newer `cee86ee2`
store-fixture dependency: both transports passed 349 checks and all eleven
compiled causal controls, including Service destruction and original-owner
cleanup retry. The joined run took 133.951 seconds; its log SHA-256 is
`2c0cad8d57d7629fd0b3f7d4297f8873288b800b1fb00ef2f3197b949bc2cee6`.

The
generated SDK validator now requires both exact typed three-argument factories
and all seven `RouterOrderedLatency` methods from the same captured AAR. Its
19 parser and owned-checkout tests passed; an actual generated AAR/APK must
still pass its own build and artifact inspection.

The original review records below preserve their historical baseline and
dependency manifests. They are not instructions to replay those snapshots over
newer `main`, or claims that already-published prerequisites remain unpushed.

## Original reviewed source record

The original review composed the Android ordered-fifty caller with the distinct
schema-four CONNECT host on `cf7aa3e156dab296f77afe140b404b499c5cf22c`. It did not
change native admission, create a second VPN service, cast CONNECT to an
ordinary or SOCKS path, or substitute throughput idle samples for fifty
responses.

## Dependency composition

The original Android50 fifteen-path freeze remains unchanged at `android-fifty-reviewed-740747a5/manifest.json` (SHA256 `08c3ed27c98ac6b5a80e9bc31b48d6adccd7164b5fd29404bb8fb083a8335d58`). Its fifteen postimages match the Android portion of `fifty-composed-manifest-v2.json` (SHA256 `78c02a631c6a71c0128c8ce20faf653d1228cda0ab8438f3e1ba0e0882593573`).

The CONNECT20 dependency is `android-connect-reviewed-36a166b2/manifest.json`, patch SHA256 `23a0dbc409028fffb31216f6912a985c16d8828f710f996efa81d71c4640ddd3`. Its six overlaps with Android50 are the workflow, session, ordinary runtime fixture, SOCKS runtime fixture, runtime inventory and workflow inventory test. A guarded three-way composition preserves both sides. The session merged cleanly; the five explicit conflicts combined the independent gate inventories and typed capture fields. None of the original checks or controls was removed.

The thirteen native MTU dependency postimages remain byte-identical to `connect-mtu-current-working/connect-mtu-current-manifest.json`, patch SHA256 `914402f7b51d8671f7c99d87c5b29d4cabe8af49f2bcc2ee8cfc62506297a36d`. They are dependency inputs, excluded from the Android publication patch. Composition records retain main preimages, both reviewed dependency postimages, the guarded merged baseline, and the new host postimages separately.

## Shipping change

`AndroidNodeChainSession.newOrderedLatency` admits an already proved typed CONNECT owner and calls `routerNodeChainCONNECTNewLatencyBenchmark(capture.connect.plan, server, measurement)`. The third argument is the exact retained host measurement service. SOCKS continues through its own factory. The existing session retains the returned facade and retries its Close before retiring the original throughput service and Box. The dialog availability text now includes HTTP(S) CONNECT paths.

The original service, measurement worker and history store are unchanged from Android50. They retain the source/config/native service and Home/path/MTU generation, wait for MTU quiescence before attaching the facade, and keep the MTU hold through original request drain, facade Close and atomic history adoption. Cancellation, changed sources, Activity disappearance, failed Close and Service destruction preserve original cleanup ownership. A new benchmark after authorized MTU reproof borrows the same throughput service.

The three original ordered nodes retain their profile references, selected modes, credentials and selected DNS policy. The real schema-four compiler continues to enforce the original paired final SS UoT-v2 declaration and CONNECT role constraints. No new source/profile reconstruction occurs in the fifty caller.

Private Router HTTP responses include connection establishment. The custom middle uses public HTTPS response timing after DNS, TCP and TLS preparation. Neither is ICMP ping or individual inter-server link speed. Complete dated receipts remain separate historical records keyed to the exact path and response kind, bounded to 128 records and 1 MiB. Incomplete, failed, stale or cancelled adoption retains prior valid history; rollback never overwrites a newer writer.

## Validation

`android/test_android_connect_ordered_latency.py` executes the shipping complete service, session, runtime, worker, store and dialog for both HTTP CONNECT and HTTPS CONNECT. It reuses all eight original fifty case families and all nine original compiled controls. An additional destruction family verifies that a failed facade Close preserves the old service's executor, facade, original measurement service and Box until retry joins; it creates no replacement Box. Additional controls remove destruction join ordering or the typed CONNECT factory dispatch. Both transports passed 349 checks each and eleven compiled controls each.

The source/profile suite runs the actual shared Go parser: original ordered 1033 checks (including SOCKS80), hostname360, CONNECT328, HY2-final167 and all29 original compiled controls. It ran with `GOMEMLIMIT=1GiB`, `GOMAXPROCS=1`, existing timeout bounds and capped Java. The final Java runner serializes all gates with `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS='-Xmx512m -XX:ActiveProcessorCount=2'`; the new fixture also explicitly caps each java/javac process at512MiB. Every gate retains its existing subprocess timeouts and checks.

The focused final regression results and exact log/source hashes are recorded in `android-connect-fifty-evidence/java-serial.json` and the frozen evidence manifest. They include the original SOCKS fifty, CONNECT/SOCKS host, ordinary execution, complete-service type, syntax and workflow gates.

## Separate acceptance boundaries

Native CONNECT50 uses the distinct factory declared in `connect-fifty-native-recovery/mobile/chain/connect_latency.go.tmpl`; its eight-path manifest SHA256 is `9b6edf7a95f6f833e0684cb56972a19f49e2eed164fc18508799b093ddbd6fbe`. Native factory execution and encrypted-path acceptance are separate from these Java tests and are not claimed here.

The SDK audit owner supplied a separate one-file validator follow-on at `connect-android-sdk-fifty-verification/manifest.json`. It checks the seven `RouterOrderedLatency` methods and both exact three-argument typed factories, preserving the prior CONNECT/SOCKS API inventory. That validator and the native fifty prerequisites must be composed by the release owner; they are not republished in this Android delta. No generated AAR, APK or installed-device acceptance was executed here. Java native handles, Android widgets and filesystem syscalls are explicit boundaries; only the source/profile parser uses actual shared Go code.

## docs/ANDROID-CONNECT-FINAL-LAYERS-2026-11-16.md

# Android CONNECT final Start Layers

Android's ordered HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT editor, Save preview and launch capture
retain independently selected Off, AES-256-GCM and AES-256-GCM plus XOR whitening
on the original final paired SS2022 member. The complete source remains schema 4
with three through eight original nodes and one through six paired WG/AWG
prefixes. The imported CONNECT member still requires Off. No factory, source
schema, session identity or saved-profile representation changes in this unit.

The removed final-Off product checks are replaced by the existing actual native
source compiler, not by host-created final configuration. `memberInput` still
requires the canonical layer choices and sends the full raw profile JSON to
`routerNodeChainMemberInput`. `NewNodeProfile` calls `externalOptionalString`,
which refuses non-string/noncanonical values, and `ValidLayerMode`, which refuses
unknown and XOR-only modes. The typed execution preview still compares the full
returned node/identity arrays with the original source before Save. The original
final cipher/key, predecessor, UoT-v2 service and proof inventory remain captured.
Native ownership/retirement and real encrypted UoT were separately validated and
published in `e31d7d20`; this consumer does not add a new native capability.

## Validation boundaries

The shipping-Java host gate passed 2,254 checks and all three compiled causal
controls: 671 lifecycle, 114 restore and 342 independent final-layer checks for
each of HTTP and HTTPS. The layered cases cover three and eight nodes, AES and
AES-plus-XOR, explicit Server and both retained Compare winners. They retain the
same original final private source and complete identity inventory, restore the
selected winner without another comparison, stage the retained MTU 1360 config,
reprove cold Last, and refuse an old Last after a final-layer change. The native
handles, OS services and source-capture fixture in this host gate are explicit
doubles; it does not prove APK, device, TUN, encrypted traffic or a remote lease.

The ordinary actual shared-Go/shipping-Java source family passed 1,353 checks
and all seven retained member/identity, complete-prefix and full-source causal
controls in 674.609 seconds, under the original 900-second local caller bound.
Its 6,055 tracked inputs remained unchanged during that run. This retains the
complete-count HTTP/HTTPS source/profile CRUD, malformed inputs and readback
coverage. The old final-Off rejection cases are replaced by actual independent
layer acceptance and canonical malformed-layer refusals in the additional gates.

The first complete layered source attempt passed all 1,704 positive checks over
counts 3–8, both proxy protocols, both encrypted final layers, and Server/Compare.
It then failed at 892.236 seconds because the new malformed-value fixture reused
an editor after Save. The shipping consumed-editor guard correctly refused that
setup before the malformed-value assertion began. The failure is retained;
it is not reported as a passing full gate. The test now reopens a fresh editor
from the original source. No production consumed-editor guard changed.

The new source coverage is divided into two required 15-minute jobs. One contains
counts 3–5, HTTP malformed-layer cases and the three compiled counterfactuals
restoring the former editor/preview/capture Off-only restrictions. The other
contains counts 6–8 and HTTPS malformed-layer cases. Both jobs are required by the
workflow aggregate and by the executable runtime inventory. The ordinary source
job remains separate. An exact disjoint 14-phase inventory prevents omission,
duplication, changed child bounds or lost generator/dispatcher calls. Every
helper build (120 seconds), actual Go fixture generator (180 seconds), javac (60 seconds) and Java process (120 seconds)
bound is unchanged.

For the final partition runs, the actual previously compiled Go helper and
private fixture were retained. All 3,056 current tracked Go/Java inputs and exact
generated helper/generator source bytes were checked against the original
compiler run. Every positive Java harness byte was unchanged; only the malformed
fixture's consumed-editor line changed. That harness was recompiled normally
against the retained 108 baseline classes. Each complete partition then executes
the shipping `run_prepared` function, including every positive/malformed case and
its applicable compiled controls. This reuse is explicit; no compiler output is
fabricated and no Go generator invocation is replaced in the shipping gates.

The extended partition passed all 1,008 checks in 455.797 seconds. The first
short partition passed its 744 positive checks, then failed creating the HTTP
malformed fixture because the filesystem was full, at 248.479 seconds. After
space recovery, the short retry passed all 792 baseline checks, including its
48 malformed-value assertions. Its first causal variant then exposed a harness
error: the injected old editor restriction rejected the execution choice before
another Save dialog existed, and the harness tried to use that cleared dialog.
That whole process failed at 305.310 seconds and remains recorded as a failure.

The correction applies only to the `editor-final-off` counterfactual. Its
separately compiled harness requires the exact injected `old Off-only editor`
message immediately after the choice before raising the intended refusal
witness. An unrelated dialog error cannot satisfy that check. The baseline
harness remains byte-for-byte equal to the proven 792/1,008-check source,
including every malformed assertion. All three current compiled controls passed
in 14.436 seconds. Their mandatory helper is called by the same short partition;
the workflow contract rejects omission of that call or relaxation of its exact
message check. Thus the final proof composes the already-executed fourteen
baseline phases with the corrected three controls, without relabeling either
failed process as a full pass.

The final workflow audit passed all 32 tests in 76.757 seconds, including
actual shell result propagation and phase/control omission counterfactuals. The source proof base includes published CONNECT native
ownership `e31d7d20` and the subsequent Apple/OpenVPN changes through `45c5f9f4`.
The publication was composed on `9bbc2dee`; every changed Android preimage
matched the original proof base, and the later desktop/OpenVPN and Apple
consumer changes were preserved. Public Android source and host inputs remain
independent of those later desktop-only additions. Installed-device/native acceptance remains separate.


## Retained evidence

The task validation directory retains the full source manifests, actual Go
helper/generator sources, private generated fixtures and the Java class hashes.
The relevant log identities are:

- Ordinary source: `21f676ef19f0b6fcdbce059c7f14b27cf1738715a53240804583dc0237d6d96c`.
- Original consumed-editor failure: `623da88ad24c3c11372ffd1aecdae2eeaf961b02043f8e758a05f90f16bf535a`.
- Short partition ENOSPC: `d73af6c79e2586da310512dcc9201dd00c0e6df8d2f4eca0234d3e905c007596`.
- Short baseline plus failed old causal harness: `af393723b2ec36a78640fe37752e00eff388a8fe185d1794dc504c7b7ba3c4c9`.
- Extended partition: `0a4e1135111f7ef2f07278b8806f071f9639d64ed059f5ab096deab5d737baeb`.
- Corrected three controls: `730854c5790e73325bab092272c43f9b6086bfd020f513165e9e70632357933f`.
- Reused compiler/108-class receipt: `2f516f16fea229c1fd991ef861ec47b640332f265e3b2ff560f53b311afa9ef3`.
- Host source/log receipt: `1c5a3de84490840387d7787e00c58924a806f1bbb417a2980711f5e835b9b42d`.

## Current private-state source contract

The private-state workflow at `261c396e` failed in job `133208058163` because
its exact profile-dispatch inventory still omitted the two newly published
CONNECT final-layer families. The source contract now requires each family's
own compiler and its bounded return branch, while preserving every existing
ordered family. Eight added omission, substitution, return-removal and duplicate
controls supplement the original eighteen dispatch controls. The complete
actual source contract passed with all twenty-six controls; production profile
behavior and Java/native execution gates are unchanged. This closes the
observed source-inventory failure and is separate from Android device acceptance.

## docs/ANDROID-CONNECT-HOST-VALIDATION.md

# Android typed CONNECT host adoption

The ordered Android path now keeps a distinct schema-four `RouterNodeChainCONNECT` owner for a paired WG/AWG first node, an imported HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT middle, and the original paired Shadowsocks 2022 UoT-v2 final. It preserves the original imported credentials and TLS server name, paired identity assets, selected final DNS, protection settings, and complete private source. Generic NodeChain execution and candidate-prefix constructors explicitly reject this distinct owner.

The existing `LayeredVpnService` owns the same single CommandServer, physical network, and system VPN. Its session dispatches through the typed CONNECT constructor, retains the returned server before explicit native binding, then uses typed Run, health, progress, hop measurement, and MTU methods. Binding failure, cancellation, pending proof/MTU retirement, and native teardown retain the original cleanup owner until its positive native receipt. The service implementation itself is unchanged.

Saved ordered profiles retain references rather than duplicate secrets. Canonical CONNECT mode names are mapped back to the external store's HTTP/HTTPS protocol names only for validated ordered policy rows. Editor preview and replay use the distinct source compiler. A CONNECT final without its exact source/key-bound UoT-v2 service declaration is rejected; no generic UDP capability is inferred from the middle.

This unit depends on the separately frozen CONNECT MTU thirteen-file increment, SHA-256 `914402f7b51d8671f7c99d87c5b29d4cabe8af49f2bcc2ee8cfc62506297a36d`. The Android source adds no divergent native implementation. AAR verification requires the exact generated CONNECT factory, capture, bind, Run, measurement, MTU, and native-close APIs. Generated SDK verification and native MTU acceptance remain separate prerequisites; Java boundary doubles do not prove those results or installed-device behavior.

The whole-service host fixture covers HTTP and HTTPS with WireGuard, AmneziaWG Fast, and AmneziaWG Strong, restoration, binding/factory failure, source retirement, pending cleanup, and missing native receipts. It executes the actual shipping session/runtime/service with explicit OS/native doubles and compiled omission controls. The ordered source/profile fixture invokes the real shared Go compiler and tests the original ordinary/SOCKS/Hysteria inventories plus CONNECT credentials, TLS name, final Home AdGuard DNS, one TUN, UoT-v2, canonical replay, saved references, and source replacement.

The existing frozen Android fifty-sample increment is independent. Its typed factory is still SOCKS-only; this CONNECT host increment does not claim a CONNECT fifty-sample benchmark or candidate optimizer. Native encrypted traffic, exact-main release, generated SDK, installed-device, and physical MTU evidence retain their own gates. No global platform admission matrix is opened by this source change.


---

## docs/ANDROID-CONNECT-PREFIX-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Android complete CONNECT Server/Compare paths

Recorded November 16, 2026 UTC. This addresses the original ordered external
node/hop requirements and preserves the requirement authority in
[REQUIREMENTS-RECONCILED.md](REQUIREMENTS-RECONCILED.md). It extends Android's
shipping host adoption of the already-published complete CONNECT compiler.
Source, host lifecycle, encrypted networking, packaging and physical-device
acceptance remain separate evidence.

## Shipping behavior

The actual Android ordered editor, private selection store, typed source
capture and returned execution validation now retain three to eight original
members for HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT Server or Compare: one to six paired WG/AWG
prefixes, the penultimate imported CONNECT proxy and an independent paired
Shadowsocks 2022 final. Every paired member keeps its private identity; only
the imported member has an empty paired-identity slot. Source and configuration
readback continue to require the complete original arrays and exact typed
execution authority.

Complete profile Add, Load, Update and Delete keep the requested execution,
original node references and current private credentials. Replacement of the
proxy credentials or a later paired prefix invalidates the older capture.
Neither saving nor deleting a complete profile copies credentials into profile
rows or deletes the referenced private nodes.

CONNECT Server/Compare still requires the original final node's Start Layer
to be Off. The editor, transactional source preview and typed launch capture
enforce that actual native boundary. Local CONNECT keeps its existing layer
behavior. The separately typed SOCKS execution retains its current exact
three-member bound; the earlier four-to-eight-node Shadowsocks hostname path
and saved Custom TCP resolver rules are preserved. These boundaries do not
close the remaining requested graph and layer combinations.

## Executed validation

The tested source was `6b9e91ab1179cb55cf1cc863dd1ee4c4c78182fd` plus the
nine-file implementation/test patch with SHA-256
`5f4b981c03feaefab205fa2dee2488ea7cb919c37429a237d33c89513530d1c7`.
The documentation was added after validation. Tests used Go 1.26.3, JDK
17.0.21 and the actual Android JSON library; source bytes stayed unchanged
during each accepted run.

| Required gate | Accepted result | Boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `android/test_android_connect_execution_capture.py` | 757 checks across nine mandatory phases, plus all nine compiled causal controls; 347.04 seconds | Actual Java editor/store/capture and shared Go compiler. Native Run, leases, TUN and restoration authority explicitly refuse in this source fixture. |
| `android/test_android_connect_execution_runtime.py` | 398 shipping Java lifecycle checks, 60 retained-restoration checks and all three compiled causal controls; 23.76 seconds | Actual Session, Runtime, execution capture, CONNECT capture and VpnService composition, with native capsules and Android OS handles explicitly modeled. |

The source gate retains the original three-node positive/refusal/readback
inventory, independently exercises four- and eight-node complete paths, and
rejects missing/replaced later members, wrong identities, incorrect source
families, two/nine-member malformed sources and invalid final layers. Its
compiled controls remove original member/identity verification, restore each
old three-node admission limit, omit final-layer admission, and corrupt the
shared constructor's complete source or packet-prefix graph. Each fails at
its intended witness.

The host gate exercises three-, four- and eight-node Server, Compare-to-Server
and Compare-to-Local ownership, Last and temporary restoration. Original
factory, proof, cancellation, replacement-owner and foreign-restore controls
remain required. A restored proved execution is retained without a new
unrelated Compare decision.

The accepted source log SHA-256 is
`091968afbe6ca6e5f9d129fe4fc9f996d6b1383812ceb9b29990d46956d6e1ee`;
the accepted lifecycle log SHA-256 is
`3a1da62a80064576026b74ddeeae825a258618fb2a87d434045be09db7b8eac9`.

## Preserved limits and outstanding acceptance

An earlier combined eight-node HTTP/HTTPS profile phase reached its original
120-second process limit. That failed attempt is retained separately and is
not acceptance evidence. The same complete HTTP and HTTPS inventories now run
in separate mandatory phases, each with the unchanged 120-second limit.
Existing compiler limits, production deadlines and negative controls were not
relaxed.

Both executable gates remain mandatory in the Android runtime contract.
Exact-current APK/ABI builds, real encrypted Server/Compare traffic, selected
DNS and leak checks, network transitions, real-device UI/permission behavior
and final package/publication acceptance require their own results. Broader
SOCKS execution, other imported positions/families, remaining Start Layers,
private deployment and all original live requirements remain accountable.

## docs/ANDROID-DNS-RETEST-FIXTURE.md

# Android DNS Retest transaction witness

Android multihop run [43163222577](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/43163222577),
job 130999202348, failed before the DNS Retest assertions because the disposable
Java projection did not declare its `dnsChecked` test hook. The fixture anchored
that declaration to the old non-volatile `selectionGeneration` field; the shipping
store now uses a volatile generation.

The hook is now attached to the exact owning class declaration, with a required
single-match check. The original store field, visibility, volatile qualifier,
writer lock, and publication code are preserved. The hook still pauses the real
compare/publication boundary so the test can attempt a concurrent selection.

The corrected source passed all 80 DNS Retest checks using the actual Java
callbacks, loopback HTTP request, and store transactions. All five existing
compiled controls were rejected at their intended assertions: atomic writer
ownership, generation freshness, latest operation, dialog dismissal, and socket
cancellation. Android widgets and native admission remain explicit fixture
boundaries; this is not a device-routing or selected-DNS tunnel receipt.

## docs/ANDROID-HOSTNAME-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Android hostname execution editor evidence — November 16, 2026

The Android ordered-path editor previously applied numeric-address parsing to every imported Server or Compare member. That prevented a saved Shadowsocks 2022 hostname from reaching the already implemented shared execution compiler.

`AndroidOrderedPathSelection.requireBound` now admits the original hostname only in the penultimate Shadowsocks position of a schema-two path with four to eight members. It reuses `AndroidStandardExitStore.validateImportedHostnamePolicy`, requiring the original Custom TCP resolver, explicit canonical resolver IP and port, and valid saved SS2022 credentials. A three-member hostname path remains rejected before changing the saved execution. Other imported protocols retain strict numeric-address parsing.

## Executable evidence

The existing required `android/test_android_imported_prefix_execution_capture.py` gate ran the shipping Java editor callbacks, stores, Add/Load/Update/Delete profile transactions and source captures against the actual shared Go compiler. All nine original phases remain. Four added hostname phases cover the four- and eight-member bounds, all three SS2022 methods, Server/Compare choices, selected transport DNS versus final Home DNS, saved resolver restoration, later-prefix replacement and malformed source readback.

The complete run passed 13 phases with 3,742 reported source/profile checks and seven compiled causal controls. The controls retain the original node-store, editor, returned-size, member and identity witnesses, and additionally restore numeric-only hostname admission or remove the four-member minimum. Each modified program failed at its intended assertion.

The source run used base `163c80fdc328d7677f617a271a492b234aa9d9e1` plus the two-file source patch with SHA-256 `ada3d1772ee642e21a39a771d6ed604fdd74f1c7b63b3a6801d3d75167220e69`. Source bytes were unchanged throughout the run. The full gate completed in 720.79 seconds, retaining the existing 120-second Java phase limits and 15-minute enclosing budget. Its log SHA-256 is `a22ba2f9913884fc036bdb16da7d0593f99c7d939c0181cafee62c7747c8073c`.

The local toolchain was Go 1.26.3 with Java 17 and the real Android JSON implementation. Initial attempts stopped at missing local JSON/JDK setup and are retained separately; they are not product failures or successful execution evidence.

## Evidence boundary

This gate compiles actual original source and exercises the shipping Java product path. Native Run, leases, TUN and restoration capsules are explicit refusing boundaries. It does not establish device networking, encrypted traffic, Android permission behavior, APK publication or private deployment. Those remain separate native/release/device gates. This change does not expand HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT or SOCKS source bounds.

## docs/ANDROID-HYSTERIA2-FINAL-LAYERS-2026-11-16.md

# Android original Hysteria2 final layers — November 16, 2026

Android's ordered Local, Server and Compare choices now retain the paired final
node's independent Off, AES-256-GCM or AES-256-GCM plus XOR whitening source.
The existing schema-1 graph still requires two through eight original paired
members, with WG/AWG packet prefixes. Imported-middle families retain their
separate Shadowsocks-final boundary. No new native factory or source schema is
introduced.

The final picker checks the exact selected node's captured member when an
authenticated layer is requested. Copying another node's Hysteria asset into a
layered bundle does not acquire this choice. Existing Off eligibility retains
its original paired/mode checks without an extra member compilation; complete
Save still invokes the original full source compiler. The remote saved-selection
guard checks the original value's type and exact spelling, including rejection
of null, non-string, unknown, alias and XOR-only values. Missing or empty values
retain the established Off convention.

Original TLS, password, CA, inner destination, outer cipher/key, whitening,
packet-prefix detour, private WG identity, selected DNS and complete profile
references remain owned by their original captures. The tests check that the
outer layer belongs to that same final node and does not substitute UoT. Last
reopens the original source and requested/selected choice; it does not repeat
Compare or claim a live native proof from a saved record.

## Executed evidence and exact inputs

The proof used `5290bb69a778e7efbee0efc04e23380903195b3f` plus the four frozen
shared Hysteria final-layer files and this Android source. Those four shared
files subsequently published unchanged in
`4ae6a3c4fc9a1480ba4c730cfb94a5d3891b0e9e`. The initial Android-only composition
used `8aabd9f879eb9ef6d7a0337eaaa487e5fc1687fc`; it excluded those
already-published dependencies. At that composition, all 692 internal/mobile
production Go files and 126 shipping Java files matched the frozen proof.
Added unrelated tests and desktop/Apple changes were preserved, not relabeled
as part of the older execution.

Publication then advanced to `d03b40c9eed6212334d9caa43d183138d928b817`.
Its seventeen-file typed SOCKS/CONNECT saved-prefix preparation foundation is
preserved. It changes the shared candidate-preparation and desktop projection
helpers, adds a distinct typed preparation source, and updates their native
bridge/tests. None of the six Android executable/source files overlaps that
change; all six final bytes still match this proof. This guarded-forward source
review is not a new execution of the newer shared package: the helper and test
image remain attributed to the original 5290-plus-frozen-dependency inputs.

| Actual focused check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Original Off Local source/editor/profile phase | 167 Java/shared-Go checks passed. |
| AES and AES+XOR Local phases | 174 checks each passed, covering 2/3/4/8-member WG/AWG paths. |
| Off, AES and AES+XOR Server/Compare phases | 87 checks each passed, including complete 2/8-member profile transactions and malformed/source refusals. |
| Original-source Last matrices | Six shared Go leaves per layer passed: both lengths, Server, Compare selecting Local, and Compare selecting Server. |
| Java Last reopening and refusal | 66 checks per layer passed against the actual generated records. |

The focused inventory totals **974 Java checks, 18 shared Last leaves and five
compiled controls**. These are local inventories, not a product-completion
denominator. Native session/proof/OS behavior remains an explicit fixture
boundary; this is not Android SDK, APK, physical VPN or encrypted native traffic
acceptance.

The actual source helper compiled in 23.803 seconds under its 120-second bound.
The source/Last test image compiled in 87.020 seconds under the original
180-second fixture budget. Unchanged Go/module and generated source hashes were
verified before reusing those images. Java compilation retained 60 seconds;
Local and execution children retained their original 180/120-second bounds.
The final continuation used a 768 MiB JVM heap and completed each child normally.

Earlier attempts remain recorded: an external observer failed before runtime
because it supplied a duplicate environment keyword; a later XOR JVM was

pass. The old-Off Local mutation correctly failed at the earlier final-picker
assertion, rather than the later setter error initially expected. Only that

inputs stayed byte-identical, and all five controls were then executed.

Two replays of the original six-family execution aggregate exceeded its

The new exact-source mandatory host/aggregate CI result remains pending. The

## docs/ANDROID-IMPORTED-EXECUTION-PREFIX-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Android complete imported Shadowsocks execution coverage

The production path published in `e319c1a62a34d4bc6fadc551e9ae1c7617b0a4fc`
is preserved. Android's ordered editor, complete saved profiles and owned native
preparation retain three to eight nodes for Server/Compare: one to six paired
WireGuard/AWG packet prefixes, one penultimate literal-address imported
Shadowsocks 2022 member, and an independent paired Shadowsocks 2022 final.
The independently typed SOCKS5 and CONNECT execution families keep their own
three-node bounds and native factories.

This follow-on adds a mandatory source/compiler/profile gate and its workflow
inventory. It does not replace the concurrent implementation or replay an older
production snapshot. All four relevant production store, editor and capture
classes are the upstream versions. The original execution fixture, selected-path
latency, lifecycle, Last and restoration changes are also preserved.

## Executed matrix

`android/test_android_imported_prefix_execution_capture.py` executes actual
shipping Java stores, ordered editor callbacks and capture classes against the
actual shared Go compiler. It completed with all 2,880 checks and five compiled
causal controls passing. Test counts describe executable assertions, not a
requirement-completion percentage.

| Original path inventory | Passing checks |
|---|---:|
| One paired packet prefix; three total nodes | 246 |
| Two paired packet prefixes; four total nodes | 288 |
| Three paired packet prefixes; five total nodes | 330 |
| Four paired packet prefixes; six total nodes | 372 |
| Five paired packet prefixes; seven total nodes | 414 |
| Six paired packet prefixes; eight total nodes | 456 |
| Mixed WG/AWG prefixes; four total nodes | 288 |
| Mixed WG/AWG prefixes; eight total nodes | 456 |

Every positive path length exercises Server and Compare with all three supported
SS2022 ciphers. Complete Add/Load/Update/Delete transactions retain ordered
private-node references, modes, selected execution and original credentials.
Saved profile records do not acquire copied proxy credentials. Canonical source
replay must preserve all members and paired identity assets. Replacing a later
paired prefix invalidates its captured execution.

Readback failures substitute a later member, later identity, omitted prefix,
forged imported identity or final source. Each is rejected for both execution
choices. The five compiled causal controls restore the old node-store, editor
or returned-source trio restriction, or remove the original member or identity
comparison. Each must fail at its particular witness. No successful native
connection, proof or restore capsule is fabricated by the source fixture.

The mixed four- and eight-node groups have separate required invocations under
the same 120-second Java limit. They retain every original case. Go fixture
generation keeps its 180-second limit and Java compilation its 60-second limit.
No production deadline or existing gate bound changes in this unit.

## Required integration

The Android multihop workflow executes the new gate and includes its push and
pull-request path triggers. The native runtime contract also requires it.
All 29 integrated workflow tests passed, including omission, duplication, substitution and
aggregate-completion controls. The connection-profile safety contract passed
with the new dedicated compiler/dispatch family and its refusal controls.

The shared fixture generator adds the two mixed-prefix inputs needed by this
matrix. Its old Local-source fixture now expects the newly supported longer
Shadowsocks Server/Compare choices to be selectable before restoring Local for
that fixture's remaining checks. Existing private identity, unsupported-position
and malformed-source rejection cases remain required. The original complete
ordered-source gate is still a separate mandatory regression; this evidence does
not label an incomplete aggregate run as passed.

## Evidence boundary

Native Run, remote leases, proof, TUN and live restoration are explicit refusing
boundaries in this source gate. Generated bindings, actual encrypted Server
traffic, APK provenance, publication and physical Android networking require
their own evidence. Permission, selected-path DNS, leaks, transitions, visual
checks, signing and upgrade acceptance are not established by this matrix.

Multiple imports, other imported positions, longer typed SOCKS/CONNECT execution
and other requested graph families remain separately accountable. Safe refusal
does not close those requirements or establish full-product delivery.

## docs/ANDROID-MULTIPLE-IMPORTED-LOCAL-2026-11-16.md

# Android complete Local imported captures

Android now retains one or several independently saved literal Shadowsocks 2022 nodes at original interior positions in a bounded three-to-eight-node Local path. The entry remains paired WG/AWG and the final remains paired Shadowsocks. Each imported member retains its own private source and public identity; paired members retain their original identity, assets, Start Layer and MTU policy. Existing single-penultimate schema-two and typed SOCKS, CONNECT and OpenVPN contracts stay separate. This unit does not admit schema-six Server/Compare or candidate measurement.

The visible editor and source capture use the complete shape. Profile selection and restoration stage every imported member by its original ID, compile the complete proposed source before writes, and roll back the original private profiles on failure. The native preview must return the exact nine-field sealed Local launch, complete nodes and identity inventory, and matching source/config/metadata receipts; it is then reopened through the sealed native parser. Ordinary Local history accepts each original interior public position while retaining the complete source/request/role/50-response contract. Selected Server/Compare history retains its existing penultimate boundary.

## Actual consumer evidence

| Executed gate | Original source | Result | Elapsed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| New independent-import Local gate | 1f34b5e36cf1e2af00269acb8be2e4235f12711f | 14 phases, 921 checks, six compiled controls | 161.665 s / 900 s |
| Profile selection ownership | 241a6da52781865e775b52bc6792c538112d8a71 | 57 checks, seven controls | 26.507 s |
| Existing imported profile family | 241a6da52781865e775b52bc6792c538112d8a71 | 927 checks, four controls | 147.481 s |
| Ordinary fifty-response host | 241a6da52781865e775b52bc6792c538112d8a71 | 245 checks, two controls | 6.986 s |

| Selected execution fifty-response host | 1771bc22110666fd72d33cdc0876c1ab9c1fee2b | 2,089 checks, eleven families, thirteen controls | 75.195 s |

The new gate covers adjacent/separated imports, early single imports, maximum paths, WG and both AWG modes, independent layers, malformed sources, rollback, stale captures, source races, actual compiler refusal, sealed-preview readback and history. The existing source/profile gates execute the actual shared Go compiler. Host fixtures retain explicit framework/native boundaries. All observed children joined and all source snapshots remained unchanged. Original child deadlines and control inventories were preserved; the three latency gates retain the original 900-second aggregate inventory, accounting for the completed ordinary phase when resuming after the fixture correction.

Two pre-runtime fixture failures were retained and corrected. The first selection fixture lacked the CONNECT member forwarder already implemented by its real Go dispatcher (javac failure, 4.094 s). The identical forwarder now lives in the base fixture, with conditional duplicate insertion in the ordered fixture. The history fixture omitted the complete-shape class now called by shipping NodeStore (javac failure, 2.176 s). It now composes the exact shipping sourceSchema, requireLiteralImportedSS and requireCompleteShape bodies, while native construction remains a strictly refusing boundary. Their passing reruns above retain every original negative control.

## Publication composition and limits

The frozen publication source is a2c86908395056baf7b417ba48100ef945135d8d on e485d89c. It preserves the newer Android typed-candidate feature and all its workflow jobs. The merged Android source inventory is 48 commands, with both new typed-connected commands and this Local gate required. The actual profile safety contract and five affected workflow inventory/omission tests passed on that composition in 1.155 s, without invoking a compiler. The builder addition is one required input: android/test_android_multiple_imported_local.py.

The final composition over `15705d18a960edeae5de1d5b3fcfe5ac9b6af5d8` also passed the actual profile safety contract and five focused workflow tests in 1.206 seconds. Those checks exercise the 48-command inventory, omitted/duplicated commands, shard result propagation, runtime capture and aggregate bypass; they do not invoke a native compiler. Newer Server candidate builder inputs remain intact.

Earlier executable receipts remain attributed to their original heads. The publication retains the tested Java/shared-source implementation; the later typed-candidate measurement class is outside these executed fixture closures. The explicit test-boundary corrections, workflow union and static composition checks are recorded separately. No primary gate was relabeled as a later-source run.

The shared/native Local foundation is independently published as 238eb571; its own source/native/physical evidence is separate. This consumer unit proves shipping Java source, shared compiler use, private-store transactions and bounded host behavior. It does not establish an APK build, Android device/kernel behavior, or a new encrypted native-path verdict. Mandatory generated SDK and device acceptance remain their own gates.

Exact commands, tool identities, generated helper hashes, before/after source manifests, intended failure witnesses and joined child receipts are retained under multiple-imported-consumer-validation. The Android publication manifest records all fifteen consumer/workflow Git blobs and SHA-256 values, with the native-builder registration supplied only as an additive hunk so newer upstream inputs are preserved.

## docs/ANDROID-OPENVPN-EXECUTION-2026-11-16.md

# Android typed OpenVPN Server and Compare — November 16, 2026

This source increment carries the original sealed ordered Local OpenVPN capture into a distinct Server/Compare preparation. It admits three through eight original members: one through six paired WireGuard/AWG prefixes, one independently paired OpenVPN member, and the paired Shadowsocks 2022 final. Original member order, source identities, private credentials, selected packet modes, independent final Start Layers and native source ownership remain bound to the capture. It does not grant a generic execution parser authority over schema 5.

## Source and saved profiles

The four-field preparation contains exactly `schema`, `execution`, `local_source` and `openvpn_local_launch`. The fourth field is the original sealed Local launch, including its original selection, source, profile and native projection. Android passes that complete launch to the distinct OpenVPN `FromLaunch` and `FromLast` factories; it never reconstructs one from stripped JSON. The typed preparation launch retains its own kind. Full source members and identities are compared on both initial compilation and reopening.

The ordered editor, Save preview and reference-only profile store now retain this typed choice. Save previews the actual shared compiler before changing durable state. Unpaired OpenVPN profiles remain usable under their existing Local contract but cannot manufacture independent Server credentials. Replacing any captured original provider, packet-prefix member or credential invalidates the old capture. Loading a saved profile resolves fresh source references before creating a new operation.

The shipping session chooses the distinct OpenVPN CommandServer factory and keeps the existing original connection, cancellation, retirement and opaque Last restoration ownership. A temporary trial restores the selected retained Last through its typed factory; it does not rerun Compare. Final Off, AES-256-GCM and AES-256-GCM with XOR whitening remain independent captured choices. No CONNECT-specific final-Off restriction is copied into this full-packet OpenVPN path.

## Verification

The full actual shared source gate passed all 28 baseline phases, totaling 1,977 compiler/store checks, and all four compiled negative controls in 821.775 seconds. This includes helper and fixture-generator compilation, Java compilation, all runtime phases and controls. The complete run is below the unchanged 900-second workflow job bound; it is not a hosted-job receipt. All 5,988 recorded source inputs remained unchanged during the run.

The source matrix runs actual shipping Java capture/editor/store code against the compiled Go source constructors. Its exported provider fixtures use real `nativeopenvpn.DescribePeer` and `ValidatePublicPair` with independently captured Local and Server credentials. All six path lengths exercise TCP and UDP, and all six exercise both non-Off final layers. Profiles and changed-readback controls retain every original source position. Four compiled Java counterfactuals remove the typed preview, sealed-launch readback, full member readback or full identity readback and must fail their designated witness. The native execution boundary in this source fixture actively refuses forwarding; source construction is not native traffic evidence.

The new shipping Java host gate passed 803 checks (689 lifecycle and 114 restore) plus four compiled negative controls. It covers all six path lengths for Server and both Compare winners, cold Last restoration, MTU, cancellation, failed operations and retry. The native/OS interfaces and native Last capsules are explicit doubles. Replacing the typed factory, using generic construction/Last, or returning a foreign restore owner fails the intended assertion.

Adjacent shipping host checks passed unchanged: CONNECT 1,570 checks plus three controls, SOCKS 785 checks plus three controls, and the existing Local OpenVPN host 159 checks plus its drain-omission control. The Android workflow contract passed 31 tests. The profile contract exercises the actual shell ABI verification loop with a complete header listing and each of the four required static methods missing. This proves the guard, not a generated AAR. Python parsing, shell syntax and diff checks also passed.

The source helper, fixture generator, Java compilation and individual runtime phases keep their original 120/180/60/120-second bounds. The new required workflow source and host jobs retain the existing fifteen-minute job bound; both contribute to the strict aggregate. No baseline case, causal witness or original deadline was removed.

## Dependency and native boundary

The source proof uses published base `af28f808a2be989cfcb2d124f2541670a0df791a` with the frozen shared schema-5 client v7 dependency, full manifest SHA-256 `37610aab926ccec249015eaef9d23db7fd3fb125970063d9eb5d92442e35f0e6`. Its v6-to-v7 delta is `93eb7ddb6b137520c1d79283f243c4af0809415619c45e758b06155b5eeef10e`. V7 preserves original source/Box ownership while requiring connected Local OpenVPN observation only for actual Local candidates; Server discovery cannot depend on an already connected Local trial. The twenty shared Go source/test inputs now byte-match published `0f07335da94b015fbc73d2581f29cbe16dbc9047`. Six bridge template inputs remain a separate native dependency: this source helper does not compile those templates. The Android-only patch does not republish private shared snapshot files. Concurrent unrelated main changes retain their own evidence.

Actual generated gomobile/AAR exports, native Android provider/CommandServer execution, VPN routing, device lifecycle and packaged acceptance require their separate mandatory native gates. The Java source and host results do not substitute for those results or establish physical Android acceptance. The broader original custom-node, independent-hop, per-hop measurement and all-device requirements remain accountable; this bounded typed path does not close every requested graph.

### Publication composition

This Android-only change is composed on `a17541aa`, which contains the distinct
mobile OpenVPN bridge and its four-field factory boundary. The current native
owner gates, concrete OpenVPN build tags and cache-input closure are preserved.
All 20 other implementation and gate files byte-match the frozen Android proof; the combined Android
build script differs only because it also retains those newer native gates.
The exact four-method AAR verification loop was exercised again after merging.
This does not replace generated AAR, packaged build or physical-device evidence.

## docs/ANDROID-ORDERED-FIFTY-VALIDATION.md

# Android ordered fifty caller and history

This increment adds the shipping Android caller for the distinct typed SOCKS ordered-fifty API. It is based on `740747a5` and requires the shared `RouterOrderedLatency` API with the exact existing `RouterHopMeasurement` as its third factory argument. The initial shared dependency manifest is `connect-fifty-current-working/fifty-current-manifest.json`, patch SHA256 `7589e990afb26743610bd148ea56427a55f5b711e625ddf02a0efa9d7ec76511`. No shared CONNECT, MTU or fifty prerequisite files are part of this Android delta.

## Behavior

Performance → Ordered path: Fifty / Retest per node opens the exact connected ordered inventory. Each row displays its human profile name, native profile ID, position and selected transport. The view explains that private authenticated Router HTTP response timing includes connection establishment; public middle-hop HTTPS response timing begins after DNS, TCP and TLS preparation. Neither interval is ICMP ping or an individual inter-server link. The existing direct TCP rankings, external-final benchmarks and throughput renderer remain separate.

`AndroidNodeChainSession` retains the returned facade. `LayeredVpnService` lends its existing measurement service and captures the exact original plan, server, service, native session directory, source/config hashes, member, Home session/path generation, physical-network identity and MTU proof observation. The worker first retains the immutable target/Home selection and acquires `AndroidMTUMeasurementGate`. It waits for existing MTU work to join before attaching the strict typed native facade, and rechecks cancellation before Start. Cancelled, stale or failed MTU waits acquire no facade. The gate remains held through original operation drain, facade Close and durable adoption. It uses the existing 100-second operation bound. A failed drain or Close retains that exact facade and MTU lease for retry. A throwing Start is cleaned up by the original facade, even if Start did not return admission. View dismissal or Activity backgrounding cancels the request without closing host throughput. Service retirement joins the facade before closing the original measurement service and Box. Authorized MTU continuation keeps the same host measurement service; a subsequent benchmark takes a fresh MTU observation.

The separate private file `ordered-node-fifty-v1.json` stores validated native receipts, bounded to 128 records and 1 MiB. Keys include source/config hashes, position, native node ID, kind and measurement meaning. Receipts require 50 successful samples, no failures, coherent finite statistics, exact source/member/request binding, matching final native status and saved result, and original worker drain. Native generation is retained only as its hashed receipt. Closed native label forms and exact field inventories reject extra addresses, tokens and arbitrary labels. Human names remain display data and are not added to the persisted native payload.

The service/Home/request guards remain held around atomic private-file publication and exact readback. A failed, cancelled, stale, incomplete or superseded adoption retains the prior valid receipt. Rollback touches only the exact bytes written by that transaction; it never overwrites another writer's replacement. Loaded receipts remain visibly historical and do not authorize readiness, reconnection, candidate adoption or rankings across paths/metric kinds. Queued UI delivery rechecks its original owner and view generation.

## Executed Java evidence

Use JDK 21 with unchanged `--release 17`, and set `ANDROID_JSON_JAR` to the Android JSON jar. The implementation session used `/workspace/scratch/233d182077b3/toolchains/jdk-21.0.2/bin` and `/workspace/scratch/bc3200a97871/android-json-test-dependency/usr/share/java/com.android.json-android-10.0.0.jar`.

| Command | Executed result |
| --- | --- |
| `python3 android/test_android_ordered_latency.py` | 341 checks, eight families and nine compiled causal controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_layered_service_compile.py` | Complete shipping service type check; four negative controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_node_chain_socks_runtime.py` | 93 checks; seven original families and six controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_node_chain_runtime.py` | 479 checks; all 25 original controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_node_chain_execution_runtime.py` | 327 checks; all 16 original controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_node_candidate_prefix_host.py` | 77 checks; all five original controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_node_chain_mtu.py` | 74 checks; all 12 original controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_product_dialogs.py` | 462 shipping action checks and all original builder/dispatch/source controls |
| `python3 android/test_android_java_syntax.py` | 122 Java sources parsed |
| `python3 deploy/test_android_multihop_workflow.py` | 14 tests, including omission/duplication rejection for the new gate |

The new fixture executes the shipping complete service, session, runtime, benchmark worker, persistence logic and dialog. Its eight families cover success and repeat measurement on the same service after an MTU epoch; incomplete/final-sample/start failures and source/config/Home/MTU changes; cancellation before/after drain and during write; retained late drain/Close; busy MTU quiescence and cancellation/staleness/failure before factory admission; service retirement ordering; strict metrics, write failure, rollback/readback replacement and restart; visible Fifty/Retest/history; and stale queued delivery. The nine mutations move factory admission before MTU quiescence or remove exact borrowed-service identity, native drain, facade join, MTU-generation checks, retirement join ordering, queued delivery validation, sample-count validation or rollback ownership. Each fails with its specific causal witness.

Native exchange/strict-parser calls, Android SDK widgets and private-file syscalls are explicit doubles in this Java fixture. The storage fixture executes the shipping transaction logic against actual temporary bytes but does not claim physical power-loss durability or Android filesystem acceptance. Java syntax is not APK/AAR type validation. Actual shared fifty exchanges, native typed factory/gomobile compilation, encrypted full-path reproof, Android installed/device behavior and CONNECT host adoption remain separate acceptance gates. This increment does not implement CONNECT dispatch or broaden ordinary ordered APIs into the typed SOCKS path.

## Review follow-on

The original fifteen-path freeze and its 297-check/eight-control evidence remain preserved in `android-fifty-frozen-740747a5`. Review identified that its factory ran before the intended MTU quiescence. The factory correctly refused active MTU, but the caller now waits first, using the same immutable target and strict native admission. The five-path follow-on changes only the worker, dialog wording, history wording, its executable fixture and this document. The 341-check/nine-control run exercises the corrected shipping caller. Visible progress says waiting for MTU, finishing the test and result saved; raw cleanup stages and storage/source/config implementation terms remain outside the user flow.

# Android SOCKS5 Server and Compare execution

## Implemented path

The Android ordered editor, saved connection profiles and connection service
now admit the distinct schema-3 execution preparation for an exact three-node
path: one original paired WireGuard, AWG Fast or AWG Strong entry, one
literal-address imported SOCKS5 member, and an independent paired Shadowsocks
2022 final. The SOCKS5 member retains its original authentication choice and
credentials. Its UDP association remains separate from CONNECT's UoT-v2
transport. The existing three-to-eight-node Local SOCKS5 composition remains
separate.

Saving Server or Compare validates the complete original source through
`NewRouterNodeChainSOCKSExecution`. The returned preparation must retain every
original member, paired identity, requested execution and configuration. The
connection capture accepts only the distinct SOCKS execution launch kind; the
ordinary and CONNECT launch constructors cannot borrow that authority.

`AndroidNodeChainSession` creates the execution's original native CommandServer
through `RouterNodeChainSOCKSExecutionNewCommandServer`. The existing single
VpnService retains that server and execution owner through binding, proof,
selected-path measurements, latency, MTU, history, cancellation and joined
cleanup. The dormant Local SOCKS source cannot claim those operations. Failed

Last and temporary restoration keep the original requested and already-proved
Local/Server choices. Restoration requires the joined native mobile capsule,

start the native service. Compare's existing same-endpoint two-probe average
and fresh selected-path proof remain unchanged. Complete profile Add/Load
retains private node references without copying proxy credentials into the

The actual shared CommandServer, typed MTU and restoration implementation and
its native-object tests are recorded separately in
[the shared mobile evidence](SOCKS-MOBILE-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md).

ordered editor callbacks against the actual shared Go compiler. Native Run,
leases, TUN and restoration are explicit refusing boundaries in that gate.

execution refusals and five substituted readback refusals. The positive matrix
contains WG, AWG Fast and AWG Strong, Server and Compare, and both SOCKS5
authentication choices. Checks cover canonical replay, distinct launch

## docs/ANDROID-TYPED-CONNECTED-CANDIDATES-2026-11-16.md

# Android connected typed candidate ownership — 2026-11-16

## Shipping behavior

`AndroidNodeCandidateMeasurement` dispatches an original connected Local SOCKS or
HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT capture to its corresponding native candidate factory. It
retains the original typed owner, CommandServer, complete captured source and
selected packet prefix. Generic connected parsing remains closed to those typed
sources. The imported member and final node remain invalid candidate-prefix
positions.

Each typed constructor returns a unique reserved handle before fallible
preparation. Java stores that handle before `Prepare`, so a failed preparation
can retire only its own operation. A refused same-key constructor has no handle
and cannot close an existing operation. Cleanup retains the original candidate
and MTU activity until cancellation, callbacks and native close join. A closed
old handle cannot retire a replacement slot.

Temporary saved-prefix measurements remain purpose-first: they start only their
own prefix service. Their typed suffix is captured provenance and is not started
or borrowed for cleanup. Server and Compare remain outside this connected Local
dispatch. The ordinary constructor contract is unchanged in this unit; its
separate reservation follow-up is not included.

The original fifty-sample scoring, operation deadline and result freshness
bounds remain. The chooser compiles the complete proposed replacement profile
through the actual shared compiler, retains the captured suffix and source
references, and saves a future profile without changing the active connection
or rerunning Compare. A row must have its own complete score before Save.

The AAR verifier checks both exact generated factory signatures and every used
typed-handle method, including `Prepare`. The new Android source/service job is
mandatory in the workflow's dependencies, result environment and success
predicate. All prior jobs, including the layered-candidate job, remain required.

## Executed evidence

Actual shipping Java capture, Store, profile compilation, chooser, Session,
Runtime and LayeredVpnService bodies execute in these gates. The source gates
use the real shared Go compiler. Android OS operations and native handle and
transport callbacks are explicit boundary models; these local results do not
establish an installed-device or generated-AAR runtime result.

The two current source replays execute positive phases only. They retain the

The native reservation prerequisite was published separately as

## docs/ANDROID-TYPED-PREFIX-CANDIDATE-HOST-2026-11-16.md

# Android typed saved-prefix ownership — 2026-11-16

This change connects saved Local SOCKS5 and HTTP(S) CONNECT paths to the explicit closed packet-prefix factories. The complete typed source is retained as provenance; the temporary service starts only the selected WG/AWG packet prefix. The generic parser remains closed to typed source, and Server/Compare selection cannot silently start a Local test.

The Android service previously chose the full typed suffix factory even when a prefix purpose was present. Prefix startup now selects the neutral CommandServer first and binds only the prefix native owner. An unstarted suffix cannot supply a native close or partial-binding cleanup receipt. Retiring the prefix still requires its own original joined native receipt.

The existing required source and host gates now cover three-, four- and eight-node typed captures, actual Store/Load, full canonical source ownership, closed application/DNS/Ready state, prefix position/MTU proof rows, malformed readback, late source changes and cancellation. The AAR verifier requires both generated typed prefix factory signatures. The nine local ABI cases exercise that exact shell guard using explicitly synthetic javap text; they do not establish that an AAR has been generated.

## Local verification

| owner-1 | 53 | 8 |

All original controls remain, including the complete SOCKS/CONNECT native-receipt cases and the original prefix supervisor. Added controls restore the old suffix-first factory, substitute suffix cleanup authority, select the wrong source factory and remove exact purpose/source readback checks. Every gate retained its original child deadline and its complete required inventory.

The source capture gate executed the real Go compiler and shipping Java source. Service/runtime tests explicitly model Android OS and native interfaces. This does not prove installed-device permission, TUN/traffic/leak behavior or publication of the current native packages. The separately owned typed native dependency and the exact-SHA Android build remain required before declaring the shipping release accepted.
