## docs/APPLE-CANDIDATE-COMPLETE-PROPOSAL-2026-11-16.md

# Apple measured next-hop proposal preservation

A measured candidate now replaces **captured prefix position + 1** in the original
complete ordered path. The source proposal retains every other member and private
node reference, their independent identities and Start Layers, the unchanged final
DNS policy, and the exact Local, Server, or Compare selection. It no longer truncates
the suffix, appends an imported node, or silently saves Local.

The original candidate input must equal the measured input. The proposed path must
have unique profile and paired proof identities. The existing complete ordinary or
typed SOCKS/CONNECT source compiler remains the final admission authority, including
for Server/Compare previews. An incompatible next-hop role, imported final, duplicate
identity, changed retained prefix, changed final credentials, or changed selected DNS
fails before Save. No connected candidate or native Server admission is opened.

Complete original RouterProfiles are retained for Save, including startup and
autoconnect preferences that are intentionally absent from native source projections.
The retained prefix source and independent identity strings are checked exactly.
Unchanged suffix members remain exact. The final member necessarily changes its
ordered references, so its complete sealed source is regenerated through the actual
`LibboxRouterNodeChainMemberInput` using the original mode, transport files, Start
Layer, DNS bootstrap, and profile with only those control references updated. The
entire returned member must match the new projection; no hash or identity field is
ignored or copied from the replacement source.

The shipping profile store preserves requested execution, all private references,
normal explicit Load, source/receipt freshness immediately before the atomic write,
and transactional refusal when source or policy changes. The measured receipt,
expiration, exact source, and joined ownership guards remain in their existing
callers. This is a future saved source graph, not an alteration to the live path.

## Exact validation

The source gate compiles the actual shipping Swift capture/proposal code against a
real shared Go source compiler. Its original **180-second** Go build completed in
**24.123 seconds**. That exact helper was retained and reused only after all tracked
Go source/module hashes and the generated helper source were checked unchanged.
Every Swift compilation retains **90 seconds**, and every source runtime phase
retains **180 seconds**.

The final source run passed **553 assertions**: the original **193** capture,
credential, receipt, expiry and proposal checks, plus **120 each** for complete
Local, Server and Compare paths. The additions cover four/eight-member paths,
non-first paired prefixes, imported SS/SOCKS/HTTP/HTTPS replacements, every retained
prefix, independent final identity, startup preferences, final DNS and execution.
All **four original compiled omission controls** rejected their exact intended
witnesses. The complete final run took **103.874 seconds**, excluding the already
verified original helper build, within the shipping caller's **300-second** bound.

The separate profile gate executes the shipping persistence, Codable models,
per-position capture, preference snapshot, original source checks, explicit Load and
atomic store logic. Only durable writes and DNS preparation are doubled. Its
baseline passed **654 assertions**, including three/four/eight-member candidate
profiles for all three requested executions. All **five compiled controls** passed in **81.965 seconds** overall. The forced-Local
production mutation fails specifically at the candidate Server Save consistency
check before an inconsistent profile can be returned; the four original blank-layer,
final-currentness, record-bound and selection controls remain.
The original **120-second** Swift compile and **30-second** child runtime bounds are
unchanged. Both complete gates fit their existing **300-second** caller bounds.
Fifteen adjacent workflow/source inventory checks and Python syntax checks also pass.

## Source composition and retained failures

The exact proof base is `1c5a0ab79322c7f8c8f3890f1830423c25a4411a` with this unit's
four code/test files. The original candidate helper exports consumed by the other
prefix tests remain unchanged; the expanded Save fixture uses its own execution
helper instead. Publication preserves subsequent Android Save and native Hysteria2
fixture/preparation changes, which do not change any consumed Apple or shared Go
source in this proof.

The first source run passed eight assertions and then exposed a real error in the
new final-member comparison: changing ordered references correctly changes its
source and metadata receipts. Field-name-only diagnostics identified that difference
without exposing credentials. The implementation now recompiles the exact original
member instead of stripping hashes. The next attempt stopped at Swift compilation
because the portable helper has a nonoptional return; using the existing explicit
`String?` calling convention fixed that type mismatch. Both failed receipts remain
separate from the successful final source run. The first profile-control run expected
a post-Save assertion, but the existing final-policy guard correctly rejected the
forced-Local mutation earlier. Its contextual witness was corrected without changing
production. An intermediate fixture-only type-name typo stopped at compilation;
the final complete profile baseline and all five controls then passed.

Full source manifests, immutable helper hash, exact child commands and finite bounds,
positive/negative transcripts and failed diagnostics are retained under
`candidate-proposal-apple-validation`. The final publication manifest records each
changed file and its original preimage. These are source and persistence proofs;
they do not claim fifty real socket samples, native forwarding, an Apple SDK/device
run, or kernel acceptance.

## docs/APPLE-CONNECT-FINAL-LAYERS-2026-11-16.md

# Apple CONNECT final Start Layers

The iPhone/iPad capture, reopen, editor and saved-profile paths now retain an
independent Off, AES-256-GCM or AES-256-GCM plus XOR whitening selection on the
paired final node of a Server/Compare HTTP or HTTPS CONNECT chain. The imported
CONNECT node still requires its original Off policy. The original final private
configuration and SS UoT-v2 service remain mandatory; the public capture delegates
their validation to the typed shared compiler instead of imposing the obsolete
final-Off restriction.

This consumer depends on the original-owner native implementation published in
`e31d7d20d5862ecd86c3a3807ce752889d7138d8`. It does not introduce a second system
VPN or substitute an ordinary network lookup for the selected final DNS path.
The existing ordered-chain size, paired-prefix, transport, credential and
current-source constraints remain in force.

## Executed source and lifecycle evidence

The following gates ran with Swift 6 and the actual Go 1.26.3 shared source
compiler. All retained their existing per-child bounds and the 300-second
aggregate caller bound. Their source snapshots did not change during execution.

| Gate | Observed result | Elapsed seconds |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| Original capture and generic consumers | 1,721 assertions and six compiled source controls passed | 209.151 |
| Complete-prefix/final-layer controls | Seven positive assertions and seven compiled controls passed | 103.081 |
| Layered control and retained restoration | Actual source/reopen and same-winner restoration passed; four compiled intent/winner/process/release controls rejected their intended faults | 142.478 |
| Layered public launch and editor | Launch, cold Last, editor save, changed-source/cancellation checks and the original compiled source control passed | 88.689 |
| Layered profile Add/Load/Update/Delete | Separate four- and eight-node phases each passed 686 shipping-Swift assertions; all four original compiled controls rejected their intended faults | 90.916 |

The additive layer cases cover both supported encrypted finals, retained Local
and Server winners, preserved selected private final keys and UoT-v2 transport,
and rejection of a changed final layer during preference saving. Four-node cases
exercise independently selected encrypted entry and final layers; eight-node
cases preserve the existing packet budget with Off on preceding nodes. Original
complete-count/Off matrices remain separately mandatory. Profile tests exercise
both HTTP and HTTPS, Server and Compare, original node references and transactional
failure behavior. Saving preferences never reports a native connection.

The provider replies, NetworkExtension manager, private-store I/O and platform
interfaces in these executable source tests are explicit fixtures. These passes
establish the shipping Swift callbacks and shared compiler behavior, not an
Xcode package, installed VPN, physical-device networking or signing result. The
authoritative native build and device acceptance remain separate evidence.

An initial layered-control run failed against the old shared final-Off guard.
That failure was retained. The shared/native prerequisite was repaired and
independently validated before the successful layered run and publication of
this consumer. No timeout or negative acceptance condition was relaxed.

## Publication composition

The tested consumer paths were composed onto `e31d7d20` without replaying the
older Apple/OpenVPN bridge changes. The eight privately staged prerequisite
paths were checked byte-for-byte against their now-published versions. All
thirteen consumer paths retained their tested bytes except the native builder,
where the additive input registrations were merged with newer upstream work.
The workflow/runtime-selection contracts require all three new layer gates;
the compiled negative controls are included in those gates. The integrated
workflow contract passed its fourteen tests.

## docs/APPLE-CONNECT-PREFIX-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Apple complete CONNECT host adoption

The Apple daily-use app now captures and presents complete HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT Server and Compare paths with three to eight original nodes. Every paired preceding node, imported proxy, final private profile, source seal and independent identity remains captured. The session identity validator and native preparation readback accept the same complete schema-four bounds. SOCKS schema three retains the existing public three-node limit in this unit.

CONNECT Server/Compare explicitly validates the selected final Start Layer as Off both while building the original source and while reopening a private captured source. This closes a real host-admission gap discovered by the executable source test. Local handling remains unchanged. Editor guidance describes the complete CONNECT range and keeps the separately verified imported-Shadowsocks hostname minimum of four nodes with its original Custom TCP resolver.

The profile and launch fixtures exercise three-, four- and eight-node Server/Compare choices. Complete Add/Update/Load/Delete callbacks preserve node references, original private credentials, DNS/protection policies and source/config identity. Restore controls retain the proved Local or Server winner, original process, opaque capture owner and joined retirement. Existing imported/OpenVPN profile derivatives keep their Local-only source scope.

All listed validations passed on source base `3360e07798625e168b1ca3be72bc3e0a85ea4c53` plus the exact nineteen frozen file outputs. The full capture gate passed 1,673 checks and all six original compiled controls. Its mandatory companion passed a focused real four-node baseline and all five added compiled controls. The original 300-second caller boundaries remain unchanged; only the five additive controls were separated after preserving the earlier timeout evidence.

The complete profile gate passed 1,522 shipping Swift checks and all three original persistence controls. Complete restoration passed its four original compiled omission controls; fresh editor/launch replay passed the original-source control and actual hostname Save cases. The unchanged typed SOCKS regression passed 371 checks and all four compiled controls. The nine-root workflow guard, profile audit and full native artifact/workflow parity audit also passed. Full receipts and exact source/log hashes accompany this note.

These are Linux Swift source/consumer and real Go parser results with explicit OS/native/provider/store boundaries. They do not establish Xcode/Apple SDK acceptance, signing, packaging, physical-device networking, private deployment, or release publication. ## docs/APPLE-CONNECTED-TYPED-CANDIDATES-2026-11-16.md

# Apple connected typed candidate ownership — 2026-11-16

The connected Local SOCKS/HTTP CONNECT/HTTPS CONNECT chooser retains its original typed native owner and command server. It no longer attempts to reopen that live graph as an ordinary node chain. The App keeps the complete original Local source when preparing a future profile, including the selected final assets and source/configuration digests. Independent saved-prefix execution remains a separate owner and purpose.

The typed native constructor reserves and returns a unique measurement handle before foreign catalog or service work. The host stores that handle before calling `Prepare()`. A failed preparation remains unprepared and cannot restart, start measurements or publish status. Cleanup uses only the retained handle. Typed constructor rejection grants no cleanup authority; the legacy ordinary factory retains its existing fallback.

Stop retains the candidate session and command server before releasing the typed host. Foreign owners and copied same-key sessions are rejected before cancellation. Failed joins retain the original owner for retry; stale closed sessions cannot close a replacement. Completed registry identities use weak references, with the existing bounded tombstone inventory unchanged. These operation-state checks do not replace native currentness or catalog validation.

## Recorded local validation

The first three executable phases used the same immutable source identity `ec8b6f4c65b6983d25bcb4886bcfc55734233adf7f1b0e2b32b80df7949a4bad`, based on published `fc203c75d36b93554119151f50e9db194ad619f2` plus this host delta. Guarded forwards through `76de49ad7af75b06853e445ffaff885de3d2d303` preserved every owned implementation and test byte and checked each upstream preimage.

The shared-source/provider/proposal phase was repeated on that newer base because the intervening commits changed paired Shadowsocks candidate eligibility and MTU policy. It passed in 97.706 seconds with source identity `29383a1a0e12efa34eb1c2d965187e0dcbc88ff55052c8276423eada6ee7a523`, unchanged throughout the run. Its complete output was byte-identical to the earlier successful source phase. The unchanged session and whole-engine phases retain their original recorded evidence; they are not reported as repeated runs on the newer base.

| Phase | Observed result | Log SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shipping session and request dispatch | PASS, 20.701 s; original cases plus SOCKS/CONNECT 3/4/8 ownership cases; original two and four typed compiled controls | `3287c363edab4fc60e683b31d6da34de6b897399125466cb933536e3d14bd907` |
| Whole shipping engine, focused typed lifecycle | PASS, 53.978 s; CONNECT 13 and SOCKS 13 startup/Stop checks, pending-owner omission control, existing proof-cancel and measurement controls | `356f92a58005cb3e09bd9a6be870ae8c583ebd09cc0ef7fd5c0947014a53a0d4` |
| Real shared constructors and Swift source/provider/proposal | PASS, 102.342 s; 321 checks across SOCKS/HTTP/HTTPS 3/4/8 and four compiled refusal controls | `06a8e6dd52628a3a620265a4ea85fa3b1fe74b354847a36ebc85a069b47dcc37` |

The existing workflow checker passed all 16 tests in 2.707 s. The packaging guard's synthetic header positive and 22 symbol/selector omission checks passed; this verifies the guard, not a generated framework. Existing mandatory gates and child deadlines remain unchanged. These are per-phase results, not a summed count of distinct product requirements.

The whole-engine fixture appends a test-only same-file extension while retaining every shipping engine byte. Its App Group directory is an explicit temporary filesystem boundary; source-publication sealing remains real. Native factory/handle objects are doubles: the reserved handle's `Prepare()` deliberately fails in the Stop fixture, so it supplies no native readiness or traffic receipt.

## Native dependency and acceptance boundary

This host unit requires the typed native bridge with the two `NewCandidateMeasurement` reservation factories and each handle's one-shot `Prepare()` and exact `Close()` methods. It must be integrated after that native prerequisite; the removed owner/position/inventory cleanup APIs are not used. Native component evidence is maintained separately from these Swift host results.

The required Apple framework build must generate and compile the actual SDK symbols and selectors. Installed PacketTunnel behavior, actual encrypted traffic, device lifecycle, signing and release acceptance remain hosted/device obligations. This change does not add connected candidate admission for Server/Compare execution owners or turn independent saved-prefix provenance into a live connected owner.

## docs/APPLE-HYSTERIA2-EXECUTION-2026-11-16.md

# Apple paired Hysteria2 execution — November 16, 2026

The Apple ordered-path editor, captured connection identity and shared source
consumer now admit Server and Compare for the paired Hysteria2 family introduced
by `8f8e9fb3`. This continues the original mode, multihop, saved-profile, selected
DNS and complete-state ownership requirements. It does not reduce the remaining
protocol, graph or Start Layer requirements to this particular family.

## Source and user behavior

The supported source has two through eight original paired nodes. Every
preceding node keeps its selected WireGuard, AWG Fast or AWG Strong mode and its
own Off, AES-256-GCM or AES-plus-XOR Start Layer. The Hysteria2 final retains Start
Layer Off, its original endpoint, verified TLS name and private trust, password
and obfuscation. Each paired member retains its independently provisioned
standard WireGuard identity asset. Imported Shadowsocks, SOCKS5, CONNECT and
OpenVPN families keep their existing Shadowsocks-final contracts.

The editor uses the existing shared execution preparation and native host; it
does not create another system VPN. Saved profiles preserve every node reference,
execution choice, policy and original credential asset through Add, Load, Update
and Delete. Planned and active maps retain the original transport at every
position. The ordered measurement capture retains the complete source/config
identity and rejects replacement connection generations.

`IOSSessionIdentity` previously accepted only a Shadowsocks raw profile for a
captured execution. It now admits Hysteria2 while requiring the actual final
member's mode to equal that raw profile. Hysteria2 additionally requires the
paired schema, WG/AWG prefix and Off final. A captured Hysteria2 source cannot be
relabeled as Shadowsocks or another transport. Existing private source bounds,
canonical metadata, full node identities and imported-family validation remain.

The shared source validator also rejects a present Hysteria2 obfuscation object
without a supported type and nonempty password, matching the pinned native
endpoint's construction rules. Absent and null optional obfuscation remain
valid. Both supported type names retain their exact source parameters; source
preservation of a type is not a claim of a successful encrypted connection with
that type.

## Verification and boundaries

The required Apple source gate executes the actual shared Go compiler and
shipping Swift capture, route selection, session identity, map and measurement
capture bodies. It covers every supported node count, all three WG/AWG base
choices, Server/Compare and all three preceding-node Start Layers. The native
service, TUN, network proof and system VPN boundaries explicitly refuse to grant
connection readiness in this source test.

The public profile gate covers all supported node counts with Local, Server and
Compare roundtrips. Existing Local and execution-session tests retain their
original cases and deliberately broken compiled variants. The new gate is
required by the native source inventory, iOS libbox workflow and the native
runtime contract. Its workflow checker rejects omitted lengths, source checks,
driver calls or original deadlines.

The complete new source gate passed **4,088 shipping Swift/shared Go assertions**
across all seven lengths and all **six actual compiled omission controls**, in
202.245 seconds under the unchanged 300-second parent deadline. Each compiler
kept its 90-second Swift or 180-second Go deadline; each executable phase kept
its 180-second deadline. No source input changed during the run. Its complete
log SHA-256 is
`d1126e178f2eefb3488d64bb8643cf55b74b116fd2fe9aabc445cb3a5f0032f5`.

| Additional executed gate | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Ordered profile persistence | 557 shipping Swift checks and four compiled controls passed in 72.343 seconds |
| Original Local capture | 1,624 shipping Swift/shared Go checks and seven compiled controls passed in 289.559 seconds |
| Required workflow/source inventory | All 15 strict checker tests passed |

The first source attempt exposed the old Shadowsocks-only session identity. A
second exposed an explicitly empty obfuscation password that the native endpoint
would refuse. Those findings produced the actual identity and validation fixes.
A later concurrent run passed all seven positive lengths but reached the
existing 90-second compiler deadline on a fault-injection variant. The complete
serial pass above retains the same compiler, case inventory and limits. Its
proof source is `191fd4299dca9fddb7bde9232485342aa2eeaf4d`, based on `dbe971b4`;
the publication also preserves the concurrent desktop controller work in
`110dbd3f`, which does not change the consumed Go or Swift source.

The original Local gate itself exited successfully. Its external input watcher
included a different suite's temporary Go driver and consequently reported that
independent temporary-file deletion; every tracked input remained unchanged.
The existing execution-session regression passed its 1,091 positive assertions
but reached the same compiler deadline on its first existing control during the
concurrent run. That incomplete control run is preserved and is not called a
complete regression pass. The complete existing gate remains required in CI.

Actual encrypted native Hysteria2 provider/Server execution has a separate
required workflow. Exact-source native packages, publication, Apple signing,
physical iPhone/iPad networking, private deployment and off-LAN acceptance retain
their own evidence requirements. Hysteria2 final AES/XOR layers and broader
intermediate protocol compositions remain open original product requirements.

## Required profile-audit correction

The exact-source [map-first workflow on `51ad480d`](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/43851946642)
failed its profile-settings source audit because that audit still required the
old Shadowsocks-only generic selector. The other 21 children of that workflow
passed. The stale expectation was reproduced locally before correction.

`deploy/profile-settings-audit.py` now compares the complete published generic
predicate: two through eight members, paired WG/AWG prefixes, and either a
Shadowsocks or Hysteria2 final; an imported Shadowsocks middle still requires a
Shadowsocks final. It retains all 37 prior mutation controls, with the generic
length-control anchor updated to the new expression. Three additional controls
reject an unrestricted final, loss of the supported Hysteria2 choice, and removal
of the imported branch's Shadowsocks-final restriction.

The actual profile-settings audit passed all 40 source mutation controls. This
corrects a required release gate without changing application admission or
claiming another Swift, native-network, package or physical-device result.

## Existing CONNECT fixture compatibility

The original CONNECT fixture also retained a now-stale negative case for a
paired WireGuard-to-Hysteria2 selection. It now explicitly accepts all two through
eight-member paired WG/AWG shapes for Server and Compare. Unsupported finals,
Hysteria2 intermediates, imported-middle Hysteria2 finals, wrong ordering and
excess length remain negative cases. Its complete-prefix control now locates the
following function declaration instead of relying on the wording of a comment.

A focused Swift run used the complete shipping `IOSMultihopNodeSelection` type
and the fixture's actual selector block. The old fixture reproduced its exact
assertion failure. The corrected block passed 40 checks and 42 additional
successful Server/Compare validations. Two actual compiled source omissions
were rejected at their intended witnesses: restoring Shadowsocks-only admission
and removing the imported branch's Shadowsocks-final restriction. All 15 strict
workflow/phase/negative-control inventory tests also passed.

This focused execution does not replace the complete Go-backed CONNECT capture
suite. Both original and complete-prefix phases, all their existing compiled
controls and their original deadlines remain mandatory in CI. No application
source, native runtime or release-acceptance boundary changed in this correction.

three-to-eight-node path. It previously accepted only the penultimate position.

the original position and count. Exact node, role, source/configuration,
request, measurement method, endpoint, 50 successful samples, zero failures,
and distribution validation remain required. Selected execution labels cannot
be substituted into this ordinary Local receipt grammar.

The history description identifies responses through the custom node, timed
after DNS, TCP and TLS preparation. Existing endpoint and DNS policy behavior
is unchanged. The result remains historical cumulative-path response time; it
does not become ICMP RTT, direct-node ranking or connection authority.

the complete extracted `NodeChainDelivery` class, and the App's actual capture,

## docs/APPLE-MULTIPLE-IMPORTED-LOCAL-2026-11-16.md

This unit lets the Apple ordered editor, source capture, presentation and connection-profile transactions retain independently captured literal Shadowsocks interiors at their actual positions. Three through eight original members remain bounded, with a paired WG/AWG entry and paired Shadowsocks final. Multiple imports or one earlier import select Local schema six; the existing single penultimate import keeps schema two. Imported credentials remain in their original private node records, and profile Load stages all affected records, recompiles the complete proposed source before writes, and rolls back failed writes or changed original source. This unit does not open schema-six Server/Compare.

## docs/APPLE-NATIVE-CONTINUATION.md

# Apple native runtime continuation — September 21, 2026

## Scope and acceptance

The user requires the requested capabilities across Windows, macOS, Linux,
Android and iOS/iPadOS. An accurate unavailable message is a safety boundary,
not delivery of that capability. This continuation preserves that requirement;
it does not redefine all-device parity as a source-marker or compilation check.

## Implemented in this continuation and its immediate predecessors

- The pinned Apple WireGuardKit fork includes the native AmneziaWG backend.
  Raw WireGuard and generated AmneziaWG Fast/Strong use that actual adapter.
  The parser passes Jc/Jmin/Jmax, S1–S4 and H1–H4 to the engine; it rejects
  incomplete AWG profiles, family-label mismatches, overflowing header ranges,
  duplicate singleton fields, duplicate peers and ambiguous interface sections.
- Logical selection honors the saved WG/AWG base and the explicit fallback
  policy. A missing preferred engine cannot silently select the other base when
  fallback is disabled. AUTO considers allowed alternatives in deterministic
  catalog/base order. Strict route-lockdown still prevents unproved runtime
  transitions; this is not claimed as full strict SMART AUTO implementation.
- CUSTOM, SMART simplification and last-good restoration pass their exact raw
  runtime to the connection function. They no longer re-resolve AWG Fast through
  the logical Raw tunnel picker and accidentally start WireGuard instead.
- Runtime replacement stops if the previous tunnel's teardown or on-demand
  preference update cannot be verified. Only Router VPN-owned configuration
  records are eligible for these operations. Node changes during asynchronous
  preparation invalidate that connection attempt.
- Fixed MTU reaches native WG/AWG, Router Libbox and external Libbox before the
  engine starts. The Start Layer composer runs first, then MTU, then the engine.
  Unvalidated saved effective-MTU history is not used as a fresh path result.
- The forwarding master is a real server control through the owned PacketTunnel:
  private literal endpoint validation, exact node/session binding, cancellation,
  bounded HTTP, and independent state readback. It is not phone-local DNAT or
  a general proxy to the Setup Center administrator interface.

## Executable evidence and its boundaries

`deploy/test_ios_native_base_selection.py` executes the shipping selector and
exact-raw strategy method. Apple CI uses the actual Network.framework DNS policy;
Linux substitutes only literal-IP parsing for that unavailable framework.

`deploy/test_ios_wireguard_parser.py` executes the actual parser with adapter data
types doubled. The required native IPA build separately checks those assignments
against the pinned Apple engine, not the doubles.

`deploy/test_ios_mtu_policy.py` executes the shipping MTU policy and verifies its
three engine call sites. The forwarding policy/UI tests execute the production
policy and model with synthetic IPC. The SDK gate type-checks the real transport.
These tests are required through the native shipping runtime contract.

The Go Start Layer contract now verifies the complete composer → MTU → engine
sequence rather than insisting on the obsolete local variable spelling. Existing
Start Layer, node proof, DNS and release checks are retained.

## Still open, not waived

The September 22 native OpenVPN integration supersedes the earlier blanket
mobile OpenVPN exclusion; its supported profiles and test boundaries are documented
in [MOBILE-OPENVPN.md](MOBILE-OPENVPN.md).

The current source still lacks full iOS multihop and routed-hop telemetry parity,
unrestricted OpenVPN profile compatibility, the complete mobile helper/PQ/MAX/ALL graph set, mobile Tor
transports, iOS protected XOR-relay support, complete strict SMART transition
ownership, and complete path-measured Auto-MTU/Jumbo/padding parity. Existing
platform-specific documentation/source gates describe narrower support; those
exclusions remain work, not completed requirements or proof of OS impossibility.

Physical-device installs, reconnect/permission/leak-negative tests, visual and
screen-size acceptance, off-LAN interoperability, live private AI Board/Portainer
and ASUS validation, and Apple distribution signing require their own evidence.
A green exact-SHA release certifies its actual source/build/package gates only.
No results for those physical/private gates are asserted by this document.

## Two-node multihop and release continuation

The two-node implementation uses an owned WireGuard entry endpoint and an
owned WireGuard/Shadowsocks/Hysteria2 exit transport inside one Libbox PacketTunnel.
The exit sockets detour through the entry; entry and exit proofs use separate
private loopback proof routes. Connect completion requires both node identities
and the same live engine owner. Saved profiles store graph references, not
credentials. Speed Lab compares the frozen graph and the system connection date,
so reconnecting the same displayed node invalidates older measurements.

The native release checks now inspect the success path of each of the four
PacketTunnel runtimes independently. Negative controls remove each runtime's
network-change guard and must fail; a global count is not sufficient evidence.
The pinned Libbox configuration gate is a macOS host program even when Xcode
invokes it while building an iPhone target. Its child process selects the macOS
SDK without changing the enclosing iPhone build. Six shell regression tests
verify SDK isolation, exact dependency pinning, failure propagation and cleanup.
The real native gate still builds the pinned core and checks the actual graphs.

User Disconnect now operates only on the unique Router VPN-owned manager. It
invalidates measurement identity immediately, serializes repeated requests,
disables on-demand reconnect with preference readback, and verifies terminal
connection state before showing Disconnected. Preference failures, changed
ownership, multiple matching managers, timeouts and an unexpected reconnect
remain unverified rather than falsely unlocking profile mutation. Twenty-four
executable tests run the actual production methods with system-manager doubles;
these are not physical NetworkExtension tests.

The private RouterProfile Codable model preserves explicit daita_enabled and
jumbo_tun booleans. Graph rejection tests now round-trip the real import model
before validation, preventing unsupported requested policy from disappearing
between import and PacketTunnel. This preserves user intent; it does not claim
that either padding or Jumbo was newly implemented.

The bounded graph does not close generalized multihop, arbitrary external/hop
families, per-hop measurement parity, additional Start Layer composition, or the
remaining requirements listed above. Native compilation/configuration parsing
and publication are separate evidence from device traffic, leaks, private
production deployment and Apple signing.


## Nested WireGuard exit continuation — September 23, 2026

The iOS/iPadOS graph now accepts a native WireGuard exit as well as the
previous Shadowsocks/Hysteria2 exits. Both WireGuard keys are parsed from the
frozen paired node bundles and independently matched to their expected node
proof identities before either runtime starts. Two different node labels cannot
reuse the same server key. The final userspace WireGuard endpoint dials only
through the entry endpoint; only one NetworkExtension TUN is created.

The selected exit-node plain DNS resolver is carried into the composed graph
and detours through the exit. Native WireGuard DNS compatibility now requires
UDP port 53, rather than silently accepting an unrepresentable custom port.
Malformed or conflicting peer, route, address-family, resolver and dial policy
is rejected. The multihop-specific MTU step preserves each frozen hop's
fixed setting rather than applying the exit's value to the entry. For nested
WireGuard it subtracts the inner UDP/IP and WireGuard envelope, including
16-byte padding, from the configured entry budget. Auto keeps the conservative
OS TUN; an incompatible requested fixed exit MTU is rejected rather than
silently clamped. This is configured encapsulation headroom, not a live PMTU
measurement. The executable policy covers 358 cases, including every entry
MTU from 1360 through 1500 for both outer address families. The preexisting separate entry/exit proof lanes, session freshness,
saved graph ownership and temporary-test restoration remain in place.

`deploy/test_ios_multihop_graph.py` executes 155 policy/graph checks and emits
five pinned-core configuration fixtures, including WireGuard exits with dual
stack and IPv4-only policy. The host-build regression requires all five fixtures
and verifies failure propagation, cleanup and Xcode SDK isolation. A local
configuration check passed all five using the official Linux binary whose
embedded revision is `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`; the archive
was verified against the upstream artifact digest. This checks the same core
configuration, not the Apple framework or live NetworkExtension behavior. Local policy
checks are not substitutes for the required pinned-core check, native IPA build,
real two-node traffic test or network-transition/leak-negative acceptance.

This remains client-owned nested encryption. A server-managed entry→exit mode
would need its own per-client forwarding, exit credential management and failure
isolation. No server-wide route or home-router setting is changed by this patch.
The new exit transport does not close the generalized graphs or the remaining
feature/device/deployment requirements above.


---

## docs/APPLE-OPENVPN-EXECUTION-2026-11-16.md

# Apple typed OpenVPN Server and Compare — November 16, 2026

This increment connects the iPhone/iPad source, session, saved-profile and PacketTunnel host consumers to the distinct ordered OpenVPN execution API. The supported composition is three through eight original members: one through six paired WireGuard/AWG prefixes, one independently paired OpenVPN member, and a paired Shadowsocks 2022 final. Local and Server provider credentials remain independently captured. The existing Local path remains separate, and the ordinary execution parser does not acquire authority over schema 5.

## Original source and lifecycle

The preparation retains exactly `schema`, `execution`, `local_source` and `openvpn_local_launch`. The final field is the complete original sealed Local launch. Apple passes that launch to the typed constructor and Last factory rather than rebuilding it from a stripped member list. Initial construction and reopening compare every original member, identity, final profile and sealed launch. Session identity retains the same ordered source and original selection, and the map presentation reads the captured path.

The shipping host selects `RouterNodeChainOpenVPNExecutionNewCommandServer` for this family. Its existing bind, cancellation, retirement and restoration sequence retains the returned native owner. Failure paths now also close an object returned together with an error; the error cannot leave that partial object adopted or unretired. Three independent compiled controls omit each newly required close and must fail the exact original-owner witness.

Restoration uses the already-proved Local or Server choice through the typed Last factory. It retains complete configuration and the original closed owner; it does not rerun Compare. Separate guards reject a different returned owner, unjoined native cleanup, changed Last data or a generic constructor substituted for the typed factory. The common test fixture now asserts its Close-hook anchor before injecting a failed close, so a fixture refactor cannot silently disable that negative case.

The ordered editor and saved profiles retain the complete references, execution choice, transport and independent Start Layers. Final Off, AES-256-GCM and AES-256-GCM with XOR whitening remain distinct supported choices. The CONNECT-specific final-Off boundary is not copied into the full-packet OpenVPN path. Invalid explicit Start Layers remain rejected. Blank native Off values in saved profiles were corrected separately in `58a73bcf284d0d91a1dc22ac98c164c90f51e7aa`.

## Executed evidence

These checks compile and execute shipping Swift consumers with the actual shared Go source compiler. Native networking, operating-system interfaces and live Last observations are explicitly modeled in the consumer harness. They are not Apple SDK, generated XCFramework, device or encrypted-traffic results.

## Build and acceptance boundary

The four required OpenVPN exports are checked in the actual generated Objective-C header guard. Signing, provisioning, off-LAN operation, network transitions, leak checks and private deployment are not established by this local consumer result. Broader original custom-node, hop, mode and all-device requirements remain in the requirement ledger; this supported ordered composition does not close every requested graph.

consumer gate files retain the frozen validated bytes. The shared native
adapter and mobile factory are now published in `3b605c0c`, `77ab253b` and

and the actual four-method OpenVPN header guard again accepted the complete
listing and rejected each missing export. The previously recorded Swift/Go
consumer results remain separate from native Xcode/SDK and device acceptance.

existing Load, original-record, private-data, and atomic restore checks remain.

## docs/APPLE-SOCKS-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Apple SOCKS5 Server and Compare execution

## Shipping path

The iPhone and iPad ordered editor and complete connection profiles now admit
the distinct SOCKS5 execution family for an exact three-node path: one paired
WireGuard, AWG Fast or AWG Strong entry, one literal-address imported SOCKS5
member, and an independent paired Shadowsocks 2022 final. The original
authentication choice, credentials, member identities, DNS and device policy
remain captured. The existing three-to-eight-node Local SOCKS5 path remains
available through its own source and runtime.

`IOSNodeChainSOCKSCapture` retains the requested execution separately from its
complete Local source. `IOSNodeChainExecutionCapture` is a closed union of
ordinary, CONNECT and SOCKS captures and calls the distinct SOCKS preparation,
launch and Last constructors. It verifies the complete returned member and
identity arrays, source digests and requested execution. Ordinary constructors
cannot lend SOCKS authority. Presentation validates the original final private
profile after removing only the existing scheduling fields.

The existing PacketTunnel starts `RouterNodeChainSOCKSExecutionNewCommandServer`
through `RouterVPNLibboxEngine`. The existing owned execution host retains the
server, binding, proof, measurements, MTU, cancellation and joined cleanup. A
second system VPN is not involved. Saved Last and temporary restoration retain
the already-proved Local/Server choice, complete original source and joined
native restore capsule. They do not rerun Compare selection.

The selected-path latency host now accepts every validated execution family
supported by its shared measurement API. The previous CONNECT-only condition
also prevented ordinary paired and imported-Shadowsocks execution from using
this host. Member roles now come from the captured `node_kind`, so a paired
penultimate member is not mislabeled as an external public path. Original
source, configuration, node, lease, MTU, session and current-owner checks remain.

## Executed evidence

The source gate compiles actual shipping Swift stores, source captures,
identity and presentation consumers against the actual Go source compiler.
Native Run, TUN, remote leases and proof are explicit refusing boundaries in
that source-only gate.

| Executable gate | Observed result |
|---|---|
| `deploy/test_ios_socks_execution_capture.py` | 371 checks and four compiled causal controls passed. WG/AWG entry modes, Server/Compare, both authentication choices, complete readback, identity, canonical replay and original final presentation are covered. |
| `deploy/test_ios_socks_execution_runtime.py` | Actual shipping factory/host/IPC and 50-sample facade tests passed, with six compiled causal controls for the typed factory, original bind, facade join, late owner, member roles and IPC dispatch. Paired paths with two, three and eight members and imported-Shadowsocks roles retain their distinct identities. |
| Typed native ABI inventory | All eight CONNECT/SOCKS signatures passed; removing each required signature was rejected. The original native-owner caller and its omission control passed. |
| `deploy/test_ios_node_chain_execution_restore_last.py` | 914 checks and all five original compiled causal controls passed. All previous 514 checks remain, with 400 SOCKS checks added for the original source, captured requested/selected choice, canonical Last, native join and original restore owner. |
| `deploy/test_ios_connect_execution_capture.py` | 619 existing source/consumer checks and all six compiled causal controls passed. |
| `deploy/test_ios_connect_execution_runtime.py` | Existing CONNECT runtime checks and all five compiled causal controls passed. |
| `deploy/test_ios_node_chain_socks_profiles.py` | 1,784 shipping Swift profile checks and all three original source controls passed. The existing Local matrix remains; Server/Compare add complete Add, Load, Update and Delete transactions with original private-node references. |
| `deploy/test_ios_connect_execution_workflow.py` | All six workflow tests passed, including mandatory inventory, omitted/substituted gates and required native callers. |
| `deploy/profile-settings-audit.py` | Source/profile contract passed with the distinct SOCKS eligibility predicate and its omission controls. |

The Last fixture executes actual shared SOCKS constructors, typed proof/server
policy and canonical Last parsing. Its native reader, close/join, effective-MTU
readback and opaque capsule are controlled native boundaries. The runtime
fixture likewise executes shipping host bodies around explicit native and OS
doubles. These are source-composition results, not encrypted device traffic.

The new source and runtime scripts are mandatory in the Apple native workflow,
the Darwin runtime-selection inventory and native source-input digests. The
CONNECT fixture now supplies both required typed ABI families. The shared Last
fixture includes the actual SOCKS launch-kind declaration. Existing negative
controls remain required; no production timeout or native test limit changed.

## Acceptance and remaining requirements

Exact-current generated Apple bindings, full Xcode app and PacketTunnel builds,
encrypted Server/Compare networking, package provenance and publication remain
separate release evidence. Physical VPN permission, selected-path DNS, leaks,
network transitions, visual checks, signing and distribution still require
their corresponding device or environment observations.

Longer SOCKS Server/Compare routes, other imported positions, multiple imports
and additional requested graph families remain accountable to their own
implementation and evidence. Refusal of an unsupported graph does not complete
that requirement. This change does not establish a full release, private
deployment or full-product acceptance.

# Apple complete SOCKS execution capture and retention

## Shipping behavior

The Apple application now retains one through six original WireGuard/AWG2 prefixes, a literal imported SOCKS5 node and the paired Shadowsocks final for Local, Server or Compare execution: three through eight total nodes. Public selection, private capture replay, native preparation readback, session identity and active-path presentation preserve the complete order and independent identity slots. Saved-profile Add, Load, Update and Delete continue to retain the original private-node references and separate credentials.

The runtime still uses the distinct schema-three SOCKS native owner and its negotiated UDP association. This change adds no new final Start Layer restriction. CONNECT keeps its separate schema-four source, final-layer admission and service authority. The merge preserves the concurrently published complete CONNECT changes from `66f73ea50da59a5c397cf8bf0ff0b7b79b6fe9d8`, including all original and additional capture controls and profile gates.

Retaining a proved Local or Server winner does not rerun Compare. The additional SOCKS Last gate exercises every node count, both retained concrete winners, original source and final profile, native factory ownership, effective MTU readback, source replacement and joined retirement. It is required by the native Apple contract and dedicated libbox workflow, and included in runtime source identity.

## Executable evidence

Before integration with the concurrent CONNECT unit, the frozen SOCKS source passed all six captured-source phases (2,286 checks in aggregate) and five compiled causal controls. Saved-profile tests passed six phases of 1,256 checks each; those phases repeat common profile checks and cover 216 distinct SOCKS setting combinations overall. Retained-choice restoration passed six phases of 386 checks and all five original compiled controls. The shipping host/factory test passed all six original causal controls with complete three-through-eight-node paths. All 70 native shipping audit tests passed. Original per-process deadlines were preserved.

The combined code passed both protocol-family host tests, the nine-root typed workflow guard, the strict profile admission audit, the portable runtime contract and native artifact/workflow parity. The scoped admission controls reject a restored three-node restriction, an overlong route, substituted middle/final roles, and validation of only the first prefix. On that combined source, captured-source verification passed again in 159.308 seconds for all six counts (2,286 checks) and all five compiled controls. Retained-choice restoration passed again in 197.584 seconds across six phases of 386 checks plus all five compiled controls. Those elapsed times are whole-gate totals; the original individual process limits remain unchanged. All 5,939 tracked source files were unchanged during each combined execution.

These are actual shipping Swift consumers and shared Go compiler results with explicit OS, VPN service, packet-engine and durable-store boundaries. They do not establish Xcode SDK builds, Apple signing, installed device networking, private deployment or release publication. Source, package and live acceptance remain separate.

## docs/CONNECT-EXECUTION-BOUND-FIXTURE-2026-11-16.md

# CONNECT execution length regression fixture

The iOS workflow [43819049116](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/43819049116)
at `58a73bcf284d0d91a1dc22ac98c164c90f51e7aa` failed its shared Go tests at
`TestNodeChainCONNECTExecutionPreparationRejectsChangedAuthority/long`.
The case still expected a valid four-member CONNECT source to be refused after
the implementation had gained complete three-through-eight-member prefixes.
The obsolete case remained in the current source at `9bbc2dee`.

The corrected case first constructs the supported eight-member source and
requires both Server and Compare preparation to retain its original source
pointer. It then adds an independently captured ninth member. Before requiring
the oversized CONNECT source to fail, the test proves that its eight paired
members still form a valid ordinary chain. Duplicate keys, malformed profiles,
or a misplaced imported member cannot substitute for the length check.

The other ten authority refusals remain in the same root: unselected execution,
foreign source/runtime/observer, copied binding, missing or failed observation,
cancelled observation, and replacement during observation. The original
complete-prefix test still exercises three, four and eight members, WG and both

and private Server captures. No production constructor, validation, timeout,
or native admission changes in this repair.

## docs/CONNECT-FINAL-LAYER-OWNERS-2026-11-16.md

# CONNECT final Start Layer ownership

This unit retains the original final SS2022/UoT-v2 member when an ordered
CONNECT path selects AES-256-GCM or AES-256-GCM plus XOR whitening. It extends the
schema-4 Server compiler and the concrete native final-owner union. The public
Android, Apple and desktop admission changes remain separate consumer units.
The imported HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT member still requires Start Layer Off.

The shared compiler admits only the final layer's matching concrete closure:
ordinary SS for Off/AES and the authenticated whitening outbound for AES plus
XOR. The original final source, key, paired identity, predecessor and separately
captured UoT-v2 service remain mandatory. Service derivation runs exactly once
when constructing the policy and again on each fresh Server-member read; an
inline `udp_over_tcp` object does not replace that service authority.

Native capture retains an exclusive original SS or whitening final, its exact
registered manager slot, concrete method/key/dialer/server, UoT client pointer,
version and dialer. Local and separately constructed Server captures use these
same checks. Replacing an equal-looking object does not transfer authority.
The authenticated-stream retirement receipt from the previously published
whitening prerequisite grants cleanup acknowledgement only. The original
operation errors and unrelated cipher-close errors remain visible; a failed
close can still be retried.

## Validation and its limits

The exact final shared source passed 89 records under `-race`: 25 complete
source/preparation cases, 19 original-service positive/negative records,
3 repeated-fresh-closure records, 9 Compare/retained-restoration records, and
33 adjacent existing CONNECT records. The new cases cover HTTP and HTTPS,
3/4/8-node sources, independent AES/AES-plus-XOR finals, original final
credentials/detours/UoT, Local and Server winners, and restored choices without
repeating comparison. Service node/source/key/version, missing service,
changed source key, inline UoT, unknown layer and wrong cipher are refused.
The existing runtime fixture provides the explicitly simulated native boundary;
these shared tests do not establish encrypted native forwarding.

Compilation took 65.125 seconds under the existing 300-second bound. Each
shared root retained its 90-second Go-test and 120-second process bounds. The
Compare/restore root took 85.277 seconds; its individual startup and restoration
contexts remained 20 seconds. No deadline was increased.

The final native gate passed under `-race` against the pinned sing-box
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray
`50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1` sources. All 70 real test records and both separately compiled causal controls
passed. The six baseline package durations were 1.863, 1.054, 46.958, 4.618,
22.333 and 2.823 seconds. Omitting the concrete key comparison or original
whitening generation readback produced the exact required normal assertion
failure, with no panic, signal, timeout or race accepted as a witness. Its Box
boundaries intentionally retain the existing component TUN, prefix-I/O and
resolver fixtures. Actual encrypted TCP/UDP UoT and authenticated retirement
have separate socket-level witnesses. This is not installed-kernel, device or
live-router acceptance.

The first native fixture manually constructed an incomplete Server capture;
its original owner check correctly refused it. The corrected fixture compiles
an actual independent Server policy and registers its concrete manager before
the encrypted operation. That exposed the old shared final-Off compiler guard,
which this unit repairs while retaining original service validation. An initial
shared test targeted a consumed construction-time map; the corrected negative
mutates the actual sealed native configuration. Both earlier failures remain
in the validation history and are not reported as passing runs.

The third native attempt emitted all 70 expected passing test records, then
failed its combined Box package at the unchanged 90-second alarm during the
race runtime's process exit. The retained stack shows `os.runtime_beforeExit`
and `os.Exit`; it does not establish an outstanding owner cleanup failure.
The strict parser rejected that attempt despite its earlier `PASS` line. The
four Box roots took 1.90, 57.77, 4.44 and 25.89 seconds. The gate now runs those
four actual roots in separate bounded processes: 25 owner records, 9 replacement
records, 9 Last records and 5 Server records. Together with 6 encrypted-retirement
and 16 concrete-final records, this preserves the exact 70-record union.
Every operation deadline, the 90-second package bound, 600-second subprocess
bound and 3100-second desktop-builder cap are unchanged. Parser tests require
that complete union, ordinary terminal results and rejection of valid-looking
witnesses followed by panic, fatal error, signal, race or timeout.

The canonical native source snapshot is based on the published native OpenVPN
Box implementation at `3b605c0`, the subsequently published exact mobile bridge
production, and this unit's CONNECT-only changes. All 3,980 captured core inputs
were unchanged between native attempts 3 and 4; only the two runner files
changed to partition the already observed real roots. The gate independently
checks its 43 original owner-source hashes before and after execution. The publication
composition on `c7f9de2d` preserves subsequent unrelated DNS acceptance, mobile
consumer, cache-input, desktop OpenVPN and direct encrypted OpenVPN changes.
Twenty source/test paths are byte-identical to the validated input; the two
builder/shell paths include only their additive registration atop those newer
changes. Integrated preparation (6 tests), strict runner controls (7 tests),
shell syntax and whitespace checks passed. This composition does not relabel
the canonical native proof base.

The retained native receipt has SHA-256
`53a554b3d65d87fd6f4737efb4fd887343a56192cf5d08febbdf5a30207c9579`;
its complete final log is
`6cf1c3e569edb5890fba7c1debb746088f83874bf8bba6d3c0fbf2d959c202aa`.
The frozen 3,980-input manifest is
`535d69b679818ef42fee975be619a7a386dca24f860745950dbda5322df055f7`.
The rejected aggregate-timeout log remains separately identified as
`ad9d3fbf614a4fc1ed7f2177d47dce79b59c6b34dc2bef4c6abc5114c7fadc2e`.

## docs/CONNECT-PREFIX-DESKTOP-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Desktop CONNECT packet-prefix execution evidence

## Delivered source behavior

The desktop controller and Windows, GTK, and macOS ordered-node editors now
admit Server and Compare both for one through six original paired WG/AWG
packet members followed by a literal HTTP or HTTPS CONNECT member and a
paired SS2022 final member. The complete graph therefore contains three
through eight nodes. The CONNECT member is always penultimate; final-node
preferences and credentials are captured from the actual last member.

Every packet-prefix member retains its original node identity, credentials,
proof, source fingerprint, and canonical Off/AES-256-GCM/whitening selection.
The CONNECT and final SS layers remain Off for this admitted composition.
The typed CONNECT preparation and source owner are preserved through native
preparation, public connection, and transactional profile save/load/update.
The existing `ordered_connect_trio_executions` capability spelling is retained
for wire compatibility; its typed source validation now checks the complete
bounded prefix. SOCKS remains governed by its separate composition checks.

This closes the desktop product/editor admission gap above the already
published shared CONNECT prefix compiler. It does not claim that every other
transport composition, mobile editor, or physical device is accepted.

## Executed verification

The controller executable was compiled with the race detector from the
`c05d634f` source baseline plus this unit. Its SHA-256 is
`e1cf63179ca1c387ebd41dfc4b90215ff810aa8a8ba933613c1a06cc38640410`.
All 16 selected test roots and all 180 exact PASS records completed without
a skipped root, failed root, or race report. The complete Go source inventory
and executable digest were unchanged before and after execution. The normal
120-second per-root test limit and 135-second outer bound were retained.

Coverage includes all one-to-six prefix lengths and WG/AWG Fast/AWG Strong
selection, both CONNECT transports, typed preparation for the three desktop
platform identifiers, four- and eight-node public Server connections, both
Compare winners, complete saved profiles, malformed and replacement-source
refusals, and the existing three-node ownership/restoration regressions.
Child, platform record, and kernel boundaries in this controller suite are
explicit test models; these results are not an installed tunnel verdict.

Additional executed checks:

| Composition | Observed result | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shipping Windows editor callbacks | 2,727 checks passed; the compiled prefix-layer guard-removal control failed its intended assertion | PowerShell composition; WPF was not available |
| Shipping GTK editor model | 1,977 general checks and 5,235 CONNECT checks passed; all 21 compiled causal controls were rejected | Model-only run; no display callbacks executed |
| Shipping Swift profile composition | 18,985 checks passed; five focused compiled causal variants were rejected | Swift on Linux with explicit AppKit/scheduler boundaries |
| Native shipping source audit | All 68 tests passed, including the updated complete-prefix bounds and preserved rejection controls | Source composition audit, not device acceptance |
| Patch hygiene | `git diff --check` passed | Whitespace and patch consistency |

The five focused Swift controls reintroduce the original-trio restriction or
remove prefix layer validation, final layer validation, the strict Boolean
hint check, or the distinct capability requirement. The old-trio mutation is
rejected by the new positive cases. The remaining controls are rejected by
their corresponding negative cases. The
full hosted editor suite remains configured; the local focused run does not
claim to have executed every unrelated Swift variant.

## Integration and remaining acceptance

The unit is published on top of the latest normal `main` ancestry. Subsequent
upstream Android fixture, installed SOCKS acceptance, imported-SS hostname
DNS, and test-partition changes are preserved. The local controller receipt
retains its actual baseline rather than being relabeled as a later combined
source build. The hostname-DNS production delta was reviewed and changes the
separate schema-2 imported-SS paths, not this typed CONNECT family-4 path.

Required installed Linux longer-CONNECT tests are being developed separately
with real packet peers, authenticated CONNECT providers, native carrier
ownership, final SS traffic, DNS, Compare, retained restoration, and negative
controls. Their eventual hosted results, all other exact-SHA native builds,
release publication, private deployment, and physical-device networking are
separate evidence. None is inferred from the model and controller results
recorded here.

## docs/CONNECT-PREFIX-LINUX-INSTALLED-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Installed Linux CONNECT execution through complete packet prefixes

This unit adds a required installed Linux acceptance gate for the published schema-four CONNECT Server and Compare family. It covers two through six original paired WG/AWG packet prefixes, one literal authenticated HTTP or HTTPS CONNECT source, and the original paired SS2022 final using UoTv2. The existing independent 14-case CONNECT gate retains one-prefix coverage. The 27 imported-SS and 16 SOCKS installed gates remain unchanged and required.

The new 40-case inventory contains 30 positive cases: each prefix count two through six, HTTP and HTTPS, Server and both Compare winners. Every positive case suspends the original selected owner through the real Speed Lab coordinator and restores the retained winner and endpoint with a fresh native identity. It requires no repeated comparison and exactly one new Server worker, or no Server worker for a retained Local winner. The remaining ten cases at six prefixes cover wrong first and later packet keys, original proxy credentials, TLS server name, paired final key, public identity, selected DNS, replaced source, changed native carrier, and held cleanup with the same retained retry owner. Prefix modes include WG, AWG fast and AWG strong. These longer installed cases use layer Off; the existing trio gate separately retains its layer coverage.

The provider retains complete global positions and original private/public proof owners. Local and Server packet clients have independently generated keys. Only the exact captured TCP predecessor reaches the literal CONNECT listener; the middle and final do not acquire raw UDP authority. Server suffixes require the real native packet carrier and are not admitted through the portable-carrier test exemption. Original protected executable identity, source and native bundle receipts are checked across the run. HTTP administration and direct launch of the original shipping worker remain explicit fixture adapters, not private-server deployment proof.

The independently launched native worker keeps its normal certificate verification and sanitized environment. The generated public-proof and HTTPS proxy CAs are supplied by a read-only bind of the captured trust file inside the original worker's private mount namespace. Admission checks the original distinct namespace, worker interface and address; cleanup unmounts the bind after original workers join and verifies the original system trust inode. The test does not rewrite production TLS configuration or disable certificate verification.

The reusable workflow runs on Ubuntu 24.04 amd64 and arm64 against the caller's exact native runtime bundle and is a mandatory release aggregate dependency. Only the exact 40 new cases receive the already established CONNECT execution limits of 240 seconds inside Go and 250 seconds for its process. The new workflow's aggregate budget accounts for all forty cases and preparation; existing per-case and unrelated workflow limits are unchanged. The verdict parser requires every original case, exact typed traffic/proof counters, one package PASS, and all four successful namespace retirement phases. Skips, missing or duplicate records, foreign records, races, fatal or signal aborts and incomplete retirement are refusals.

## Verification boundary

The local source is frozen on published `6e14d4962d59a5cef1c1428505e7a97d65faaaf5`, including the original Server carrier cancellation race repair and exact per-slash encrypted-test selector repair, plus this isolated fixture and gate unit. Canonical preparation uses pristine pinned sing-box `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray `50231ea` through the complete shipping native preparation pipeline. The prepared native inventory contains 5,358 files; source and immutable image identities are retained alongside build and test records.

The Python checks passed all 107 records: new CONNECT-prefix runner and workflow 26, existing SS runner 19, SOCKS runner 18, CONNECT runner 14, and release aggregate 30. The complete native artifact/workflow parity audit also passed. The privileged Linux/amd64 controller race image compiled in 218.999 seconds within the unchanged 300-second limit, using `GOMAXPROCS=1`, `GOGC=20`, and `GOMEMLIMIT=800MiB`. Its SHA-256 is `afe18da9714adffe603dab06e439fd144cf74392edb996e327ee5b9e126c313a`. The captured Go source inventory was unchanged through compilation, and the exact new installed root is present in that image. The native peer race image compiled in 27.252 seconds on an unchanged-source warm-cache retry within the same 300-second bound. Its SHA-256 is `0f94d858d6d1cee4408d88397c45de031663d1b7c79eaad946bdb209a202e3cd`. The first attempt reached the unchanged 300-second compilation limit without an image or compiler diagnostics and supplies no passing verdict; its source inventory and timeout log remain retained. Only its completed, reproducible compiler temporary directory was retired with file hashes, inode/size/mtime checks and process-reference checks; source, modules, logs and manifests remain intact.

That single immutable native image passed all 60 explicit component records across six roots in 44.526 seconds. These cover actual TCP and verified-TLS CONNECT authentication with original correct and wrong credentials, HTTP and HTTPS complete source positions for two through six prefixes and all three packet modes, original provider/global-route positions, and ten key/source/identity refusal cases. Runtime used `GOMAXPROCS=2`, `GOGC=50` and `GOMEMLIMIT=1800MiB`; the original three-second authentication operation and 120/135-second root/process limits were retained. Source and image identity were checked before each root and at completion. An initial validation-script PATH error occurred before any native test ran; the script's pinned Go PATH was corrected without changing source or image.

The component suite proves source policy and actual original proxy authentication through loopback sockets. It does not supply the installed encrypted-prefix, kernel, native-carrier or private deployment verdict; those are distinct required hosted cases.

This workspace does not have `/dev/net/tun`, `nft`, `newuidmap`, `newgidmap`, or `sudo`. It does not provide a local installed TUN/nftables/route/DNS protection verdict. No substitute device, fake operating-system observation, or weakened host admission is used to turn compilation into installed acceptance. Both hosted architectures and all forty installed cases remain required at publication. No live private router deployment is authorized or claimed.

## docs/CONNECT-WHITENING-RETIREMENT-2026-11-16.md

# Original whitening owner and joined retirement receipts

This prerequisite adds observation and cleanup receipts to the existing authenticated whitening transport. It does not enable a new public CONNECT Start Layer or grant a new connection, packet, key, or dialing capability.

`Owner.CaptureOriginalContext` captures the exact original owner and live generation. Its returned observation rejects a copied owner, replacement context or generation, Reset, Close, cancellation, and a caller whose deadline expires while the owner lock is busy.

`AuthenticatedStream.CaptureJoinedRetirement` can be captured only from the original live authenticated stream and a raw connection already held by the caller. The receipt remains unusable until the original raw connection, both I/O directions, and cipher have joined Close and the original owner map entry has retired. It retains exact stream, cipher, raw, owner, and generation identities. Both authenticated stream and packet constructors initialize the close-completion channel.

The receipt records joined cleanup only. `Close` continues to return its original error on every retry; consumers must still apply their existing operation and error-classification checks. Replacement or unrelated cipher errors gain no new acceptance from this library change.

## Validation

The complete `internal/startwhitening` package passed `go test -race -json -count=1 -timeout=90s ./internal/startwhitening`: **32 test roots, 67 records, no skips or failures**, in 13.637 seconds including compilation, within the existing 180-second process bound. The package used Go 1.26.3 with one compile CPU and an 800 MiB compiler memory limit. The four changed Go files and every other package Go source were checked against the exact passing source manifest after copying to the publication worktree; no package source changed after that run.

The added tests use the shipping owner, an actual `net.Pipe`, and an explicit blocking cipher lifecycle double. They cover held-close refusal, exact close-error retention across retries, copied and foreign owners, typed-nil raw input, raw and cipher replacement, retained owner-map entries, cancellation, and caller deadlines. Existing stream and packet ownership tests also ran in the same package suite.

The proof was captured on repository base `0f07335da94b015fbc73d2581f29cbe16dbc9047`. The publication unit was prepared on `58a73bcf284d0d91a1dc22ac98c164c90f51e7aa`, then integrated onto `b0f13141a21a750aeeeed21efb06411c2ba59c8b` while preserving its newer Local OpenVPN restoration work. All fifteen package Go inputs still match the passing source manifest. Native CONNECT concrete-final adoption, actual encrypted SS2022/UoT-v2 traffic, native Box execution, and public host final-layer admission remain separate work. This package result is not a native, kernel, device, or installed-path acceptance claim.

## docs/CONNECTED-IMPORTED-SS-CANDIDATES-2026-11-16.md

# Connected candidates on complete imported-SS paths

The connected candidate planner now accepts every original WG/AWG packet
position in a complete schema-2 path with one through six packet prefixes,
one independently captured SS2022 middle, and the original paired Shadowsocks
final. Previously, `NewCandidatePlan` refused these paths unless they had
exactly three members. The change removes only that count fence.

A source plan does not grant a live route. The existing measurement constructor
and every subsequent observation still require the complete original chain's
fresh Healthy/Ready state, private proofs, public proof, selected DNS, source
and configuration, engine reader, session, network and candidate catalog. An
observed owner change revokes the measurement permanently. A held connection
close retains its original measurement lease until retirement joins.

The measurement remains fifty routed destination TCP connections. It does not
authenticate the candidate's Shadowsocks credentials or measure application
throughput. The imported stream and paired final do not acquire this packet
prefix timing capability. Saved closed-prefix preparation retains its separate
source and application-closed lifetime; it cannot substitute for a ready
connected complete path.

The new peer fixture installs its one literal candidate route through the
existing original-handler decorator before any native peer device starts.
Existing callers pass no decorator and retain their original construction.
There is no mutation of a live handler's route map.

## Executed evidence

| Gate | Required outcome | Actual result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared race image | Original 180-second compile bound | Passed in 57.477 seconds |
| Shared source/runtime | Seven complete roots, 71 records; 180/195-second root/process bounds | All 71 passed, no skips; 87.894 seconds total |
| Compiled readiness omission | Remove only fresh `HealthyContext` from candidate `current`; retain constructor admission | Build passed in 28.474 seconds; exact `changed complete path retained candidate capability` witness failed as required |
| Canonical native peer race image | Original 600-second bound | Passed in 288.685 seconds |
| Actual encrypted connected path | 24 cases: WG/AWG Fast/AWG Strong, two/six prefixes, every original position | All passed; 1,200 completed destination TCP samples, each case with 50 closes and one released lease |
| Actual native refusals | Eight complete cases under unchanged 90/100-second bounds | All passed: last-prefix key, middle key, final identity, selected DNS, catalog, network, native retirement and held-close cancellation |
| Existing Box owner image | Original 600-second bound | Passed in 94.436 seconds |
| Existing Box owner root | All 17 original records under 120/130-second bounds | All passed in 7.592 seconds |
| Final composed Python validators | Strict native runner and merged phase registry | 10 and 33 tests passed |

The 32 native cases ran as separately bounded whole roots, with 64 required
run/pass records across those invocations and 34 distinct root/leaf names.
Their reported test times total 81.84 seconds; the longest was 6.03 seconds.
Each positive first executed the actual full-chain `Run`, private proof, public
proof and selected DNS before calling `NewMeasurementContext`. The earlier
encrypted packet counters increased through the selected original position.
Wrong-key refusals require actual earlier private proofs and counters at the
intended failing hop; an unrelated early bootstrap failure cannot satisfy them.
The selected-DNS refusal requires a real DNS attempt after prior private proof
and forbids final public success.

The shared route is explicitly doubled. The native component executes actual
WG/AWG and SS encryption, private HTTP/TLS proof, DNS and destination TCP, while
its outer host/session observation is an explicit component boundary. The
separate Box root retains actual Box/manager/readers with doubled transport and
kernel I/O. These results do not claim installed kernel/TUN, physical-device or
deployment acceptance.

## Source identity and integration

Shared execution used private snapshot `e0659011` on published `70e6b344`.
Native execution used private `ce6cd8c1` on published `bdab0b5b`, including the
subsequent twenty-line native wrong-key assertion improvement. All shared Go
source and test bytes remained unchanged between those snapshots. Native

`50231eaf` by `build-native-runtime.prepare_native_sources`.

The source review found the connected-SS production, tests, source-owner code,
runtime and existing Box owner fixture unchanged. The actual imported-start,

retain their separate owners. The registry preserves both mandatory families.
The old images remain evidence for their recorded snapshots, not a claimed
fresh compile of the composed tree.

The four shared roots are mandatory disjoint core phases. All 32 native cases
are mandatory preflight cases of the existing Linux node-host TUN runner,
using its original retained peer image and source/image checks. Builder inputs

The initial local native command stopped before compilation because its
