## docs/DESKTOP-OPENVPN-FRESH-OBSERVATION-2026-11-16.md

# Desktop OpenVPN fresh observations — 2026-11-16

This unit preserves the complete desktop controller adoption published as `110dbd3f383753ea3277cf12d0c309b06cf8381d` and Apple Hysteria2 unit `51ad480d4a719ed49a4a84c89fc5129ef9e94bc8`. It changes only one fresh source/physical observation and the typed Ready observation. It does not open another graph family or alter platform admission.

The parent retains the same original native selection and validates it before reading its typed owner. The combined owner read freshly validates the exact preparation and returns the original policy/source pointers plus copied metadata and physical capture. Ready freshly validates the original projection, preparation, private physical owner and MTU plan, then obtains copied proof inventory and the same selected DNS capture without re-entering multiple equivalent public getters. These stack-local reads store no validity result or native receipt.

The original source, native physical chain, physical wrapper, original desktop policy, projection config and selected DNS detour are independently mutated by the retained maximum-graph record test. All must still fail. Returned metadata, proofs and DNS captures are mutated separately to prove callers cannot change the retained values.

## Validation boundaries

The earlier unpublished controller snapshot reproduced a four-second Result/current refusal and two-second freshness refusal under concurrent load. Its unchanged isolated eight-node Compare→Server run passed in 19.91 test seconds; its CPU profile attributed 3.99 cumulative CPU seconds to readSourceObservation, 1.93 to readPolicyCapture and 0.88 to Ready.Validate. That source/model snapshot is preserved separately and is not relabelled as this published controller. It motivated eliminating duplicate work; no operation or test deadline changed.

The published controller's complete 114-record mandatory inventory remains unchanged. Local selected controller evidence uses every shipping production file and a recorded original test-fixture dependency closure, omitting only unrelated test files after prior complete-package compiler SIGKILLs. It is not a complete-package build or installed OS/provider acceptance.

The Darwin MTU comparison helpers were reviewed because their family-specific comparison list did not mention OpenVPN. Every actual live MTU operation subsequently enters the existing full currentLocked check, which already retains exact OpenVPN source/physical/preparation/child authority. No actual I/O bypass was identified; redundant comparison-only edits were intentionally omitted.

## Final receipts

The tested source is `51ad480d` plus seven residual files, captured by the complete source manifest SHA256 `b7bf012e64a60d1b2447ae90a5febe394ab726328387d7d9194f62226d19481d`. The later composition preserves `638808fa` (Apple audit and documentation only); none of the seven tested source files changes.

| Actual check | Result | Original bound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared package race compilation | 72.084 seconds | 240 seconds |
| Controller race compilation, all shipping production + 121/570 original fixture files | 138.735 seconds | 240 seconds |
| Fresh-validation omission race compilation | 78.602 seconds | 240 seconds |
| Canonical desktop records | 103/103 records; 12.337 seconds | 120-second test, 135-second process |
| Maximum graph owner/copy records | 27/27 records; 9.025 seconds | 120/135 seconds |
| Published public controller + original preparation | 40/40 distinct records across 12 roots; 180.543 aggregate test-process seconds | 120/135 seconds per original root |
| Omitted fresh `p.Validate()` | Intended `owner-source` assertion failure; 2.842 seconds | 120/135 seconds |
| Shipping observation audit controls | Three roots passed; 55.810 seconds | Existing controls unchanged |
| SpeedLab imported-current audit | Seven roots passed; 4.741 seconds | Existing controls unchanged |
| Published strict controller runner checks | Five tests passed | Existing inventory/bounds unchanged |

The omitted-validation executable must fail exactly at `TestDesktopOpenVPNExecutionMaximumRecords/server/owner-source` with `fresh OpenVPN physical read accepted changed original owner source`; a compiler error, timeout, panic, race, different leaf, or missing terminal record receives no credit.

The current isolated eight-node Compare→Server run passed in 17.377 wall seconds while retaining its original four-second Result/current and two-second freshness budgets. The same image receipt is reused once in the 40-record composite inventory; this is not a second execution. Its CPU profile has 16.12 seconds duration and 18.11 seconds sampled CPU, with cumulative source observation 3.02 seconds, policy capture 1.20 seconds and Ready validation 0.51 seconds. These are current-source measurements, not a controlled percentage comparison against the earlier different fixture snapshot.

Exact image SHA256 values:

- Shared: `9bfd0c087f585393deebd0eb032c9a4c4a477ca5f63d6e4788cabe4571095710`.
- Controller: `d51c63d388d3c13fda7342866140a361a596b46a080d2cfa5854dc793e305037`.
- Omission: `bd990950bd6f9e9d431599211bd9a5ed48ae99755ecb428a2ecd456ab95c3946`.

All use pinned Go 1.26.3 and `-race`. Compiler resources were bounded to one CPU, `-p=1`, GOGC20 and an 800MiB memory target; test runtime retained the normal two CPUs, GOGC50 and 1800MiB target. Per-build temporary directories are distinct. Exact commands, source and fixture-closure manifests, strict JSON records, CPU profile and failed earlier observations remain under `openvpn-desktop-observation-validation` and the earlier `openvpn-desktop-public-validation` receipt directory.

## docs/DESKTOP-OPENVPN-NATIVE-CHILD-2026-11-16.md

# Desktop OpenVPN execution child — November 16, 2026

The desktop native child now consumes the original typed OpenVPN Server or
Compare preparation for the bounded ordered graph: one or more paired WG/AWG
prefixes, an independently provisioned OpenVPN member, and the paired SS2022
final. The child uses the same retained execution, physical-policy, selected
proof and teardown owners as the existing desktop host. This continues the
recovered external-node/ordered-path and Local/Server/Compare requirements;
it does not establish every requested graph or a complete installed product.

## Captured native ownership

The child validates the original preparation, physical source, native and
policy pointers, and both captured OpenVPN configurations before construction.
Its context-bound owner rechecks those identities and cancellation before
use. Linux, Windows and Darwin physical adapters retain the existing owned
network policy and teardown; the imported OpenVPN remote does not acquire an
unrelated direct-network permission. Partial construction retains its cleanup
owner. Typed adoption, Ready and Last records use the shared validators
published in `8177db3c`; restoring Last retains the already-proved execution.

The native builder requires the new source, lifecycle, longest-route, Box and
compiled-control phases. The current desktop CONNECT gates and Apple layered
inputs remain intact. The separate public controller/editor integration is
not included in this component publication.

## Executed evidence

The actual complete pinned native core was prepared by the shipping preparers
and compiled with race instrumentation on Linux. No reduced native package
substitute was used. The following **116 native test records** passed across
twelve roots:

| Phase | Records | Observed scope |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Source | 67 | Original typed command, source and construction refusals for declared Linux/Windows/Darwin targets and three-, four- and eight-node inputs |
| Longest route | 5 | Actual retained observer for eight-node TCP/UDP Server and Compare cases |
| Lifecycle | 18 | Saved Local/Server choice, foreign owner and selected proof refusal, cleanup retry and late construction |
| Box | 26 | Actual unstarted Box, OpenVPN endpoint/manager pointers and retained close/join receipt |

The six independently generated real shared-source fixtures and their producer
root also passed in each phase. These repeated seven-record producer runs are
not counted as additional native records.

Three separately compiled controls failed with their exact intended witnesses:
an incorrect schema dispatch lost the typed source; removing the original
physical-pointer check admitted a replaced construction; removing the Ready
error guard admitted a substituted owner or proof. Compiler errors, timeouts,
races and unrelated failures cannot satisfy these controls. Nine runner tests
also validate exact inventories, missing or forged source and receipt rejection,
and mandatory shipping registration.

The original 120-second Go test, 600-second native process, 45-second child
admission and phase bounds remain. The four longest observer leaves completed
in 5.28–5.73 seconds. An initial longest-route fixture incorrectly generated a
96-digit password; it now uses the existing canonical 48-digit format. Strict
production validation was preserved. The first retry compiled successfully
but ran out of scratch space before observation. That failure is retained;
the successful retry used the same corrected source after recorded retirement
of reproducible build cache.

The source phase preceded only that test-fixture correction; its 67 roots do
not call the corrected lifecycle fixture. Longest, lifecycle, Box and all three
controls then passed on the corrected frozen source. The final 25-file source
manifest has SHA-256
`90fee9e3e10eb3e4bd423131e1ea0a00aeaacae784a5c81216c6646e9ea7b875`;
the complete validation receipt has SHA-256
`10a55c7193cfee5a4f29410200c626b2ddeb9af5d9b86921a95694d9f114ecec`.
Twenty-four source preimages were unchanged through `9bbc2dee`; only the
central builder needed the additive composition preserving newer gates.

## Evidence still required

Lifecycle cases explicitly model TUN, physical activation and selected-path
proof callbacks. The Box cases use real unstarted managers and do not prove
encrypted provider traffic. Declared Windows/Darwin inputs executed on Linux;
they do not establish native execution on those operating systems. Exact-SHA
hosted builds and package execution, public controller/editor acceptance,
installed kernel/firewall behavior, physical-device networking, signing,
private deployment and whole-release publication remain separate checks.

## Original desktop gate source inventory

The shipping native builder invokes the original OpenVPN owner gate with
`--tagged-only`. Its source verifier previously used the mobile CONNECT file
inventory, although the canonical desktop preparer intentionally omits those
mobile bridge files and includes the desktop reference peer. The verifier now
uses an explicit desktop inventory for that existing caller. Missing files do
not select a mode; the default mobile inventory is unchanged.

All 12 OpenVPN runner tests and the original 14 owner runner tests passed.
The inventory test removes and changes each required file in both projections
and rejects the opposite projection. An independent check of the actual
canonically prepared desktop core reproduced the old missing-mobile-bridge
error and passed the explicit desktop verification, with source identity
`fc2aeed1be5e30336b2f5b278f1cc91641d12153207e9e5209a734746c14a543`.
This repairs source verification before the existing native gate; it adds no
runtime claim and changes no test deadline or production owner.

## docs/DESKTOP-OPENVPN-PARENT-EXECUTION-2026-11-16.md

# Desktop OpenVPN Server/Compare parent integration — November 16, 2026

The desktop public controller now carries an independently paired imported
OpenVPN middle through Server or Compare for complete 3–8-member paths. It
retains the original Local source, independent Server pairing, ordered packet
prefix, paired SS2022 final and saved source tokens. Eligible paths use the
existing typed OpenVPN preparation and physical owner; generic preparation is
not relabeled as schema 5. Unpaired imported OpenVPN remains a separate Local
path and does not acquire Server credentials from a public capability hint.

The parent keeps the exact preparation, original desktop policy, physical
owner, staged command, transfer identity and selected epoch through typed
adoption and Ready. Ready requires actual selected DNS/public proofs and the
original MTU/protection capture. The OpenVPN middle's public proof remains
visibly distinct from the paired private-node proofs. Saved restoration keeps
the selected winner and original source rather than starting a new Compare;
retirement remains joined to the captured native child and protection owner.

The existing editors and their complete-profile actions are retained exactly
from the separately published [editor unit](DESKTOP-OPENVPN-EDITOR-2026-11-16.md).
The parent connects those published capabilities to the
[native child](DESKTOP-OPENVPN-NATIVE-CHILD-2026-11-16.md),
[typed record protocol](DESKTOP-OPENVPN-EXECUTION-RECORDS-2026-11-16.md), and
[earlier packet-prefix measurements](OPENVPN-CANDIDATE-PREFIXES-2026-11-16.md).
Independent pairing remains operator-provisioned through the existing private
import/setup contract; this change neither manufactures pairing nor exposes
its private credentials in profile hints.

## Local evidence and limits

- The first parent compilation failed before tests on one selection slice type
  and four test-only accessor calls. Existing conversion/validated launch-input
  APIs corrected those exact calls; the failed log is retained.
- A subsequent full race build using local 1-CPU/600-MiB settings linked the
  actual image but reached the unchanged 240-second command cap during the
  remaining vet work. It is not a complete build/vet pass. Its immutable
  `c07f8c49` source image later passed all 20 required roots: 93 source/protection
  records and 21 public/restore records, 114 distinct IDs, in 304.016 seconds
  summed per-root wall time. This covered Server and both Compare outcomes at
  3, 4 and 8 members, both saved winners and changed Ready receipts.
- After absorbing published editor/wrapper and shared-prefix prerequisites on
  `dbe971b4`, the whole canonical race-and-vet build passed in 113.427 seconds
  under the original 240-second cap with the shipping 2-CPU/GOGC-50/1800-MiB
  environment. Seven intact current-source roots passed 53 records: original
  preparations, source/protection refusals, both 8-member Compare outcomes,
  saved restoration and Ready refusals. These overlap earlier test IDs and
  are not added to the 114 as a unique-test total.
- Both required compiled omission controls passed their strict causal verifiers.
  Removing the typed preparation branch compiled successfully in 118.31 seconds,
  then produced only the intended `linux/3/server` preparation refusal. Removing
  the exact retained physical-owner comparison compiled in 178.58 seconds,
  then produced the intended public 4-member Compare/Server owner-substitution
  refusal. These are actual assertion failures after successful mutant builds,
  not compiler failures or synthetic runner results. Original sources remained
  unchanged. The 240-second compiler and 120/135-second test/process bounds were
  preserved.
- Five controller-runner tests passed separately. Nine intact impacted source
  audit roots passed in 72.946 seconds, covering the changed owner/product
  predicates, existing CONNECT/SOCKS ownership negatives, OpenVPN pairing and
  platform negatives, complete-member identity, Darwin bounds and the unchanged
  full positive scoring aggregate. The complete 71-root source mutation suite
  remains mandatory in CI; this local selection is not labeled a full-suite pass.

Current source identity is
`c1d86f21b54d6b9c035156efc4109580c9fdbdf861cf59930136cd0f3ce89173`;
the canonical image SHA256 is
`a733f785235ffdac0ed18c6da9004e5d3ee35fa46e4053978f9e03773cd460a3`.
The earlier linked-image source/image identities and all failed attempts are
kept separately in the guarded validation receipt. The exact published
Windows, Swift and GTK editor source and execution-log hashes were checked;
no older alternative editor implementation or redundant UI result is merged.

These Go tests run the shipping parent/controller and shared constructors with
explicit native-child and kernel-observation models. Linux transaction and
portable Darwin argument tests do not establish Windows WFP or Darwin PF/TUN
traffic. The existing editor receipts exercise PowerShell/Swift callbacks and
GTK models on Linux; they do not establish WPF/AppKit rendering or physical
GUI/device acceptance. Required hosted native gates retain their original
inventories and deadlines. Full current-host native construction, encrypted
provider traffic, installed protection, signing, packages and deployment remain
separate delivery evidence. The original broader graph and platform
requirements remain open wherever their own source or acceptance is unfinished.

## docs/DESKTOP-ORDERED-EXECUTION-COMMAND-EVIDENCE.md

# Desktop ordered execution command prerequisites

This unit retains the typed physical execution adapters introduced by `b0a77da3ddcfe08a7d55c05d159e4869f4ce9505`; it does not add another physical factory. Its distinct changes bind a staged desktop execution preparation to the original session, target, physical source, complete configuration and execution-source digest, retain the existing physical MTU transaction around the exact native execution engine, and permit Darwin readiness records only for the closed execution physical policy.

Server and Compare can share the initial physical configuration. The separate execution-source digest is therefore required: a changed execution setting must not borrow the same physical/configuration hashes. The command capture rejects missing, Local, generic, changed-execution and ambiguous staged sources, as well as changed session, target, configuration, physical source and graph bytes.

The MTU companion requires the original Box, exact sealed physical policy and `binding.OwnsEngine(engine)` before delegating to the existing physical grant transaction and actual native execution manager. Its lifetime callback observes the retained physical generation so that an authorized old-reader detachment does not itself invalidate the transaction. This is an adapter prerequisite; this unit does not claim an installed execution MTU replacement has run.

## Exact native preparation and verification

The native source was prepared from checkout `d6de40859713754bf39724cecdacac66a0e46fc5` plus this unit using the complete official `prepare_native_sources` entry point, Go 1.26.3, pinned sing-box `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`, and pinned Xray `50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`. All 1,020 common Xray files were unchanged. Strict preparation and post-test verification compared all 2,439 mapped source records.

Both actual generated packages were built with `go test -c -p=1 -mod=readonly -race`, the shipping desktop tags (`with_wireguard,with_gvisor,with_quic,with_utls,with_openvpn,with_naive_outbound,with_purego`) and `-ldflags=-checklinkname=0`. The first Box linker was killed under shared resource pressure; the unchanged serial retry passed with `GOMAXPROCS=1`, `GOGC=10`, and `GOMEMLIMIT=512MiB`. No test deadline was increased.

The executed checks were:

- `^TestRouterVPNDesktopNodeChainExecutionPhysical`: all 13 exact mandatory verdicts, using the upstream real Box/encrypted-prefix constructors and an explicitly unstarted TUN double.
- `^TestRouterVPNDesktopNodeChainExecutionCommand`: all 19 exact mandatory verdicts, including six platform/settings replays and eleven source/ownership substitutions.
- The existing `^TestRouterVPNDesktopNodeChainPhysical` and `^TestRouterVPNDesktopNodeChainCommand` roots from the same actual race binaries.
- `go test -race -p=1 ./internal/mobilemultihop -run '^TestDesktopNodeReadyDarwin' -count=1 -timeout=90s -v`, including closed execution readiness and changed receipt/type refusals.
- `ExecutionAdmissionGate` and the two existing native MTU inventory regressions: three Python tests passed. The gate rejects each missing verdict, duplicate output, skips and race output; it does not weaken the exact native inventory.

Native binary executions retained their 90-second limits. The actual preparation, build, test and before/after source receipts are in the task evidence directory `desktop-execution-evidence/adopted-run`.

## Remaining shipping boundary

The command capture is not yet a public Server/Compare admission path. Actual CLI dispatch, authenticated platform execution capture, the one-child execution/measurement/MTU adjunct, parent launch adoption and Last restoration remain follow-up work. Stop must cancel proof work while retaining the original Box until remote lease and execution cleanup positively join. Cross-child restoration must retain the original complete source and Last decision, join the old child first, and prove the restored path before Connected. These prerequisite tests do not establish privileged installed TUN, PF/WFP/nft, or physical-device acceptance.

## docs/DESKTOP-SAVED-PREFIX-CATALOG-2026-11-16.md

# Desktop saved-prefix candidate catalog

The saved-prefix chooser previously built the connected full-path candidate plan
before constructing its own closed preparation. Longer saved paths containing a
penultimate external Shadowsocks member therefore failed at the wrong admission
boundary, even when the shared prefix-only constructor accepted the complete source.

The chooser, preparation, fresh-source checks and runtime recapture now select the
exact requested scope. A prefix catalog retains its own immutable preparation and
has no connected candidate plan. The operation retains that exact preparation
owner, the complete original source, all saved candidate files, catalog revision,
network identity and cancellation lifetime. Fresh recapture must match the original
closed source. Ordinary connected and complete-path catalogs keep their existing
independent admission rules.

Only the selected paired packet prefix can become runnable. The imported suffix and
original final remain complete Save provenance, including their DNS/device-policy
restrictions. This change grants no application ingress, DNS forwarding, Connected
state, or connected measurement authority. It does not admit typed SOCKS/CONNECT
original captures; those require their separate typed preparation constructors.

## Validation

The full shipping controller test package compiled with race instrumentation and
vet on `1c5a0ab79322c7f8c8f3890f1830423c25a4411a` plus this scoped change. The
seven complete selected roots passed all 29 records. They exercise the actual
chooser, catalog, preparation, closed native projection, source recapture and unused
retirement for four/eight-node literal and hostname Shadowsocks paths. Refusals
cover complete-scope substitution, imported/final positions, equal-valued foreign
preparation ownership, changed scope, candidate credentials, final source bytes and
a replacement physical-network observation. Existing ordinary complete preparation,
prefix cancellation and receipt-purpose tests remain passing.

A compiled counterfactual restored the actual former chooser call through the
connected catalog. The four-node literal positive test then failed with the exact
expected chooser-refusal witness, exit 1, and no timeout, race, abort or missing test.
The passing controller and counterfactual used the same flags and original bounds.

These are executable source/controller tests, with an explicit physical-network
observation fixture. No child process, VPN packet, private DNS request, physical
device or installed native widget was exercised by this unit. Actual encrypted
native prefix tests and exact-SHA release gates remain separate requirements.
The complete Go source gate discovers both new roots from the compiled test image.

| Evidence | Recorded result |

| Seven positive/refusal roots | 52.853s total; each 120s Go / 135s process bound |
| Counterfactual compile | 123.755s / 240s |

## docs/DESKTOP-SELECTED-LATENCY-NATIVE-2026-11-16.md

# Selected execution latency native producer and bounded evidence

The desktop child retains its actual `NodeChainExecutionRuntime` and one
original `NodeChainExecutionMeasurement` for selected fifty-sample requests.
Retest creates another latency facade on that same measurement and requires
byte-identical selected capture; it never reruns Compare. The parent controller
and Windows, AppKit and GTK consumers are a separate coordinated publication.

Local proof covers eight mandatory roots across two explicitly identified
images: five complete roots/16 records at `33f46221`, then three complete
roots/29 records at corrected `78108a6a`. This is **not** one fresh 45-record
run on the publication tree. Both compiled omission controls remain mandatory
in exact-SHA CI: the local control attempt could not acquire the shared lock
and started no compiler or runtime child. No full-native-gate, encrypted-routing,
physical-device or installed-host pass is claimed by this unit.

## Retained owner and protocol

The first actual latency facade supplies the opaque selected capture. Every
request retains the original activity, drains and closes that facade, then calls
`ValidateClosedCaptureContext` while the original measurement remains alive.
Only after those joins may a saved result be published. Cleanup retires the
measurement before its controller, native engine or MTU replacement retires the
underlying resources. Source, configuration, publication and request files keep
their original inode and byte bindings.

`node-chain-execution-latency-capture.json` contains schema 1, kind
`routervpn-node-chain-execution-latency-capture-v1`, the original typed Ready
object in `capture`, and actual opaque facade JSON in `selected_capture`.
The publication is immutable. Fresh typed Ready validates independently; its
association permits only a forward heartbeat timestamp and newly measured
positive finite prefix response durations. All other fields remain equal,
including comparison winner/scores, source, node identities, proof flags,
selection epoch, proof generation, reader and MTU.

The separate `node-chain-execution-measure-{request,cancel,result}.json`
mailbox leaves the ordinary grammar intact. Requests contain exactly schema,
operation (`selected-latency-50`), request_id, generation, deadline_at, path_id,
node_id, position, capture and selected_capture. Cancel contains the exact
request bytes. Result echoes these ten fields and adds status, completed_at
and optional saved receipt. Limits: 16 KiB request/cancel, 32 KiB publication,
8 KiB opaque selected capture and 128 KiB result.

## Exact component boundary

The component uses the existing three-node paired source:

| Position | Profile | Node kind | Configured mode/layer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | ordered-router-0 | router-vpn | WG / Off |
| 1 | ordered-router-1 | router-vpn | WG / Off |
| 2 | ordered-router-2 | router-vpn | SS2022 / Off |

Its physical metadata is Linux with retained MTU 1420. The fifty-sample target
is only the final paired node at position 2, using its captured authenticated
private `/health` endpoint. This is not imported-public TCP measurement or a
two-through-eight-node encrypted matrix.

Real shared selection, HTTP/TLS, Manager leases, measurement/facade and native
child mailbox code execute. Native transport routing, remote worker proof
completion and TUN readback are explicit component doubles. The tagged fixture
copies the existing shared runtime/HTTP fixture bodies and binds the original
physical projection directly. Production runtime/facade bodies are not
replaced. Counters count actual authenticated HTTP benchmark requests,
separately from periodic private proof requests.

Explicit Server, Compare selecting Local and Compare selecting Server each
perform two actual fifty-response runs. Each asserts 100 benchmark requests,
the same service/capture, and unchanged selection count. Cancel-before-start
asserts zero initial samples followed by a real fifty-response Retest. These
facts do not imply all-family or physical routing acceptance.

## Local evidence and preserved failures

Original source `33f46221c5c2a391b2ecab0854b4d4f166770bc2` was based on
published `7f602c07`. Canonical preparation passed in 15.549 seconds under
600. Its actual race image is
`9a81828c32604ef7e2ddf41301d52b16d1e8fbd28a37cd41cb09e0687c5074a5`.

| Original image whole root | Records | Result / test seconds |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| HTTP50Server | 1 | PASS / 94.18 |
| HTTP50CompareLocal | 1 | PASS / 72.15 |
| HTTP50CompareServer | 1 | PASS / 94.67 |
| CancelledBeforeStart | 1 | PASS / 45.81 |
| RequestGrammar | 12 | PASS / 0.01 |

The baseline then failed `OriginalOwners/capture-inode` during fixture
cleanup. Admission correctly rejected the replacement inode, but the fixture
left it in place and exact-owned cleanup correctly refused to delete it. The
failed 716.791-second phase, complete log and image remain preserved. The
test-only correction checks rename/write and restores the original inode
after the refusal and zero-sample assertions, before cleanup. No production
ownership check, test case or deadline was weakened.

Corrected source `78108a6a4af3127c626560dc493068c9c74c2431` was based on
published `b717b9ab`. Preparation passed in 31.828 seconds. Exact race/test
dependency enumeration recorded 8,112 consumed files in each tree and the same
package inventory. The sole changed consumed file was the corrected latency
test; there was no consumed intersection with b717's reservation changes.
`go.sum` matched. Module equivalence normalized only the exact old/new pinned
Xray replacement roots, retaining both raw `go.mod` hashes.

The corrected actual race compile passed in 233.108 seconds under 600. Its
155,961,992-byte image is
`f95b79ab658ed36f8dee694088302ebd82f998f9b77fca526033463b275c45de`.
The affected phase passed in 290.188 seconds:

| Corrected image whole root | Records | Result / test seconds |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| OriginalOwners | 9 | PASS / 36.54 |
| RejectsSyntheticController | 1 | PASS / 1.14 |
| FreshProofAssociation | 19 | PASS / 4.06 |

The local control attempt returned exit 75 after 55.187 seconds of lock
contention: **no control compiler or test child started**. Earlier preparation
attempts v3/v4/v5 also retain no-child lock refusals. Both compiled controls are
still registered: omission of the original measurement pointer check must fail
`OriginalOwners/copied-measurement`; omission of selected capture equality must
fail `OriginalOwners/foreign-selection` at the specified witness. These remain
pending exact-SHA CI execution, not local passes.

Task receipts remain in `selected-latency-native-validation`:
`native-v6/{prepare-receipt,baselines-receipt,partial-baseline-inventory}.json`
and `native-v7/{prepare-receipt,consumed-dependency-equivalence,affected-compile-1,
affected-inventory,affected-receipt,controls-receipt}.json`, with complete logs
and source/image hashes. Historical receipt identities are not rewritten.

## Bounds, composition and remaining acceptance

The mandatory shipping gate remains eight whole roots/45 exact records plus
both compiled controls. Baseline: one 600-second compiler and eight 120-second
Go / 135-second process roots; maximum 1,680 seconds within the 1,800-second
phase. Controls: two 600-second compilers and 120/135-second executions;
maximum 1,470 seconds within 1,600. Local resources were two Go CPUs, GOGC 50
and GOMEMLIMIT 1800 MiB, with the original read-open shared lock. No deadline
was increased or child filtered from a selected whole root.

Publication is composed on `e6f7e61ed70e0fe42eb8d39dbd517f7196ea3ab5`.
All 14 executable owned files remain byte-identical to corrected `78108a6a`;
only this document changes. Later upstream changes add Python/workflow
inventory fixes and a separate selected-entry candidate API/test/registry.
That new shared Go API is an additive compilation input, has no initializer
or changed existing latency/runtime body, and is not called by this producer.
Composition alone is not a native rerun. Five lightweight strict runner tests
passed on the composed source; their synthetic records validate the parser,
source projection, inventories, causal anchors and registration only.
Root also independently ran the current desktop SOCKS source validator's 19
tests and CONNECT source validator's nine tests on the composed tree; all
passed. Those checks cover the retained upstream source-inventory fix and
negative controls, without compiling or exercising native transport.

The full baseline and both compiled controls on the published SHA remain
required for acceptance. Parent/UI integration, real encrypted transport,
installed-host and device gates remain separately scoped. Schema 6 Server/Compare
and selected throughput are follow-on work and are not admitted by this unit.

## docs/DESKTOP-TYPED-SAVED-PREFIX-CANDIDATES-2026-11-16.md

# Desktop typed saved-prefix measurements — 2026-11-16

The desktop candidate chooser now retains the full saved Local SOCKS5 or HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT source while measuring an earlier WG/AWG packet prefix. The complete three- through eight-member source, credentials, final-node policies and original owner remain captured. The distinct typed preparation constructors start only the selected packet prefix; application traffic, DNS and ordinary Connected authority remain closed.

The catalog accepts exactly one original source family. Generic connected-path admission still requires its own generic policy. A saved typed suffix cannot be converted into a generic connected source by projecting or editing its envelope. Complete-path temporary preparation retains the existing typed platform gate. The independent prefix operation uses its existing native prefix platform gate and source compiler, because it never starts that suffix.

The initial full-package functional run exposed a real earlier admission check: it required capability for the unstarted typed suffix before reaching prefix preparation. This change narrows that check to complete-path preparation. It does not remove the full-path restriction or grant connected-path capability.

## Verification

The corrected actual desktop source passed 13 focused test roots with 80 exact test records. Coverage includes SOCKS5, HTTP CONNECT and HTTPS CONNECT at three, four and eight members; unchanged generic Shadowsocks preparation; complete-scope refusal; imported/final positions; changed credentials, assets and network; equal-looking replacement owners; mixed policies; and restoration of the untouched saved state.

Four compiled regression controls were rejected at their intended assertions. Three restore the complete old generic-only prefix dispatcher and exercise each typed family. The fourth restores the old full-suffix capability check. All controls retained the same 240-second compilation bound, 120-second Go test bound and 135-second process bound as the positive run.

This was additional **non-race, full-package functional validation**. The earlier local race compiler was terminated before producing a test image. A later functional compile reached its original limit during linking; the retained warm build then exposed the admission bug above. Those failures remain recorded. The shipping race/native gates are unchanged and remain required; this result is not a race pass.

The tests execute the shipping controller, capture and shared source compilers. Physical-network observation and native lifecycle boundaries in these controller fixtures remain explicit. Actual platform packet traffic, generated runtimes, installed-device acceptance and exact-SHA release publication are separate evidence.

## Source and evidence identities

| --- | --- |
| Corrected composed source | `76adf093eb1924b6fb3054a0258a4e92e6891918a1fa3e380457691915e067e8` |

The executable used the `8aabd9f8` source with four desktop changes and 16 then-separate shared prerequisite files. All 16 prerequisites exactly match the now-published source through `460a1d0d`; none is replayed by this commit. The four desktop files retain their tested bytes while newer Apple, Android and Hysteria2 work is preserved. The shared native scope is documented in [the typed-prefix prerequisite](TYPED-SAVED-PREFIX-CANDIDATE-PREPARATION-2026-11-16.md). The new controller roots also run in the release's complete Go source inventory. Working logs are scratch artifacts; their identities are recorded here for traceability.

## Follow-up race validation

The same original 13 roots and 80 exact records subsequently passed with race instrumentation. This run used the retained full-package controller executable from the canonical race-and-vet compilation for `9d5307d0` plus a separate 26-path Darwin paired-Shadowsocks draft. That compilation completed in 172.603 seconds within its original 240-second bound. No compiler option or test deadline was increased, and this run did not rebuild the executable or repeat the four earlier compiled regression controls.

Before execution, the image digest and the exact bodies of the four desktop files and 15 recorded shared prerequisite files were checked against `9d5307d0`. The orchestration-only `deploy/test_mobile_multihop_groups.py` was absent from that compiler's recorded source inventory; it is explicitly excluded from the image comparison. An initial pretest check stopped on that missing inventory entry before running any test. The corrected evidence check retained all 13 roots and 80 records and records this source-inventory limit.

Each root used the unchanged 120-second Go timeout and 135-second process timeout. This closes the focused local race-execution gap for those unchanged source bodies. It does not establish an exact-current-main build, native encrypted traffic, installed-device acceptance, or release publication. The earlier compiler failures and non-race regression-control results above remain separate historical observations.

## docs/EXACT-SHA-RELEASE-EVIDENCE.md

# Exact-SHA release evidence

Router VPN treats source, shipped artifacts, native compilation, runtime proof, and production deployment as separate evidence classes.

A repository generation is GitHub-complete only when one exact current source SHA has green evidence for:

- source, security, recovered requirements, and durable-state transactions;
- an exact-source snapshot;
- Windows installed and Portable packages for x64 and ARM64;
- macOS packages for Intel and Apple Silicon;
- Linux packages for x64 and ARM64;
- Android APK and real iOS/iPadOS native build output;
- ARM64 server images and Portainer preflight;
- aggregate package/provenance checks; and
- a production compose materialized entirely from that same SHA.

Reusable release workflows include the caller run ID in their concurrency ownership key. A standalone diagnostic dispatch therefore cannot cancel jobs owned by the authoritative Build-all run for the same source SHA.

Old green artifacts or statuses never certify a newer source generation. Production remains on the last deliberately verified generation until the complete exact-current chain is green.


---

## docs/HYSTERIA2-FINAL-LAYER-NATIVE-2026-11-16.md

# Hysteria2 final Start Layers: native acceptance

This change adds actual encrypted final-layer execution to the existing paired
Hysteria2 Server acceptance gate. The final node can retain its own
AES-256-GCM or AES-256-GCM plus XOR whitening, independently of the preceding
WG/AWG nodes. Off remains supported. The shared compiler and native host
consumers have separate evidence; this document describes the original native
worker, reference peers and mandatory release gate.

## Preserved boundaries

The captured graph still contains two through eight paired nodes. Its entry
belongs to the original client and lease; the Server worker executes the suffix
after that entry. The native fixture exercises zero, one and six remote packet
prefixes. It does not infer a client entry lease from suffix traffic or admit an
imported middle with a Hysteria2 final.

The final encrypted layer uses the original SS2022 or whitening implementation.
It decrypts only to the captured Hysteria2 private UDP tuple. Its key, outer
endpoint, private destination and counters are distinct from all prefix layers.
The inner Hysteria2 transport retains QUIC/TLS, private trust, server name,
password and Salamander mask. The test's private port 443 is a fixture parameter;
the production compiler retains the actual captured port.

The co-located component fixture uses a distinct loopback address for its final
outer layer so it cannot collide with a prefix whitening listener. A zero-prefix
Server suffix instead uses its isolated peer namespace's original public /32.
No production endpoint, selected Home DNS policy or proof is substituted.

The new fixture is registered for both the Linux peer and the existing Darwin
test-source projection. Projection changes only the original build constraint;
it is not evidence that the Darwin peer ran.

## Mandatory whole-worker cases

All previous 22 Server cases remain required. The following 14 are added:

| New final-layer cases | Count |
| --- | ---: |
| Direct AES and AES+XOR suffixes | 2 |
| WG/AES and AWG/AES+XOR suffixes | 2 |
| Maximum-six AES, AES+XOR and independently mixed-prefix/AES+XOR suffixes | 3 |
| Wrong final key or whitening for direct and maximum-six suffixes | 4 |
| Maximum-six AES with failed selected DNS | 1 |
| Joined active UDP for direct AES and maximum-six AES+XOR | 2 |
| **Additional required cases** | **14** |

The gate therefore requires **36 exact whole-worker cases on each architecture**.
Every case must retain its original source and capture hashes, complete prefix
inventory, exact final-layer tuple and counters, selected DNS result, and joined
worker and peer. A rejected final layer may retain valid preceding prefix
proofs, but cannot deliver a QUIC packet or an application proof to the final
provider. A direct suffix must leave the unused WG reference counters at zero.

The original per-case bounds remain 90 seconds for Go, 105 seconds for its
namespace child and 125 seconds for the outer observer. Each native compilation
retains 600 seconds. Missing, duplicate, skipped or foreign cases cannot satisfy
the gate. The independent API-token correction from `a56cd4bb` is retained.

## Executed local evidence

The canonical preparation used the original pinned core and Xray revisions,
Go 1.26.3, race instrumentation, the full native tags and read-only module
resolution. These receipts describe private proof commit `a55a0493` based on
`a56cd4bb`, not an exact build of a later composed publication commit.

The canonical digest of all checked prepared inputs was:

`33b2ab1c97432fa63f3dcf83c59cb8a0f982db154d039925ee2ed07148ee002e`

| Actual compiled image | Seconds / bound | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Original encrypted reference peer | 217.111 / 600 | `578a490e926a799597f91b92b5b70d92d12f052b72b37cd7860d9e3900d90c13` |
| Shipping `cmd/sing-box` worker | 79.926 / 600 | `04dfa697e4dcbb6511b0eda3d9cf79fcc221d2c6ad7d284e78dbb470370b2bcd` |
| Whole-worker acceptance driver | 59.818 / 600 | `f963b8a170c0b874eb58bf182b88fd7929aac859e4bc91d702ddab0c8bd8db8a` |

The immutable peer passed the new nine final-layer leaves plus their root in
15.638 seconds under the unchanged 90/105-second component bounds. These execute
real WG/AWG, SS2022, whitening and Hysteria2 traffic: AES, AES+XOR, both mixed-six
compositions, wrong key, wrong whitening, wrong private destination, held-request
cancellation and joined close. No kernel TUN or host firewall is modeled as an
accepted device.

The same peer also passed all 37 original Hysteria2 traffic records in 19.393
seconds under 90/100 seconds, and all six original prefix-layer records in 6.108
seconds under 90/105 seconds. Existing authentication, trust, routing, DNS,
cancellation and socket-retirement checks remain required.

The provider's original private-source export passed in 0.115 seconds. The
driver passed all 36 new source cases plus their root in 6.567 seconds, and all
14 original source/carrier cases plus their root in 2.486 seconds. The source
roots retain 60/75-second bounds. These 59 provider/prefix/source records have
receipt SHA-256:

`e9569eaff60df0f5f7fcad952635c182cd0c1c1d1172d54a2a2ee1bf5f91d700`

The dedicated runner passed 21 checks, including compilation of the actual
request constructor and provider admission with 15 topologies, 12 independent
identity refusals and the former duplicate-token counterexample. Nine original
Hysteria2 runner checks and four Darwin source-projection checks also passed.
These runner checks do not supply encrypted traffic receipts.

The first component attempt correctly rejected an incomplete Start Layer fixture
which omitted its original TUN/route declaration. Only that fixture was repaired;
the strict source validator was preserved. A later local observer omitted the
required `case=None` argument when parsing the completed original source root.
Its exact successful child log, unchanged image and source digest were rechecked
through the unchanged strict shipping parser. Neither failed attempt is counted
as a passing native run, and their diagnostics remain recorded.

## Remaining evidence

This local environment previously refused the first required namespace network
operation, `ip link set lo up`. It has not supplied a completed 36-case veth/TUN
receipt. The required hosted workflow must build the exact published commit and
complete all 36 cases on Linux amd64 and arm64. The earlier 22-case Off-final
results do not establish the new final-layer cases.

Installed client networking, public host/controller operation, real-device leak
and network-transition checks, signing, private deployment and the complete
release retain their own acceptance requirements.

## Selected DNS failure boundary review

Read-only review found that a failed-selected-DNS case could previously accept
an earlier unrelated private-proof failure: both the driver and strict receipt
parser required failed readiness, but did not require the selected resolver to
have received a query. This affected the existing direct/max-six cases and the
new max-final-AES case. The actual shared proof order is private proofs, then
selected DNS, then public proof.

The driver assertion and parser now require positive Hysteria2 stream/datagram,
private-proof and DNS-query counters, zero authentication failures, and zero
public queries/cover replies for precisely those three cases. Failed readiness
and joined worker/peer retirement remain mandatory. All 36 cases, other fault
boundaries and every operation/process deadline are unchanged.

The new parser controls independently invalidate each counter requirement and
causally remove the exact parser guard; the latter recreates acceptance of an
earlier failure with no private proof or DNS query. These synthetic transcripts
are validator-only evidence. The previously recorded native peer/worker images
are unchanged. The earlier driver image predates this assertion and is retained
as its original snapshot. The subsequent driver-only rebuild is recorded below;
the mandatory hosted 36-case namespace run must execute the new Go assertion
against actual failed selected DNS.

All 22 Python-only runner tests passed in 0.231 seconds, including 21 individual
counter mutations across the three selected-DNS cases and the exact guard-removal
control. The existing actual Go constructor test was explicitly excluded from
this parser-only invocation; it retains its separate compilation requirement.

The retained a56 hosted native logs also confirm compatibility for the existing
direct and max-six failed-selected-DNS cases on both amd64 (job 133271343548) and
arm64 (job 133271343956). Each original receipt has stream, packet, private-proof
and DNS-query counters equal to one, zero authentication/public/cover counters,
failed readiness, and completed worker/peer retirement. These four historical
receipts satisfy the new phase predicate. They do not exercise the added Go
assertion or the new layered case. Raw-log hashes and exact event locations are
retained in `dns-existing-native-compatibility.json` alongside the review receipt.


## Rebuilt driver and final validator checks

The actual acceptance driver was rebuilt at private proof commit
`f85edbb0875e305f13d7286e72fe8c10dfb27145`. A before/after inventory of all 4,994
prepared Go/module files confirms that only the generated Hysteria2 acceptance
driver changed. The original reference-peer and worker images, all earlier
receipts, and the earlier driver image were retained unchanged. This remains
the recorded a56-derived canonical source composition; it is not relabeled as
a native build of the later publication head.

The new driver compiled with race instrumentation and all original native and
acceptance tags in **92.181 seconds under 600 seconds**. Its SHA-256 is:

`9e35ade10a76588715b963a543a899ee230c97186d527e0c1cbf5882b5b7a3f6`

The checked prepared-source digest is:

`45f943cf8dc72f57daee59f9e2ed38e8c9648be442ac76d31a7c8ceeb0c55b24`

That exact image reran all 37 Hysteria2 source records in 7.073 seconds and all
15 original source/carrier records in 2.051 seconds. Each retained its original
60-second Go and 75-second process limit. These are repeated source checks,
not additional encrypted-traffic cases or execution of the DNS-fault namespace.

The complete updated validator suite then passed **23 tests in 4.861 seconds**.
This includes the actual compiled request constructor/provider admission,
independent-identity refusals, duplicate-token counterexample, and the new
selected-DNS counter controls. The observer completed in 5.129 seconds; the
constructor's original 30-second test and 60-second process limits were kept.
The final validator log has SHA-256:

`a1f9f5f0e436a90938831bc3a37c54ac462c992fcc2d37655770a5e1f260e576`

The whole-worker namespace, both hosted architectures, native platform packages
and exact publication/release checks remain mandatory. No historical or local
source-only result supplies those missing verdicts.

## docs/HYSTERIA2-FINAL-LAYER-SOURCE-2026-11-16.md

# Captured Hysteria2 final Start Layers: shared source boundary

This unit permits an independently captured AES or AES+XOR Start Layer on the
paired Hysteria2 final of a two-to-eight-node ordered graph. Every preceding
node remains an original WG/AWG packet source. Imported graph families retain
an SS final, and Hysteria2 does not become a TCP candidate-measurement source.

The final closure compares both complete native objects with the original sealed
single-node configuration before namespacing them. TLS name and CA, Hysteria2
password and mask, inner destination, outer cipher/key/destination and the
original selected DNS remain captured. The composer routes only the outer layer
through the preceding packet prefix. A Server suffix containing just the final
keeps that outer layer as its physical transport. Server plans retain independent
provider identity credentials; this unit creates no new credential substitution.

This is a shared source/runtime foundation. It does not establish encrypted
native traffic, installed-host acceptance, physical-device acceptance or current
release CI. The corresponding native provider/worker and public consumer gates
must pass before the complete feature is considered delivered. The accompanying
[Apple consumer unit](APPLE-HYSTERIA2-FINAL-LAYERS-2026-11-16.md) aligns its actual
source, identity, map, Local selection and editor tests in the same publication.
At this snapshot, desktop and Android public final-layer adoption and native
acceptance remain separate units; their completion is not inferred here.

## Executed evidence

The complete `internal/mobilemultihop` and `internal/multihoprelay` test packages
compiled with `-race`, `-p=1`, `-mod=readonly`; compilation retained the original
240-second process bound. Each selected whole root retained the original
180-second Go test bound and a 195-second process wrapper. There were no skips.

| Inventory | Roots | Distinct passing records |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| New Local complete lengths, closure/source corruption and proof lifetime | 10 | 108 |
| New Server complete lengths, original plans, Server/Compare, Restore and Last | 11 | 87 |
| Original Local/Server Hysteria2 compatibility | 5 | 49 |
| Original and layered relay descriptor/request/registry ownership | 2 | 58 |
| Total | 28 | 302 |

The 21 new shared roots are mandatory, independently bounded phases in the core
CI inventory. All 29 grouping tests passed, including missing-root and missing
or duplicated phase-assignment controls. The previously published imported-SS
candidate registrations are retained.
Final composition on `59bc7cb9` also preserves the newly published imported
candidate source-ownership phase and all six of its required roots; all 30
merged grouping checks pass.

The source matrix covers every total node count from two through eight, both
final layers and independent Off/AES/AES+XOR prefix layers. Entry modes rotate
between WG, AWG Fast and AWG Strong; later packet positions retain their own
original sources and keys. All five platform projections retain their seals,
selected final DNS and conservative 1280-byte TUN. Positive maximum-layer
fixtures explicitly capture a 4096-byte packet ceiling; no operational deadline,
restored MTU rule or packet-budget check was increased.

Restore exercised both concrete winners under the original 10-second contexts.
Last exercised both winners at two and eight nodes under the original
35-second contexts, retaining cold private storage, fresh authenticated discovery,
lease ownership, one-shot adoption and no new comparison. Its whole root passed
in 96.373 seconds. These shared runtime tests use an explicit native engine
double; real identity/HTTP/DNS and cleanup assertions are not an encrypted native
transport receipt.

## Immutable source and image accounting

The frozen source base was `89bf8613a31f3ed1bf4af134332cd6ed786d0573`, plus the
four reviewed production changes and test inventory in this unit. The grouping
file already included the later-published imported-SS registration changes.

| Image | SHA-256 | Compile seconds |

V1 correctly rejected the new five-node Server fixture: its selected WG packet
assets had the explicit 4096 ceiling while the retained WG identity assets still

identity. AWG and Hysteria2 retain their separate standard-WG identity sources.
The failed image, source bytes, log and receipt remain recorded; no production
check changed. All 11 new Server roots passed on v2. The 108 Local records and

Final composition preserves the later imported-SS candidate preparation change,

corrections. Those changes do not modify the four production sources or the
shared execution/Last bodies exercised here. `NodeChainCandidateInputSupported`,
used by the Hysteria2 candidate-refusal test, is unchanged.

Machine-readable source manifests, exact inventories, per-root JSON events,

## docs/HYSTERIA2-SERVER-FOUNDATION-2026-11-16.md

# Paired Hysteria2 Server and Compare source — November 16, 2026

The original paired ordered source can now retain its Hysteria2 final through
shared Server planning and Local/Server comparison. This continues the original
graph execution, selected DNS, independent node identity and complete-state
restoration requirements. It is a shared-source implementation milestone;
native provider traffic and public host adoption require their own evidence.

## Preserved source and policy

The supported source is the original schema-1 paired chain with two through
eight total nodes. Preceding nodes retain WireGuard or AmneziaWG and their
independent Off, AES or AES+XOR Start Layers. The Hysteria2 final retains its
original Off Start Layer, literal endpoint, private TLS trust, server name,
password and Salamander configuration. Existing imported-middle families keep
their separate final-node contracts.

Server planning still requires an independently provisioned WireGuard identity
asset for each paired member, including the proxy final. The complete Hysteria2
transport must match between the independently provisioned bundles. A changed
password, endpoint, TLS name or trust, obfuscation, DNS policy, node identity or
identity key cannot substitute for the captured source.

The server suffix adds no TUN or listener during compilation. Its final proof
retains the original private node identity, and its selected Home AdGuard policy
stays on that final path. The authenticated client front keeps its original
UDP-over-TCP transport declaration. No Hysteria2 packet keys are invented.
Local and Server candidates retain the same source, closed application gate,
proof inventory and DNS policy. Restoring a proved choice retains that choice;
Last restoration freshly proves the original captured winner.

## Executed checks

The complete `internal/mobilemultihop` package, without omitted source files,
compiled with Go 1.26.3 race instrumentation in 74.792 seconds under its
300-second local compile bound. Its immutable test image has SHA-256
`87d1dfed152f18755f4cf09c86147355d3b821f3e47ce3f966dd167e66161242`.
All eight selected roots ran from that exact image and unchanged source. Each
kept a 120-second Go deadline and 135-second local process deadline.

| Shared checks | Passed records |
| --- | ---: |
| Original final closure for all lengths and independent prefix layers | 10 |
| Both captured Local and Server paths | 7 |
| Independently provisioned plan identities across WG/AWG bases | 10 |
| Changed transport, DNS and identity rejection | 10 |
| Complete-state restoration of each proved choice | 3 |
| Fresh Last proof for two- and eight-node paths | 7 |
| Existing Hysteria2 scope and closure checks | 9 |
| Original generic Last restoration regression | 3 |

The total is **59 executed shared records**, including their root records.
The restoration cases use actual local HTTP/TLS and captured leases, with
native routing/proof boundaries explicitly modeled. They are not evidence of
an encrypted Hysteria2 provider connection or a system tunnel.

The complete relay package race run passed **114 records** in 13.572 seconds,
including all **43** new Hysteria2 descriptor records. Those cover one through
seven server-suffix members and reject a final layer, packet key, public proof,
external-node substitution or imported-family substitution. The Go test bound
remained 120 seconds and the local compile/run bound remained 300 seconds.

All **27** shipping shared-test partition checks passed. Each of the six new
shared roots is required, assigned exactly once, and keeps the existing
180-second hosted phase deadline. Omission or duplicate assignment fails the
partition check, and a future Server root still enters the general Server
phase. Existing root inventories remain required.

The original proof commit was
`6774860526f4c69f842ec7b558a2bfdeeac40359`, based on `261c396e`.
Validation receipts retain the source snapshot, commands, elapsed times,
immutable image and log hashes. The final publication preserves the later
Android profile-inventory and desktop OpenVPN source-verifier corrections;
those do not alter the compiled shared or relay inputs.

## Remaining acceptance

This unit does not enable a public host by weakening its capability guard.
The real native Server worker must still establish the captured Hysteria2
provider, prove its selected DNS and exact final identity, move actual TCP/UDP
traffic, reject wrong credentials/trust and join all owned resources. Native
host/controller and editor adoption, exact-SHA packaged builds and publication,
physical-device networking and private deployment remain separate evidence.
Encrypted Hysteria2 final Start Layers and other graph families remain outside
this bounded source change; their original requirements are not discarded.

## docs/HYSTERIA2-SERVER-NATIVE-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Hysteria2 Server native acceptance — November 16, 2026

This unit adds a mandatory actual Server-suffix gate for the original paired
schema-1 Hysteria2 graph. It preserves the shipping worker, source and capture
identity, private proof inventory, selected final DNS, authenticated front,
and strict Stop/worker/peer joins. No production admission or timeout is
weakened. Hysteria2 remains the final Off-layer node; each preceding packet
node retains its independently captured Off, AES or AES+XOR layer.

## Exact topology boundary

The captured entry belongs to the client/lease side of the complete product
graph. The Server worker executes the suffix after that entry. The native
acceptance fixture captures the original entry identity but does not run a
second entry endpoint or infer an entry lease from the suffix proof.

| Complete captured graph | Remote packet prefixes exercised by this gate | Actual suffix |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| 2 nodes | 0 | Hysteria2 final |
| 3 nodes | 1 | Original WG/AWG prefix, then Hysteria2 |
| 8 nodes | 6 | Six independently captured WG/AWG prefixes, then Hysteria2 |

The eight-node boundary therefore retains seven paired packet-node assets in
the complete graph: the captured entry plus six remote prefixes. It does not
inherit the smaller OpenVPN family limit. Intermediate total lengths four
through seven remain covered by the separate complete source/consumer matrix;
they are not counted as executed native lengths by this unit.

## Required native inventory

The existing 17 cases remain required and five actual layer cases are added.
Every case runs in its own original namespace/process budget, with an exact
terminal Go verdict and one mandatory structured receipt. Missing, repeated,
foreign or malformed cases and counters fail the gate.

| Cases | Count |
| --- | ---: |
| Original direct, WG, AWG-fast, AWG-strong and maximum-six suffixes | 5 |
| Direct wrong password, mask, SNI, provider trust, selected DNS, public trust or node identity, plus joined active UDP | 8 |
| Maximum-six wrong first key, wrong middle key, failed selected DNS and joined active UDP | 4 |
| WG with AES, AWG with AES+XOR, and maximum-six independent mixed layers | 3 |
| Wrong AES layer key and wrong XOR whitening | 2 |
| **Total required native cases** | **22** |

The mixed six-prefix layer order is AES, AES+XOR, Off, AES+XOR, AES, Off.
The original concrete SS2022 and whitening layer owners decrypt and forward
only the captured private WG/AWG tuple. Each layer receipt retains its mode,
outer endpoint, inner endpoint, private destination and independent raw,
authenticated, forwarded and rejected counters. A failed first layer must
record rejection without delivering a WG packet or reaching Hysteria2.

The actual Hysteria2 provider retains QUIC/TLS, its private CA and SNI,
password and Salamander mask. Direct mode uses a test-owned public /32 inside
the isolated peer namespace and requires the unused WG reference counters to
remain zero. Positive cases require private identity proofs, public HTTPS,
selected final DNS and TCP/UDP delivery. Wrong authentication, trust or keys
cannot produce inner application traffic. Stop must join the original worker
and peer and make the retained active UDP flow unusable.

## Executed component evidence

The final peer was compiled from the canonical pinned core with Go 1.26.3,
race instrumentation and the original full native tags. Compilation passed
in 69.113 seconds under its unchanged 600-second bound. The image SHA-256 is
`52dcab752ed679a510392f9638b0c28752d7c43a5ac163c9c0f52f3e71b85bf1`.
The frozen prepared-source receipt SHA-256 is
`2990c7ae3e9248a8ffb72616b5db33bf70f865429588c34a43a6cdf195cac7f8`.
That receipt records the isolated native fixture on the published shared
foundation `8f8e9fb3dc251ea33378371b33fe56d99a4b8761`. Subsequent unrelated
desktop/mobile publication does not relabel this local image as an exact
latest-head build; the required hosted workflow builds the composed head.

From that same immutable race image:

- The new actual layer component passed all five leaves plus its root
  (six records) in 5.583 seconds. It covers AES, AES+XOR, the mixed-six
  composition, wrong AES key and wrong whitening, with real WG/AWG,
  SS2022/whitening and Hysteria2 traffic and joined original owners.
- The complete existing Hysteria2 component regression passed all 37 records
  in 19.489 seconds, preserving its original authentication, TLS, mask,
  routing, selected DNS and close controls.
- The actual private-provider source export passed in 0.115 seconds and
  retained a mode-0600 source file with its original private trust. The
  exported source alone grants no traffic or readiness authority.

The five layer leaves retain the 90-second Go and 105-second process bounds.
The provider export retains 30/45 seconds. The source, original worker and
driver compilation receipts are recorded separately below; none is a
completed 22-case namespace receipt.

The whole shipping `cmd/sing-box` worker compiled with the same race/native
tags in 37.758 seconds under 600 seconds. Its image SHA-256 is
`b18739e88ca125e259012c304a6ae87330b1e04e03b11632a6e6c79c0b186a9e`.
The acceptance driver compiled the complete `cmd/sing-box` package plus the
original acceptance build tag. The final driver passed compilation in
67.865 seconds under 600 seconds, with image SHA-256
`5e7915bf4b84b56828de098df50976af42dc140035588ec64fec35b0ed687f44`.

The first source execution correctly rejected the nine direct fixtures: the
physical Hysteria2 profile named `203.0.113.25`, while its independent Router
identity asset still named the prefix-only private endpoint `10.77.0.1`.
The fixture now provisions that same original WG identity at the captured
physical address, retaining both keys and all other bytes. Each direct case
also requires the actual shared constructor to reject the former mismatched
identity. All 22 cases plus the root now pass from the final race driver in
4.533 seconds. The complete original 14-case source/carrier root also passes
in 2.813 seconds and retains the captured carrier MTU of 2092. Both roots
keep their original 60-second Go and 75-second process bounds.

The corrected source receipt SHA-256 is
`2262c254b603713cd102cf5546a0828b879f0347163109b79a02d2db14cbb44e`.
Its complete checked-source comparison differs from the peer/worker receipt
in exactly the new Hysteria2 acceptance template. No production or peer
input changed. The original failed source log, driver image and source
receipt remain preserved; a failed fixture is not reported as native proof.

All 18 dedicated runner checks, nine existing Hysteria component-runner
checks and four original Darwin peer-projection checks pass. They retain
omission, duplication, terminal-verdict, source-identity, exact layer tuple,
wrong-authentication, join, workflow and standalone/native-boundary controls.
The projected Darwin source is checked, but no Darwin execution is claimed.

## Required hosted boundary

This workspace successfully entered new user, mount, network and PID
namespaces and made the private mounts. Its first actual network operation,
`ip link set lo up`, was refused with `Cannot open netlink socket: Operation
not permitted`. It therefore cannot run the veth/TUN namespace acceptance
locally. Component traffic is not labeled as a system-tunnel or full native
Server acceptance pass.

The required workflow runs all 22 cases on both Linux amd64 and arm64, uses
the exact checked-out source SHA and pinned compiler, builds the actual
worker/peer/driver, checks source and image identities before and after, and
uploads the mandatory final receipt. The existing encrypted Server workflow
calls this workflow so the authoritative release includes it. The native
case limits remain 90 seconds for Go, 105 seconds for the namespace child
and 125 seconds for the outer process; compilation remains 600 seconds.
Compile-only mode cannot satisfy a native receipt or standalone release run.

This evidence does not close actual encrypted Local OpenVPN ownership,
client entry-lease acceptance, installed host/controller networking, physical
device checks or complete exact-SHA release publication. Those boundaries
retain their own required gates.

## docs/IMPORTED-SS-DESKTOP-OBSERVATION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Imported SS desktop observation and complete controller evidence — 2026-11-16

This follow-up preserves the Windows/Linux longer imported-SS implementation published in `06e5a1e3b68cbad54cdadb9f01e6576c06a54d7b`. Its eligible graph remains one through six paired WG/AWG2 packet prefixes, a literal imported SS2022 penultimate member, and a paired SS2022 final member. The separately typed CONNECT and SOCKS execution families retain their existing bounds. The feature, editor, common membership, and saved-profile production changes from that commit are not replayed by this patch.

## Fresh source, readiness and retained status

The implementation reuses the combined original-source and physical-read methods published in `ec5e9e93cd34976e6bd19e5ef207e96ff51dccbd`. It carries that fresh observation through the owned desktop Current path, avoiding repeated validation through adjacent getters. A ready record similarly reads one freshly validated projection and copies its original positional proof/DNS capture. Raw native assets, original policy and source pointers, preparation identity, physical owner, live host/process/network checks, original MTU derivation, and before/after Current checks remain required. The four-second selected-result and status deadlines are unchanged. No reusable source-validity or live-readiness receipt is added.

Status finalization now waits for the exact selected receipt mutex before taking `app.mu`. After waiting, it rechecks the same retained owner, original source, command, active graph, state token, selected proof, winner and epoch. Already app-locked observation still refuses a busy receipt immediately. The deterministic test holds the original receipt after a successful actual model Current observation, verifies that `app.mu` remains available while status waits, and covers both winners with original, changed winner, changed epoch, changed source, changed owner and cancelled-caller cases. Cancellation must finish while the receipt remains held.

An earlier eight-node publication failure was observed at the unchanged four-second Result boundary. A later four-node HTTP 409 did not reproduce under diagnostic repetition, so it is not assigned to the independently proved mutex race without a matching trace.

## Required controller inventory

The strict gate retains all original trio records and combines the published longer-path cases with complete packet-count/mixed-mode source coverage. The original 240-second compilation, 120-second root, and 135-second outer root limits are retained. Every root runs under the native race detector; missing, duplicate, foreign, skipped, raced or failed records remain fatal. Source and original executable fingerprints are checked before and after the roots, including the two shipping-audit files now explicitly included in this gate's source identity.

| Inventory | Roots | Required records |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Original trio preparation, source, public paths, restore, missing/foreign middle and saved profile | 6 | 57 |
| All one-through-six packet counts, mixed-mode original preparation and positional source refusals | 3 | 41 |
| Published longer source preparation/refusals, foreign native middle and saved bounds | 4 | 28 |
| Four/eight-node Server and both Compare winners | 6 | 12 |
| Four/eight-node chosen-source restore and saved-profile round trips | 2 | 14 |
| **Total** | **21** | **152** |

The saved tests retain all published cases and additionally cover Update for each choice, eight positional/source mutations, and a valid longer CONNECT Local capture that cannot obtain Server/Compare authority by changing its saved intent. The retained child, original source, private/public proof positions, selected DNS, exact comparison result, Last choice, live MTU and failed-retirement retry remain required.

```sh

python3 deploy/test_desktop_imported_trio_controller_runner.py
```

The roots totalled 223.43 seconds; the longest root was the complete four/eight-node restore matrix at 48.16 seconds. The original trio restore passed in 12.75 seconds. Every root retained its original bound.

- Original race executable SHA-256: `278717adaee219aebbd6a848bebbbdebf0571a82711ced064f8c3f486dcad8e8`.

## Native acceptance boundary

These are actual native-Go source/controller/ownership tests with the existing explicit process and kernel models.
