## docs/IMPORTED-SS-LINUX-INSTALLED-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Installed Linux imported Shadowsocks execution gate

This unit adds a mandatory installed Linux acceptance path for the existing schema-2 Server/Compare family: one through six original paired packet prefixes, one penultimate imported Shadowsocks member, and one original paired final member (three through eight members total). It preserves the portable trio fixture and existing encrypted prefix callers. It does not add a new product topology, permit a relative-position suffix, or claim physical-device or remote private-server deployment acceptance.

The dedicated amd64 and arm64 release jobs require the exact published source, native runtime bundle, compiler, test images, source inventory, every native leaf, and completed namespace retirement. A missing prerequisite, omitted case, unsuccessful retirement phase, changed source or image, skipped test, or partial receipt fails the gate. The original 120-second Go case and 135-second namespace subprocess bounds remain unchanged; the wrapper is 150 seconds. The 120-minute job and 110-minute acceptance step budget cover the complete 27-case inventory, preparation, component checks, and compilation.

## Actual source and transport boundaries

The protected controller captures the original Local source through the shipping resolver and public connection handler. The Server source is rebuilt from those captured raw node/profile/identity inputs using independently paired Server client keys at the original paired suffix positions. Entry position zero and the imported member never acquire fabricated Server keys. Original profile IDs, private/public node proofs, literal destinations, cipher parameters, imported credentials, selected DNS, and full global positions remain captured.

For more than one packet prefix, the fixture launches the exact captured shipping native worker in its own owned network namespace. Its first actual Server packet destination has a non-loopback route to a separate encrypted provider namespace. The carrier therefore exercises the real required native packet owner. Only original encrypted bytes cross the narrowly bound carrier bridge; it does not supply successful proof results. Local and Server keep distinct independently paired packet providers, while their imported and final encrypted services retain the same captured identities. The one-prefix case remains the portable imported suffix and explicitly reports that no native packet-prefix carrier is required.

The narrow shared reference helper retains the existing zero-based caller behavior while permitting the Server construction to start at global position one. Its Server array has no fabricated entry owner. The additional Linux lease-handler projection uses the exact shipping handler with identifier/import renaming and a distinct captured authentication receiver. It does not change the existing portable runner or manufacture policy receipts.

The installed driver uses the original protected process, TUN, nftables transaction, physical capture, selected DNS, readiness proof, public Connect/Disconnect handlers, and Speed Lab retained-choice restoration coordinator. It requires a fresh child/reader after restoration while retaining the same selected result; Server restoration launches exactly one successor worker and Local restoration launches none. Held cleanup operates on the original native gate and must retain that exact owner for the successful retry. The peer's original join receipt remains retained across a namespace-cleanup retry.

## Required native cases

The 18 positive leaves contain Server, Compare selecting Local, and Compare selecting Server for every packet-prefix count from one through six. The existing WG, AWG2 Fast, and AWG2 Strong packet modes rotate across original positions, and the three supported SS2022 methods rotate across those arms. This is the stated 18-case coverage, not every possible cipher Cartesian product. Start layers are off in this unit.

The nine additional six-prefix leaves cover wrong first Server packet key, wrong later packet key, wrong imported key, wrong final key, wrong imported public identity, failed selected DNS, replaced original source, changed native carrier route, and held cleanup. Actual per-position encrypted arrivals and private proofs must reach each expected boundary and must not appear beyond a failed cryptographic/public boundary. The counters forbid direct or entry public-path bypasses and retain original mode/parameter digests. The receipt also binds the requested and chosen execution, comparison count, restoration launch count, carrier requirement, joined workers, and owned namespace cleanup.

## Local validation and exact scope

The validation checkout starts at `ec317faff198f667c3a06a90136dbde2ef7766d4`, including the published `33dd7f32` observation/status repair and `3f9f68df` DNS-owner changes. Native sources were canonically prepared from authentic pinned core `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray `50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1` clones. No original owner receipt or baseline identity was forged. An attempted partial reprepare correctly refused a transformed TUN dependency; validation was restarted from authentic clean clones through the complete canonical preparation. Later Python workflow/validator changes do not alter those tested native component bytes; the published workflow must build its own final exact-source bundle.

All local Go compilation used the captured Go 1.26.3 toolchain, `-race`, `-mod=readonly`, `-p=1`, one compiler at a time, `GOMAXPROCS=1`, `GOGC=25`, `GOMEMLIMIT=1200MiB`, and task-owned workspace temporary storage. The original 300-second compile and 120/135-second component bounds were retained.

| Validation | Result | Evidence boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| New source positions: all six counts and three packet families | 19 records passed | Actual compiled source constructors, including the root |
| New provider positions | 7 records passed | Actual encrypted provider ownership and original global proof routes |
| New source refusals | 11 records passed | Original key, identity, node, import, mode, and inventory controls |
| Existing imported Server prefix capture | 6 records passed | Unchanged existing provider callers |
| Existing ordered peer capture | 13 records passed | Existing identity/inventory refusals |
| Existing ordered AWG capture | 11 records passed | Existing complete mode/parameter capture |
| Existing complete ordered peer traffic root | 99 records passed | Actual encrypted native traffic, private/public/DNS controls |
| Installed controller | Race compilation passed; exact new top-level test present | Compilation only; no local privileged namespace acceptance |
| Strict installed runner | 19 tests passed | Validator/orchestration controls only |
| Workflow closure | 7 tests passed | Mandatory workflow/source/timeout wiring only |
| Aggregate closure | 27 tests passed | Required release-input and artifact inventory controls only |
| Shipping artifact/workflow parity | Passed | Static shipping-source/workflow parity only |

The new native component total is **37 records**, and the existing native regression total is **129 records**, with no failed or skipped records in those selected roots. The compiled native peer image SHA-256 is `92300c0adda2afef648158e989b4a8128675614cb9ff920c61a785bb0733f8bd`; its new native template SHA-256 is `5ffb69ae99d2f2350013cd1fb9ef02b43d52c52a07be48e95cff7c0aa023736c`. The privileged controller image SHA-256 is `7585c9cc45a868184c2135874a77bacbff65779e2ae35fa3f962634aaac3cfb7`, with driver SHA-256 `7028d1ea2f7c15df1edf5a103705015fd2fb2b593d015f2e23a5d71307d13028`. Initial fixture compile errors were corrected before the successful controller build; none were treated as native evidence.

Two additional targeted causal overlays were prepared for independent Server client-key substitution and preservation of global Server proof positions, but were not compiled or executed. They are not counted as passed evidence. Concurrent main `052366b1bb53214aa40350dab4637aedd021e5b2` changed shared CONNECT/server/carrier paths before those optional checks began; integration must preserve that ancestry and validate any actual affected dependency separately. The original prepared source and the recorded component results above remain unchanged and apply to their stated captured base.

The shared guardian selector correction published separately as `752656458980d59faa2da45e60276929808ff61d` must be preserved when this unit is integrated. It anchors slash-separated test components independently, so the real selected leaf executes rather than producing a zero-test success. Its nine actual Go selection checks include the original erroneous selector as a rejected control. This Python selector change requires no relabeling of the native component binaries above.

The complete installed 27-leaf kernel gate was **not run in the local unprivileged workspace**. Both mandatory hosted architecture jobs must pass against the published exact source before installed Linux acceptance is complete. No local model, synthetic receipt, component binary, or successful compilation substitutes for that result.

## Reproduction

The hosted entry point is:

```sh
python3 deploy/test_linux_imported_execution_host.py \
  --bundle /path/to/exact-source-native-bundle \
  --require-commit "$GITHUB_SHA" \
  --receipt /path/to/linux-imported-execution-host.json
```

It requires `ROUTERVPN_LINUX_IMPORTED_EXECUTION_HOST_ACCEPTANCE=1` on the matching native Linux architecture, the captured compiler, and the real namespace/TUN/nftables prerequisites. The source/provider roots are `TestRouterVPNDesktopNativeImportedSourcePositions`, `TestRouterVPNDesktopNativeImportedProviderPositions`, and `TestRouterVPNDesktopNativeImportedSourceRefusals`. The native installed root is `TestDesktopLinuxGuardianKernelImportedExecution`; its exact 27 leaf names are enforced by `deploy/test_linux_imported_execution_host.py` and its strict runner controls.

Local logs and original receipts were retained under the task-owned `imported-ss-linux-validation` workspace: `component-receipt.json`, the three component JSONL transcripts, four `*.regression.jsonl` transcripts, `controller-compile-receipt.json`, compiler logs, canonical preparation logs, and the unexecuted targeted causal-overlay script (without a success receipt). These local artifacts are evidence of their stated component or compilation scope only.

## docs/IMPORTED-SS-SERVER-PREFIX-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md

# Imported Shadowsocks Server packet-prefix evidence

This increment extends the existing schema-2 server suffix from a single
imported Shadowsocks node and paired final to the existing ordered capacity:
one to six paired WG/AWG prefixes in the complete path, one penultimate
Shadowsocks 2022 import, and the original paired Shadowsocks final. The entry
remains client-owned; up to five additional packet prefixes belong to the
server suffix. This does not yet extend the client Server/Compare controller.

The operator plan captures each server-side packet prefix independently.
Every position retains its node identity, source, keys, Start Layer, and proof;
the imported member retains its distinct public-path identity and remains
penultimate. Physical admission belongs to the first actual server prefix.
The imported endpoint stays literal and gains no ambient resolver authority.
Selected DNS and the paired final keep their original owners. SOCKS and
CONNECT retain their separate schema-3/schema-4 trio boundaries.

The existing maximum path length and transport-capacity limits are unchanged.
Deep layered configurations that exceed the original Local packet capacity
are still rejected. This change does not permit arbitrary imported positions
or multiple imports.

## Executed checks

Using Go 1.26.3 with the race detector and constrained build concurrency:

```sh
go test -p=1 -race ./internal/mobilemultihop ./internal/multihoprelay \
  -run 'TestNodeChainServer|TestNodeChainImportedServer|TestNodeChainImportedPrefixCapture' \
  -count=1 -timeout=120s
go test -p=1 -race ./internal/mobilemultihop \
  -run '^TestNodeChainImportedServerPrefixStartLayers$' -count=1 -timeout=60s
python3 deploy/test_server_node_chain_encrypted_runner.py
```

The first command passed in 77.529 seconds and 1.973 seconds for its two
packages. The Start Layer/capacity command passed eighteen cases in 23.731
seconds. All twenty-three strict runner tests passed, including omission,
duplicate, failed-cleanup, packet-position, and wrong-key receipt controls.
A compiled causal overlay restoring the old server compiler rejects the first
longer four-node case; the new positive test fails at that actual admission.

Canonical preparation used fresh checkouts at the existing pinned native
core and Xray identities. The actual race-built native driver passed all
twenty-two imported source cases in 7.774 seconds. The native peer component
passed WG/AWG packet-prefix compositions one through five in 1.256 seconds,
using the actual cryptographic devices and exact next-packet routing. The
canonical source verification checked 2,717 owned input records.

## Required remaining evidence

The encrypted Linux gate preserves its eight original imported cases and
requires fourteen additional longer cases. These include WG/AWG variants,
the maximum suffix, original authentication/DNS/LAN/retirement controls, and
wrong keys at the first and a later packet prefix. The original readiness and
native execution deadlines are unchanged.

Local compilation and loopback peer checks do not establish namespace
readiness, installed-client networking, a published release, or private
deployment. The hosted AMD64/ARM64 encrypted cases remain required. The
longer schema-2 client preparation, discovery, Server/Compare measurements,
restoration, and platform adapters are follow-on work.

## docs/LINUX-CARRIER-READINESS-EVIDENCE.md

# Linux fixture carrier readiness

The generated-command TUN run at `81ea491551d34488283f686b67602745b794f1c8`
passed on AMD64 and failed on ARM64. The ARM64 job
[130967856177](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/43153464309/job/130967856177)
recorded two additions during one TUN route operation: the intended IPv6
default route on interface 4 and an IPv6 kernel LOCAL /128 route on interface
3, the fixture's original carrier. The production ownership predicate refused
that foreign addition. The other five TUN scenarios passed in the same run.

The fixture had set its selected ULA with `nodad` but started the test immediately
after bringing up both veth endpoints. The separately generated link-local
address still undergoes DAD; its LOCAL route was not part of the ready baseline.
The runner now observes both original carrier interfaces, their selected
IPv4/IPv6 addresses, completed DAD and complete IPv6 kernel LOCAL routes before
starting the owned executable. It retains the original interface identity across
observations. A failed or replaced address, foreign route, missing route or
five-second setup deadline fails the fixture. Waiting never disables DAD or IPv6.

This changes only the isolated test fixture. The production TUN route admission,
foreign-change refusal, original test-process deadlines and all six native
scenario verdicts remain unchanged. The TUN's own address lifecycle remains
inside the actual native acceptance test.

Local checks executed the shipping readback predicate and bounded polling loop,
including independently delayed endpoints, missing LOCAL routes, duplicate or
failed addresses, interface replacement and a carrier that never becomes ready.
Three causal mutations (ignore tentative state, ignore missing routes, ignore
replacement identity) were rejected. The existing verdict inventory and Python
compilation checks passed. These checks are invoked by the mandatory guardian
namespace regression, whose original native cases also execute the new readiness
barrier. Actual privileged namespace and ARM64 TUN replay remain hosted evidence;
the local executor cannot open the required netlink socket.
# Native JSON owner correction

At `0277f9fc9e425989dbe9850abf94f5369899a2ef`, both jobs in [Linux generated Node command TUN acceptance](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/43156396698) failed before the native test with `fixture carrier has a foreign local route`. The readiness command used a `dev` filter while requiring the returned JSON to contain `dev`. The upstream [iproute2 `print_route` implementation](https://github.com/iproute2/iproute2/blob/main/ip/iproute.c) emits `RTA_OIF` only when `filter.oifmask != -1`; a device filter therefore suppresses that field even for JSON output.

The fixture now reads the complete local IPv6 table in each of its two private namespaces. It selects the exact original carrier using the returned device field. Loopback routes are allowed outside that carrier, while missing device fields, another device claiming the carrier's address, and non-kernel carrier routes remain rejected. DAD completion, exact address/LOCAL-route equality, original interface identity, and the five-second setup deadline remain required. No production guardian, TUN route guard, or runtime deadline changed.

Portable checks execute the actual readiness predicate and loop with the upstream field-suppression behavior at the command boundary. All readiness and inventory checks pass, including four causal controls: missing DAD validation, missing LOCAL-route equality, replaced interface ownership, and restoration of the erroneous device-filtered command. Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass. Actual namespace/TUN acceptance on both native architectures remains pending the hosted run of this correction; this executor cannot open the required netlink socket.

## docs/LINUX-GUARDIAN-SELECTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Linux guardian test selection — November 16, 2026

The existing namespace runner grouped a complete `root/subtest` path inside
one regular expression. Go's test matcher splits paths only at slashes outside
regexp groups. Consequently, leaf selections such as
`NodeHostTUN/selected` matched no top-level test. A Go test executable still
returns zero with `PASS` when it selects no tests; the existing strict native
verdict checks correctly reject the missing required roots and leaves.

`namespace_test_selector` now anchors each path component and alternates
complete paths independently. It preserves exact top-level groups, nested
leaves, literal regexp characters and multiple leaves under one root. It does
not form a Cartesian product between different roots and their children.
Empty, partial and duplicate paths are refused.

## Actual validation

`deploy/test_linux_guardian_selection.py` compiled an ordinary Go testing
fixture with the selected Go 1.26.3 compiler and the race detector. Nine tests
passed in 0.912 seconds. The fixture exercises test selection only; it does not
claim TUN, nftables, private-server or physical-device acceptance.

- Exact root and leaf selection passed, including nested and literal names.
- Reintroducing the original grouped path ran zero tests and returned zero;
  the actual native verdict parser refused that output.
- An unanchored leaf selected an additional sibling and was refused.
- A Cartesian product selector selected unrelated children and was refused.
- The original namespace function is checked to use the tested selector once.
- All 14 existing Linux CONNECT host-runner tests passed separately in
  0.048 seconds, including the original finite per-case timeouts.

Fixture source SHA-256:
`b705dc3534bfbf6623257b1ee30b484e7ccdeb04121d5f0468dc94a7282f4cfa`.

Compiled test image SHA-256 for this local observation:
`f9b30672c3c64605d05c0539f3f4fe4ea48533b679addda7dd8522816f52f60c`.

The regression is required by the existing AMD64/ARM64 namespace workflow and
the full Linux guardian validation. Its source is included in the native
runtime's exact-source identity. Existing kernel-case deadlines, namespace
isolation, privilege checks, ownership checks and verdict requirements are
unchanged. Hosted execution of the actual networking cases remains separate.

## docs/LINUX-SOCKS-PREFIX-INSTALLED-GATE-2026-11-16.md

# Installed Linux SOCKS prefix execution acceptance

The release aggregate now requires an additional installed Linux SOCKS execution gate on both amd64 and arm64. It uses the captured shipping controller and native worker, TUN, nftables, owned network/mount namespaces, original encrypted providers and an authenticated SOCKS control/UDP association. Its private administration endpoint and direct worker launch are explicit fixture boundaries; it does not claim private-server deployment.

## Required scenarios

The new gate retains 28 distinct installed cases. Fifteen positive cases cover two through six packet prefixes with explicit Server execution and both Compare winners. The mode rotation exercises WireGuard, AWG Fast and AWG Strong at every supported prefix count and retains independent original keys and parameter fingerprints at later positions. Thirteen controls cover first/later packet keys, SOCKS authentication and downgrade, negotiated relay domain and route, control EOF, public identity, final key, selected DNS, source replacement, carrier mutation and held cleanup.

Each positive path restores its already-proved concrete winner without rerunning Compare. Counters bind the actual original prefix positions, modes, parameter hashes, native carrier, selected DNS and independent final proof. SOCKS retains its negotiated UDP association; CONNECT/UoT evidence cannot satisfy this gate. After Disconnect, the worker must already be joined before the provider acknowledges retirement. The provider must observe natural control EOF and join its own leases; shutting down the provider cannot manufacture a zero-association result.

The existing sixteen installed SOCKS trio cases and their source/workflow remain unchanged. The new 28-case gate adds coverage for longer paths rather than replacing those cases. It retains the original per-case bounds: 240 seconds for the Go case, 250 seconds for the namespace and 265 seconds for its outer process; native compilation remains bounded at 300 seconds. The new workflow has a finite aggregate budget of 170 minutes within a 180-minute job.

## Local evidence and limits

On source base `3360e07798625e168b1ca3be72bc3e0a85ea4c53` plus the thirteen frozen changed files, the real native peer compiled with race instrumentation in 97.581 seconds and the privileged controller package in 213.181 seconds. Both retained their original 300-second compilation limits.

All 36 required native component records passed across four roots: original source positions, independent provider positions, malformed/private-source refusals and real SOCKS authentication/UDP-association retirement. The peer used the existing canonical native checkout read-only. Comparing current shipping copy/relocation outputs found 859 byte-identical derived files; an explicit two-file overlay supplied the published desktop prefix admission guard and the new peer test.

The strict new receipt/orchestration validator passed 21 tests, the workflow guard seven, and the aggregate gate 31. The original SOCKS trio validator passed 18 and the existing CONNECT prefix validator 19. Full artifact/workflow parity passed. The 13 tested outputs were integrated with the current Apple inputs and then rebased onto `539cf805d9565f9d25532856988f35232f709bc6`, preserving its Server transport-lifetime repair. Twelve tested outputs remain byte-identical; the builder adds the four new identity inputs while preserving every existing current input.

The 28 full installed kernel scenarios have not run in this local environment. They remain mandatory hosted release gates. Component success, native compilation and synthetic negative receipt tests do not establish installed networking, physical-device acceptance, private deployment or a green release.

Frozen change patch SHA-256: `5658430e5f800567f8600afd257cb714da6e2c70751cad30d231b2c00a16723b`.
Native peer image SHA-256: `7d19989c47aaa2004d709d576c57becb37a1b37e6205f3eed29a3024dd53c927`.
Privileged controller image SHA-256: `cea86a759becbddefeb15b35ae136d58009407ed57b99ff84ab7b4b9e2e7a24c`.

## docs/LIVE-RELEASE-EVIDENCE.md

# Physical/live exact-SHA release evidence

GitHub completion and whole-project release are separate. After one exact `main` SHA passes the authoritative native/package/image/compose chain, use `deploy/live-release-evidence.py` for the remaining physical/live gates. The tool never turns source or CI success into a live PASS.

Initialize a manifest for the exact already-green release SHA:

```bash
python3 deploy/live-release-evidence.py init release-evidence.json --sha <40-char-main-sha>
```

After executing a real gate, keep the raw evidence outside the public repository and record its digest:

```bash
python3 deploy/live-release-evidence.py record release-evidence.json \
  --gate physical-android --status pass \
  --evidence /private/evidence/android-network-transition.txt \
  --note "real-device validation"
```

PASS is refused without at least one evidence file. The manifest stores each evidence file's SHA-256, size, resolved source path and UTC capture time. Final validation re-hashes those raw files, so missing or modified proof loses credit. It does not copy evidence into the repository, so do not commit private bundles, provider keys, router credentials, admin output, or sensitive packet captures.

Required live gates are Windows, macOS, Linux, Android and iOS/iPadOS physical network behavior; off-LAN methods; native visual/DPI QA; private server features; live AI providers; applicable Apple provisioning/signing/distribution; one deliberate exact-SHA production deployment; ASUS forwarding/JFFS fail-open revalidation; and final regression. Original requirement #339 qualifies macOS signing/notarization as optional polish when practical. The combined Apple gate's existing "where applicable" qualification preserves that scope; it does not waive legitimate iOS installation/provisioning or permit an unproved signing/notarization claim. See [the recovered source qualification](AI-DEVELOPMENT-HISTORY.md#note-reconciliation-2026-10-30-sources).

```bash
python3 deploy/live-release-evidence.py status release-evidence.json --sha <same-sha>
python3 deploy/live-release-evidence.py validate release-evidence.json --sha <same-sha> --require-final
```

A newer source commit means a new release SHA and a new live manifest. If a live test exposes a source defect, fix `main`, rerun the exact-head GitHub chain, and restart live evidence for the new SHA.

To include live proof in the weighted 88/12 release score, keep the manifest and raw proof outside Git and run the scorer against the already-green SHA:

```bash
export ROUTER_VPN_LIVE_EVIDENCE_MANIFEST=/private/evidence/release-evidence.json
export ROUTER_VPN_RELEASE_SHA=<same-40-char-sha>
python3 deploy/weighted-release-audit.py
```

Without both variables the manual/live gates deliberately remain false. The six weighted manual buckets cover all thirteen live gates: every shipping native platform, off-LAN methods, visual QA, private server features, live AI providers, applicable Apple distribution, the exact-SHA production deployment, ASUS fail-open forwarding revalidation, and final regression. A manifest for another SHA, an empty evidence list, or missing/tampered raw proof cannot award manual credit. The scorer's weighted metric is an implementation gate, not a percentage of the original requirements completed.

## docs/MACOS-IMPORTED-EXECUTION-PREFIX-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# macOS complete imported Shadowsocks execution

## Scope and shipping path

This change closes the remaining AppKit and Darwin admission restriction for
three-to-eight-member imported Shadowsocks Server/Compare paths. Each path
retains one to six paired WireGuard/AWG prefixes, one literal-address imported
Shadowsocks 2022 member, and an independently paired Shadowsocks final. The
original request remains the full requirement set in REQUIREMENTS-RECONCILED.md;
this evidence covers this particular source and UI integration.

`RouterVPNConnectionProfiles.swift` checks every original catalog member in
the ordered draft and Connect request. Every paired member must retain its
paired kind and canonical independent Start Layer. The imported member must be
penultimate and have the actual Boolean execution hint. Complete profile
Add/Load/Update preserves every source token, position, mode and execution.
Private credentials remain in the controller's original node store.

The existing `ordered_imported_trio_executions` capability key stays compatible
with the controller. Its historical name does not truncate the complete
Shadowsocks path. Typed CONNECT keeps its separate exact-trio capability and
admission; this change does not widen its source family.

`desktopDarwinImportedTrioExecutionMembers` now checks the complete three-to-eight
member source and every paired Start Layer. The public platform selector still
requires the existing retained native architecture and supported Darwin CPU.
The original source compiler, physical policy and Darwin parent/child ownership
remain on the shipping path. The previously published Windows/Linux fixture
now expects this supported Darwin source shape while retaining all original
preparation, identity and replacement-owner assertions.

## Executed evidence

The complete AppKit source fixture passed **16,669 checks and all 59 compiled
causal controls** with Swift 6.0.3. This includes actual extracted draft,
Connect, manager and Settings callbacks; source refresh, same-ID relinking,
generation changes and response ownership remain exercised. New coverage adds
four through eight members, mixed WG/AWG paths and complete profile operations
at three, four and eight members. The controls include the original trio-only
predicate, omitted later-prefix layer validation and omitted paired-kind
validation.

After reconciling the concurrent Windows/Linux changes, all **60 generated
Swift programs and 59 required negative witnesses** were reconstructed and
compared byte for byte with that completed run. The only upstream shared-runner
change adds a Windows-selectable diagnostic marker; every AppKit call retains
the original `PROFILE-CHECK:` default and ninety-second compiler/run limits.
This identity check is not presented as another execution of the programs.

On source based on `6ae72d87b2575faa5d4a83d94d57714d68cad31a`, the actual Go
source/compiler tests passed **75 required records across six roots** under
race instrumentation in 35.923 seconds. These retain the original three-node
admission, source refusals and distinct public capability, and add all longer
lengths, all three packet modes, all three SS2022 cipher representations,
mixed prefixes, independent layers, source-position and identity refusals.
The published Windows/Linux preparation root also passed all eight original
four/eight-member cases, nine records including its root, in 10.907 seconds.
Its copied-preparation refusal remains intact.

Two separate compiled Go overlays also failed at their required behavioral
witnesses: restoring the old trio-only admission rejected the valid four-node
source, and skipping later paired members accepted an invalid Start Layer.
The original source bytes remained unchanged. An earlier compiler process was
terminated by a signal before tests ran and was not counted as negative proof;
both final controls compiled with a lower memory target and kept the original
120-second test and 600-second compilation/process limits.

Both targeted Darwin shipping-audit tests passed, including independent lower
and upper bounds, the penultimate import, complete-member validation and the
later-prefix layer loop. The rest of the published common source audit and
the Windows/Linux implementation are preserved.

## Evidence boundary

The Swift fixture executes extracted shipping Swift with explicit AppKit,
HTTP and deferred scheduling boundaries. The Go fixture executes actual
catalog/source compilation and physical-policy identity, without a running
privileged Darwin process. These are source/component results. Exact-current
macOS builds, installed PF/utun behavior, encrypted native traffic, rendering,
signing, physical devices and private deployment remain separate evidence.
No production deployment or router configuration was changed by this unit.

## docs/MULTIPLE-IMPORTED-CANDIDATE-PREPARATION-2026-11-16.md

# Candidate preparation through complete imported paths

The shared candidate layer now accepts a separate, sealed schema-six source for a selected WireGuard or AmneziaWG packet prefix before or after independently imported Shadowsocks members. It retains the complete original path for a later explicit Save while constructing only the prefix being measured. The generic, SOCKS and CONNECT parsers keep their separate closed contracts.

`NewNodeChainMultipleImportedCandidatePreparation` and its matching parser preserve the original source, target catalog, selection, prefix and every preceding imported public proof. The capture dispatcher recognizes this distinct source without granting authority to an ordinary parser. Public progress rows use the `public-path` kind and a truthful public HTTPS label; private node proof labels retain their existing meaning.

Before sampling, each preceding imported position must pass its own original public TLS proof, and paired members must pass their private proof. Temporary candidate preparation leaves application and DNS gates closed. The connected `NewMultipleImportedCandidatePlan` path requires the exact original complete Local `NodeChain`, its retained Ready owner and current selected-path proof. An equal replacement source or a later Ready owner cannot inherit that authority.

The saved execution choice is retained as source provenance. This shared addition does not advertise interior candidate execution on Server or Compare, a proxy-only prefix as a completed destination TCP handshake, or arbitrary mixed-protocol interiors. Native bridge, actual encrypted provider, desktop and mobile consumer acceptance remain separate work.

## Executed verification

On source checkpoint `71c21f75126235f86002da6349a319faeb4d587e`, all **17 focused Go roots and 156 distinct race-instrumented records passed**, using Go 1.26.3, default vet, readonly modules and the original 180-second per-root limit. The entire test binary compiled in 27.598 seconds under its original 300-second cap. All processes joined, source and image identities remained unchanged, and 39 Python registration checks passed.

The seven new groups account for 100 of those records:

| --- | ---: |
| Original source layouts and selected positions | 43 |
| Source and catalog refusals | 14 |
| Desktop physical preparation boundary | 10 |
| Prepared prefix proof and lifecycle | 7 |
| Connected original Local fifty-sample measurement | 7 |
| Incorrect, absent or stale preceding proof refusals | 9 |
| Connected source, Ready and ownership refusals | 10 |

The other ten groups preserve the existing generic and typed candidate source, prefix-only projection, independent layers, full-suffix Save provenance, fifty-sample runtime and invalidation contracts. These are shared-runtime tests with explicit engine and probe doubles; they are not native encrypted traffic or physical-device evidence.

The source checkpoint is based on published `067f17c61ae2463e8c7132abaa88901afc47e5a4`, preserving the current Server candidate worker and original entry-candidate projection. Its eight implementation and test-registration blobs are identical to the separately preserved `4e68e3969351a521421741d465911cfa9f65bb1b` draft. No test result is borrowed from that earlier uncompiled draft.

## docs/MULTIPLE-IMPORTED-EXECUTION-OWNERSHIP-2026-11-16.md

# Multiple imported execution, restoration and measurement ownership

This unit connects the existing schema-six Server plan and relay foundation to the shared client execution state machine. It retains the complete original Local source for a paired WG/AWG entry, independent literal SS2022 interior imports at their original positions, remaining paired packet interiors, and a paired Shadowsocks final. The selected execution can be Server or Compare. Existing schema-one through schema-five admission, digest and singleton contracts remain unchanged.

## Implemented behavior

The distinct preparation requires the original typed import registry and its exact sealed launch. Mobile preparation has four fields: schema, execution, local source and multiple-imported Local launch. Cross-family fields, copied owners, missing imports, changed source and changed projection are refused. Discovery still authenticates the original descriptor and challenge. Each public import retains its own source digest, actual position and expected public IP; no imported node receives a fabricated private Router API identity.

The shared runtime admits Local and independently provisioned Server candidates through the original execution manager. It requires an explicit native observer for the original Box and imported transport owners. Proof inventory uses the selected path's actual tags while retaining the complete original source and all public positions. Compare retains the existing two-response average, a common external endpoint, repeated winner proofs, cancellation, and original operation deadline. An unavailable or invalid candidate does not receive a score.

Joined restore and Last discovery preserve the selected execution, complete source, original owner and issued interface readback. Restoring a proved Server choice does not rerun Compare. Mobile restore carries an owned capsule; identical JSON cannot reconstruct that authority. Desktop physical handoff, adoption and Ready records have a distinct schema-six family and retain the complete original import inventory.

Selected fifty-sample capture adds an optional `multiple_imported` object containing schema 6, the complete original source hash, and ordered public position/tag/source-hash records. The six-field legacy capture is unchanged. Server hop measurement uses each selected original public prefix and checks complete TLS responses, ownership, cancellation and freshness before publishing results.

## Executed evidence

The shared and measurement packages were compiled in full with Go 1.26.3, `go test -c -race -p=1 -mod=readonly`. Compiler bounds remained 300 seconds. Each complete selected test root retained its 180-second Go timeout and 195-second process bound. The observer used the shared exclusive heavy-worker lock, checked frozen source and image hashes, matched exact pass inventories, refused skips/races, and joined each child.

| Executed scope | Complete roots | Distinct passing records |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Shared preparation, capture and policy ownership; Server/Compare runtime; restoration, retirement and Last; desktop physical/adoption/Ready contracts | 35 | 386 |
| Server original public inventory and fifty-sample measurement, including incomplete TLS, stale and cancelled results | 4 | 32 |
| Total | 39 | 418 |

Twenty-two shared roots (206 records) passed on private source `a9938461b01cc36d9d31b3ac3fec899514779f81`. The other thirteen shared roots (180 records) and all four measurement roots passed on `579519e3c1b9a9581de71331925ab2e24530e859`. These sources differ only in the ownership-refusal test described below. The corrected seven-record refusal root also passed three consecutive race-instrumented repetitions, so the observer recorded 432 passing verdicts for 418 distinct records. Repetitions are not additional coverage.

The publication was composed on `250840cb40211f5310da7d874669bf98928e9088`, preserving the newer native entry-candidate and Apple Local profile changes. Its complete `internal/mobilemultihop`, `internal/hopmeasure`, module files and shared gate match the final executed source byte for byte. Parent-application and native-host integration work is not included in this unit.

| Test image | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- |
| Shared image, first source | `956218f1a7b747c19458df56d5e9775a3d7137ba16a9a80330333d008a6d2175` |
| Shared image, corrected refusal fixture | `790be2ef3d56002e240b8abd17c35821e3bb3a908c12b635231e53e8a088e2ba` |
| Measurement image | `19db2e2e5dc7aecd486e3f1e1c2c590229b25fbd07023493d395285a738b3f92` |

The exact per-root aggregate receipt has SHA-256 `d8f498d9da9e080bc680ad3dbf3ba433afa9a30a9c287796a4d03ef1e9449fc2`. Per-root commands, logs, durations, source manifests and process receipts are retained in the task workspace under `desktop-multiple-imported-execution-validation`, runs `run-a993-4` and `run-579-3`. The original required shared registry preserves all earlier phases and adds separate schema-six roots with omission and duplicate-assignment checks.

## Failures corrected without changing production deadlines

The first larger Compare fixture exceeded its existing 20-second operation deadline. Profiling found repeated full graph traversal in imported-policy capture checks. The separately published `6ce93a724b3fd7e4953d0e00a662d646ef9bfce3` retains the original validated policy and seal while still checking its complete current source/config/metadata/private-capture seal on every observation. It does not cache successful network proofs or alter timeout values. The maximum public-import Compare fixture then completed in 14.19 seconds.

The first ownership-refusal run exposed a test race: fixture cleanup rewrote the running preparation while an owned stop operation read it. The corrected test creates detached copies for copy/substitution cases and first proves the detached binding valid before removing its owner. It restores only its idle borrowed measurement projection before closing the measurement. Original runtime-owner loss and synchronized selected-epoch cancellation remain separately checked. No production synchronization, validation or rejection case was removed.

## Evidence boundary and remaining integration

These tests execute the real shared execution manager, authenticated private HTTP, verified public TLS, complete discovery/restore validators and fifty-sample measurement logic. Native transport, route, DNS and interface callbacks in these shared fixtures are controlled doubles. Linux, Windows and Darwin names identify distinct handoff contracts executed on this Linux test host; they are not native operating-system or device acceptance.

Actual Box/worker schema-six dispatch, encrypted provider/namespace traffic, desktop process factories, Android and Apple host adoption, public UI capability, and selected-capture consumers remain separate integration units. This commit does not advertise those unfinished hosts as available or establish a green release, deployment, signing or physical-device acceptance.

## docs/MULTIPLE-IMPORTED-LOCAL-CORE-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Ordinary Local multiple-import core: November 16, 2026

Schema6 retains one or more independently imported Shadowsocks providers at
their original interior positions in a three-to-eight-member Local chain. The
paired WG/AWG entry owns physical egress; remaining paired packet members keep
their original sources; the paired Shadowsocks final owns application DNS and
its independent Off, AES or AES+XOR layer. Imported providers retain separate
credentials, literal addresses, expected public IPs and proof positions.

The compiler, runtime, ordinary native Box, desktop physical bootstrap policy
and hopmeasure code now preserve this complete source. Public measurement uses
the captured position/tag/source tuple for each imported member, with a fresh
complete-source validation and the exact original source pointer. Nested packet
MTU arithmetic includes intervening imported closures, adjusts only the compiled
Auto capacity and retains fixed-MTU refusals and original source values.

This is the shared/native core slice. Schema6 Server/Compare, typed SOCKS/CONNECT,
imported hostnames and measurements through arbitrary non-packet candidate
prefixes remain closed. Mobile and desktop UI consumers are separate units.
Nothing here substitutes ordinary reachability for the candidate feature's
50 routed TCP-handshake samples.

## Source identities and publication boundary

The initial composed core was `767cfe9d1ddeefa3bcc851dc8e6f0c13dfb103e8`,
based on published `76de49ad7af75b06853e445ffaff885de3d2d303`. Its 37 changed
code/workflow inputs include the previously published generic packet-MTU
prerequisite through that parent. The only subsequent compilation-affecting
change is the dedicated Box test fixture in
`5d8a7ae53bf4103edfeed44bc7d6afc0906effe2`: it constructs and starts the actual
DNS router from the original captured configuration and managers before
checking public-route authority. Shipping production was unchanged.

All 37 candidate inputs match the frozen `5d8a7ae5` manifest exactly. The final
publication also updates the existing SS counterfactual full-file checksum
after its actual compiled control passed, plus this document and the status
entry. No unvalidated consumer implementation or consumer-only build input is
included. The canonical preparation uses core
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray
`50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`.

Publication is composed over `ba825498556f4efb6cd06fdb3255ccb54774744a`,
preserving the intervening Windows receipt reader, mobile typed-candidate
prerequisite `95b41b68` and Apple typed consumer work. The Apple change does not

multiple-import Box test mapping is added alongside the upstream typed-candidate
mappings. The other 36 frozen inputs are byte-identical. The executions below
retain their original source identities; they are not relabeled as a fresh
native run of this composed publication. Exact-current hosted compilation and
release gates cover that final composition.
The composed generator's exact mapping/input inventory and 31 Python validator
tests passed: 11 typed component, seven typed encrypted and 13 schema6 native
runner checks. Those are source/runner checks, not another native execution.

## Completed proof, with original execution identities

These are separate retained executions, not one uninterrupted all-phase run.
Counts called records include selected root records; the encrypted count is

## Why the original encrypted evidence remains applicable

The corrected preparation compared all 5,593 core files and 1,020 Xray files
against the original canonical preparation. The only Go difference is the root
Box package's dedicated `_test.go` fixture. That file is not part of the Box
shipping dependency imported by the peer package. All encrypted peer inputs and
their shipping dependencies are unchanged.

Three other differing files are preparation metadata: the template hash in the
source receipt, the exact absolute Xray replacement path in `go.mod`, and the
desktop source receipt's recomputed source/module digests. Xray content equality
was established before normalizing only the two recorded replacement roots;
the receipt fields were checked individually against recomputed values. No
wildcard source normalization was used. The initial strict byte-comparison
refusal is retained. The 40 encrypted receipts keep their `767cfe9d` identity;
they are not relabeled as executions on `5d8a7ae5`.

The original canonical Go/module digest is
`11fc75bc6490c44029aeeabc45623c5f0f74a4c12ce4d846e3d9e888f8bca5e1`;
the corrected preparation's digest is
`a397b12edda9eabf853b745ea5f7d76ef4867fd11c55d7587d503c79f9e11496`.

## Preserved failures and physical continuation

The original Box image compiled successfully in 198.028 s. Its positive root
then refused all three layouts because the older shared fixture supplied DNS
transport and network managers but no DNS router. The shipping public-route
guard was correct. The dedicated test-only wrapper now initializes and starts
the real router from those original owners, closes it explicitly, and is used
by both new Box roots. Neither the shared fixture nor production was weakened.

The first corrected Box compiler ended after 169.084 s with actual filesystem
`no space left on device` diagnostics. It produced no image or test verdict and
did not reach the unchanged 600 s ceiling. The successful warm retry reused the
same verified canonical core/Xray source; its failed receipt and log remain.

After the 17 Box records and source-pointer control passed, the local combined
driver refused before physical execution: its setup had prepended Go's PATH
twice. An unchanged standalone invocation also refused before tests because
tool-call/proxy environment values had rotated. Both refusals and the original
driver are retained. No environment values are exposed in the proof report.

A separate explicit resume driver checked the original compiler command,
flags, resource settings, compiler identity, canonical source/Xray manifests
and retained Box image bytes. It recorded a fresh complete environment
fingerprint and required it to remain unchanged across physical117, SS7 and
the SS causal commands, except for owned temporary paths. Whole-environment
identity between compilation and this continuation was **not established**.
The final receipt states that limitation. No baseline image was rebuilt or
previous receipt relabeled to hide it.

## Bounds, mandatory integration and acceptance limits

Native execution retained two CPUs, `GOGC=50`, `GOMEMLIMIT=1800MiB`, `-p=1`,
`-race`, read-only modules and the original native tags. The peer compile is
bounded at 300 s; Box and control compiles at 600 s. Each encrypted case keeps
its 85 s fixture, 90 s Go and 105 s process bounds. Box roots and source control
retain 120/135 s; physical117 retains 90/105 s; the SS baseline/control retain
120/130 s. No deadline was increased to obtain these results.

The new mandatory native gate has a worst-case ceiling of 6,225 s:
300 + 60 + (40 × 105) + 600 + 60 + (2 × 135) + 600 + 135.
Linux's workflow allocation adds 110 minutes to the previous 200 minutes,
yielding 310; Darwin's 60 and Windows' 220 remain unchanged. The separate local
physical continuation adds at most 965 s (105 + 130 + 600 + 130), for 7,190 s
combined ceilings. These are scheduling bounds, not claimed execution times.

The canonical TUN/interface and initial physical-to-loopback mapping are
explicit component fixtures. Encrypted graphs, peer protocols, original public
routes and selected DNS use shipping code. The 117 physical records exercise
actual native factories with platform metadata and policy/ownership fixtures;
they do not activate installed OS protection or prove physical-device traffic.
The three existing Hysteria2 physical compiled controls were not rerun here;
their mandatory shipping gates remain unchanged. Local evidence is Linux amd64;
the required hosted amd64/arm64 current-source gates, complete release,
installed devices and deployment remain distinct acceptance work.

After the proved golden refresh, affected Python validators passed 65 tests:
native runner13, SS contract8, OpenVPN contract7, physical inventory3 and shared
registry34. These are validator-only checks, separate from the actual images.

## docs/MULTIPLE-IMPORTED-SERVER-FOUNDATION-2026-11-16.md

# Multiple imported Server source and relay foundation

This unit adds distinct operator-owned schema-six Server plan and source constructors for a paired packet entry, one or more independent literal SS2022 interior imports at their original positions, any remaining paired packet interiors, and a paired Shadowsocks final. `NewNodeChainMultipleImportedServerPlan` consumes complete independently selected Local/Server bundles. `NewNodeChainMultipleImportedServerPolicy` reparses its exact private suffix source. Generic Server and client execution constructors retain their existing admission boundaries.

Every imported row retains its original source digest, profile identity, public proof role and expected public IP, with no fabricated Router API, credential or packet identity. Paired Server clients retain independent private packet keys. Relay requests carry exactly the complementary Local packet positions: entry position zero plus every paired non-final position, excluding every imported row and the final. Each suffix position keeps its own front credential. The final application DNS digest and original descriptor/capture ownership remain exact. Schema-one through schema-five digest domains and positional contracts are preserved.

A private scope binds the original Server policy, source, members, policies, nodes and compilation closures. For an SS-first suffix, the first actual packet retains the existing nonpacket-root configured-capacity policy; the omitted client entry is never invented as an outer packet interface. Later packet edges retain the full original nested carrier envelope. Each real proof/application/selected-DNS projection must remain inside the original first transport's exact tuple set, and their aggregate must equal the complete set. A subset, added tuple, missing projection or substituted owner is refused. Local and legacy per-projection equality remain unchanged.

## Rebuilt source and actual validation

The executed source is private commit `982a059230046611a099d59a38c484821e8ad1ed`, based on published `9e1e78bddfecf1ac85561e144b4a9c38fc00977d`. This is a new source identity reconstructed after workspace loss. No lost unpublished source or its old test results supply evidence for this unit. The publication adds this document without changing the executed Go or Python bytes.

Both complete packages were compiled with Go 1.26.3, `go test -c -race -p=1 -mod=readonly`, under a 300-second compiler limit. Each complete selected root retained its 180-second Go timeout and 195-second process limit. The observer used the shared exclusive build lock, owned temporary directories, `GOMAXPROCS=4`, `GOGC=50`, and `GOMEMLIMIT=1800MiB`; it checked source hashes before and after execution, exact run/pass inventories, unique package completion, absence of skips/failures, and joined children.

| Validation | Roots | Passed records | Observed elapsed |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Complete shared package race compilation | — | — | 24.242 s |
| New shared source, owner, MTU, physical-union and proof cases | 9 | 109 | 52.989 s total |
| Existing shared source, plan, proof and candidate-grant cases | 17 | 142 | 47.709 s total |
| Complete relay package race compilation | — | — | 5.036 s |
| New relay complement, substitution and lease cases | 3 | 47 | 4.122 s total |
| Existing relay capture and candidate-grant cases | 13 | 93 | 17.301 s total |
| Required shared-phase registry self-tests | 35 checks | 35 | 0.934 s |

All 391 selected Go records passed with the race detector. New shared phases are individually mandatory in the existing core group, with omission, duplicate-assignment and overlapping-selector controls. The original Local eleven-root registry and all existing phases remain selected.

The shared image SHA-256 is `bdb7827c99e2ddbcb569d6ba8375bf2054509184f7fe07c7dce16fb7366c0444`; the relay image is `db004e8ac4ca794e6e6f112bfcad8f80fb32cd0969d1fa3a75220f7eff5feed9`. Full input manifests, compiler receipts, exact per-root JSON verdicts, process limits, source snapshots and logs are retained in `multiple-imported-server-rebuilt-validation` under the task workspace.

The first available image passed 99 new records but exposed a test-only MTU readback mistake: the fixture passed a mutable Go integer to a parser requiring canonical decoded JSON numbers. The corrected fixture reads the actual serialized native endpoint by its original proof tag. The complete 109-record matrix above was rerun from the corrected image; the failed log remains separate. An earlier observer session became unavailable without a terminal receipt or image and has no compiler or runtime verdict.

## Evidence boundary

The public proof tests execute authenticated private HTTP, verified public TLS, distinct original imported public IP responses, selected-DNS identity checks and owner-change refusals. Their native dial/DNS interface is a controlled route double. Relay tests execute the real manager, capture and receipt validators with controlled worker replies. Neither supplies encrypted native SS2022 traffic, a Linux packet namespace, a connected Box, or client Server/Compare readiness.

Native carrier acquisition, worker/Box schema-six dispatch, retained client preparation and restore, selected measurement metadata, desktop/mobile factories and host admission remain separate integration requirements. This unit does not widen those boundaries or claim a green full release.

## docs/NAIVE-H2-CONNECT-SELECTED-DNS-2026-11-12.md

# Naive H2 over CONNECT: selected DNS and dormant lower resolver trees

## Admission boundary

The imported compiler now admits the original selected UDP, TCP, TLS, HTTPS,
and HTTP/3 DNS policies for a strictly validated literal Naive H2 transport,
including a selected resolver hostname with its explicit bootstrap. An optional
final AES/XOR peer must also retain its original literal address.

Both complete resolver trees remain in the executable graph. The selected DNS
and its explicit bootstrap use the established final Naive owner. The lower
resolver copies remain attached to the original CONNECT/final-layer owner;
they are recorded in desktop capture without manufacturing a lower UDP socket.
Exact tree equality, endpoint, port, TLS name, path, query options, original
credentials, strict transport validation, and retained-source identity checks
remain required. Hostname lower peers do not acquire this exception.

The Apple picker/save precheck follows the same boundary. Android continues to
use the shared compiler. The desktop capture test now requires the accepted
selected DNS transport, endpoint, TLS/path and complete lower tree while its
physical egress remains only the original CONNECT TCP tuple.

## Actual pinned native evidence

The runtime was prepared from clean Git checkouts at sing-box
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray
`50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1` using the shipping preparers.
Go `go1.26.3` compiled the race-enabled native image. During final fixture
iteration, one explicitly declared test overlay supplied the updated
`node_naive_dns_test.go.tmpl`; it did not replace production transport source
or relabel another prepared-source receipt.

The official Cronet module was checksum-verified and staged using packaging's
own helper. Its version is `v0.0.0-20260912104006-c10c03c318db`, the Linux amd64
library is 11,915,768 bytes, and its SHA-256 is
`23109c55b08829bb1a68682a61a64852b1bbf243753b153cbd8f6e4ea2c05294`.
The formerly available older fixture library has the same digest, but the
actual execution used the freshly staged official module.

Three independently bounded native phases passed exact RUN/PASS inventories:

| Native phase | Required verdicts, including root |
| --- | ---: |
| Naive selected DNS over CONNECT | 49 |
| Existing literal Naive endpoint controls | 24 |
| Existing literal Naive over CONNECT and final-layer controls | 53 |
| Total | 126 |

The new phase covers seven selected DNS forms across HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT and
no final layer/AES/AES-XOR: UDP, TCP, TLS, HTTPS, HTTP/3, hostname HTTPS with
explicit UDP bootstrap, and hostname HTTP/3 with explicit UDP bootstrap.
Separate refusal cases change the selected port, path, TLS name, trust, or
explicit bootstrap port. Query-shape controls reject foreign question names,
classes, types, IDs and message shapes.

Every transport case retained zero lower DNS exchanges/lookups and zero lower
UDP attempts. Native selected DNS returned the exact expected A answer with
one application receipt; hostname cases also required one explicit bootstrap
receipt. The pinned TCP/TLS multiplexer asynchronously sends ID 1/A and
ID 2/AAAA connection-reuse probes. These are counted separately, exactly once,
and joined inside the existing operation deadline; they cannot satisfy the
application receipt. The HTTPS peer serves HTTP/2, avoiding an unrelated
HTTP/1 fallback and its second hostname lookup.

Image, declared overlay, and Cronet hashes remained unchanged across all three
phases. The image SHA-256 was
`9e860610702cd6c20a725f287141f5ef6b6d71b8100f114c14da68f931f9169a`;
the declared overlay SHA-256 was
`3c1cb6ebc55d9e7c97e4cbbc1d28c11451fcaad627aa1af9669e7f5b09fbcf9c`.
The selected-policy log SHA-256 was
`3da14486568164d88fd57cb3a0c6884a5e4a53efa66993ddb0eec3dd24c606b9`.

This is actual pinned encrypted loopback/native-DNS evidence, not OS TUN,
physical-interface, device or live-router acceptance. Those existing release
gates remain mandatory. The new phase retains its own 90-second execution
bound; no existing deadline or negative-control inventory was reduced.

## Producer and source checks

- Shared Naive H2 race tests passed, including the complete original independent
  layer inventory and selected-DNS ownership/refusal cases.
- The new complete-tree regression covers both CONNECT carriers, all three
  final layers, HTTPS/HTTP3 selected policy and UDP/TCP explicit bootstrap.
  Missing, foreign, cyclic, changed-port, changed-transport, changed-TLS and
  physical-bypass lower trees are rejected.
- Official Swift 6.4.2 picker/save checks linked to the actual Go compiler passed

- Mandatory-runner tests retain every original verdict, add the complete new

## docs/NATIVE-CONNECTED-TYPED-CANDIDATES-2026-11-16.md

# Native connected candidate ownership for SOCKS and CONNECT

## Behavior

The mobile bridge provides connected candidate measurement handles for the
complete original Local SOCKS5 and HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT chains. Each handle is
bound to the already-proved runtime, native engine, original source catalog
and selected packet prefix. A saved-prefix preparation cannot substitute for
that connected runtime.

Construction returns a unique reservation before calling the host catalog or
constructing the measurement service. The host stores that exact handle and
then calls its one-shot `Prepare`. Preparation keeps the original construction
deadline. Failure, expiry and cancellation close that handle; cleanup cannot
find and retire another operation by matching owner, position or inventory text.

Copied handles or owners, changed engines, stale slots, changed catalogs and
replacement runtimes are refused. `Close` joins admitted preparation, the
measurement service and outstanding catalog callbacks before clearing its own
slot. Native lifecycle cleanup checks original owner identity even if a copied
owner clears its candidate slot. Late cleanup cannot clear a replacement slot.

## Executed verification

The final 21 source files were frozen over `c89ec73290b8c94e3638d749244405733064dad9`.
Both full native test images compiled with race instrumentation and default Go
vet: the registered peer on the original desktop native core, and the complete
mobile Libbox client on its separately checked native core. All source inputs,
overlays and retained cores were checked again after execution.

The encrypted suite passed all nine required cases:

| Transport | Original chain sizes | Candidate measurements |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SOCKS5 | 3, 4, 8 | 50 successful TCP establishments per case |
| HTTP CONNECT | 3, 4, 8 | 50 successful TCP establishments per case |
| HTTPS CONNECT | 3, 4, 8 | 50 successful TCP establishments per case |

These are 450 measured candidate connections, with the original full-chain
proof, source ownership and cleanup still required. The partitioned Go output
contains 18 actual records: nine leaves and their repeated roots, or 11 distinct
test names. These counts are not additional independent network cases.

Periodic tunnel proofs also create connections. An earlier exact-delta check
incorrectly included that unrelated traffic, producing a delta of 65 alongside
50 candidate samples. The peer now separately counts a connection only after
the exact control target receives zero payload bytes, reaches remote EOF, and
its original socket closes successfully. Ten accounting records passed, and a
separately compiled omission of the zero-payload guard failed normally at the
required payload assertion. Proof traffic remains active throughout the test.

The final owner hardening passed 30 affected native component records and four
separately compiled copied-owner controls, including copied owners with cleared
slots. Earlier complete component execution on `47e4cd58` passed 36 records and
ten controls. Those earlier results are retained with their own source identity;
they are not represented as a new full run on `c89ec732`. The mandatory current
component gate requires all 38 records and 12 controls. The two focused Python
runner suites also passed all 18 checks on the final source.

The original bounds remain: 600 seconds per complete native compilation,
180 seconds for each encrypted Go case and 190 seconds for its joined process,
and 30/45 seconds for the accounting test/process. Negative controls require
their intended assertion and a normal failing exit. A timeout, build failure,
race, panic, skipped test or unrelated failure cannot satisfy a control.

The focused workflow and authoritative release require the native component and
encrypted gates. Preparation, required source inputs and aggregate artifact
validation include the new files. Existing ordinary and saved-prefix candidate
requirements remain in place.

| Evidence | Source / scope | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Frozen 21-file source manifest | Final native source over `c89ec732` | `ce6dd9421bb6bdc0a3862b890bfffe0565be0dfbfbab18cee22f1d23ef2b6a58` |
| Affected ownership execution | 30 records and four compiled controls | `a018720cd54c58da1c29e870d0d18f43638a8b2abd00dcac0666766949f04958` |
| Encrypted and accounting execution | Nine cases, ten accounting records, one compiled control | `33870c4080e4163a82db0468f79edd913c5e62922426e3cbe3106b15c87bd473` |
| Earlier component execution | 36 records and ten controls on `47e4cd58` | `92a3506ef9ba6ac618e5e7470d94079cd44d91bfd5b33d5178481697de03c20f` |
| Final validation index | Separate source identities and joined worker result | `8fd1676948e8fa231d51a37660c110ac2057aa3a693780e2855d014c585d01e5` |

## Delivery boundaries

This change supplies the native bridge and required gates. Android and Apple
host adoption have separate session, source, binding and packaging checks.
Server/Compare execution-owner candidates and ordinary reservation cleanup
remain separate follow-ups.

Candidate TCP timing does not authenticate a replacement node or establish a
new working VPN configuration. Installed-device networking, leak/transition
tests, signing, private deployment and a green release of the final published
commit require their own evidence.

## docs/NATIVE-MTU-ENGINE-OWNERSHIP-EVIDENCE.md

# Exact native engine authority for ordered MTU

The shared ordered MTU pause binding now exposes `OwnsEngine`, an observation of its retained private engine pointer. The actual native `closedMTUContext` requires that exact pointer before replacing or admitting a reader. Equal reported session/source/configuration identities, or another adapter delegating observations to the original engine, cannot substitute for ownership.

The native adapter introduced in `55ee21293ed556aa4dd89102656f8bee961f7f1f` is preserved. This change adds one ownership condition and a causal regression to that adapter. It does not replace its actual manager transaction, native interface readback, ingress transfer, retained execution selection, or joined retirement. The independent earlier incomplete adapter draft was superseded and was not applied over upstream.

## Executed evidence

- The shared runtime pause/resume receipt test passed with `-race` in 2.968 seconds. It accepts the original pointer and refuses nil, typed nil, a different pointer, an underlying delegated engine, a non-comparable value wrapper, and a zero binding without panicking.
- The actual pinned native Box, inbound manager, selector, gate and ingress MTU component checks passed with `-race`: four roots and 22 required results. These cover candidate, rollback, unchanged and pending-close transactions; original source, reader, bounds and selected execution; joined retirement; and bounded real-TUN readback refusal.
- The new native case keeps the same valid closed capsule and reported identity but substitutes another engine wrapper. It must refuse before native acquisition. Removing only `binding.OwnsEngine(e)` compiles and reaches the intended `changed ownership acquired native MTU transaction` assertion; a compilation error or data race is not accepted as this causal result.

The native run used an explicit three-file Go overlay on the freshly prepared Last-adoption core documented in `CONTINUATION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-12-NATIVE-LAST-ADOPTION.md` (strict baseline digest `cdd7fb09026ef9acf35c6704a9aea16acfab1117842cc126b62365d5259ed4a8`). The overlay supplied the generated shared method, native guard and native test. The original prepared files and their receipt remained byte-identical. This is a scoped component result, not a claim that the frozen core contains every subsequent main-branch change.

The mandatory owner runner retains its exact anchored root selection and all Last/MTU/close cases. Its complete current inventory is 20 roots, 54 required results and 14 causal controls; all seven runner boundary tests pass after integration. Full native and release CI must still execute this integrated source. The component TUN readback and shared closed-pause capsule are explicit fixture boundaries; this does not establish a physical device session or private deployment.

## docs/OPENVPN-CANDIDATE-PREFIXES-2026-11-16.md

# Saved OpenVPN paths and independent prefix measurements — November 16, 2026

This closes a specific remaining chooser gap in the recovered ordered-node
and 50-sample measurement requirements. A saved Local path with an OpenVPN
intermediate can now prepare an earlier paired WireGuard or AmneziaWG prefix
for candidate measurements. The complete saved path remains the source of
identity and Save provenance; its later OpenVPN member and final are not
started by this temporary preparation.

## Actual behavior and preserved ownership

The compiler still validates the complete original path. For this family the
selected prefix must precede the captured penultimate OpenVPN member. The
OpenVPN and final positions, invalid bounds, copied owners, changed proofs,
changed private sources and duplicated candidate identities remain refused.
The existing candidate target compiler still requires a supported original
literal TCP endpoint. OpenVPN does not acquire an inferred TCP timing target,
and a TCP connection response is not labeled ICMP or provider authentication.

The prepared graph contains only the selected contiguous packet prefixes.
It retains the original device TUN policy, route restrictions, physical
source and MTU bounds while application and DNS ingress stay closed. A
configured MTU is not reported as a current-path measurement. Changing an
uncontacted OpenVPN source changes the complete provenance identity without
adding that endpoint to the runnable graph.

The existing owned measurement service performs fifty real TCP connections,
keeps its original request and hold, and retires that hold exactly once.
Cancellation and retirement still reject replacement owners and stale work.
A valid periodic re-proof may repeat only the same captured prefix order;
it cannot contact a later member or query the saved DNS resolver.

## Executed checks

| Layer | Actual local result | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared compiler and runtime | 46 race-instrumented records across nine roots; includes all six WG/AWG Fast/AWG Strong × TCP/UDP fifty-connection cases | Original native transport and physical boundaries are explicit fixtures |
| Old restriction control | A separately compiled image restoring only the old blanket OpenVPN refusal failed the new three-node case with its required assertion | No compiler failure, timeout or race is credited as the expected rejection |
| Android capture | 195 actual compiler/source checks and five causal controls passed | Shipping private-profile/source capture; no Android device tunnel claim |
| Apple capture | 312 shipping Swift/shared Go checks and six causal controls passed | Swift 6.0.3 on Linux with explicit host boundaries; no PacketTunnel device claim |
| Prepared native core | All 45 distinct required records passed, with 47 actual records because the OpenVPN parent runs once for each selected child | Actual pinned Box/Libbox managers, gates, private HTTP and TCP; privileged TUN, physical monitor and encrypted packet I/O are explicit doubles |
| Native verdict runner | All 18 Python tests passed | Complete required phase union and rejection of missing, duplicated, foreign, failed or substituted transcripts |

Shared checks cover three-, four- and eight-node saved sources, every selected
packet-prefix position, uncontacted source provenance and owner replacement.
The native additions exercise three-, four- and eight-node graphs through the
original fifty-connection helper. The original WG/AWG, changed-graph,
closed-gate, callback, cancellation and joined-retirement inventory remains
mandatory. All thirteen existing compiled native causal controls remain in
the shipping gate; this local positive run does not claim to have rerun them.

The first expanded native command correctly failed its existing 150-second
package deadline after the original WG/AWG cases and the new three-node case
passed. The gate now runs the original complete inventory and each added
OpenVPN child in explicit phases. Each command retains its 150-second Go
limit and all phases share one decreasing 900-second process budget. The
successful run took 375.253 seconds, with 41 original records and the exact
three added children. The 90-second measurement request and five-second
Drain observation limits were not changed. Earlier failed or interrupted
attempts remain recorded separately.

The shared, Android and prepared-core observations use the frozen `261c396e`
base plus these changes. The Apple capture observation retains its recorded
`3b4b5d71` base; it is not relabeled as an exact-current Xcode or native-core
build. The publication preserves later Hysteria2 preparation and desktop
editor work; exact publication-SHA cross-platform builds remain required
separately.

Receipt SHA-256 identities:

- Shared positive: `a7fe118b9edc912c1cbf90da426648d8e9c046306ac9f380ca077c0db5070a96`.
- Compiled old-refusal control: `2be541afbd9beec22180c3ca1353acf7f9e817b4230a2ad23054a8e555a8c7d1`.
- Native phased positive: `023040e97874d7829285e58e020bf87eee5a9b154e71c605f8a18d73fd90d209`.
- Android capture: `44f76fb2d7a1200780b83834a7091a283f3affe55a76bf9e7ec9fc80e4324adc`.
- Apple capture: `ab2bff42399d29326e80497ed7095fae1b5a44f4bc645bb59c51334fc7a6dffe`.

## Remaining requirements

This is prefix-only chooser preparation, not proof of a complete encrypted
OpenVPN connection, every requested graph composition or full product delivery.
The separate longer imported-Shadowsocks chooser restriction, broader candidate
transports, current packaged release, physical-device behavior and private
deployment retain their own implementation and acceptance evidence.
