## docs/OPENVPN-CAPTURE-VALIDATION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Fresh OpenVPN graph validation — November 16, 2026

The current desktop four/eight-member Server/Compare lifecycle test reproduced
an eight-member Compare failure during capture of the original saved choice.
The failure occurred under its unchanged twenty-second operation context, both
with a locally constrained runtime and with the published two-CPU runtime
configuration. A standalone profiled case passed narrowly; it did not erase
the complete-root failure. The profile attributed 1.75 seconds of cumulative
CPU samples to repeatedly JSON-encoding the original OpenVPN graph seal.

## Change and retained authority

Only an OpenVPN-containing `NodeChain` uses the new internal seal domain. Each
read freshly hashes the exact source JSON, configuration and metadata bytes,
with separate named fields and byte lengths. The source identity, complete
proof inventory, selected DNS, physical destinations, original configuration
identity and projection remain in the typed seal. Other graph families keep
their existing representation.

This avoids repeated escaping of already-encoded strings. It does not cache a
validation result, change a connection deadline, remove an original-pointer
check, or replace a fresh native/selected-path observation. The original
OpenVPN owner, provider pair, node identities, connected generation, capacity,
saved choice and joined retirement checks remain intact. The internal seal is
not a field of a serialized launch record; source, configuration, metadata
and public launch identities retain their original derivation.

The existing ownership test first obtains a valid physical-policy observation,
then changes each raw input and every remaining typed field. It also retains
the existing copied/equal/foreign owner, substituted source, DNS, provider pair
and canonical parser refusals. The expanded root passes all 35 records.

## Actual focused validation

Validation used the published `c7f9de2d5a44b1c4ab2012aeaa01a535cb9b49f0`
source plus this three-file code/test change. The actual Go 1.26.3 race image
compiled in 51.694 seconds under the existing 300-second ceiling. Compilation
used one CPU and a 900 MiB memory target; execution used two CPUs and an
1800 MiB target. Each root retained its 120-second test timeout and 135-second
outer bound. The inner twenty-second lifecycle and thirty-five-second saved
choice contexts were unchanged.

All ten focused roots passed **111 records**, without a skip, panic or race:

| Root | Records |
| --- | ---: |
| Desktop prefix runtime, measurement, Last and Stop | 5 |
| Desktop original owner and captured-field refusals | 35 |
| Original OpenVPN source and five-platform projection/reconstruction | 7 |
| Original prefix pointer ownership | 10 |
| Complete-prefix source refusals | 13 |
| Sealed mobile execution projection | 5 |
| Desktop saved-choice runtime reproof | 4 |
| Desktop canonical parser refusals | 13 |
| Fresh Local receipt after joined restore | 7 |
| Unjoined/foreign restore refusal | 12 |

The complete four/eight-member Server/Compare root passed in 35.57 seconds
of test-reported time. Its eight-member Compare leaf, including fixture
construction, passed in 15.18 seconds; the corresponding baseline leaf had
failed at 22.69 seconds. These are observed fixture timings, not an installed
device benchmark. The complete focused runtime phases took 196.357 seconds.

A separately compiled causal control changed only the fresh metadata capture
to an empty value. It compiled in 43.992 seconds, then the unchanged
`native-metadata` refusal case failed with the intended
`foreign physical authority admitted native-metadata` witness in 1.442 seconds.
The positive source stayed unchanged throughout both images and execution.

The positive image SHA-256 is
`e06a7c839f570566e173cbac01121047c849c9b71c033b51149a02126103121d`;
the causal-control image SHA-256 is
`48f03f25cc74344459397720228a8b96a841110e7f32f1d8d69b6b9fea06592b`.
The positive receipt is `openvpn-desktop-validation-cost/positive.json`,
SHA-256 `69fa2d24382c8a77bb0c72d5f7b817247fdded30c564799b29f87ad361903042`.
Its complete source manifest has SHA-256
`f7496747b1f5cc2724a30a1212264ea027411367e25319c5341b36d14f7bb474`.
Earlier failing receipts, unchanged source identities and the profile are
retained in `openvpn-desktop-foundation-validation`.

These are actual shared compiler/runtime results with explicit native-boundary
doubles. The separate desktop native child, installed interfaces, encrypted
provider paths, exact-current packages and physical devices still need their
own evidence. This optimization does not itself enable a new product path or
claim a green release.

## docs/OPENVPN-DESKTOP-EXECUTION-FOUNDATION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Desktop ordered OpenVPN execution foundation — 2026-11-16

This unit binds typed schema-5 Server/Compare preparations to the original desktop physical policy. It preserves the original plain Local source and independently paired Server descriptor, the native desktop graph, original packet-prefix positions, physical DNS/bootstrap policy, configured ceiling and restored TUN MTU, and the exact concrete winner stored in Last. It does not enable desktop public admission or claim a native desktop host, TUN, installed-device or deployment pass.

## Ownership and serialization

`NewDesktopNodeChainOpenVPNExecutionPreparation` accepts only an original validated `DesktopNodeChainPolicy`. The new concrete physical policy retains exact preparation, original policy, plain source and native-chain pointers. Equal-value reconstruction and copied wrappers do not transfer that authority. Its configuration derives from the original native physical graph; source identity remains the original plain schema-5 source with its sealed Local launch and independent provider pair.

The desktop preparation adds a distinct `desktop_openvpn` metadata field, and restored Last additionally retains `desktop_last`. Generic/mobile parsers remain closed to these fields. A future desktop native constructor must bind this concrete physical owner to the actual original Box, endpoint, process/network and joined close receipt. The existing mobile four-field construction boundary remains independent.

The three target-OS source matrices cover every total length from three through eight, both Server and Compare, mixed WG/AWG packet-prefix modes, TCP/UDP OpenVPN and independent final layers. The current component runtime uses actual loopback proof/lease/measurement/Last controllers with an explicit native boundary double. That evidence does not replace actual encrypted OpenVPN or privileged host acceptance.

## Fresh validation and bounded runtime

A desktop binding validates its original desktop policy once per fresh read. That original policy validates both its plain source and native physical chain; plain/mobile execution retains its separate original source validation. All exact pointers, seals, pair fields, fresh native observations, proofs and cancellation checks remain mandatory. A compiled omission of the original desktop validation admitted a changed native configuration and was rejected by the negative test.

The typed Last wrapper captures exact original projection/preparation/binding/physical/policy/source/native pointers across the unchanged core Last operation. The core performs saved-record validation, proof verification, fresh current and health observations. The wrapper requires the same pointers and live context before returning that original record. A mutation during Last observation must refuse rather than export a saved record.

Desktop preparation seals freshly hash the raw bytes of source JSON, configuration, Local source, sealed Local launch and saved Last on every read. Named fields, byte lengths and SHA-256 values, all remaining typed metadata, source/configuration hashes, DNS and physical captures are bound under a separate desktop-only domain. This avoids repeatedly JSON-escaping large already-encoded strings. It does not cache validity, hashes or live proof; serialized source identities are unchanged. Mixed mobile/desktop authority cannot receive this seal.

## Validation record

The final V5 frozen image passed **all nine new roots, 95 records**, under `-race`, with no skips or races. Each root ran separately under its original 120-second test limit and 135-second outer limit. Their total wall time was 232.874 seconds; the 20-second prefix-runtime and 35-second Last-operation contexts were unchanged. The actual image SHA-256 is `89ecb39caaa657504d8af56d2c341b74ef9bcc81802b9423865c3127c2f2caa8`; compilation passed in 57.830 seconds under the original 300-second ceiling. All source bytes remained unchanged through compilation and execution.

| Required root suffix (`TestDesktopOpenVPNExecution…`) | Records |
| --- | ---: |
| `OriginalPhysicalLinux` | 13 |
| `OriginalPhysicalWindows` | 13 |
| `OriginalPhysicalDarwin` | 13 |
| `OriginalOwnerRefusals` | 25 |
| `CanonicalParserRefusals` | 13 |
| `ConcretePhysicalAndCover` | 4 |
| `SavedChoiceBinding` | 5 |
| `LastRuntimeReproof` | 4 |
| `PrefixRuntimeAndStop` | 5 |

The standalone eight-node Compare replay passed its complete Run/measurement/Last/Stop path with the same original 20-second context; its leaf wall time, including fixture setup, was 19.59 seconds. The complete final runtime root then passed all four/eight-node Server/Compare cases in 47.149 seconds. Last reproof passed both real fixture-selected winners and the during-observation native-owner corruption refusal in 30.936 seconds.

The V5 compiled mutation replaced only `capture(p.config)` with `capture("")`. It compiled in 58.720 seconds, then the original `preparation-config` negative failed with `foreign physical authority admitted preparation-config`, without a panic or race. This is the intended causal witness that fresh configuration binding is required. Its image SHA-256 is `550d5dc00deecf664473b9a5c6e974f8e2a2b0a457f3bf4ff1dedc53e4632dcd`.

All 26 grouping-runner self-tests passed. The actual V5 compiler inventory contained 1,015 roots, covered once across 79 phases, with all nine new roots mandatory and individually bounded. This inventory check does not claim all 1,015 roots executed locally. The final source manifest is `desktop-v5-frozen-files.json`, SHA-256 `04d0f271cfc8e43a9d749e9f9f72c52bb6986a47654a2672766f3b1006eef82e`, against parent `3f1b4fc33f765967e33102d3e6448e5f39a566f5`.

Earlier evidence is retained at its actual source:

- V2 completed all nine then-existing new roots, 87 records, and twelve existing-family compatibility roots, 80 records, under `-race`. This predates the current Last/restore integration and is not relabeled as current-source proof.
- V3's actual compiled omission of original desktop validation produced the intended `foreign physical authority admitted native-config` witness; its image SHA-256 is `cb21bad7149850e31c2e1ab99a56b291aeb89e9809fe25705eb1fcff6d1f9634`.
- V1's eight-node Compare failed its original 20-second context; a quiet replay confirmed the failure. Its profile showed duplicate validation of the same original Local source through the desktop owner. V2 removed only that duplicate and passed the complete bounded case.
- After integrating the newer opaque Local restore owner, V3 failed during Last under the same 20-second context. V4 removed only duplicate wrapper validation and added exact-pointer/during-observation negatives, but the quiet eight-node case still reached its deadline during Run. Its profile identified repeated preparation JSON escaping; this led to the separately reviewed fresh raw-byte seal in V5.
- A configured-ceiling versus restored-MTU assertion and an ambiguous Compare fixture were corrected without altering production thresholds or choosing a synthetic winner. Both Last choices now use symmetric 200 ms versus 1 ms fixture delays, and the actual Compare decision must select the expected winner.

No 20-second runtime context, 120-second root timeout, 135-second outer bound or 300-second compile ceiling was widened. Root registry phases retain all original cases; each new root has its own original bounded phase.

## Reproduction and source scope

Build the unchanged source with `go test -race -c -p=1 -mod=readonly -o desktop.test ./internal/mobilemultihop`. Compilation used Go 1.26.3, one CPU and an 800 MiB Go memory target; runtime used two CPUs and an 1800 MiB target. Run each full root above using `desktop.test -test.v=test2json -test.count=1 -test.timeout=120s -test.run=^ROOT$`, and require its own terminal pass and every child run/pass. Run the mandatory registry checks with `python3 deploy/test_mobile_multihop_groups.py --self-test`. The ordinary CI runtime group includes every new root.

The final frozen proof parent was `3f1b4fc33f765967e33102d3e6448e5f39a566f5`. The later Apple/Android/bridge commits changed no relevant shared production; the new Apple paired fixture is preserved during integration. Final results and source/image identities are retained in `openvpn-desktop-validation/desktop-v5-final-results.json` (SHA-256 `df085d2c0680cbb40fa29e084df040f02dce8d5105109b60c2a5af5f73d99ac0`) and the per-root JSON logs. The V4 and V5 CPU profiles showed preparation-seal cumulative samples of 1.49 seconds in the failed partial V4 path and 0.26 seconds in the complete V5 path. This is an observed hotspot reduction, not a normalized benchmark.

The next required unit is the distinct native desktop physical-owner constructor and actual host adoption. Matching mobile source/configuration metadata alone must not grant desktop physical authority. Public desktop guards, native TUN acceptance, installed-host gates and physical-device evidence are not claimed by this foundation.

## docs/OPENVPN-DIRECT-SERVER-ACCEPTANCE-2026-11-16.md

# OpenVPN Server suffix acceptance — November 16, 2026

This gate covers the existing schema-five Server graph whose remote worker
contains an imported OpenVPN member and an independent paired SS2022 final
member, without an additional remote WG/AWG prefix. The client's entry remains
outside that remote worker. It continues recovered requirements 214–224 and
addendum 336; it does not establish every requested graph or device scenario.

The source baseline for the completed local validation is
`9dfc56c6c0c61b22f84e686400d3edfb5d8e50e7` plus this unit.

## Required release path

`server-openvpn-direct-encrypted.yml` runs the actual prepared Server worker,
independent provider peer, and driver in owned Linux network namespaces on
AMD64 and ARM64. It is an unconditional reusable job of the existing
`server-node-chain-encrypted.yml`. The release candidate's
`server-ordered-encrypted` job calls that workflow, and the aggregate native
release requires that job. Standalone push and pull-request runs retain their
source path filters; an exact commit is required for standalone acceptance.

The gate requires all fourteen cases:

| Transport | Original final layers | Additional refusal or retirement cases |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TCP | Off, AES-256-GCM, AES-256-GCM + XOR whitening | Wrong Server provider password, wrong final key, failed selected DNS, Stop with an active pending UDP exchange |
| UDP | Off, AES-256-GCM, AES-256-GCM + XOR whitening | The same four cases |

Each accepted case requires actual provider stream and packet counters,
separate public-middle and private-final proofs, selected DNS, and joined
worker/provider retirement. Wrong Server credentials must produce an
authentication refusal without transferring application data. Foreign final
credentials and middle-only credentials cannot reach the final service.

## Original provider and namespace ownership

The reference uses a test-owned `203.0.113.24/32` alias inside its isolated peer
namespace. A narrow opt-in in the existing provider fixture permits only the
captured original literal remote, matching physical address, and two separate
provider credential identities. The ordinary provider fixture retains its
existing loopback boundary.

The reused reference prefix supplies the existing final services. It is not a
member of the product's remote graph and is never its packet carrier. Its
packet and proof counters must remain zero. The source controls additionally
require `NativeCarrierRequired()` to be false and reject both a shared provider
identity and a foreign captured source.

The new namespace program changes only the exact case inventory, selected
test root, and owned alias command. The original process, namespace,
bootstrap, proof, and cleanup bounds remain in force. The native receipt
parser requires exact case selection, complete counters and joins, strict
types, and a complete successful test inventory. Missing, duplicate, skipped,
foreign, or late output cannot produce an acceptance receipt.

## Completed local validation

The actual shipping preparers ran against pristine pinned checkouts:

* Native core: `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`.
* Xray source: `50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`.

The complete source verifier checked **2,809 records** before and after the
build. Their combined identity was
`4dfe3754da27d0de1b10f00053942757797dc966b56fcd6cfbe837726f07af71`.
Compilation used pinned Go 1.26.3, race instrumentation, read-only modules,
the actual native build tags, and the original 600-second compile bounds.

| Actual output | Compile time | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Shipping worker | 97.024 s | `984ea348886f0d0fb16bef9d1fe8a04d9aaffef26a0177120abaf44c93addee9` |
| Independent provider peer | 75.150 s | `db09a1177fd67888ae2a73f50e41f2c15153f6d5e127fd0c227d52334f0a3843` |
| Acceptance driver | 48.277 s | `8fe9f7895fa400c1580c1a5be4e7c1832b39bfded9cfc22ad1f0947d93522353` |

Both actual provider source-export cases passed. All fourteen actual source
cases passed, including independent credentials, captured transport, source
refusals and the absence of a remote packet carrier. The existing source/MTU
inventory also passed. Total execution of this compile-and-source mode took
235.090 seconds, with all checked source bytes unchanged. Temporary private
exports and generated executables were removed by the original gate after
their identities were recorded.

The gate's fifteen runner controls passed at this baseline. They cover exact
case selection, receipt types, missing and duplicated output, source ownership,
namespace deltas, workflow inclusion and prevention of compile-only bypasses.

The retained compile/source receipt has SHA-256
`d659aaa41a6b4345085dc58678f64d519b0898e82dbaefeda69b80b2c1ee1ced`;
its log has SHA-256
`69430e5d2c19c9c0665417a2fc53b6e56f844a4ab5e26735778ed5d4af85dca7`.
An earlier copied-core preparation was refused by the existing generated-source
ownership guard. The first pristine compile then exhausted scratch disk space.
Neither attempt counts as a passing build; the successful attempt followed
removal of reproducible compiler-cache archives, without changing source or
test bounds.

## First hosted run and captured-final fixture repair

The first hosted run at `c7f9de2d5a44b1c4ab2012aeaa01a535cb9b49f0`
failed on both architectures before encrypted traffic. Jobs `133174688237`
and `133174688794` rejected the first `tcp/off/original` source because the
fixture compiled an unused loopback WireGuard reference while extracting the
independent final member. The real literal-unicast validation correctly
rejected that reference. The earlier source-only fixture had concealed this
with a substituted WireGuard address; that substitution is removed.

The original final-member builder is now shared with the ordinary fixture,
and the direct fixture calls only that final member. It preserves the actual
provider's credentials, source and final identity without introducing a remote
packet prefix. Source checks also prove that an absent unused reference socket
does not change the final authority. Production validation and encrypted
acceptance requirements are unchanged.

The repaired source passed all fourteen direct cases using the actual TCP and
UDP provider exports, all fourteen ordinary encrypted-source cases, and
sixteen runner tests. Both provider and complete acceptance-driver binaries
were compiled with race instrumentation. A separately compiled causal control
restoring the old full-WireGuard extraction failed the original first case
with the same literal-unicast rejection observed in CI. Source bytes remained
unchanged throughout validation. The retained validation receipt has SHA-256
`9a5ad80c2640fa04dff9ac2c7dde528651ad91db32588c7311dc657c1d4218e1`.
These results establish the fixture repair, not encrypted namespace acceptance.

## Hosted pending transport-counter correction

The direct ARM64 job `133184788625` at
`3b4b5d714d5111739e2148ea7be87fee3e1e51c1` passed all fourteen repaired source
cases, then failed the first encrypted case at its pending-traffic assertion.
The native constructor authenticates the retained OpenVPN transport before
exposing Pending IPC. The independent provider counts raw TCP accepts and
physical UDP reads, so these transport counters legitimately include the
handshake before application admission.

The pending check now distinguishes that control traffic from decrypted
application traffic. It still requires the original closed front, refused
private Router destination, no admitted traffic, zero decrypted provider
streams and packets, and zero middle/final proof, DNS, payload and service
counters. Negative raw counters are rejected. No counter baseline is
subtracted and no production authentication, admission or timeout is changed.

All seventeen runner tests passed. The additional compiled fixture exercises
the actual raw TCP/UDP wrappers with loopback sockets, rejects each forbidden
application counter independently, and rejects three compiled mutations: the
old raw-zero assertion, an omitted decrypted-stream guard, and an omitted
private Router guard. The runner took 4.872 seconds; the complete validation
driver took 6.14 seconds. The frozen two-file manifest has SHA-256
`8ae1d5e822da2536a7001f9d1409d955e39402abd9a3e0d33a6d5bea7e5dafb4`;
the runner receipt has SHA-256
`b439e1188dc1caf868721c9211ba27d73a38d7851195d92d3a104d042c05f681`.
These focused counter tests do not establish OpenVPN authentication or the
fourteen full encrypted native cases. Wrong-password constructor observation
remains a separately bounded fixture correction.

## Captured wrong-password construction observation

At `04d3d3a0`, both hosted architectures passed all six TCP/UDP and
Off/AES/AES+XOR positive cases. Each required Ready, selected DNS, UDP traffic,
zero unused-prefix counters and joined worker/provider retirement. ARM64 job
`133200131385` and AMD64 job `133200131914` then reached the wrong-provider-password
case. Authentication was refused, but the fixture requested an IPC snapshot
while the original constructor still awaited its captured bootstrap deadline.
The ordinary ten-second response limit expired before that constructor returned.

That negative case now observes only the original worker's fixed-vocabulary
construction diagnostic, at or after its captured deadline, with the existing
ten-second response-observation allowance. It requires the exact OpenVPN-owner
deadline failure, no cancellation, a refused snapshot with no receipt, refused
bootstrap and private Router front, a real positive authentication-refusal
counter and zero decrypted application/proof/DNS counters. The original partial
owner must then pass the existing Stop and terminal-join checks. Every other
case retains its existing Pending/bootstrap path and fifteen-second readiness
observation. Production construction, authentication and IPC timeouts are unchanged.

All eighteen runner tests passed in 8.642 seconds. Deterministic refusals cover
early or absent captured deadlines, wrong diagnostic stage or operation,
malformed/missing/cancelled/nondeadline diagnostics, absent or negative
authentication counters and forbidden decrypted destinations. Three compiled
controls separately remove the deadline, authentication or closed-destination
requirement and must fail with their exact witnesses. The frozen source
manifest has SHA-256
`6d044154458cf1f3f6878f5dce292ae4db7ca1732acd5059f94bb788c68a43bb`;
the runner receipt has SHA-256
`1dda830b1d7640e7ab3df2878eda2e83e6384b2628b6b0baa7bdde4a88c784db`.
These helper tests repair the observed fixture ordering; the two wrong-password
and six remaining negative/retirement cases still require native execution on
the published source before the fourteen-case gate can be called complete.

## Acceptance still required

Local compilation and source cases do not establish encrypted network
acceptance. This environment's network namespace setup is denied, so the
local result explicitly has scope `compile-and-source-only` and an empty
native-case receipt list. The required hosted AMD64 and ARM64 jobs must execute
all fourteen encrypted cases on the published source. Actual entry API lease
acquisition, public client adoption, installed-device behavior, private
deployment and physical off-LAN acceptance remain separate requirements.

## docs/OPENVPN-LOCAL-ENCRYPTED-OWNER-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Original encrypted Local OpenVPN owner evidence

The required Linux native build now runs `test_node_chain_openvpn_encrypted_local.py` against the canonically prepared core. Its original `TestOpenVPNExecutionEncryptedOriginalLocal` image exercises a three-member Local WG → OpenVPN → SS graph with the shipping Box, concrete OpenVPN endpoint, mutual TLS and encrypted data channel, final SS transport, selected resolver, private/public identity checks, and actual joined Box retirement. This change adds tests and required build wiring; it changes no production admission, transport or deadline.

The application TUN/default-interface monitor, the first physical tuple's loopback mapping, and the discovery advertisement are explicit component fixtures. The advertised Server source has independent provider credentials and pairing keys, but this gate does not claim an actual Server lease. It does not prove installed TUN/kernel protection, full execution Run/Last, longer Local packet prefixes, AWG, or application admission. The current real Server gates remain independently required.

## Exact native matrix

One retained race image passed all fourteen cases, with one exact root and one exact selected child per invocation (28 strict test records):

| Cases | Required observed behavior |
| --- | --- |
| TCP and UDP, each final Off/AES/AES+XOR (6) | Original connected endpoint receipt; encrypted discovery/adoption and Local selection; final private and TLS public identities; selected DNS; exactly one public HTTPS byte; retained generation and joined retirement. |
| TCP and UDP, wrong provider password (2) | Actual authentication refusal, zero decrypted stream/packet deliveries, and retained construction identity for independent candidate eligibility. |
| TCP and UDP, failed selected DNS (2) | Actual private/public final identities first, then an actual captured public-name DNS query and refusal; zero public download bytes and no selected-DNS proof. |
| TCP and UDP, foreign final identity (2) | Actual final health response with the wrong identity, no subsequent final/public origin proof or download. |
| TCP and UDP, reconnect (2) | A real endpoint reconnect produces a later generation; the engine refuses substitution for the retained connected receipt. |

The fixture reads the original resolver tag, UDP address/port and final detour from the sealed Local config. It changes only that one reference peer's physical DNS destination to a test-owned UDP responder. Both `example.com` and the captured public measurement hostname are supported. An independent query counter proves that the public lookup reached this responder through the encrypted final owner; malformed/foreign queries are refused. A/AAAA handling retains the captured IPv6 policy. The responder and original Box both have bounded joined cleanup.

Each successful traffic case recorded final-private=1, final-public=1, captured-public-DNS=2 and public-download=1 for the exercised resolver policy. Failed-DNS cases recorded actual captured DNS with no download. These are actual traffic assertions, not replacement lookup return values or ambient resolver results.

## Actual compiled controls

Each control changes exactly one retained production assertion through a Go overlay; the production files remain unmodified. A control is accepted only with its exact failure witness, complete failing root/child/package verdicts, and no race, timeout, panic or cleanup failure.

| Removed requirement | Compiler / 300 seconds | Rejection process / 75 seconds | Witness |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Original source pointer | 64.420 | 1.433 | `equal Local source replaced original Box owner` |
| Retained connected receipt | 96.394 | 5.220 | `reconnected Local endpoint replaced retained original receipt` |
| Original Box join | 84.451 | 6.682 | `unjoined Local Box manufactured original retirement` |

The original baseline compiler passed in 120.671 seconds. All fourteen baseline processes passed in 3.695–6.362 seconds. Bounds remain 300 seconds per compiler, 60 seconds per Go test, 75 seconds per runtime process, 20 seconds for the operation, and 45 seconds for the original provider lifetime. The ten required runner tests reject omitted, duplicate, skipped, reordered, foreign, false-terminal and cleanup-contaminated evidence, as well as omitted or mis-targeted build wiring.

## Source and retained failures

The executed core was canonically prepared from `4ae6a3c4fc9a1480ba4c730cfb94a5d3891b0e9e` with pinned native commit `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and pinned Xray commit `50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`. The final fixture was frozen at private commit `be0b0e12`; baseline image SHA-256 is `2fd5b474d9dd079e68560c25ecb991c6985ba8b51a3b218ac383dab32f831319`. The full prepared-core receipt remains explicitly on that source. The original OpenVPN native owner/public/lifetime paths and shared OpenVPN source files were unchanged through `70e6b344`; later candidate/Hysteria additions do not turn this into an exact-current whole-core run.

The baseline receipt SHA-256 is `66e6673f87c4e11ce8aa0f28055377e92e55a8d539cd03d32cd2554f41566467`; the three-control receipt SHA-256 is `83b659228d1f2e53af22528df53444220a472be0582c91c17492947830bb9769`. They retain every source/overlay/image/log identity and process result under `openvpn-local-encrypted-validation`.

Earlier conservative 1-CPU/800-MiB compiler attempts reached their unchanged 300-second local bounds and produced no image; they remain recorded as failures. A quiet 2-CPU/GOGC50/1800-MiB build completed under the same bound. The first native image then correctly rejected the fixture's use of the generic parser on a sealed launch. The corrected fixture uses the actual typed launch reopener and requires a distinct pointer with identical source/config identities before testing the ownership refusal. The original image/failure and all logs remain retained. Completed causal images and the superseded image were losslessly compressed, round-trip verified and retired with ownership/terminal receipts; the successful baseline image remains available.

The required current-head native build must execute this matrix and all three controls again. This evidence is a bounded Local encrypted component proof, not a complete release verdict.

## docs/OPENVPN-LOCAL-RESTORE-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# OpenVPN Local restore ownership evidence — November 16, 2026

## Corrected behavior

An opaque ordered OpenVPN restore now retains the proved Local winner while
accepting the replacement native endpoint's own connected generation and time.
The shared preparation keeps its original connected observation and exact
engine pointer. A fresh private receipt belongs to the replacement engine;
the old preparation anchor is never reset or overwritten.

The original implementation stored the connected observation only on the
preparation. Opaque restore reuses that preparation, so a new Local endpoint
could fail against the old endpoint's generation or connection time. The older
restore fixture selected Server and returned constant endpoint values, which
did not exercise this Local-owner boundary.

## Authority and lifecycle

The separate `NodeChainOpenVPNExecutionRetiredObserver` contract exposes
`OpenVPNExecutionRetiredContext(context.Context, *NodeChain)` on the original
native engine. During `Run`'s existing bounded context, fresh same-preparation
restoration requires all of the following:

- Positive original shared cleanup, the original opaque capsule, projection,
  identity and retired owner.
- A successful joined retirement observation from the exact old native engine
  and source, repeated around the replacement-owner observation.
- A different engine pointer, EngineID and SessionID, with the replacement's
  exact identity and construction owner rechecked after the second retirement
  callback. A network or owner change during admission refuses the grant.
- An immutable private connection receipt bound to that preparation, native
  engine and captured identity. Later generation, connection time or capacity
  changes fail the selected Local path.

Ordinary runtime construction cannot borrow the original receipt from another
engine, even when the reported values match. An independent Server candidate
continues to use its own proof without requiring a connected Local provider.
Same-Box MTU resume inherits the exact existing receipt; it cannot authorize a
new endpoint generation. The existing mobile restore path still proves its
captured reader/native retirement and creates its separate preparation.

The shared scoring formula, common randomly selected endpoint, caller clocks,
original source/profile ownership and already-proved winner/target restoration
are unchanged. Restore repeats the selected path's proof and probes without
rerunning Compare.

## Executed validation

The unit was tested from source base
`0f07335da94b015fbc73d2581f29cbe16dbc9047` with the pinned Go toolchain,
race instrumentation, `GOMAXPROCS=1`, `-p=1`, `GOMEMLIMIT=900MiB` and `GOGC=50`.
Each run retained source hashes before and after execution. All 73 distinct
records below passed with no skipped tests, race reports or source drift.

| Scope | Passing records |
| --- | ---: |
| Fresh Local restore for 3/4/8 nodes over TCP and UDP | 7 |
| Unjoined/failed native retirement, missing observer, wrong owner/pointer/IDs, late network/owner change, cancellation and deadline refusals | 12 |
| Retained generation/time/capacity and cancelled/held receipt observations | 6 |
| Ordinary construction with equal or different endpoint values on another engine | 3 |
| Same-Box MTU resume with retained or changed generation | 3 |
| Twelve existing scoring, candidate, receipt, mobile/Last, generic/CONNECT/SOCKS/OpenVPN restore and MTU roots | 42 |

The existing two-valid-candidate regression executes the actual shared formula
`(last_node_response_time + external_server_response_time) / 2`, common target,
fresh winner trial and final repeated measurements. All five new roots are
mandatory alongside the original twenty OpenVPN execution roots. The existing
180-second phase and 240-second caller limits remain. The 25 Python inventory,
parser and dispatch controls also passed.

Three separately compiled production omission controls produced their exact
expected test failures: omitting the fresh restore grant reproduced rejection
of a new Local generation; omitting old native retirement admitted an unjoined
owner; omitting the original engine pin allowed another engine to borrow equal
values. Their complete run/failure witnesses and original source hashes are
retained outside the shipping source. The omitted code was never published.

## Preserved initial fixture result

The initial six-case run compiled and passed five leaves. Its four-node TCP
case failed before restoration because the 1 ms/200 ms response-delay setup
actually measured a lower Server average. That complete failed result and its
exact fixture bytes were preserved. Production code was unchanged by the
fixture correction.

The new Local-owner fixture deterministically acquires the real authenticated
Server lease, receives the existing TLS test endpoint's private-address answer,
and requires Compare to reject that response with no score. It requires the
original PUT/DELETE and stopped Manager worker before retaining Local. The
corrected four-node focus passed, followed by the complete six-case matrix.
Existing tests with two eligible candidates remain unchanged.

## Evidence boundary

The tests execute the shared Go implementation, authenticated Manager leases,
HTTP/TLS proof transport, actual Compare, opaque Restore, Last and shared MTU
resume. Native OpenVPN endpoint, routing, Box retirement and reader observations
are explicit boundary models. The new two-owner fixture uses the existing
35-second Last/mobile lifecycle budget; original single-owner fixture clocks
and production deadlines are unchanged.

Native Box/provider integration must implement and verify the retirement
callback against its retained construction and successful original Box.Close
receipt. Native packaged execution, real encrypted provider traffic, device
network behavior and full release acceptance remain separate required evidence.
This unit adds no platform/UI admission and does not close those requirements.

## docs/OPENVPN-MOBILE-BRIDGE-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# OpenVPN mobile execution bridge — 2026-11-16

This change connects the original schema-five OpenVPN execution preparation to
the actual libbox CommandServer factory. It preserves the sealed Local launch,
independent Local and Server credentials, selected execution, saved Last record
and opaque restoration. Android and Apple host admission are separate changes;
this bridge alone does not establish an installed mobile connection.

## Behavior

Distinct OpenVPN constructors parse original Local source or its sealed launch.
The execution envelope has its own kind and retains the complete preparation,
source/configuration digests and optional Last record. Reopening requires an
exact canonical envelope. Generic execution parsing remains closed to this
family.

The factory creates the exact caller-local outbound registry and supplies the
original preparation to the native Box context. Bind and subsequent native
readback require the same plan, preparation, source, CommandServer,
StartedService, registry and private owner. An ordinary server, equal-content
replacement or copied owner cannot satisfy that association. A second factory
reservation is refused, including conflicting CONNECT or SOCKS ownership.

Opaque restoration selects the original OpenVPN policy from the retained
restore owner. It does not rerun Compare or replace the already proved choice.
The native retirement checks published in the preceding source unit remain in
force.

Both mobile build paths select the new bridge tests with the concrete OpenVPN
build tag. The mandatory bridge gate includes the new factory omission. All
three bridge validation scripts now participate in the mobile and desktop
runtime cache input closures. A validation-only change cannot retain the prior
runtime evidence identity.

## Executed evidence

Validation used base `3f1b4fc33f765967e33102d3e6448e5f39a566f5`, the actual
mobile-prepared core pinned to `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`
and Go 1.26.3:

- The complete current libbox/daemon execution inventory passed under race
  instrumentation: 47 roots and 164 required records. This includes the new
  OpenVPN source and factory cases as well as the existing native lifecycle,
  measurement, MTU, saved Last and restoration cases.
- One independently compiled omission removed the original OpenVPN factory
  check. Its exact ordinary-server leaf failed with the required witness,
  `ordinary service borrowed typed factory`. A build failure, timeout, panic
  or race report would not count as the expected refusal.
- Seventeen preparation controls and seventeen runner controls passed. The
  preparation checks verify actual mobile and desktop input-closure membership.
- The actual mobile preparation completed after the cache-input correction.
  All captured source inputs and the prepared core remained unchanged during
  the compiled gate.

The cache-closure regression initially failed because the validation scripts
were absent from the input lists. Adding the missing entries closed that
specific failure; the failed source-phase logs were retained. No production
ownership or rejection rule was weakened.

Prepared-core source identity:
`3972a223412736891ceff5ebfafd9f5fff790bd6d692bb4160335e913b1ce659`.
Source manifest SHA-256:
`9285bb43ffc897a9669c8793ff5ead1957ba42559419039362636e6c9867925c`.
Compiled validation receipt SHA-256:
`60b9f01763a4cc51fbba98fbedc433f15fce48e20ac7d434d5d03546e38023b8`.
Positive log SHA-256:
`ee32919f4728153937f51c2e202a76ce540646bb2d093d9599216dbf5e8d7074`.
New omission log SHA-256:
`f924d9b0cdf0349f5d3a2da750bded51272158653b9015aa547cfb50386a6fdb`.

## Boundaries

The bridge tests exercise actual shared preparation and CommandServer
construction with explicit platform/runtime component fixtures. They do not
start an encrypted OpenVPN provider or a device tunnel. Previous compiled
omissions remain mandatory in the shipping gate but were not all rerun in this
focused local validation. Generated mobile bindings, app-host compilation,
exact-SHA release, signing, private deployment and physical-device acceptance
require their own evidence.

## Mobile source boundary follow-up

The mobile CommandServer factory now accepts only the canonical schema-five
preparation with its four original fields: `schema`, `execution`,
`local_source`, and `openvpn_local_launch`. Desktop preparations require their
separate physical-owner factory. The check runs at factory construction; it
does not add repeated JSON parsing to live native readback.

The exact added production and test templates were compiled with the actual
pinned libbox, concrete OpenVPN build tag and race instrumentation. The full
compile took 554.785 seconds within its unchanged 600-second limit. Three
OpenVPN roots passed all 23 records; both adjacent CONNECT/SOCKS original
factory roots passed all 18 records. Two separately compiled omissions failed
at their intended witnesses: an ordinary server borrowing the typed factory,
and a desktop field entering the mobile-only factory. Neither timeout nor
compilation failure counted as a successful negative control.

These tests used the native adapter at `3b605c0c` plus the additive bridge.
The published `77ab253b` production templates match those validated templates
after this boundary addition. Its later cache-input closure and preparation
regression are preserved; all 17 current preparation and 17 runner controls
passed after composition. The race-test image SHA-256 is
`65b1eee981da0777f915948a7871d7eb1f7e2fcc453129418822137e68236d2c`.
The positive OpenVPN log SHA-256 is
`6eec43739c78a6188bf5cecb3c2bf4bbffb2cb7ddbcfbc6f1b95e8cb08256ae7`;
the compiled boundary omission log SHA-256 is
`ea27208bef83a508ee26a9aa359a10626cdeafa1a311b323c961929cde251d63`.
This remains source/ABI/ownership evidence, not an encrypted Local tunnel or
physical-device acceptance result.

## docs/OPENVPN-NATIVE-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Original native OpenVPN execution owner — 2026-11-16

This change supplies the actual Box owner for schema-five ordered OpenVPN
Local/Server/Compare preparation. It retains the original preparation, sealed
Local source, endpoint manager, concrete OpenVPN endpoint and native close
receipt. It does not add the separate libbox, Android, Apple or desktop host
admission paths, or establish installed-device/network acceptance.

## Behavior

The engine captures construction ownership before any endpoint starts. An
independently authenticated Server candidate does not require the unused Local
provider to connect. A Local observation must read the original endpoint's
connected generation, time, remote tuple and capacity. Once pinned, a different
observation or a later pending state is rejected.

Public-path measurements use a dedicated typed dispatcher. Server measurements
travel through the original authenticated Server front; Local measurements
retain the selected resolver and original OpenVPN endpoint. Generic imported
measurement admission remains closed to this family. Saved Last preparation
keeps authenticated discovery and the same native owner. MTU reader changes keep
the original OpenVPN construction and captured connected receipt.

Restoration's retirement observer requires the original retired engine, closed
and drained application gate, and the captured Box close receipt joined against
that exact Box. An early done channel or another equal-content source cannot
replace completed original retirement.

The concrete OpenVPN import lives behind `with_openvpn`. The actual mobile
build without that tag compiles its refusing stub. Both mobile packaging paths
run the new positive, stub and compiled omission gate. Desktop runs the tagged
gate, because its additional endpoint fixtures require the shipped OpenVPN
implementation. Existing mandatory gates remain in place.

Both new validation scripts participate in the mobile and desktop cache input
closures. A validation-script-only change therefore invalidates reused runtime
evidence.

## Executed validation and identities

The complete new gate ran on base
`b0f13141a21a750aeeeed21efb06411c2ba59c8b`, using the actual mobile-prepared
core pinned to `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`, Go 1.26.3 and race
instrumentation:

- One shared fixture-generation root, seven records, producing independent
  private Local/Server sources for 3-, 4- and 8-node TCP and UDP chains.
- Seven actual native Box roots, 56 records: original construction, foreign
  ownership refusal, connected-receipt retention, Last, MTU, joined retirement
  and authenticated Server public dispatch.
- One separate actual build without `with_openvpn`, one refusing-stub record.
- Five independently compiled omissions, each rejected at its named witness:
  original construction identity; pinned connected receipt; pending after the
  first connection; original native close join; typed public dispatch.
- Eleven validation-runner controls, eighteen preparation controls and fourteen
  unchanged previous-runner controls passed. There were no skipped required
  records, race reports or source changes during the successful new gate.

The first native run compiled but exposed a fixture error: discovery and saved
Last records used the generic fixture's peer address instead of the captured
OpenVPN entry's address. The corrected test helper reads the exact original
entry configuration. Production peer validation was not relaxed. The failed
run and its source snapshot were retained separately.

New-gate source identity:
`2bf954af9e91d8c36a256310811f67239157be48679644b5b60a1e4d84f49b3f`.
New-gate receipt SHA-256:
`ff0383e014c10813026cb7df1a4a2fdafff670413af19d05805d56f2919b1293`.
Complete successful new-gate log SHA-256:
`1380342a514eda5d99b603130017e784fa1324776b06e5109a373797cf0eafa9`.

The disjoint upstream whitening change at
`473b600727c87b944cb9233a27043ca239433745` was preserved. After refreshing
the actual prepared core with that source, the unchanged previous native gate's
44-root, 181-record positive inventory passed under race instrumentation.
This checks the corrected discovery/Last fixture across the existing graph
families and compiles the composed native package. Its previous omission
controls were not rerun. Original per-package and outer bounds were unchanged.

Composed positive source identity:
`f149f72fee9f5d4124ed12898634627ae8486d100aa9024a5fc94f382020c94c`.
Composed positive receipt SHA-256:
`51313374ba0839c2c581549ccc0ee45b4dfe66973d1ec63701c155b2965fc0d3`.
Composed positive log SHA-256:
`fa0e27ef243634ab1272fb038b274df09f39be2f543410efa4cb34e040228b98`.

## Evidence boundaries

The tests use the actual endpoint class, managers, selector, gate, shared
discovery and native close code. The OpenVPN endpoint remains unstarted;
connected observation equality inputs, TUN and packet-prefix I/O are explicit
component fixtures. These results do not establish a successful encrypted
Local connection, privileged routing, native mobile host admission, release
publication, signing, private deployment or physical-device acceptance.

## docs/OPENVPN-ORDERED-CLIENT-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Typed ordered OpenVPN client execution foundation — 2026-11-16

This unit implements the shared schema-5 Server/Compare execution and restoration boundary for recovered requirements 214–224 and addendum 336. It builds on the independently paired ordered OpenVPN Server compiler published in `2fb8f5f76a4a60ff11570cd0676ff89a3666d269`. The eligible graph remains one to six original WG/AWG packet prefixes, an imported literal TCP/UDP OpenVPN penultimate member, and an independently paired SS2022 final member, including its original Off/AES/AES+XOR layer. Ordinary paired OpenVPN and schema 1–4 entry points retain their existing boundaries.

## Original source and typed API

`NewNodeChainOpenVPNExecutionPreparation` accepts the original captured `*NodeChain`; `ParseNodeChainOpenVPNExecutionPreparation` reopens its canonical typed preparation. The schema-5 preparation has exactly `schema`, `execution`, `local_source`, and `openvpn_local_launch`. The last field retains the complete original sealed Local launch, including an existing iOS/Android projection. A generic source or preparation parser cannot admit this family. `OriginalOpenVPNSource` returns only the validated original pointer. `NewNodeChainOpenVPNExecutionPolicy` requires that same preparation and the independently paired Server descriptor.

The private binding compares its exact preparation/source/middle pointers, current source seal, original sealed launch and every retained immutable pair/capture field. It freshly validates the complete original source on every observation. `NodeChainOpenVPNExecutionObserver` and `NodeChainOpenVPNEngine` remain separate required native interfaces. The owner observer validates the original construction even while the Local provider is pending or has refused authentication. Only a Local trial additionally requires and retains its actual connected remote/MTU observation; later changes are refused, and a blocked receipt lock retains the caller's deadline. Entry discovery and a Server trial retain native construction ownership without depending on unrelated Local authentication. Server still requires its independent authenticated lease and original public/final/DNS proofs. No immutable input seal replaces a live endpoint observation.

Discovery preserves original global packet positions and independent Server source identities. Local and Server middle hashes intentionally differ and are joined only by the canonical provider-pair digest. Shared proof, selected DNS, comparison target/mean, original lease, failed cleanup, selected hop measurement, Last and mobile restore retain their existing ownership checks. Mobile restore changes only the issued original TUN readback through its opaque process-owned capsule; it requires original shared/native retirement and fresh discovery before adoption. It cannot be reconstructed from JSON or a caller-provided MTU.

## Bounded executable evidence

The final frozen v7 source base is published `af28f808a2be989cfcb2d124f2541670a0df791a` plus this unit. This includes the independently owned three-file OpenVPN source-receipt repair, manifest SHA256 `05d83f4279b0a0cb90689a0f7f086e3a2affc663907aa79a8488336087b1783b`. That dependency preserves member, policy, proof and sealed-source checks while avoiding repeated canonical parsing of the exact immutable source. Its independent 252-record and three-control evidence is recorded in its own published unit.

The shared test image was compiled with `go test -race -c -p=1 -mod=readonly ./internal/mobilemultihop`, with the original 300-second outer compile bound, one compiler CPU and 800 MiB memory target. Runtime used two CPUs and the original 120-second root/135-second wrapper bounds. Individual 10-second lifecycle, 20-second complete-prefix lifecycle and 35-second mobile/Last bounds were unchanged.

* All 20 required new roots passed on the final v7 image: **137 Go test records**, with no skips or race report. They cover all six prefix counts in WG/Fast/Strong, both OpenVPN transports, independent final layers, original sealed mobile projection, changed source/pair/config/proof/connected owner, caller-bounded held receipt, actual authenticated discovery and Manager leases, Server/Compare, failed score, fresh winner, retained-choice restore, Last, four/eight-node selected measurement, failed original Stop and Android/iOS opaque MTU restore. Ten records specifically prove independent candidate authentication: discovery/Server with pending Local state, Compare with failed/lost Local state, failed Server proof refusal, and Local-winner connected receipt replacement refusal.
* **74 existing-family records passed on v6** (all relevant schema 1–4 and MTU branches are byte-identical in v7): 53 ordinary/schema-2/CONNECT/SOCKS preparation, identity and runtime/restore records, plus 21 existing CONNECT/SOCKS mobile-MTU and ordinary MTU-bound/forgery records.
* The v5 image SHA256 is `8f582558149b060f8f42fb1b437d296a979290b40fbbf31cb4b5b7693fc0aa30`, compiled in 44.801 seconds. It passed 124 of the final required records. Its mobile restore root correctly exposed a missing typed OpenVPN branch in `NodeChainExecutionProjection.MTUPlan`: the restored TUN was compared with the original MTU configuration after successful fresh discovery.
* The one-line correction routes the existing opaque OpenVPN mobile capsule through the same configured-prefix/TUN comparison already used by CONNECT/SOCKS. The v6 image SHA256 is `584ef098888259c4280de0c5974ee3ee2a8a2bdff1dc9f080b41d4cd74369780`, compiled in 41.426 seconds. It passed all three corrected mobile records in 24.772 seconds aggregate, plus the 74 adjacent-family records. The other 124 new records use byte-identical v5 source outside that exclusively mobile branch.
* The original 10-second Compare-to-restore witness failed on earlier images (10.589 seconds on current public-point-cache source). Fresh immutable binding checks, the original-source receipt, and dispatching only the required OpenVPN observer removed repeated source work without caching live validity. The unchanged witness passed in 7.420 seconds on v5 and 8.530 seconds on v6. Complete four/eight-node Server/Compare plus selected measurement and joined Stop passed five records in 37.844 seconds aggregate under each original 20-second lifecycle bound.

The final v7 image SHA256 is `ce5db2310fe9ed23ca89ba441387766b826890b5d3805a0a9380a6002f24040a`; it compiled in 44.660 seconds with source hashes unchanged. All 20 required roots/137 records were rerun on that single image after the candidate-independence correction. The original 10-second restore witness passed in 9.535 seconds total wrapper time; the two original 35-second mobile restore cases passed in 29.155 seconds aggregate. Complete four/eight-node runtime/measurement/Stop passed in 30.184 seconds aggregate. No deadline was increased.

The hop-measurement package passed **18 race-enabled records**, including all six original positions/roles, invalid source/role/position refusals, actual 50 original HTTPS responses and rejection of a failed fiftieth response. Its image SHA256 is `83e17254359f606f0180f268ad34923a247c91f5d94df6372a5bc2ce62b36c2f`; compile took 4.734 seconds and execution 1.653 seconds.

The mandatory group runner registers all 20 roots explicitly, each in its original bounded phase. All **25 runner controls** passed. Inventory from the actual immutable v7 image found 1,000 shared package roots and verified that every one of the 20 new required roots is present in exactly its assigned phase. Omission/overlap/required-inventory checks remain in force.

## Evidence boundary and follow-on

These tests execute the actual shared compiler, strict parser, discovery, authenticated loopback HTTP/TLS proof/Manager path, comparison, measurement facade, restore and cleanup state machines. Native OpenVPN endpoint, OS TUN/readback and remote worker boundaries are explicit doubles in this shared suite. The separate Server native unit provides its own real encrypted component evidence. This unit does not claim actual native client Box adoption, installed Linux/Windows/macOS acceptance, mobile-device acceptance, or deployment.

The native client Box adapter and the distinct libbox typed factory/bridge remain separate required work. Android, Apple and desktop public adoption must keep their own original physical/runtime owners and acceptance gates; widening a product guard alone is insufficient. The uncompiled libbox bridge draft is intentionally excluded from this shared source commit.

Reproducible local evidence is retained in `openvpn-prefix-validation`: exact per-file source manifests, v5/v6 compile receipts, test2json logs, the failed intermediate witnesses, `client-shared-validation-summary-v7.json`, `client-v6-mtu-restore-tests.json` and `client-v6-compatibility-tests.json`. `openvpn-client-consumer-snapshot-af28-v7/manifest.json` freezes the source-consumer snapshot (SHA256 `37610aab926ccec249015eaef9d23db7fd3fb125970063d9eb5d92442e35f0e6`); its status explicitly distinguishes the still-uncompiled bridge.

## docs/OPENVPN-ORDERED-EXECUTION-FOUNDATION-2026-11-16.md

# Ordered OpenVPN Server/Compare foundation — November 16, 2026

This implementation lane continues original requirements 214–224, addendum
336, and the recovered August 13/14 custom-node, hop and exit requests. It
extends the existing Local schema-five family; it does not replace the
separate already-shipping paired external OpenVPN execution path.

The source baseline is `9ff381aa543b9509fb13e7aa80c70583472e5e73`.
This unit implements the shared Server, private operator plan and provisioning
boundary. Typed client execution, native Server construction and host adoption
remain separate follow-on units. No native, installed, package or physical
acceptance follows from this source validation.

## Source and ownership boundary

The existing family is one through six original paired WG/AWG packet prefixes,
one literal TCP/UDP imported OpenVPN penultimate member, and an independent
paired SS2022 final. Existing final Off, AES and AES/XOR closures remain intact.
These are implementation bounds, not a recovered user-approved hop maximum.

The shared/server lane owns schema-five policy, canonical pairing identity,
typed execution, relay descriptor/request validation and operator provisioning.
The native Server lane separately owns the original OpenVPN endpoint observer,
worker dispatch and actual native component fixture. Public desktop, Android
and Apple admission remains closed until its own typed runtime adoption passes.
No generic parser may infer OpenVPN execution authority from a schema number.

## Private pairing and public discovery

Only typed schema-five `NodeChainServerSource` accepts `openvpn_pairing`:

```text
private_key: the Server role's dedicated pairing key
local: canonical nativeopenvpn.PublicDescriptor for the Local provider
server: canonical nativeopenvpn.PublicDescriptor for the Server provider
```

The Server's original imported member contains its own provider profile and
credentials. Its pairing descriptor must match `DescribePeer` of that exact
profile/key. Existing `ValidatePublicPair` rejects shared provider certificate
keys, common names or usernames and unequal transport policy. The Local source
uses its existing `external.openvpn_pairing`; neither private role is copied
into the opposite compiled graph. Public discovery carries only the canonical
domain-separated `openvpn_pair_sha256`, never the private descriptors' provider
names, passwords, certificate keys or pairing keys.

## Native API

The distinct source constructors are:

```go
NewNodeChainOpenVPNServerPolicy(source string) (*NodeChainServerPolicy, error)
NewNodeChainOpenVPNServerCarrierContext(ctx, lifetime context.Context, source string) (*NodeChainServerCarrier, error)
```

One fresh `ReadOpenVPNServerCapture(capture)` returns the exact configuration
and `NodeChainOpenVPNServerCapture`: its original binding, configured middle
capture, device policy and an independent copy of the proof inventory.
`Middle.ConfiguredMTU` is a demanded ceiling; it is never a connected native
capacity receipt.

The native owner retains the original policy pointer, Box, endpoint manager,
actual `*openvpn.ClientEndpoint`, configuration and connected
`nativeopenvpn.PacketObservation`. Before and after proof, DNS and traffic it
must observe the original remote, generation, connection time and packet
capacity. A configured value, equivalent reconstructed endpoint, replacement
connection or restored numeric generation cannot authorize the owner.

Shared Server proof additionally requires the existing
`NodeChainOpenVPNEngine.OpenVPNMiddleStateContext` method and keeps the first
valid observation unchanged throughout that proof. Native cancellation and
joined Box retirement retain the original lifetime/cleanup contracts.

## Mobile handoff contract

The planned distinct `RouterNodeChainOpenVPNExecutionFromLaunch` and
`RouterNodeChainOpenVPNExecutionFromLast` factories consume the existing sealed
schema-five Local `routervpn-node-chain-launch-input`. Hosts must not strip
its receipt into a reconstructed plain source. The typed preparation kind is
`routervpn-node-chain-openvpn-execution-preparation-input`; generic execution
factories remain closed to schema five.

## Validation record

The final shared source was frozen at the above baseline plus this unit and
compiled with Go 1.26.3, `go test -race -c -p=1 -mod=readonly
./internal/mobilemultihop`. Compilation passed in 43.910 seconds under the
original 300-second wrapper. The image SHA-256 was
`1c06bb68550c080c1afe6ffe2b9d77cc2dfe8baac67a110bbcf01b4fde19eb13`.
A file hash manifest confirmed unchanged compiled source.

Each root ran from that immutable race image with the original 120-second Go
and 135-second external limits, two runtime CPUs, and no skipped tests:

| `TestNodeChainOpenVPNServerRefusesForeignAuthority` | 17 |

| Total | 95 |

The longest root took 21.544 seconds. All six roots are explicit required
members of the existing shared CI core group in a disjoint bounded phase.
The grouping runner's 24 executable self-tests passed, including removal of
each new required root. Existing whole-root batching and deadlines remain.

`go test -race -p=1 -mod=readonly -count=1 -json -timeout=120s
./internal/multihoprelay
-run='^TestNodeChain(OpenVPN|CONNECT|SOCKS|Imported).*Capture'` passed all 263
run/pass records in 10.508 seconds. This includes schema-five source/request
domains, all original packet positions, pair-digest ownership, rejected family
substitution, and existing SS/SOCKS/CONNECT capture regressions.

`python deploy/test_server_node_chain_pairing.py` passed all 33 required
transaction tests in 80.574 seconds. It built the real agent, generated 33
original source fixtures and 29 complete private bundle plans, checked all 34
original private-reader records, and retained all 20 Go-generated digest
vectors. The six OpenVPN fixtures cover TCP/UDP, WG/Fast/Strong, short/long
prefixes and independent final Off/AES/AES+XOR layers. Existing 31 transaction
tests remain; two new methods exercise actual schema-five source publication,

The installed command is redirected only to that freshly built actual agent.
No native offline checker was configured for this local run.

`python deploy/test_server_node_chain_preparation.py` passed its nine exact

The reusable test helper `nodeChainOpenVPNServerPlanFixture` returns the

Earlier bounded evidence is retained separately: the first 36-case source run

compile found and removed one unused test import. The first operator run's two

No failed result is counted as successful evidence.

The connected proof fixture doubles only endpoint-state observation and native
routing/DNS ownership. It executes real authenticated HTTP, verified public
TLS, exact response parsing and joined connection close through shipping
`NodeChainServerPolicy.Prove`. It does not establish an encrypted OpenVPN
connection. Native provider authentication, original Box/endpoint ownership,
full joined retirement, installed host behavior, exact-SHA release and private
physical acceptance remain mandatory and are not marked complete by this unit.

## Operator file contract

A schema-five file plan uses an explicit `openvpn` row at the original
penultimate position. It names `local_capture_file`, `server_capture_file` and
`pairing_file`. The first two contain the exact original `config` and `capture`
strings. The last contains only the Server dedicated key and the two canonical
public pairing descriptors. Each file uses the existing owned regular mode-0600
snapshot and total source byte bound; every original inode and byte sequence is
rechecked before publication. Schemas one through four retain their existing
forms and reject the schema-five private pairing field, including null.

## docs/OPENVPN-ORIGINAL-SOURCE-OBSERVATION-2026-11-16.md

# Preserve original OpenVPN source observations — 2026-11-16

Repeated OpenVPN execution observations were reparsing the same complete
schema-five JSON source after the original constructor had already validated
and retained it. A profiled shared execution path identified this parsing in
`NodeChain.validateCapture` among the remaining costs of bounded restoration.

This change follows the existing schema-two imported-source receipt pattern.
The schema-five constructor now retains its exact canonical source, original
middle input, bounded member count, and private chain/member/policy/node owners.
Every observation freshly compares those values and the receipt's own identity
and seal. It therefore does not need to deserialize those unchanged source bytes
again merely to recover their already captured schema, count and middle slot.

The constructor still performs its full source parsing, schema and identity
validation, independent packet-key checks and complete graph compilation before
issuing the receipt. Authorized projections still use that constructor and
receive distinct owners. The source is never reconstructed from a selected node
name or from a copied receipt.

## Checks that remain fresh

`validateCapture` still checks the complete chain seal, every original paired
node, the imported OpenVPN member, its `ExternalPolicy.DesktopCapture`, and the
complete proof positions, identities and roles. Connected remote, generation,
capacity, cancellation, DNS and runtime proof checks are unchanged. The receipt
stores source provenance; it stores no reusable validation or connection result.

The additional exact policy-pointer check also refuses a same-content replacement
of the original imported member's policy. A replacement receipt cannot borrow
authority by copying its fields because the receipt retains its own pointer.

## Executable verification

Go 1.26.3 race-instrumented validation completed all 15 selected OpenVPN test
roots, covering **252 distinct root/subtest records**. This includes:

- 56 new cases spanning minimum/maximum route length and TCP/UDP. They reject
  copied or foreign receipts, altered and resealed sources, changed member,
  policy and prefix owners, bad counts, missing receipts, changed private
  selection/policy, and altered proof roles.
- Fresh projection owners for Linux, Windows, macOS, Android and iOS.
- Every existing one-to-six-prefix WireGuard, AWG Fast and AWG Strong source and
  projection case over both OpenVPN transports.
- Existing generation/capacity, source-substitution, independent Local/Server
  pairing, Server proof, and role-substitution checks.

The first combined local invocation exhausted its **120-second aggregate test
budget** while iterating the existing projection matrix. That failure is retained
as evidence, not relabeled as a successful aggregate. Its two completed roots
were preserved. The same immutable race binary then completed every remaining
root in 18 bounded groups; the full prefix matrix was partitioned by original
mode and transport, with all six counts required in each group. Each group kept
the 120-second test limit. No source, case, production deadline or probe count
changed to complete this inventory.

Three separately compiled defects were rejected at their intended witnesses:
removing the receipt-self check, the original-source comparison, or the original
policy-pointer comparison. Their compile/run durations were 26.24, 26.60 and
24.46 seconds under the existing 30-second test/90-second process limits.

## Evidence identity and limits

The positive race image was compiled against `0b47442293da2bded515fbcfce975ad5a16d0151`
plus the three changed Go files. Subsequent unrelated REALITY work in
`75d012763f27634c52621ac21e0bde1ae99bc51a` was preserved; the three negative-control
builds also compiled that newer package source. The three OpenVPN file hashes
remained unchanged throughout the positive and negative checks.

- Race image SHA-256:
  `d67cd8d5193f9045fe413d18a1b26ebf8a05ca982305d852272588e476549725`.
- Retained initial aggregate log SHA-256:
  `4b368e5ead5d9e5e19d1372026f96e04824b3b6dd55d8c2f88818c12967115b6`.
- Original-source owner implementation SHA-256:
  `e3012352bc34e6083f4fb4b90d094081cae102e317e757689730ebda8d373d1f`.
- New source-observation tests SHA-256:
  `7969d5dc110e987ac201ab13672c3a7a2c2aabfa737dd0c70fb8389f1b1f83d4`.

This is an independently tested source-observation repair. It does not establish
completed native OpenVPN Server/Compare client integration, an exact-SHA release,
private deployment, or physical-device acceptance. Those remain separate gates.

## docs/OPENVPN-SERVER-NATIVE-OWNER-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Original OpenVPN Server endpoint ownership

The schema-five Server worker now constructs the distinct OpenVPN Server policy and carrier. After its original Box starts, the worker retains the concrete `openvpn.ClientEndpoint` from that Box's original outbound manager. It retains the exact source/configuration binding and first authenticated packet observation: provider remote, generation, connection time and packet capacity. Configuration does not grant connected authority.

Each proof/current read checks the original worker, policy, Box, output inventory, concrete endpoint and fresh sealed Server capture before and after the endpoint observation. The first observed capacity must meet the captured configured ceiling. A replacement owner, reconnect, changed capacity, foreign source, cancellation or expired caller refuses authority. Existing work/transport lifetime separation and strict joined Box/carrier retirement remain unchanged.

The mandatory native Linux release path now executes `deploy/test_server_node_chain_openvpn_native.py` on both release architectures. Its 17 separately bounded actual component cases cover TCP and UDP providers, one and five remote packet prefixes, WireGuard and both AmneziaWG modes, final Off/AES/AES+XOR, independently issued provider credentials, wrong authentication, reconnect and wrong final-layer keys. Existing Server command and retirement roots remain required; the new immutable packet receipt root adds 17 leaves without removing prior inventory.

The component gate uses the actual native Box, WireGuard/AmneziaWG endpoints, concrete OpenVPN client, TLS/authentication provider, SS2022 final, selected DNS and shipping Server proof. The shipping OpenVPN owner helper and lifetime methods are projected byte-for-byte apart from the Go package declaration. Only command-container fields and first physical UDP tuple mapping are component fixtures. This is not installed carrier, TUN, firewall or host acceptance, and does not claim the zero-remote-prefix boundary.

Local native Linux/amd64 race validation on the frozen c36b5e35 source plus this unit passed all 17 cases, with exactly 34 root/leaf verdicts. The baseline image compiled in 242.122 seconds under its original 300-second limit. The longest case completed in 10.409 seconds under its original 60-second Go / 75-second wrapper limits. Three separately compiled source defects—foreign outbound map, copied original owner, and replaced connected receipt—each failed the real network fixture with its precise required assertion and joined cleanup. The gate retained exact source, projection, environment and image identity throughout.

The independent Local/Server certificate fixture uses two genuinely different client certificates and account credentials issued by the same original provider authority. The shorter configured MTU is applied consistently to both paired source roles while the actual provider packet capacity remains independent. Wrong credentials and wrong final-layer keys continue to fail real authentication/encrypted proof; no production validator or failure assertion was relaxed.

The complete shipping `cmd/sing-box` race image compiled on the frozen 75d01276 source plus this unit in 121.794 seconds under its original 600-second limit. All 23 original/new Server roots and 120 required records passed in 9.995 seconds under the unchanged 90-second Go limit. Two separately compiled worker defects (receipt replacement and under-capacity admission) failed their exact required assertions. All 37 runner/preparation tests passed, including the four existing compiled lifecycle controls and all omission/refusal checks. The existing 12 other worker mutation controls remain required by the shipping Docker gate.

The publication source also preserves the subsequently published source-owner observation receipt and fixed peer diagnostics. The OpenVPN production helper, native adoption and provider/component test bodies retain their tested bytes. The evidence above retains its recorded source identities; it is not relabeled as a new hosted run. Hosted native release and installed carrier acceptance remain required.
