## docs/ORDERED-PREFIX-CANDIDATE-NATIVE-EVIDENCE.md

# Encrypted retained-prefix candidate timing evidence

`TestRouterVPNDesktopNodeChainEncryptedPrefixCandidates` supplies the mandatory
encrypted transport witness for prospective candidate TCP timing. It uses the
shared `hopmeasure.NewPrefixCandidatesContext` service with an explicit native
adapter boundary, real native WG/AWG outbounds, and independent reference
peers. It does not instantiate a system TUN or substitute for the production
host mailbox, catalog capture, or full `NodeChain` readiness tests.

## Original source and retained transport

Each of the WG, AWG Fast and AWG Strong cases creates two independent paired
packet prefixes and an original SS2022 final source. The candidate is that
source's exact literal endpoint, `10.77.0.1:8388`, measured through retained
prefix position 1. The compiler validates the complete candidate inventory;
its original source digest and capture digest identify the result. The test
checks the endpoint and original credential against the compiled source.

All measurements in one case reuse the same native outbound instances. The
adapter boundary checks their captured pointer and original source/config
identity, observes the complete captured inventory, and authenticates the
original prefix's private health service through that retained outbound.
These are real encrypted network observations. The adapter's activity hold is
an explicit fixture boundary; real host MTU exclusion and system-network
ownership remain responsibilities of the separate native host integration.

A successful result requires fifty fresh TCP connections and fifty closes,
fifty accepted connections at the independent SS listener, and traffic through
the original encrypted entry. The service must report fifty samples of
`routed-tcp-connect`, with a positive trimmed mean and the exact candidate
source/capture digests. No SS plaintext or final private API request may occur.
The result therefore proves TCP reachability and connection timing, not SS
credential validity, application readiness, ICMP latency or final-node proof.
Private endpoints and credentials must not appear in the public receipt.

## Required negative controls

The 28-verdict inventory includes each of the three transport cases and these
checks per case:

- Fifty original destination handshakes and the complete scored receipt.
- Rejection of an uncaptured candidate, outbound tag or endpoint, and of a
  lazy SS prefix as a destination-handshake timing capability.
- A legitimately recompiled changed SS credential source, retaining the same
  profile ID and catalog revision, invalidates the old result after one real
  connection. Restoring the old source cannot revive that capture.
- Replacing the retained prefix owner invalidates the result after one real
  connection; changing back cannot revive it.
- Cancellation while closing an actual native connection retains the original
  activity hold. A bounded close timeout cannot claim drain; releasing and
  joining that original close releases the hold once and publishes no score.
- A wrong live authenticated private node proof produces no candidate score.
- Closing the actual SS destination listener refuses the candidate connection
  and produces no partial or zero-valued score.
- Closing the retained encrypted prefix's UDP bind prevents a score and does
  not fall back to another prefix or an ambient connection.

The Linux native-host runner executes this witness before its separate
installed namespace/TUN scenarios and records its output digest. Missing,
duplicated, skipped, failed, panicking or race-reporting verdicts fail the gate.

## Causal reference-fixture correction

The first execution of the listener-refusal control failed for all three
transport modes. The independent SS listener had been closed and accepted no
new connections, but the old reference handler still allowed fifty successful
virtual TCP handshakes and a scored receipt.

The cause was specific to the pinned reference forwarding adapter:
`desktopReferenceHandler.NewConnectionEx` called `SetDeadline` on a gVisor
`gLazyConn` before dialing its configured physical target. In the pinned
implementation, `gLazyConn.SetDeadline` calls `HandshakeContext`, so this
operation completed the virtual destination handshake before actual target
acceptance. It could therefore measure the forwarding stack alone.

The reference handler now attempts the exact target connection first and sets
the downstream deadline only after success. On target refusal, existing owned
cleanup closes the still-lazy connection, which rejects its pending handshake.
The echo-only branch retains its own immediate deadline. No production
candidate, WG/AWG, SS or system-network policy is weakened or changed.

This fixture uses TCP forwarding to connect an isolated virtual endpoint to
its original independent loopback listener. Its destination-handshake witness
therefore includes that reference forwarding mechanism. It establishes that
actual target acceptance precedes the successful virtual handshake in this
fixture; it does not claim that an arbitrary remote deployment has no
intermediate TCP-terminating proxies. The permanently required listener-loss
control rejects the premature-handshake behavior observed above.


---

## docs/ORDERED-PUBLIC-POINT-VALIDATION-2026-11-16.md

# Bound repeated public-point validation — 2026-11-16

The actual encrypted Server jobs for `17092d376b2708caa5c1ccbe5493ce617b703989`
(run `43806025753`) passed the one-prefix WireGuard and AmneziaWG cases, then
failed `http-connect/prefix-max-five` under the existing 15-second readiness
window on both Linux ARM64 and AMD64. All five private prefix identities had
been proved. The ARM64 snapshot had not reached CONNECT; AMD64 had opened its
stream without completing the public proof. These are later failures than the
already-fixed transport retirement and AWG receipt encoding issues.

## Observed cause and change

A race-instrumented component using the real five-prefix WireGuard Server Box,
CONNECT provider and Shadowsocks final spent 4,863 ms on 20 ordinary native
capture observations. Its CPU profile identified repeated fixed-scalar X25519
public-point checks through descriptor, request and capture validation. These
checks repeated for the same immutable public bytes during each observation.

`nodeChainPacketPublic` now retains up to 256 successful public-point results,
keyed by the complete copied 32 bytes. Canonical base64, public-field encoding,
and high-bit alias checks still run **before every lookup**. Every miss runs
the exact previous X25519 `NewPublicKey` / fixed scalar `NewPrivateKey` / `ECDH`
sequence and clears the temporary shared value. Failed checks are never cached.
A short mutex protects bounded FIFO insertion and lookup; cryptography runs
outside the mutex, and concurrent misses are checked again before insertion.

The cache stores only a mathematical property of an immutable public point.
It stores no credential, descriptor, session, lease, policy, network observation,
DNS result, connection proof, or execution choice. All corresponding freshness,
identity, duplicate-owner and teardown checks continue to execute. No deadline,
probe count, network policy, public-key alias rule, or negative control changed.

## Verification

The exact source passed 643 selected Go race-instrumented test records, with
zero failures or skips. These include the new warm-cache/cold-cache equivalence,
low-order and malformed encoding cases, bounded eviction and concurrent use;
the existing complete ordered capture and packet-prefix identity matrices; and
external packet wrong-owner/key/alias and concurrent canonical-owner cases.
The Go execution took 7.946 seconds under the original 120-second bound;
compile plus execution took 12.119 seconds under the 180-second outer bound.

Three separately compiled source mutations failed at their intended witnesses:

- Removing the canonical check admitted a newline alias through an already
  warmed entry and failed `TestNodeChainPacketPublicMemoCanonicalCacheHit`.
- Skipping the original ECDH miss check failed the comparison with the complete
  previous canonical/ECDH validator on a rejected point.
- Removing bounded eviction failed the fixed-memory and revalidation test.

The same actual five-prefix native component was rebuilt with only the point
cache substituted into its original shared implementation. The previous ECDH
body was checked byte-for-byte. Both builds used the same original 300-second
compiler bound; both component runs retained their existing two-second proof
request and 85/90-second test/process bounds.

| Actual component observation | Before | With bounded point cache |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| 20 native capture observations | 4,863 ms | 683 ms |
| Five private prefix responses | 176–197 ms each | 32–42 ms each |
| CONNECT public proof | Failed its 2-second request bound | 372 ms |
| Final private node proof | Not reached | 233 ms |
| Selected DNS through final SS UoT | Not reached | Passed |
| Original captured stream/packet shutdown | No positive completion | All original owners closed and joined |

The changed component test completed in 2.58 seconds; the process, including
race/profiling shutdown, completed in 3.782 seconds. These are single observed
component timings, not a general product benchmark. This fixture exercises real
encrypted transports and native Box owners, with explicit first-physical-tuple
and OS-interface-monitor adapters. It does not replace the full command worker,
kernel carrier, mixed-prefix hosted gate, installed applications or device tests.
The exact-SHA hosted Server jobs must establish that separate acceptance.

## Reproducible evidence identities

- Shared race log SHA-256:
  `18021ef83c8921d0fade591a7c0d60b820b3b36f88ca65f2b01645f6d2a591d4`.
- Unchanged cache source SHA-256:
  `493e939967eab7fed29a9348aae188a3f5ff020848e54033427270805bed0663`.
- Before-component log SHA-256:
  `506bf627918268fdadc46f3e8f2f44f966b892de471cadca659650a362412605`.
- Changed-component log SHA-256:
  `26ed66cc985a14b257cb06f8a88d4fc0c7a47c4f6a6a4dc87ab43fb72a23d5df`.

The source-only, native-component and hosted release evidence remain separate.
This change is a performance repair, not a declaration that all Router VPN
requirements, private deployment, or physical-device acceptance are complete.

## docs/ORDINARY-CANDIDATE-RESERVATION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Ordinary candidate reservation ownership

The ordinary connected-candidate factory now returns an exact reservation before calling the catalog or constructing a measurement service. Android and Apple store that handle before invoking its one-shot `Prepare` method. A failed preparation is cleaned up through that retained handle. The removed owner/position/inventory cleanup export can no longer close an existing or replacement reservation with matching values.

`Close` joins admitted preparation before draining the service and catalog. A failed join retains the original handle and slot. Duplicate construction, copied owners or handles, foreign slots, and stale handles from an earlier reservation cannot borrow cleanup authority. The existing cancellation, MTU exclusion, and native retirement boundaries remain required. This unit adds no execution-entry or remote-prefix admission.

## Source and recovery identity

The 18 code, fixture, and registration paths are frozen by manifest `647cce0efd20cde231b783740a9fbc5900d7176bb531f31505785b45f55cbaf1` on `c9bff6d86f56c68a70c94c8f0efce01ff2e5fb32`. Seven complete recovered bodies match their original hashes. Eleven regenerated test, guard, or registration bodies have new identities and were validated as new source. Their preimages also match published `7f602c07f72a38418660223a216d6d32b267820d`; this evidence document is the nineteenth publication path.

Five complete historical receipt bodies and four Android logs were recovered and match their prior hashes. The historical native 13-record/three-control results survive only as summaries and hashes; their original full records are unavailable. Fresh native evidence below replaces that evidentiary gap. Historical Swift session 20876 has no observed terminal result and remains unverified.

The engine continuations use the exact recovered Swift session and delivery fixture in a separately frozen `a7e4eb89` source closure. Native and ordinary host runs use the captured `c9bff6d8` closure. A separate complete two-root race run at `7f602c07` checks the subsequent imported-policy dependency change; it is not described as a complete native or host rerun at that revision.

## Observed validation

All compiled phases retained their original per-process and test deadlines, race detection where applicable, and default Go vet. The complete mobile package was compiled for the native tests.

| Scope | Result | Receipt SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Reconstructed ordinary native baseline | All 13 records passed; driver 449.435 s, package tests 3.388 s. | `40c8834a0fb2c9a71182ae9da600363cbfba468c56f05ee33c43394831105f76` |
| Three native compiled counterfactuals | All three failed at their intended ownership witnesses; driver 74.864 s. | `02653cb810148f16e0c6fa2dba70e52c89ac108d05d8244462e075d1682264e7` |
| Ordinary Android source and session | 215 checks and all 15 causal controls passed; driver 160.826 s. | `abc9eee7841cbf7d0fbb7e8dcce1480d9736a9c79a44098229c1aa6dfffbdda9` |
| Apple candidate session | Shipping session positive, all six causal controls, synthetic SDK guard with 33 omissions and removed-cleanup refusal passed; driver 22.594 s. | `725d6b67993fe614525f13b0eea2933dc2c6991ea3efca2650c84a0052ef23bc` |
| Current published shared dependency | Both complete roots and all 26 records passed; package 39.180 s. The strict retained-log replay below establishes the final receipt. | `ca7010c1208563c18e448c21b70a84953fda1aaa6fb518710b5a83a00848c3a3` |

The native counterfactuals retain these exact observed witnesses:

| Control | Required witness |
| --- | --- |
| Duplicate factory cleanup | `duplicate failure gained original reservation authority` |
| Stale handle with the same inventory | `stale same-inventory close retired replacement` |
| Copied original owner | `copied owner retired original candidate lifetime false` |

The current shared roots are `TestNodeChainCandidatePreparationImportedSSKeepsOnlyPacketPrefixes` and `TestNodeChainMultipleImportedServerLegacyAdmissionClosed`. Their original Go command joined with exit 0 and its package terminal was PASS. The scratch wrapper then passed text to a published parser that requires bytes, causing a `TypeError`. That failed scratch receipt remains unchanged: `b8228d12b3fff4efc7d7764f507547887e2c649039ddf125f450c409bf6215ef`. A parser-only replay reproduced the type error, then supplied the original bytes to the unchanged strict parser, verified the exact two roots/26 records and original exit/status, and checked every captured source and log hash. No compiler or test rerun occurred. The failed sequential-wrapper receipt also remains a failed attempt.

## Engine continuation and preserved failures

The original 300-second full delivery attempt and the first 300-second delivery continuation both timed out. Those aggregate outcomes remain failures. No shipping gate, default inventory, or child deadline was increased.

Finite selections of the original AST statements completed the remaining controls with fresh unmodified positive harnesses and the original 90-second compile, 25-second runtime, and 300-second caller limits. The recorded union covers all 23 original control names exactly once:

| Scope | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Lifecycle continuation | Five original positive harnesses and ten compiled controls passed in 219.213 s. |
| First delivery continuation | Four positive variants completed; seven controls have exact terminal refusal witnesses before the 300-second aggregate timeout. |
| Tail A | One fresh 1,453-check positive and three controls passed in 122.329 s. |
| Tail B | One fresh 1,453-check positive and three controls passed in 103.972 s. |

Control index `a0467b8831c17cd4783dbb09b9a85de32bb39136ead375d94053de66033d41bf` binds the complete inventory, logs, witnesses, and both failed aggregates. Finite completion is not a claim that the full default engine gate passed in one 300-second invocation.

The first native preparation copied an already prepared core. Current multihop preparation changed two generated shared files while the retained Tor ownership receipt still identified their previous bytes. The Tor preparer correctly refused to overwrite them before compilation. Receipt `4f8d4aecb82bb0c6c1d1d1723b8657d658469bd45b802186f6f5e5791061ac51` and the failed copy are preserved; neither ownership receipts nor guards were removed.

Replacement preparation used pristine local Git clones without hard links, verified the exact committed core/Xray pins and clean status, and ran the actual OpenVPN, multihop, Tor, and Apple Xray preparers. A later source-checker failure revealed that the typed projection checker looked for CONNECT templates in the ordinary preparer's registry. The separately published two-file registry repair `7f602c07` was borrowed explicitly. The checker repair changed no prepared core bytes and required no preparer rerun. The prepared-source receipt remains `52b38aa2ee7c7907c453d5058c10e5dc01bc80ae2abafa38c92e3e2cdc93b7b3`, covering 4,525 core and 1,020 Xray files.

## Required registration and acceptance boundary

The Android and Apple SDK guards require ordinary `Prepare` and refuse the removed cleanup export. Both SDK build paths invoke the native baseline and three compiled omission controls against their actual prepared mobile core. The desktop builder retains the independent runner/source checks; it does not represent its desktop core as the mobile SDK package. All existing typed, saved-prefix, and default gate inventories remain required.

Native component tests use the complete mobile package and explicitly modeled original state/catalog boundaries. Java and Swift source tests compile shipping host code against declared SDK/native boundary doubles. These results do not establish encrypted-path, installed-device, physical-network, signing, or packaged-release acceptance. Automated native package and release gates remain separate requirements. Those automated gates themselves do not establish physical-device or physical-network acceptance.

## docs/PACKET-CARRIER-MTU-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Captured SS2022 packet envelopes — November 16, 2026

## Change

The MTU plan validator now follows a captured WG/AWG packet through every
intervening SS2022 datagram carrier to the original preceding packet interface.
The old check examined one wrapper. The new helper rejects missing, duplicate
or cyclic ownership and bounds the complete walk to sixteen owners.

The calculation includes each literal IPv4/IPv6 destination address, AES
identity-key headers, ChaCha's nonce, both request and response framing, and
port-53 control-packet padding from the pinned sing-shadowsocks v0.2.8 packet
writers. Only the outermost carrier contributes an IP/UDP header. Original AWG
handshake, cookie, enabled junk and transport padding remain part of the budget.
The existing `EndpointEnvelope` and `PacketEnvelope.Capacity` implementations
are unchanged; minimum MTU, fixed-budget rejection and control limits remain.

The helper preserves native-valid UDP network representations and explicitly
disabled UDP-over-TCP options. It refuses enabled UDP-over-TCP, TCP-only paths,
malformed options, invalid literal endpoints or ports, and more than 64 keys.
The native parser rejects a literal empty network string; it is not treated as
the valid absent/empty-list default. Selected source bytes are never rewritten.

Implementation and regression sources:

- [Captured carrier arithmetic](../internal/awgpolicy/packet_carriers.go)
- [Envelope and boundary cases](../internal/awgpolicy/packet_carriers_test.go)
- [MTU plan integration](../internal/mobilemtu/policy.go)
- [Complete-path and option compatibility cases](../internal/mobilemtu/packet_carriers_test.go)

## Exact local validation

This independent four-file unit was composed on published parent
`4636b2341d203a9c5d4f77f32b5b7327efe8753d`. The complete package suites ran on
that composition with Go 1.26.3, Linux AMD64 and the race detector:

```text
go test -race -p=1 -count=1 -timeout=180s ./internal/awgpolicy ./internal/mobilemtu
```

Both package verdicts passed: awgpolicy 1.036 seconds and mobilemtu 2.037 seconds.
The joined command took 7.784 seconds within its unchanged 300-second process
bound. All captured Go/module inputs were byte-identical before and after the
run. The nonverbose package output does not provide an individual test count.

The source inventory SHA-256 is
`7a0be9f6cfad6e1ded1a09ab19de4cdc7d2800a456fa273dd6985f49b0b8fdc6`;
the actual test log SHA-256 is
`98a1e1996c11f287729a1caf7a380c61eddcc41b2ac6698436e44b8c2a9bfd59`.

## Delivery boundary

This unit changes packet-envelope validation. It does not by itself expose
multiple imported nodes in a native app, project a later packet endpoint's
Auto setting, measure live PMTU, or establish native/device acceptance. The
schema-six compiler and native host work require their separate shipping
integration and execution evidence. No deployment or complete-release result
is claimed by these package tests.

## docs/PAIRED-SS-CANDIDATE-LAYERS-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Captured paired Shadowsocks candidate layers — 2026-11-16

Paired Shadowsocks candidate rows now retain the captured Off, AES-256-GCM or AES-256-GCM+XOR stream closure. The shared compiler derives the literal TCP endpoint from that concrete closure, including the original whitening listener where selected. It preserves the complete original source digest and proof identity. Imported members retain their existing family and layer restrictions. UDP-only, nested, hostname, substituted and malformed closures do not acquire a guessed TCP target.

The metric remains fifty routed TCP connection establishments. It proves the original route and listener response, not Shadowsocks authentication. Changing a captured secret invalidates the catalog even if the listener address stays unchanged. Android, Apple and desktop catalogs defer final admission to the shared constructor. A measured future profile preserves the complete path, selected execution and final layer; active source records and private credentials are not rewritten into the saved profile.

The tested source was frozen at `39eb6ff7966b28092da5d4f58ad118e9edaac25c`, based on `70e6b34488563ff94f63f34e10ee508e938470d9`. Two later fixture-only commits correct the new Android test's package-private helper access and connected-result callback, ending at `d682054600688e17c65e97ba00daace65b349338`. All production, shared Go and native input bytes remained identical. The original manifests, every failed attempt and successful receipts are retained under `candidate-layered-ss-validation` in the task workspace.

The full shared race test image compiled in 83.934 seconds under its original 300-second bound. The exact 114-record inventory passed: 34 new closure/lifecycle records, 14 adjacent candidate records and 66 existing typed SOCKS/CONNECT refusal records. Every phase retained its 180-second runtime bound and strict descendant and package verdicts.

Canonical native source preparation used pinned sing-box `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray `50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`. The source inventory digest was `f0d2559337e2b2002b2b2ad652b2173b263187aeb83dcfcc78395985c8c565fb`. The complete native peer race image compiled in 454.470 seconds under 600 seconds. Image SHA256: `add9794ac016da0efae7791a55ed3e234699cb4858f5e9c08dee6736119e4e10`.

Six native phases exercised WG, AWG-fast and AWG-strong, each with AES and AES+XOR. All 46 distinct records, 54 executed records including repeated parents, and 300 positive TCP samples passed. Foreign tuples, changed captured keys, cancellation joins, a failed fiftieth sample and a refused original listener were mandatory. Each invocation retained the original 90-second test / 100-second process bounds; actual phases took 2.30–3.12 seconds. XOR used the actual shipping start-layer relay, built with normal VCS stamping from a byte-identical standalone checkout. Relay SHA256: `0af2d4de1eb05718593638aecf769730b99d004740dcf37e7dc9ed5cee94405b`.

The Android shipping Java/source gate passed 75 checks across all three layers and both compiled old-Off controls in 22.955 seconds. The Apple shipping Swift/source gate passed 54 checks and both old-Off controls in 38.668 seconds, below its original 300-second caller bound. Both use the real shared Go compiler with explicitly declared native/storage lifecycle doubles. Neither result is a device or VPN kernel verdict. All 81 workflow, phase inventory and native transcript checks passed; three Python imports required the normal deploy module path on the local invocation, with no source edit.

Retained unsuccessful attempts include an ambient-Go precheck refusal before native compilation; linked-worktree VCS stamping failures before the relay build; Android Java visibility failure; and the Android fixture waiting for a temporary-lease callback on a connected measurement. The latter was corrected to consume the shipping connected `changed` callback while retaining the original measurement owner through Save and joined cancellation. None of these failures was converted into a pass or fixed by changing a production guard or deadline.

The first full desktop race-package compilation reached its unchanged 240-second cap at 240.005 seconds with no image. A second, complete non-race `./cmd/client` compilation on the composed source ended at 231.129 seconds, before its existing 600-second bound, with `compile: signal: killed`; the cause was not established. It also produced no image. No desktop runtime or desktop race success is claimed by either attempt. A third attempt compiled the same complete package in 76.058 seconds under 600 seconds with inlining disabled only for `router-vpn/cmd/client` (`-gcflags=router-vpn/cmd/client=-l`, GOMAXPROCS=2, GOGC=20, GOMEMLIMIT=800MiB). It compiled the complete shipping test package with Go’s normal platform selection, without a fixture subset. All fifteen required desktop records then passed in 1.425 seconds under the original 120-second test / 135-second process limits. The image SHA256 is `753daa2073ddea056b73cbd1fac9e73371c2b2a19d4f401e93bd67a424923de1`. This is a local full-source, non-race, package-only no-inlining result; normal optimized and race builds remain required in CI. Their source, gate commands and deadlines were not changed.

Composition onto `684a1c272679e796f68f5077d2a7618f8d136520` preserves the connected imported-SS and typed candidate foundations. Fourteen of the twenty code/gate files match the successful original proof exactly. Six retain intervening upstream context and additive registrations. The actual `nodeChainCandidateTarget` function remains byte-identical (SHA256 `c6ff7cbfa0aa3dd35bbbe16a3711c4a3ea4a5cd875d146ca60b3c584f463e386`). The native fixture union retains the intervening single-packet final-provider guard verbatim and supplies a distinct two/three-packet, route-only decorator wrapper with no replacement final provider. Its common peer body stays unchanged. Thirty-five composed registry and native transcript controls pass. The older native image is not relabeled as execution of this merged fixture; exact composed native acceptance remains mandatory in the shipping gate. Native component success does not claim installed Linux kernel, Windows WFP, Darwin PF, APK/device or live-router acceptance. The required installed-host gate invokes the new exact native inventory before namespace cases and rejects omitted, duplicate, skipped, raced, panicked or abnormal verdicts.

Final publication composition rebases the reviewed unit onto `e72620f3051e3b2d89d58e6f0ac454f4e819474e`, retaining its concurrent imported-candidate catalog fixture byte-for-byte. That upstream-only test change was not an input to the preceding fifteen-record local image on `684a1c27` plus the reviewed unit; the twenty code/gate files are otherwise unchanged from that composed source. The only final evidence correction replaces a transcribed native-inventory digest with the exact original receipt value.

A subsequent guarded publication preflight found `7829f31e394217bc981b7803f9c077f2a4fd528c` on main. Final composition preserves both of its Windows callback fixture/diagnostic files byte-for-byte; neither overlaps this twenty-file code/gate unit or changes the previously recorded proof inputs. ## docs/SAVED-SS-CANDIDATE-PREFIX-2026-11-16.md

# Saved SS packet-prefix observation — November 16, 2026

## Delivered source boundary

This unit fixes a measured validation-cost failure and adds executable source, native-owner and mobile-capture coverage on top of the closed-prefix foundation in `1e2eb94ccb8a49688ab8a5f09909f8d3dae6f298`.

The private `newPacketCandidatePlan` constructor remains unchanged. The public `NewCandidatePlan` still refuses connected imported-SS paths longer than three members. A closed preparation may measure an earlier paired WG/AWG packet prefix of a saved three-to-eight-member path. The imported penultimate member and final member remain complete saved-source provenance, with their original credentials, resolver policy and owners validated; they do not enter the executable prefix. Application and DNS ingress stay closed.

This does not make a saved hostname SS transport an eligible candidate TCP target or prove its authentication, public exit or complete connection. Candidate TCP timing remains distinct from those operations. The separately published desktop catalog correction `5fce4d2c` and complete Android proposal correction `7221e7fc` are preserved.

## Why the observation changed

The actual eight-member native component case previously expired at its unchanged 90-second request deadline after 40 of the required 50 candidate connections. Its nested native observations repeatedly validated the same complete captured source: separate source/configuration getters each performed a preparation validation, which itself validated the original twice.

The correction binds the exact candidate/original pointer before the candidate's complete validation, then reads both identities after one fresh preparation validation. It does not cache validation, change any digest representation, skip native manager/network/ingress/gate observations, or change per-dial checks. A failed grouped identity read returns both strings empty. The native test also changes the original owner during a retained dial and requires the post-dial and subsequent observations to refuse it while retaining original cleanup.

## Current executable evidence

The code was frozen and tested on `caedc250c0ce58ed53b76c413fec74ead05d56d3`, then all ten changed preimages and outputs were checked before forwarding unchanged to `5fce4d2ccede7465c45ed369953cde679521f90a`.

| Check | Actual result | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| Complete shared race image | Compile passed in 94.237 seconds; 34 roots and 188 Go records passed | Original source 2/38, additional cipher/desktop source 3/33, full candidate runtime 29/117; each phase retained 180-second Go and 240-second caller bounds. |
| Fresh native composition | All 10 selected native records passed | Current eight-member/50-sample case plus eight graph/gate/denied-close owner records; actual Box/managers, private HTTP and candidate TCP; explicit TUN/physical/encrypted-transport fixture boundaries. |
| Current native eight-member case | All 50 completed; case time 32.75 seconds | Original 90-second request, 150-second Go and 900-second positive aggregate limits preserved. The first command included a cold race build and took 314.194 seconds; owner checks took 17.784 seconds. |
| Android capture/source compiler | 381 checks and five compiled refusal controls passed in 234.418 seconds | 195 original, 85 imported-literal and 101 imported-hostname checks; shipping Java and actual shared compiler; no device VPN acceptance. |
| Apple capture/source compiler | 816 checks and six compiled refusal controls passed in 99.221 seconds | 312 original, 234 imported-literal and 270 imported-hostname checks; shipping Swift on Linux with explicit framework/Libbox boundaries. |
| Current Android Save ABI composition | Actual Go build and javac type analysis passed in 5.159 seconds | 61 prepared Java sources and 141 class images; exact current Store/Profile/PrefixCapture bodies; compile-only verification, with no runtime cases claimed. |
| Required runner contracts | 29 shared partition tests and 19 native-runner tests passed | Source inventory and strict parser checks; synthetic verdict rows are not native execution. |

The additional source tests cover WG/AWG Fast/AWG Strong, literal and hostname provenance, multiple SS2022 ciphers, three/four/eight-member paths, seventeen fresh-identity mutation leaves, independently changed or copied original owners, fifty actual candidate attempts, cancellation and joined retirement. The desktop physical-policy projection checks cover Linux, Windows and Darwin at four/eight members, preserving the stronger policy of the saved imported member without executing its suffix.

The fresh native run retained all 1,633 shipping-input hashes and 4,853 prepared Go/module hashes before and after execution. Its clean core and Xray revisions were `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and `50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`; all actual preparers, module tidy and module verification passed.

## Preserved failure and counterfactual evidence

The original native 40/50 timeout remains recorded, as does an earlier disk-exhausted compile that executed no tests. Two early shared-fixture errors confused an imported resolver address or test password with legitimate identical packet-prefix values; the corrected assertions bind the actual resolver tag and a separately compiled SS test key without removing the remote/key/source checks.

On the retained earlier draft, all native three/four/eight-member cases passed after the cost correction, and a compiled old-trio predicate counterfactual rejected the precise four-member constructor witness. Those earlier results retain their original source identities; they are not added to the current counts or described as a replay against the newer private constructor.

The current managed-core refresh was refused by the original Tor generated-source ownership guard before compilation. The refusal and partially refreshed source hashes remain recorded. A fresh pinned private checkout then passed all original preparation guards and the current native cases. No ownership receipt was removed or bypassed.

## Required release and device evidence

The shipping native gate still requires its complete 49-record union, 53 executions because family parents repeat across phases, and all 13 existing compiled causal controls. The original phase and three OpenVPN phases remain required; three imported-SS phases are added. The original six shared prefix roots and their source phase are preserved, with the new source ownership matrix assigned its own required bounded phase.

These local component results do not establish all-platform packaged builds, the complete native gate on every target, provider authentication, private deployment, installed-device networking or physical visual acceptance. Those retain their separate exact-SHA and device evidence requirements.

## docs/SELECTED-EXECUTION-FIFTY-DESKTOP-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Selected-execution fifty-sample desktop consumer evidence

Date: 2026-11-16. Scope: the desktop parent and desktop consumer changes in this unit. Native producer, encrypted transport, packaged OS, and device acceptance are separate evidence.

## Behavior

The shipping desktop parent reads the capture published by the retained native selected-execution facade. It verifies the original typed Ready record, projection, reader, source and configuration fingerprints, selected Local/Server choice, full member inventory, and current session before issuing an operation. A refreshed validated heartbeat may update its timestamp and response timing; it cannot replace the captured proof generation, endpoint, MTU, comparison result, or selected execution.

The selected operation uses its own bounded request/cancel/result exchange. Every original request field is echoed, including the opaque native capture. Cancellation joins the original operation before releasing its MTU hold. A result is accepted only after the producer removes the original request and supplies a complete, drained fifty-sample receipt. The parent never repeats Compare selection to measure or restore the winning path.

Selected receipts use separate transactional durable history. They retain their complete captured identities and measurement meaning. An old or cancelled operation cannot overwrite a newer connection or newer durable history writer. The public edge response metric remains explicitly routed HTTPS response time; it is not described as ICMP latency or private-node response time.

Swift, PowerShell, and GTK consumers validate the selected capture together with every displayed row and receipt. Ordinary Local captures remain separate. This unit covers canonical two-to-eight-member selected paths, including their supported single imported penultimate member. Independent earlier/multiple imported selected paths are tracked in the separate schema-six extension.

## Executed local checks

| Check | Exact source/evidence | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Full `cmd/client` race-enabled compilation | `765ee02d160090807a60ab5f7c8c609e24e8cceb`; Go 1.26.3; 59.194 seconds, original 300-second bound | PASS |
| Eight complete selected parent roots, with every required nested case | Same source; 105 unique PASS records; actual parent API, private-file exchange, source/reader checks, MTU hold, rollback and durable history | PASS |
| Windows required-root/parser runner | Same source; 6.702 seconds | PASS |
| Swift shipping Foundation controller and transport constructor | `773189b17446bb73c7c8e3df945576726c7905cd`; Swift 6.0.3; 1,406 shipping checks | PASS |
| Swift causal controls | Same source; 17 separately compiled changes rejected by their intended fixture; baseline plus controls: 18 programs | PASS |
| PowerShell shipping model, actual local HTTP transport and causal controls | Same source; PowerShell 7.6.9; 64.488 seconds | PASS |

The Go parent fixture models its native child/kernel transport explicitly. It exercises the actual shipping parent API and durable state, but it does not establish encrypted native transport or physical-device behavior. The independent native facade gate supplies separate evidence for its actual selected HTTP fifty-sample producer.

The race-compiled parent image SHA-256 was `429c2e4bab89169033e3982e8d213ea8653cd39b9d739ed8292b545e2c458dae`. The baseline Swift image was `447f956c5ac01b752e67a5d53331c285b268c7c202e22cecd96042f9dd571b61`. The original test source identities above remain the evidence identities; a rebase is not a new platform test.

# Server candidate grant contracts — November 16, 2026

This unit adds an authenticated, single-target TCP-connect grant contract and
original-lease manager/HTTP boundary for a selected remote WG/AWG packet member.

are unchanged. A server-generated operation identity separates old credentials
from replacement operations; admission allows at most 50 connects, one in flight.

Expiry revokes authority immediately and retains the exact owner until callbacks
and native cleanup join. Canonical requests remain within the existing 1,024-entry
bound so exact late cancellation can be acknowledged. Capacity eviction removes
only expired requests without a live operation. A seen Create is rejected before
eviction; an unknown or evicted expired DELETE cannot manufacture an acknowledgment.
Close acknowledgments bind the original operation and canonical request digest.

## Preparation and retained failed attempts

The first encrypted peer compile exposed the missing candidate-process companion and did not reach acceptance. Environment interruption and missing dependency-cache failures are retained separately. An attempted in-place preparation correctly refused the missing original sing-tun checksum after a prior local replacement and tidy; no checksum or ownership guard was weakened.

The failed partial core/Xray trees were archived exactly. Fresh committed clones at the original canonical paths used core pin `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray pin `50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`. Missing pinned modules were restored using the official Go proxy and checksum database, and actual preparers plus module verification completed. Recovery had changed the physical location behind the canonical Xray path; a same-device/inode check proved the alias, and normal `go mod edit` restored the original logical replacement. The original complete-source guard then passed with exactly two generated differences: the reviewed peer projection and its ownership digest. No source allowlist was broadened.

The final prepared-source manifest is `bab21d8313cbcbcb9124ee1d0e3d65b00cdd6bb8b4d999875706c9fdec299dc4`, with 4,593 shipping inputs and 2,862 checked-source records. Raw failures, archived partial source, original baseline manifests, reconciliation, and successful receipts remain separate.

## Encrypted acceptance remains required

A local capability probe entered a new network namespace but the first netlink operation returned EPERM. No privilege workaround was attempted. Compilation, source cases, modeled native ownership, and the agent checks above do not establish encrypted provider traffic, device behavior, or a complete release verdict. The required Linux amd64/arm64 hosted encrypted gates retain actual traffic and cleanup authority.

Final encrypted command receipt: `47e007796f26fcd533c8b6abd19ffd0a01e6a9ea0a25aaa43d12704e3eac300b`; stdout: `43a8232fc41fba9ce05d93ff78bb74209b3d2e8054f364fe0b6a6c83119f7389`; combined terminal receipt: `134b920e8d74c858f39bc917d3c9c82c8572a05ede29b7fb44013bfd00a87c2c`.

## docs/SERVER-DNS-FAILURE-DIAGNOSTIC-2026-11-16.md

# Encrypted Server DNS failure diagnostic — 2026-11-16

The actual encrypted Server acceptance case `prefix-hostname-wg-one` reported
`hostname-question-aaaa` on both Linux architectures at `3f6e8420`.
The exact captured transport policy requested an IPv4-only question. Bare and
actual Shadowsocks-constructor reproductions retained IPv4-only lookup, so they
did not identify the full worker's failing call path.

This change adds a failure-only diagnostic replay. The original unmodified
worker, reference peer and acceptance case remain the release authority. Only
the observed hostname/AAAA failure triggers a separately identified Go overlay.
Regardless of the diagnostic result, the original exception is re-raised.
The original predicates, required cases, deadlines and successful receipt
conditions are unchanged.

The overlay observes Router and Client Lookup, Exchange and ExchangeAsync
entries, then executes their original bodies unchanged. Output is limited to
24 unique tuples drawn from fixed phase, question-type, domain-class, transport,
strategy and caller categories. It never emits an actual domain, address,
credential, config, stack or native error. The joined driver validates and
re-encodes the allowed fields before printing them. The helper remains a
template and cannot enter an ordinary Go package build.

Both diagnostic inputs are registered in the runtime build cache inputs.
A helper-only correction therefore invalidates cached validation.

## Executed validation

Validation used the complete current source closure at
`473b600727c87b944cb9233a27043ca239433745` over the original pinned relay core,
without modifying that core:

- Three actual race-instrumented helper/filter/SS roots passed, including real
  Shadowsocks TCP, UDP and ListenPacket lookup, direct AAAA Exchange
  classification, concurrent output bounds and private/duplicate-field controls.
- All 28 original and new encrypted-runner tests passed. These include proving
  that a successful or failed diagnostic cannot replace the original failure.
- The complete diagnostic shipping worker compiled under race instrumentation
  in 94.10 seconds.
- The actual encrypted acceptance driver compiled under race instrumentation
  in 37.33 seconds.
- The original 600-second compilation bounds and native/process/namespace
  bounds remain unchanged. Source and overlay identities were checked before
  and after each phase.

Worker SHA-256:
`3f551994aa497541ccca0d9ded643b9a7880ef9d76367d84dfb0b7e8dea56f26`.

Driver SHA-256:
`76e69e14e1beb632c55d055123a123c89adc518d99bcd8c7d7ede17dfb1df52f`.

Validation receipt SHA-256:
`94567f21957d6f82e2516f3aad24ff6337a5b370434692bfb56f5c68369b8883`.

The disjoint native client owner commit `3b605c0c` is preserved during
integration. Its only owned-file overlap is the runtime builder; only the two
diagnostic input entries are added there.

## Remaining evidence

The local namespace check could enter a user namespace, but the required veth
setup failed with `Cannot open netlink socket: Operation not permitted`.

It executes the actual pinned `dns/transport.NewTCPRaw` implementation against the existing hostname handler, with an explicitly modeled loopback listener and lower dial. It verifies the original ID0 A result, waits for the real reuse probes, and performs later application lookups whose wire IDs are assigned by the original multiplexer. The probe counters cannot satisfy the original application-query assertion. The test deliberately preserves the transport's reused-ID behavior rather than inventing a one-use restriction.

The encrypted Server gate runs this required root from its existing peer image before its original source and encrypted cases. It requires one exact successful terminal transcript. The original 46 encrypted cases, architecture requirements and existing per-case deadlines remain intact. This component observation does not replace those encrypted namespace cases or physical-device acceptance.

## docs/SERVER-TRANSPORT-DRAIN-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Server transport retirement — 2026-11-16

## Failure and change

The hosted ordered Server acceptance run `43799624677` at
`a1a1bbeaf100c9fc2b1dcc38890ca1a657ccec82` failed on both AMD64 and ARM64 at
`http-connect/prefix-wg-one`. The worker could not join its original connections
on Stop and reported the sanitized `close-connections` deadline stage. The
preceding direct-proxy cases had passed. This was a later shutdown stage than
the earlier front/packet retirement repairs.

The worker previously cancelled the context shared by admission, proofs and
native packet transport before attempting graceful connection retirement.
WireGuard's actual ClientBind then rejected sends needed for CONNECT's original
TCP FIN/peer-EOF exchange. The repair separates logical work cancellation from
the transport owned by that same worker. Stop, parent cancellation and proof
expiry still stop admission immediately. The worker joins front and proof work,
drains its original sockets, and retires the typed protocol owner before
cancelling and joining the transport watcher and closing Box/carrier resources.

Transport retention remains bounded by the original eight-second command drain
budget. A single watcher starts at the first logical cancellation; another
cancellation or failed retry cannot renew it. Failure retains the exact original
close object and error. Cancellation of the transport does not acknowledge an
unjoined protocol owner. Partial construction also retires the captured context
and watchers. The new `close-transport` diagnostic uses the existing fixed safe
vocabulary and never formats underlying private native errors.

## Focused evidence

The seven implementation/test outputs were frozen on base
`56d5f95a57773912035b12f773ffaee93572aa0f`. Their preimages were verified unchanged
on integration parent `66f73ea50da59a5c397cf8bf0ff0b7b79b6fe9d8`.

- All 17 affected Python runner tests passed in 26.116 seconds. They include
  actual extracted shutdown/ledger bodies, the real service registry and stage
  error wrapper, loopback/UoT objects, the original compiled controls, and seven
  new lifecycle cases with four compiled causal omissions.
- The lifecycle cases cover explicit Stop, parent cancellation, the original
  expiry cancellation handle, zero-socket partial construction, failed-close
  retry, cancellation while an original close is held, and the unchanged
  eight-second force bound. The expiry callback's generation/proof guards were
  not replaced or relaxed.
- A separate race-instrumented protocol diagnostic used actual gonet TCP,
  shipping ClientBind over real loopback UDP, and the actual CONNECT connection
  close implementation. It incorporated the exact repaired lifetime bodies.
  Explicit Stop and parent cancellation both joined FIN/EOF before retiring
  the original transport. The counterfactual early-cancel arm reproduced the
  original 1.02-second protocol timeout and four refused ClientBind sends.
  All four exact test records passed; compilation took 6.275 seconds and
  execution 2.257 seconds.
- The mandatory full native runner preserves all 94 original records and adds
  eight records, for 102 across 22 roots. Its original native bounds remain
  unchanged. Two additional causal arms exercise the real native command
  package in the hosted gate.

The local protocol diagnostic explicitly models the unencrypted packet-link
adapter. The extracted worker tests explicitly model absent Box/carrier
boundaries. They do not establish a full native worker build, encrypted network
namespace acceptance, installed-host behavior, deployment, or device acceptance.
The exact-SHA AMD64 and ARM64 hosted encrypted Server runs still have to pass.

## docs/SOCKS-CLIENT-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md

# SOCKS client execution source and controller evidence

This increment adds a distinct typed schema-3 client preparation and policy for
one WG/AWG entry, the original SOCKS5 import, and the original paired SS2022
final. It builds on the typed remote server suffix published in `c7f43af4`.
It does not establish native Box, desktop, Android, or Apple Server/Compare
acceptance. Those adapters are the next integration work.

`NewNodeChainSOCKSExecutionPreparation` retains the exact original
`NodeChainSOCKSSource` and private local runtime. The generic preparation,
policy, restoration, and launch APIs remain closed to this family. A distinct
canonical restoration parser creates a fresh typed construction; it cannot
lend that source pointer to another live preparation. Policies from fresh
authenticated discovery and original restoration proposals bind to the same
preparation and source. Native implementations must separately provide the
required original SOCKS observer before discovery or runtime construction.

The Local SOCKS outbound retains its original credentials, packet-prefix
detour, and actual UDP-association configuration. The original SS final retains
that SOCKS detour. Neither gains CONNECT UDP-over-TCP. The separate
authenticated private server front retains its existing UoT-v2 contract and
lease credentials. Both choices retain the original selected DNS policy and
physical packet-entry inventory. The application gate starts closed.

The shared runtime continues to use the average of final-node HTTP response
time and response time from the same random external HTTP endpoint. Both
candidates and the fresh winner proof use that same endpoint. A timeout makes
the candidate ineligible; the winner gets a separate trial and must prove its
original path before application activation. Restoration keeps the original
choice and private proposal instead of repeating Compare.

## Executed evidence

On the exact source of this increment, the following command passed in
160.013 seconds with Go 1.26.3 and the race detector:

```sh
GOTOOLCHAIN=local GOMAXPROCS=2 GOGC=50 GOMEMLIMIT=1800MiB \
  go test -race -p=1 ./internal/mobilemultihop \
  -run '^TestNodeChain(SOCKSExecution|CONNECTExecution|Execution(Preparation|Runtime|Discovery|SelectedDNS))' \
  -count=1
```

The new tests cover WG, AWG2-fast and AWG2-strong with password and no-auth
SOCKS sources, Server and Compare, canonical restoration, unchanged original
outbounds, separate front credentials, source/owner replacement, copied
bindings, missing/failed/cancelled observers, wrong schema/request families,
authenticated discovery, joined server lease stop, same-endpoint scoring,
fresh winner proof, timeout disqualification, retained restoration, and failed
public/DNS/final proofs. Existing CONNECT and generic execution tests also ran.

The runtime fixtures execute real HTTP/TLS clients and the actual lease
Manager, controller, comparison, and restore logic. Their native registry,
routing, selected DNS, and remote worker I/O boundaries are explicitly modeled.
These tests do not prove encrypted traffic, a native TUN, a kernel firewall,
or any installed platform. The earlier mandatory hosted encrypted SOCKS
server component gate remains required; the local nonloopback/netlink
environment boundary in the server-owner evidence record still applies.

## Remaining scope

Native Box construction, original association observation and joined close,
durable Last integration, and desktop/mobile adapters must adopt this typed
preparation before users can select SOCKS Server/Compare on those platforms.
This increment deliberately refuses longer SOCKS Server/Compare sources and
does not claim arbitrary mixed imported chains or the full historical
requirements complete.

## docs/SOCKS-CLIENT-NATIVE-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md

# SOCKS native execution construction evidence

This increment connects the typed schema-3 client preparation published in
`74f6f3db` to the native Box execution owner. It preserves the original SOCKS5
association capture and unchanged paired SS2022 final, while the separately
authenticated server front uses the existing private UoT-v2 contract.

The distinct construction retains one original preparation, source, registry
capture, complete closed graph digest, and Box. Construction is single-use;
another source, preparation, Box, copied construction, or changed graph cannot
borrow it. Every native execution observation also checks the original SOCKS
outbound and its captured packet predecessor. The existing original manager,
selector, gate, TUN, DNS, and source checks remain in force.

Public middle measurement uses the original selected resolver for Local. Server
sends only the authorized measurement hostname through the original private
front, retaining remote selected-DNS authority. Selection changes, including
away-and-back changes during a request, invalidate the operation. The shared
native Last path binds the schema-3 policy to its original preparation and
fresh authenticated discovery; saved results never grant application readiness.

Preclose retains the original association capture even after source
cancellation. A bounded caller can stop waiting while the original close
continues; only that retained close may satisfy a later join. This is separate
from the actual Box close receipt and cannot establish native DOWN by itself.

## Executed native component evidence

The exact source was projected through the canonical preparation into pinned
sing-box `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`, including its original source
ownership records. With Go 1.26.3, the following race run passed in 64.839 seconds:

```sh
GOTOOLCHAIN=local GOMAXPROCS=2 GOGC=50 GOMEMLIMIT=1800MiB \
  go test -race -p=1 -mod=readonly \
  -tags=with_wireguard,with_gvisor,with_quic,with_utls,with_openvpn,with_naive_outbound,with_purego \
  -ldflags=-checklinkname=0 . \
  -run '^TestRouterVPN(ExecutionNativeSOCKS|CONNECTExecutionNative)' \
  -count=1 -timeout=120s
```

The nine new root tests cover the three packet-entry modes in Server and Compare;
actual native SOCKS and SS constructors; fresh discovery and gate-closed adoption;
changed sources, preparations, Boxes, copied constructions and options; retired
or cancelled owners; a held original close and its later join; selected resolver
and public hostname restrictions; original private-front credentials; real
SOCKS5 password authentication and UDP ASSOCIATE through loopback TCP/UDP peers;
UDP payload round-trip and joined control retirement; wrong credentials;
source cancellation; original Last discovery and cancellation/retry; and refusal
to mix Local and execution construction authority. Existing CONNECT native
execution tests ran in the same command.

The existing generic `NativeLastUsesOriginalDiscovery` and
`NativeRetainsOneGraphThroughDiscovery` race regressions passed separately in
3.253 seconds. All five changed native templates were byte-compared against
their exact tested canonical projections.

The registry, native protocol factories, managers, selector, application gate,
authentication, UDP association, and socket ownership run their actual code.
The TUN, OS monitor, packet predecessor, and selected DNS transport are explicit
component fixtures. The loopback packet predecessor provides no encrypted or
physical routing evidence. The final SS constructor is real, but this suite
does not claim encrypted final traffic, an installed TUN, kernel protection,
or Android/Apple device acceptance. The separate hosted encrypted SOCKS server
suffix gate remains mandatory.

Android and iOS native builds now run these exact new roots in a separate
90-second command, preserving the existing families and their original bounds.
The desktop build's existing complete compiled-root discovery includes them
in its execution phase. Preparation checks verify that the new templates are
owned, idempotent, and selected by each shipping build.
The node-chain preparation suite passed all 18 checks, CONNECT preparation
passed all 5 checks, and execution-owner preparation passed all 10 checks,
including the executable original daemon-close fixture.

## Remaining integration

The mobile CommandServer, retained mobile MTU restoration, desktop physical
owners, and platform UI/host adapters still need to adopt this native
construction. This increment does not make those unimplemented paths ready,
does not expand the trio-only SOCKS Server/Compare topology, and does not claim
the full historical requirements complete.

## docs/SOCKS-DESKTOP-CHILD-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Typed SOCKS desktop child adoption

This increment connects the schema-3 desktop preparation to the native child
coordinators on Linux, Windows and macOS. The child captures the original SOCKS
source, association owner, private option registry and preparation before native
Box construction. It cannot enter through the Local SOCKS, paired or CONNECT
source family. The imported proxy and negotiated UDP relay gain no direct host
permission; the original packet entry remains the physical scope.

Each platform prepares and activates the same sealed physical policy. Linux
retains its original parent admission, physical cover and source cleanup. Windows
retains the inherited journal and physical transaction. macOS retains one
exclusive policy together with the inherited process capture, original workspace,
utun preparation and source cleanup owner. The application gate remains closed
until the execution controller proves its selected path.

The child publishes distinct SOCKS adoption and readiness records, tied to the
original preparation, transfer, selected native identity, selection epoch,
reader, interface, MTU and selected proof. Last restoration retains the original
winner and common endpoint, performs fresh discovery and enters the saved-choice
proof factory. It cannot rerun Compare or use an ordinary Last publisher. The
existing physical MTU transaction now dispatches the original schema-3 policy;
the execution engine still owns the paused proof, replacement reader and resumed
selected path.

## Required component evidence

The mandatory shipping gate runs on each matching desktop host, before packaging.
It requires 55 exact records on Linux and Windows and 82 on macOS. These include
actual unstarted Box construction with the original SOCKS and paired SS factories,
changed graph rejection, target binding, private command replay, distinct adoption
and heartbeat publication, saved winner/endpoint, retained failed cleanup and a
late constructor after cancellation. Darwin adds inherited control, exclusive
policy, early Stop and stale private mailbox checks.

The native TUN and the child proof/installed-parent observations are explicit
component doubles. The source compilers, native option registries, Box, private
files, command dispatch and original child retirement logic execute. This gate
does not establish an installed tunnel, kernel permit, encrypted end-to-end
route, or physical device acceptance.

The baseline and mutation phases each preserve the original 120-second test
deadline and 600-second compiler/process deadline. Each phase joins two serial
processes under its separate 1200-second parent bound. Race is enabled on the
shipping hosts supported by the pinned toolchain; Windows ARM64 retains the
existing toolchain exception. Two compiled controls must fail at their intended
witnesses when schema-3 command dispatch is disabled and when readiness stops
rejecting an invalid selected proof. Original prepared source bytes must remain
unchanged after each overlay.

On Linux amd64, the exact final source passed all 10 roots and 55 required
records under Go 1.26.3 with `-race`. Both compiled causal controls failed at
their intended witnesses. A final rerun used the strengthened event parser,
which additionally refuses a parent test completing before its children and
output arriving after its test completion. The gate's seven adversarial runner
checks passed.

The existing SOCKS construction gate also passed its two roots, 20 records and
copied-owner causal control. Existing paired and CONNECT command, runtime and
saved-choice regressions passed all 10 selected roots and 54 records in 31.526
seconds. The construction gate's seven runner checks passed unchanged.

Validation used a fresh canonical desktop preparation of sing-box
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray
`50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`. All 20 changed native templates
matched that prepared core byte for byte before and after the controls. The run
used two compiler workers, `-p=1`, `GOGC=50`, a 1800 MiB memory target and a
workspace temporary directory. No compiler or test deadline was increased.
Windows and Darwin execution require their mandatory matching hosted runners;
the local Linux result does not stand in for those platform-specific checks.

The 55-record native child baseline and both compiled controls were then repeated
successfully on project revision
`ec5e9e93cd34976e6bd19e5ef207e96ff51dccbd`, including its newer source
observation and sealed imported-owner validation. This used another fresh
canonical preparation with the same pins, resource limits and deadlines. All
20 child templates and the five changed shared source/test files retained their
recorded source and prepared hashes after the controls. The construction and
paired/CONNECT regression results above belong to the earlier `213e8ba9` base;
this latest-source rerun covers the 55 child records and two child controls.

## Remaining public adoption

The public desktop profile/Connect controller, saved session and MTU restart
adapters still need their distinct schema-3 branch. Installed encrypted acceptance
must exercise that complete path. These child components do not finish the
historical requirement set or expand the exact three-member Server/Compare
topology. Longer Local SOCKS remains a separate supported source family.

# Desktop SOCKS execution editors — November 16, 2026

The Windows, Linux and macOS native editor sources now retain Server and Compare for the typed desktop SOCKS execution parent described in `SOCKS-DESKTOP-PARENT-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md`. The admitted shape is the original paired WG/AWG2 packet entry, literal imported SOCKS5 middle and paired SS2022 final. The existing longer Local graphs and separate Shadowsocks/CONNECT execution families retain their own admission rules.

## Captured choices and source ownership

All three frontends select `ordered_socks_trio_executions` for the remote execution choice and require the original middle's Boolean `ordered_socks_execution_middle_supported` hint. The ordinary Local, Shadowsocks and CONNECT capabilities cannot substitute for this typed capability. The entry retains Off, AES-256-GCM or AES-256-GCM with XOR whitening independently; the middle and final retain Off. Wrong types, noncanonical strings, changed protocols, missing or duplicated sources, relinked tokens and unsupported graph lengths remain refused.

Only complete ordered node references, the execution choice, selected final identity and original profile tokens cross the connection boundary. Private credentials remain in the original node store and typed backend capture. The frontends cannot manufacture native ownership or bypass the parent compiler/proofs. Complete profile Add/Load/Update continues through the existing captured source and response guards.

## Executed local checks

| Boundary | Actual evidence | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Windows ordered editor and payload callbacks | PowerShell Core on Linux: 2,375 positive checks and all 18 separately executed causal controls passed. The four new controls independently reject a borrowed global capability, false/non-Boolean source hint, malformed entry layer and layered middle/final. Every process retains its 90-second wrapper limit. | `WPF=False`; actual Windows widgets and PowerShell 5.1 remain native-host gates. |
| AppKit source projection | Swift 6.0.3 compiled and executed 17,857 checks. All 66 causal programs were compiled and rejected their intended behavioral failures, in the two observations described below. | Actual AppKit rendering, macOS lifecycle and physical network behavior are separate. |
| Linux ordered source model | The real GTK/JSON-linked source model compiled with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` and passed, together with all 19 model-applicable compiled causal controls. | This uses `--model-only`; it does not establish widget callback execution. |
| Native desktop verdict validators | 10 desktop editor verdict tests, 8 macOS profile verdict tests and 16 callback-runner tests passed. | These verify complete dispatch, process bounds and strict transcript acceptance; they are not native widget results. |

The first AppKit attempt exceeded the unchanged 90-second Swift compiler limit before a positive verdict. Its log is retained. The next complete invocation passed the baseline and 65 causal controls, then correctly rejected the removed SOCKS connection-body guard at the Connect callback. That last fixture had incorrectly expected the draft callback's different assertion. The fixture now requires the actual exact Connect assertion. The final control was recompiled and executed under the same 90-second limits and passed its causal check. All **67 generated Swift programs** (baseline plus 66 controls) are byte-identical between those observations and the merged editor source; only that expected assertion string and independent Windows runner metadata changed. This is complete program coverage from preserved observations, not a claim that the failed full invocation exited successfully.

The current full GTK attempt compiled the shipping fixture and passed its source-model section, then stopped at `GTK ordered: real GTK display` because this executor could not open the test display. No GTK callback or rendered-widget pass is claimed. The hosted GTK suite still requires the complete actual callback run and all 24 compiled controls. Its new SOCKS profile fixture refreshes the actual displayed source catalog before Add/Update/Load and restores the original catalog afterwards.

## Mandatory native inventories

The native macOS validator requires all 66 controls in their actual order and one complete positive/terminal summary. The desktop editor validator requires all 18 Windows controls and all 24 GTK controls; omission, duplication, foreign entries, failed or timed-out processes, unjoined descendants and a model-only widget claim remain rejected. The Windows aggregate budget now accounts for the 22 original profile processes, one editor baseline and 18 separate editor controls, each with the original 90-second bound. The actual Windows native host/shell identity checks, GTK display requirement and existing hosted job bounds remain intact.

Relevant local observations are `desktop-socks-windows-editor-complete.log`, `macos-socks-public-profiles-complete.log`, `macos-socks-public-body-control.log`, `macos-socks-public-witness-identity.json`, `desktop-socks-gtk-model-final.log`, `desktop-socks-gtk-native-attempt.log`, `desktop-socks-editor-native-verdicts.log`, `desktop-socks-macos-native-verdicts.log` and `desktop-socks-editor-callback-runner.log`. The committed source and mandatory native gates provide the reproducible execution paths.

These changes close the editor admission gap for the stated typed SOCKS graph. They do not close longer remote SOCKS graphs, arbitrary mixed imports, other final protocols, physical/off-LAN acceptance, signing, deployment or unrelated original requirements.

## docs/SOCKS-DESKTOP-EXECUTION-FOUNDATION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md

# Typed SOCKS desktop execution foundation

This increment adds a distinct schema-3 desktop Server/Compare preparation,
physical policy, adoption record, readiness record, saved Last binding and native
construction. The original `DesktopNodeChainSOCKSPolicy` remains the authority
for the complete source, target, session, TUN MTU, lower transport and original
SOCKS association. The independently paired final remains unchanged. The desktop
source is not admitted through the CONNECT or generic paired parser.

The preparation keeps application forwarding closed. Its original physical
policy pointer survives the authenticated in-place execution transition. Selected
DNS follows the execution selector while retaining the original resolver and
bootstrap policy. Static physical analysis removes only the verified original
desktop lower wrappers, then requires the same entry tuple inventory. The imported
SOCKS control and negotiated UDP association stay behind that entry; no proxy
address gains a direct physical permission, and the original final is not
converted to CONNECT/UoT.

Private adoption and heartbeat records bind the same original preparation,
physical source, transfer, selected epoch, Box/network/session identity and
observed interface/MTU. They grant no process, socket, TUN or lease authority by
themselves. The parent must still check its original live native owner. The saved
Last record retains the selected Local or Server result and original endpoint;
fresh discovery and selected-path proof remain necessary after retirement.

The separate native construction captures the original preparation, physical
policy, SOCKS source, caller-local registry and association owner before parsing
the complete native graph. A copied construction, foreign preparation/policy,
changed source or registry, canceled context, or ordinary Local construction
cannot borrow it.

## Executed shared evidence

With Go 1.26.3, `-race`, `-p=1` and the unchanged 180-second package bound, the
new `^TestDesktopSOCKSExecution` suite passed in 43.935 seconds. It covers all
desktop targets, all three paired entry modes across Server/Compare, both proxy
authentication modes, unchanged original members and physical scope, exact
canonical records, copied/foreign owners, changed derivation metadata, rejection
of longer execution topology, and retained Last restoration. Its Last case runs
the actual HTTP/TLS administration, authenticated Manager and proof logic; the
native route, DNS and worker boundaries are explicit fixtures.

Focused existing-family regressions passed in 82.970 seconds: CONNECT desktop
construction/transition/Last, original SOCKS execution, retained mobile SOCKS MTU
restoration, and the new generic imported-public capture. The concurrent Apple
generic execution capture and native public dispatch are preserved.

## Native construction result

The exact source passed through a fresh canonical desktop preparation
of sing-box `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray
`50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`. The actual `cmd/sing-box` native gate
passed both original roots and all 20 root/child records under `-race`. A compiled
overlay removing only the original construction identity check made the copied
construction case fail at its intended witness. The original prepared source was
unchanged. The two baseline roots completed in 3.70 and 7.12 seconds respectively.

All 15 changed shared Go sources, after the canonical preparer's declared import
relocation, and both new native construction templates were compared byte for
byte with the prepared core used by that gate. The run used the
ordinary full desktop native tags, Go 1.26.3, two compiler workers, `-p=1`,
`GOGC=50` and a workspace temporary directory. It did not create a live TUN or an
OS firewall lease.

The gate runs for every shipping desktop host before packaging. It retains the
existing 120-second test bound and separate 600-second compiler/process bounds.
Race remains enabled on supported targets, with the existing Windows ARM64
toolchain exception. The runner's seven checks require complete, unique,
successful records and reject missing cases, skipped/racing/failed work, foreign
or late events and partial output. They also require the gate in the common
shipping host branch, independently of the platform-specific acceptance suites.

## Remaining adoption

This increment does not enable public desktop SOCKS Server/Compare. The native
platform physical activation, child/parent session and retained MTU transfer,
public Connect/profile controls, and installed encrypted acceptance still need
to adopt these distinct owners. Native construction and shared models do not
establish kernel, installed TUN, encrypted path, or device acceptance. The supported
SOCKS Server/Compare topology remains the exact paired packet entry, one imported
SOCKS middle and paired final trio. Full historical requirements remain broader.
