## docs/SOCKS-DESKTOP-PARENT-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Typed SOCKS desktop Server/Compare parent — 2026-11-16

This unit connects the published schema-three SOCKS execution child to the
shipping desktop public controller, saved connection profiles, protection owner,
selected result, retained Last restoration, and macOS inherited process owner.
It preserves the complete original SOCKS source, including authentication and
UDP-association ownership; a generic or CONNECT preparation cannot replace it.

## Implemented source boundary

The admitted execution shape is exactly one paired WG/AWG packet entry, one
original SOCKS5 middle, and one paired Shadowsocks 2022 final. The entry retains
its independent canonical Off, AES-256-GCM, or AES-256-GCM plus XOR layer; the
middle and final remain Off. This is the current implementation boundary, not a
claim that broader requested ordered graphs are complete. Existing Local SOCKS
prefix support and the complete imported Shadowsocks execution prefix remain
separate. OpenVPN Server/Compare and arbitrary mixed imported execution graphs
are not added by this change.

The public method capability is `ordered_socks_trio_executions`; the distinct
per-profile source hint is `ordered_socks_execution_middle_supported`. Save,
Load, Update, public Connect, status, and restoration retain original profile
credentials and member identities. Source hints never grant a process or
physical permit. The original typed compiler, physical preparation, live child
receipt, selected proof, and retained process/network owner remain necessary.

Server and Compare both use the existing execution session. Compare's selected
execution, common endpoint, and fresh proof remain child-owned; restoration
uses retained Last and the selected MTU instead of rerunning Compare. Failed
child retirement retains the same owner for retry. A completed public request
does not revoke the retained connection, and a cancelled observer cannot supply
new authority. Darwin process arguments, host ownership, retirement, and MTU
capture retain the exact schema-three preparation and sealed physical pointer.

The Darwin protection dispatcher now admits this exact SOCKS execution trio
while validating every original saved member and the complete aggregate policy.
It still rejects longer SOCKS execution rows. Strongest-member Always protection
survives retirement and release. PF observations in the component fixtures use
an explicit temporary backend; they are not installed kernel acceptance.

## Executed evidence

- The actual Linux/amd64 `cmd/client` race image passed the strict parent baseline:
  16 roots and 139 required test records, with no skipped, foreign, missing, or
  race verdicts. The inventory covers the three target source preparations,
  authenticated and anonymous SOCKS, all three packet modes, Server/Compare,
  changed credentials or paired identity, substituted owner rejection,
  protection admission, Darwin inherited arguments, public HTTP/controller
  publication and status, selected Last/MTU restoration, changed Ready receipts,
  complete saved profiles, and the Linux transaction's original physical owner.
- Baseline source identity:
  `99be3132903865926354670a93af3503e888847d8b502999761ff1a2aa7670d7`.
  Baseline race-image identity:
  `af6a0230e4858bb8490cdfda58e0ef3aabaf5a695579ca0388effd7710325fbd`.
- Both compiled race causal controls passed at that same shipping-source
  identity. Disabling the schema-three constructor fails the original typed
  preparation assertion; dropping the retained SOCKS physical-pointer check
  fails the public Compare Server substituted-owner assertion. After those
  compiled runs, a parser-only review correction requires normal exit status 1
  and rejects panic, fatal, signal, timeout, build/link abort, stderr, and race
  diagnostics even after a valid assertion. The recorded actual control stdout
  transcripts pass that stronger parser. Their hashes are
  `2668999eef8f00a6d2339f3b13d2d9271f1caf99f89794de05f0966362e50453`
  and `cb8d967f2cd66175e0b8e528ff7bd6ec1a1033b232380df3fbae86570fe7ae76`.
  Production Go is unchanged; final runner input identity is
  `b625545aab5c61038f8d6da62e5a25512ce0527e959f99afa36179a426897e88`.
  This parser replay is not presented as another compiler or native run.
- The runner's seven checks passed, including missing/foreign/race/skip verdict
  refusal, exact inventories, native-host bounds, original compile and test
  deadlines, causal mutation anchors, and preservation of timeout output.
- Six existing CONNECT and imported-Shadowsocks compatibility roots passed 71
  race records, including original CONNECT source/owner/protection, HTTPS
  Compare Server publication, complete four/eight-node preparation, and the
  eight-node public Server path. The rebuilt compatibility image has the same
  identity as the baseline image above.
- Darwin/amd64 and Windows/amd64 `cmd/client` test cross-compilation passed
  within the original 240-second bounds. Their executable hashes are
  `c1cd5cbfb06b21a2efdf280741ae1148e0f9171bbe6157e2725bc45e0710e75a`
  and `0a7d6acad6a64759f9c20cca01bacd76471ae328185a5ff2b2989147e42c3e84`.
  Neither executable was run on its native host here.
- The complete shipping-source audit passed 68 tests. It retains the existing
  CONNECT and generic execution controls and adds 23 SOCKS source mutations:
  nine association/physical-owner controls and fourteen public/source-admission
  controls. Comments or string decoys cannot satisfy the actual function guards.
- The complete PF journal gate passed 20 baseline tests, 113 owned-PF tests,
  and 52 causal controls. Three new controls independently remove SOCKS
  execution admission, incorrectly widen its trio bound, or drop the original
  saved transport check; each fails its corresponding real assertion.

The public controller tests execute production HTTP decoding, operation capture,
source compilation, staged preparation, selected-result verification, status,
retained Stop, saved-profile operations, and restoration. Their child process,
kernel observations, and encrypted-path responses are explicit component
models. The separately published native-child evidence remains documented in
`SOCKS-DESKTOP-CHILD-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md`; it is not relabelled as an installed
parent or privileged kernel result.

## Required host gate

`deploy/build-native-runtime.py` requires
`test_desktop_socks_execution_controller_runner.py` and both baseline and causal
phases of `test_desktop_socks_execution_controller.py` on the matching native
host. Linux requires 139 baseline records, Windows 126, and Darwin 118. Darwin
runs its own captured retirement owner checks instead of a Windows child model.
The existing Windows ARM64 race exception is retained; other host phases use
`-race`. Each compiler retains its 240-second bound; each root retains its
120-second Go test bound and 135-second outer bound. Source and image identities
are checked around every baseline root and causal result. A target's native
execution cannot be inferred from another host or from a cross-compile.

Run the component gates from the repository root:

```sh
python3 deploy/test_desktop_socks_execution_controller_runner.py
python3 deploy/test_desktop_socks_execution_controller.py --go /path/to/go --phase baselines
python3 deploy/test_desktop_socks_execution_controller.py --go /path/to/go --phase controls
python3 deploy/test_native_shipping_audit_gates.py
python3 deploy/test_darwin_pf_journal.py
```

Installed Linux/macOS/Windows packet-path acceptance, Apple signing and device
execution, and any live router deployment remain separate evidence boundaries.
This source unit does not claim whole-project completion or a green release.

## docs/SOCKS-FINAL-LAYERS-FOUNDATION-EVIDENCE.md

# Captured SOCKS final-layer foundation

This additive source unit permits an independently captured Off, AES-256-GCM,
or AES-256-GCM plus XOR-whitening final in the exact three-member typed SOCKS
Server suffix. It preserves SS2022 credentials, the original negotiated SOCKS
TCP-control/UDP-association owner, exactly two native outbounds, no CONNECT UoT
on either suffix outbound, original source seals and leases, and joined cleanup.
Generic and CONNECT constructors remain closed to the SOCKS source.

The public desktop predicate and all native editors still require final Off.
This foundation does not establish public Server/Compare, installed kernel, or
full entry-to-final encrypted acceptance for the added final layers. Follow-up
public/backend and editor admission must retain that distinction and require
the hosted native evidence before claiming support.

## Mandatory acceptance

The existing authoritative release invokes `desktop-native-runtime.yml` for all
six native targets. Its ordinary build step now requires the final-layer runner.
The existing matching-host Linux block in `build-native-runtime.py` retains the
original SOCKS Server invocation and appends the new runner and native gate on
both Linux architectures. The original `TestOriginalSOCKS` ownership phase also
remains unchanged. The two new Python files and the consumed native workflow
are included in the exact runtime source fingerprint.

The new gate requires all 18 shared source verdicts, all 12 encrypted suffix
verdicts, and an actual counterfactual restoring the native Off-only guard. The
counterfactual must fail the exact whitening leaf at its original capture
witness; success, unrelated failures, duplicates, omissions, race reports and
foreign records are rejected. Every package retains a 120-second Go test limit
and 600-second outer compile/test limit. Three mandatory serial phases therefore
have an 1,800-second maximum; their caller has 1,900 seconds including finite
orchestration overhead. Existing stage and workflow job limits are unchanged;
this arithmetic concerns the new caller only, not the maximum of the full job.

The encrypted identity-negative case witnesses a wrong-identity response from
the actual final service. It is not a shipping Connected-proof-consumer verdict.
Other cases require changed key/whitening/route/token/DNS/cancellation responses
and retained association cleanup from the original encrypted provider.

## Local evidence and limits

On base `c05d634f9975ca79b8b7561011e409f9c9161e01`, the exact 18 new shared
records and 21 original shared records passed under race detection in 10.457
seconds. After reconciling the published hostname and Naive units onto
`4dfd50da17c437ec1a27e89ca8cfc4bd6a6b29e3`, the same exact 39 shared records
passed under race detection in 9.630 seconds. All six strict runner roots, the
full artifact/workflow parity audit and all 29 aggregate roots also passed.
The final actual native peer race image from the earlier `c05d634f` composition
compiled in 39.665 seconds;
its SHA-256 is
`90d2d558984c2eacb5f36ffe1f490ded5de36cd5f896f2405fcd8e9ce26c613a`.
The strict runner and the existing complete artifact/workflow parity and release
aggregate checks passed. Runner transcripts are synthetic contract tests, not
native networking evidence.

Actual encrypted execution stopped before traffic at the native interface query:
`route ip+net: netlinkrib: operation not permitted`. The same retained image's
unchanged original SOCKS Server suite reached the same environment boundary.
No interface-discovery bypass was introduced. One initial new route-negative
fixture also failed its provider constructor because it removed every route;
the corrected fixture retains a valid provider and changes only the requested
private TCP destination. Its final compiled replay clears that constructor and
reaches the same native interface boundary.

The twelve native verdicts and Off-only causal rejection remain required hosted
acceptance, not local passes. Source, compile, failure and corrected-fixture logs
are preserved in `socks-execution-final-layers-validation`, including the prior
seven-file frozen snapshot. This unit does not claim the final-layer requirement
is complete until native acceptance and the separate public/editor adoption are
finished.

## docs/SOCKS-LINUX-INSTALLED-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Installed Linux SOCKS Server and Compare gate

This unit adds a required native amd64/arm64 Linux acceptance gate for the published schema-three SOCKS execution family. The supported graph remains exactly one paired WG/AWG entry, one imported authenticated SOCKS5 middle, and one paired SS2022 final. It does not widen the graph or authorize a live private-server deployment.

The new provider fixture retains the original packet entry and paired final identities. Its actual SOCKS listener negotiates a distinct UDP socket under each authenticated original TCP control. The entry packet path admits only those live negotiated relay tuples. It does not grant UDP authority from a configured TCP port, substitute CONNECT, or wrap the final in UDP-over-TCP. The original shipping relay process implementation and lease-handler implementation are projected unchanged; the separate fixture launcher requires schema three and `SOCKSServerDevice` from the original typed SOCKS suffix. It rejects `NativeCarrierRequired`, which identifies a WG/AWG-first server suffix and must remain false for the SOCKS association family. HTTP administration and direct launch of the captured shipping executable remain explicit fixture adapters.

The 16 required installed cases cover WG and both AWG modes, entry Off/AES-256-GCM/AES-256-GCM plus whitening, Server, both Compare winners, and fresh retained restoration. Refusals cover wrong original credentials, authentication downgrade, a domain relay reply, a foreign advertised relay, TCP control EOF, wrong public identity, wrong paired final key, and failed selected DNS. Additional cases replace the original source and hold cleanup to prove bounded refusal with the same retained owner. The gate requires actual encrypted entry and final counters, authenticated public TCP and negotiated UDP counters, original selected DNS, exact comparison score and endpoint, fresh restoration without a second comparison, and joined worker/control/association retirement.

The reusable workflow consumes the current caller's exact native bundle on Ubuntu 24.04 amd64 and arm64. Both jobs are mandatory release aggregate dependencies. Exact source and image identity are checked before and after the native cases; durable receipts require a published commit. No case filter or optional success path is exposed. Existing CONNECT and generic host gates remain separate. Only the exact 16 new scenario names receive the established CONNECT execution budget of 240 seconds inside the test and 250 seconds outside it; all other guardian budgets retain their original values.

## Local verification and environment boundary

Canonical preparation uses pristine pinned sing-box `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray `50231ea` checkouts with the current complete native preparation pipeline, including the configured DNS transport ownership change. The local build and component results are recorded alongside the final patch manifest; installed success is not inferred from compilation or from mocked operating-system observations.

The final source combines published `c05d634f9975ca79b8b7561011e409f9c9161e01` (including the longer CONNECT foundation and required SS gate) with this isolated SOCKS unit. The privileged Linux/amd64 controller race image compiled in 232.051 seconds under the original 300-second bound, using `GOMAXPROCS=1`, `GOGC=20`, and `GOMEMLIMIT=800MiB`. Its SHA-256 is `e33829572b80ae5617d01278e5e5a9544ed7cf3da5adec4e369590976095484e`. Both required source/profile roots passed all 10 records from that unchanged image; the full captured Go source inventory was checked before and after execution. An earlier controller compiler was killed before tests and supplies no passing verdict.

The final combined `protocol/routervpnamnezia` Linux/amd64 race image compiled in 298.888 seconds within the unchanged 300-second bound, with the same constrained compiler settings. Its SHA-256 is `e8347e07a3a7a55f3a7a7fd339ad5626a91ec1d92348f83aca0e8ffbc93ca552`. That single immutable image then passed all 53 selected component records: the 16 original SOCKS policy-admission records and the 37 imported-SS source/provider compatibility records. The SOCKS regression checks all three entry modes and rejects cancelled, nil-policy and foreign-schema admissions. It uses the existing loopback source fixture with explicit TUN/platform observations; it is not a privileged host verdict. Complete captured native source and image identity remained unchanged through compilation and execution.

The exact physical endpoint guard passed all 15 component records and five compiled causal controls. The removed-purpose, omitted-namespace, replaced-interface-name, omitted-prefix-mask, and same-host-only variants each compiled and then failed only their required normal test assertions. The final Python integration checks passed: SOCKS runner 18, SOCKS workflow 7, SS runner 19, SS workflow 7, existing CONNECT runner 14 and workflow 7, release aggregate 28, and the complete shipping artifact/workflow parity audit. Both new workflows remain required alongside CONNECT.

The actual intended namespace prerequisite syscalls were checked in a disposable child: user, network, mount, and PID namespace creation succeeded without changing the parent. This workspace has no `/dev/net/tun` and lacks `nft`, `newuidmap`, `newgidmap`, and `sudo`. The local task does not provision substitute devices or relax the installed host guards. Actual TUN, nftables, route/DNS protection, protected installation, and complete native-carrier acceptance therefore remain mandatory hosted results.

The endpoint component controls compile the exact original physical-purpose check with three explicitly substituted operating-system observation calls. They test original and foreign namespace/interface/address/purpose observations. They establish the source guard's behavior only; they are not native interface or kernel proof. Negative-control parsing requires the exact normal test failure and rejects panic, fatal error, signal, build abort, race, skip, duplicate, missing, or foreign verdicts.

Hosted acceptance is still required at publication. These local results do not claim that the 16 privileged scenarios, either hosted architecture, native widgets, or live private-server deployment have passed.

## docs/SOCKS-MOBILE-EXECUTION-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md

# SOCKS mobile execution and retained MTU restoration

This increment connects the schema-3 SOCKS Server/Compare preparation and native
Box owner to the actual mobile CommandServer. The four separate public functions
are `NewRouterNodeChainSOCKSExecution`,
`NewRouterNodeChainSOCKSExecutionLaunch`,
`RouterNodeChainSOCKSExecutionFromLast`, and
`RouterNodeChainSOCKSExecutionNewCommandServer`. The launch kind is
`routervpn-node-chain-socks-execution-preparation-input`.

The factory retains the original plan, preparation, SOCKS source, caller-local
outbound registry, native association capture, CommandServer and StartedService.
Bind and every later native observation require those original owners. Another
server, equal-content preparation, copied plan or capture, retired owner, and the
separate Local SOCKS factory cannot borrow that authority. Capture cleanup joins
the retained original close even after cancellation; it does not replace the
independent native Instance close receipt.

The typed retained mobile capsule can be created only after both the original
shared execution and its actual native reader have joined retirement. It retains
the original source and policy pointers, observed reader nonce and generation,
target, interface, and MTU. The resulting preparation changes only the TUN MTU
to that original observation. The original source identity, selected execution,
same-endpoint comparison, credentials and ordered prefix envelope remain intact.
Fresh authenticated discovery is mandatory before adoption, and the selected
path must prove itself again without repeating Compare or querying the retired
reader. Ordinary JSON replay cannot recreate the private MTU authority.

The separate canonical launch parser preserves a saved Last record and its
selected Local or Server result. A saved record grants no live readiness. Missing
Last data, a schema downgrade, changed configuration identity, duplicate JSON
members, noncanonical bytes, or the generic launch kind are rejected.

## Executed shared and preparation checks

The shared race command covering ordinary, CONNECT and SOCKS mobile restoration
passed in 96.285 seconds. After adding the explicit authenticated-discovery
negative controls, the final SOCKS restoration race command passed in 39.401
seconds:

```sh
GOTOOLCHAIN=local GOMAXPROCS=2 GOGC=50 GOMEMLIMIT=1800MiB \
  go test -race -p=1 ./internal/mobilemultihop \
  -run '^TestMobileNodeChainSOCKSExecutionRestore' -count=1
```

These tests run the actual shared compiler, authenticated Manager, HTTP/TLS
proof transport and restore runtime. The TUN and original native readback are
explicit component fixtures. Android explicit Server and iOS Compare with a
Local winner both preserve the selected result and original owner while refusing
canceled or foreign-policy restoration.

The mobile preparation suite passed 14 checks. It verifies complete old-to-new
source inventory upgrades and refuses a partial SOCKS source/test pair. The
mobile runner suite passed 16 checks, including mandatory inclusion of all three
new SOCKS roots and the typed-factory causal control. Android and Apple build
scripts require the four exact generated public ABI signatures; both scripts
passed shell syntax validation.

## Native component result

The full libbox/daemon race baseline passed against a fresh canonical mobile
preparation of pinned sing-box
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d` and Xray
`50231eaff98ccc31b5cbd247a721c16e97fe5ec1`, using Go 1.26.3. It executed all 45
original roots and produced 148 passing root/child records. The three new SOCKS
roots contributed 20 records: all three paired entry modes in Server/Compare,
canonical source and launch identity, original CommandServer ownership and
empty-reservation retirement, refused foreign/copied owners, and both retained
Last choices without fabricated live proof.
All 23 native execution templates and the nine changed shared Go files were
byte-compared against their exact canonical projections in that tested core.
The native execution template digest is
`9c0eaa33e4fb8d57ae17658a0f707357bbc535b0d000714585dee0b1b67ca3dc`.

All 28 causal controls passed. Removing the original SOCKS factory check made
the exact ordinary-server negative fail with its required witness. The retained
CONNECT, restoration, Last, observation, measurement, MTU, lifetime, remote
cleanup and actual daemon acquisition/retirement controls also produced their
required failures, with no races or changed original sources. The gate preserves
the original 180-second runtime, 900-second baseline build and 600-second
individual control bounds.

The first preparation attempt composed mobile preparation over a previously
desktop-prepared tree and produced duplicate generated TUN lifecycle fields.
That attempt failed compilation and supplied no native test verdict. The fresh
tree uses the shipping mobile preparation order and the original ownership
checks; generated core fields were not edited to bypass that failure.
The fresh build then reached its final native links but exhausted the separate
temporary filesystem. Retrying with task-owned workspace `TMPDIR` and
`GOTMPDIR` produced the passing baseline above without changing source or limits.

## Remaining acceptance

This shared bridge does not itself establish an Android or Apple device result,
an installed system TUN, kernel protection, or encrypted physical packet-entry
acceptance. The separate hosted encrypted SOCKS server suffix and native client
association gates remain required. Android host integration is a dependent
increment. Apple host/UI and desktop physical execution adapters still require
their own typed adoption and acceptance. The supported SOCKS Server/Compare
topology remains the exact three-member path. This is not completion of the
full historical requirements.

## docs/SOCKS-SERVER-OWNER-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md

# Typed SOCKS server suffix: source and validation boundary

This change adds the administrator-captured schema-3 server suffix for one
original SOCKS5 import followed by its independently paired SS2022 final. The
entry remains the original WG, AWG Fast or AWG Strong identity that authenticates
the separate server lease. It creates no synthetic server packet endpoint.

The generic server compiler remains restricted to its existing paired and
imported-SS families. `NewNodeChainSOCKSServerPolicy` and
`NewNodeChainSOCKSServerPlan` are distinct typed entry points. Their descriptor
and request use separate v3 digest domains. The private operator compiler,
staged-source restoration and offline validation capture select that family
explicitly. Operator plans reject reuse of the client's paired identity as the
server peer, including the final SS member's independent WG identity material.

The compiled suffix keeps the original SOCKS credentials and literal endpoint.
Its public-path proof has no invented Router node identity or private API
credentials. The final retains its own private proof, key, endpoint and selected
DNS. The physical compiler records only the fixed TCP control tuple: the UDP
relay does not exist before SOCKS negotiation and is not a firewall exception
or a guessed static endpoint.

The native worker installs a caller-local SOCKS constructor, captures the actual
Box outbound and owns its physical dialer. Only the original library operation
can acquire its permanent TCP control and one authenticated negotiated UDP
socket. The retained association owns late handles, failed closes and the
joined control/UDP retirement. A bounded adapter exposes only the same TCP
half-close through native tracking wrappers; reads, writes and full close
remain on the original tracked connection. Copied captures, reconstructed policy objects,
foreign leases and canceled observers cannot acquire or retire the original
owner. The server worker restores the typed source, checks that same owner on
observation, and drains it before the original Box close can finish.

The private authenticated client/server front keeps its existing UoT-v2 wire
contract. The independently imported SOCKS suffix uses actual SOCKS UDP
association; its SS final does not acquire CONNECT's TCP-only/UoT transport.
The existing CONNECT family remains separate.

## Local validation

The focused shared/operator race suite passed against this change:

```sh
go test -race -p=1 ./internal/mobilemultihop ./internal/multihoprelay ./cmd/router-agent \
  -run 'TestNodeChain(SOCKSServer|CONNECTServer|SOCKSCapture|CONNECTCapture)|TestOrdered(RegistryParserCapture|PrivateCompilers)' \
  -count=1
```

This includes WG/AWG Fast/AWG Strong with authenticated and anonymous SOCKS,
source/capture/family/identity refusals, independent plan keys, private compiler
rejection without secret output, restoration, and existing CONNECT regressions.
The nine `deploy/test_server_node_chain_preparation.py` tests passed. Clean
canonical native preparation succeeded at the pinned core and Go toolchain.
The actual native owner and worker race suites also passed:

- `protocol/routervpnnodesocks`: `TestOriginalSOCKS*`, including retained control
  EOF, one retirement budget, failed half-close and concurrent close. The new
  server tests use the actual `route.ConnectionManager.TrackConn` over loopback
  TCP and require its manager entry to survive until remote EOF, then disappear
  on exactly one original full close. Missing half-close, cyclic/deep wrappers,
  typed-nil wrappers and a failed tracked half-close retain refusal/cleanup
  evidence. Completed successful and failed `Capture.CloseContext` results
  remain identical across 256 already-canceled caller retries.
- `cmd/sing-box`: `TestServerChain*`, including front credentials, pending proof,
  joined close, late native returns and original worker resource ownership.

## Required hosted encrypted check

The final source compiles with the native race build. The encrypted component
suite is mandatory in the Linux branch of `deploy/build-native-runtime.py`:

```sh
go test -race -p=1 -mod=readonly -tags="$NATIVE_TAGS" \
  -ldflags=-checklinkname=0 ./protocol/routervpnamnezia \
  -run '^TestRouterVPNNodeSOCKSServer' -count=1 -timeout=120s
```

`TestRouterVPNNodeSOCKSServerEncryptedOwnership` requires the real native suffix,
actual nonloopback-bound SOCKS control and negotiated UDP sockets, original
SS2022 ciphertext, paired private proof, public proof, selected DNS and joined
close for all three entry identity modes. Its companion refusal suite covers
wrong SOCKS credentials, authentication downgrade, a domain-valued relay,
wrong final SS key, cancellation with a live association and a foreign lease.
The suffix Box uses the real native interface monitor; no fake physical dialer
or empty platform interface inventory substitutes for that construction.

These encrypted tests could not execute their path in the local command
sandbox. All nine cases stopped during host interface discovery with
`route ip+net: netlinkrib: operation not permitted`. The observed host addresses
from `/etc/hosts` also failed actual TCP and UDP binds with `EADDRNOTAVAIL`, and
a UDP connect to a documentation destination, without sending a payload,
failed with `ENETUNREACH`. No skip, fallback address, weakened production guard,
or successful encrypted result was substituted. Hosted execution must pass
before claiming encrypted suffix acceptance.

## Remaining scope

This is a server-suffix source/owner unit. It does not complete the shared client
SOCKS Server/Compare execution policy, desktop/mobile adapters, remote-worker
end-to-end acceptance, device acceptance, or any live router deployment.
Longer imported server suffixes, multiple imported members and arbitrary
imported positions remain separate unresolved work. Local source, build,
hosted execution, packaged artifacts, physical devices and live evidence remain
distinct; this document makes no project completion claim.

## docs/SS2022-ORDERED-MTU-EVIDENCE.md

# Ordered SS2022 datagram envelopes and tight native MTUs

This evidence concerns the supported local three-member chain: a paired WG,
AWG Fast or AWG Strong entry, one imported SS2022 middle, and a paired AES-256
SS2022 final node. It does not establish physical path MTU or installed OS TUN
acceptance.

## Codec sizes and what they mean

The pinned `sing-shadowsocks2 v0.2.1` client encodes the following additional
bytes for an unpadded UDP message with a literal destination. The mandatory
`TestRouterVPNDesktopNodeChainImportedSS2022Envelope` executes that codec and
checks all six entries using a 1252-byte application payload.

| SS2022 method | IPv4 destination | IPv6 destination |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| AES-128-GCM | 50 | 62 |
| AES-256-GCM | 50 | 62 |
| ChaCha20-Poly1305 | 74 | 86 |

The independent `sing-shadowsocks v0.2.8` reference server's UDP reply header
contains an additional eight-byte remote session ID per SS layer. For example,
a 1252-byte payload through the paired AES final and an AES middle produces a
1380-byte IPv4 UDP packet inside WG/AWG on the request path and a 1396-byte
packet on the reply path. A ChaCha middle makes those packets 1404 and 1420
bytes. Those sizes include the enclosing IPv4/UDP headers and exclude the
physical WG/AWG ciphertext envelope.

These are datagram sizes, not TCP segmentation costs or an OS TUN ceiling.
The pinned native stack fragments and reassembles IPv4 datagrams. Subtracting
these numbers from every virtual packet would reject paths that actually
carry the datagrams successfully. Short DNS messages can also contain codec
padding; the numerical table intentionally describes unpadded messages.

## Required native delivery evidence

`TestRouterVPNDesktopNodeChainImportedMiddleTightMTU` constructs the real
native WG/AWG and SS outbounds and independent encrypted reference peers. It
recompiles the original first node's selected asset and independent identity
asset with an explicit MTU of 1280 or 1400. It does not edit a compiled endpoint
after capture. The server's actual WG/AWG stack MTU remains 1400, and the
captured shared virtual TUN remains 1280.

The matrix covers all three entry modes, all three imported SS2022 methods,
both tight first-interface MTUs, IPv4 and IPv6 application destinations, and
two payload sizes per destination:

- The UDP payload corresponding to the captured 1280-byte virtual packet:
  1252 bytes for IPv4 or 1232 bytes for IPv6.
- An explicit oversized native control corresponding to a 1500-byte virtual
  packet: 1472 or 1452 payload bytes. This control exercises native proxy
  datagram handling; it does not claim that the captured host TUN accepted a
  1500-byte packet.

Before the UDP checks, the fixture authenticates the original first private
health endpoint and checks its node proof. It then observes each real inner IP
packet at the reference WG/AWG device, before encryption and after decryption.
Every request and reply must arrive with identical application bytes. Every
observed IP fragment must have the exact expected length, offset and
more-fragments flag, and the complete set must reconstruct the cipher-specific
request or reply envelope. Observation order is allowed to differ, as real
packet delivery can reorder fragments.

The native matrix passed under the race detector. This includes fragmented
ChaCha replies whose complete 1420-byte inner packet exceeds the reference
interface's 1400-byte MTU, and oversized controls through 1280-byte entries.
The Linux installed-host runner executes both roots before its separate
installed namespace/TUN scenarios, requires all 134 exact verdicts, and
records the output digest. Its regression rejects omitted or duplicated
verdicts, failure, skip, panic, race diagnostics and unsuccessful execution.

## Deliberate limits

The encrypted reference uses IPv4 WG/AWG carrier addresses. IPv6 cases verify
application destination encoding and delivery through that carrier; they do
not establish IPv6-carrier fragmentation or a physical IPv6 path MTU. The
paired final remains AES-256. The client codec table directly checks ChaCha's
IPv6 address encoding, while the native chain's ChaCha middle uses an IPv4
next-hop address. Reply sizes for other address placements follow the pinned
server codec layout and are not additional physical measurements.

No production TUN ceiling, TCP policy, MTU controller, cache, Fixed setting,
Auto/Retest probe, cancellation or retained ownership rule changes in this
unit. Existing candidate-sized authenticated UDP probing still determines
live path behavior. Fragment delivery on an isolated native reference does
not guarantee delivery on arbitrary underlays, establish a nonfragmenting
path MTU, or replace the mandatory hosted installed-TUN and device gates.

The corrected helper derives each original WG identity independently while retaining the actual eight-member source and the forbidden ninth-member boundary.

Source registration and all new template inputs are included in the native builder's input digest; existing owner receipts upgrade only through the exact registered new fixture inventory.

Public desktop, Android and Apple chooser/session adoption is owned by separate follow-up units. This foundation alone does not claim those public flows or a fully green release.

The final source composition preserves published `4ae6a3c4fc9a1480ba4c730cfb94a5d3891b0e9e` and is guarded-forwarded to `8aabd9f879eb9ef6d7a0337eaaa487e5fc1687fc`. Its 21 mandatory Hysteria final-layer roots and all existing grouping controls are preserved alongside the six new typed candidate roots. Runtime evidence above refers to the frozen pre-Hysteria-layer input snapshot; this source composition does not relabel that execution as a new native run on the later parent.

The final composed grouping and native verifier suites passed all 50 Python tests. The native overlay and all 4,853 original prepared inputs remained byte-identical through the positive and omission phases. The source gate, canonical generated factories, full-source digest distinction and original source-receipt upgrade checks remain mandatory; all new source/template/test inputs are covered by the existing transitive builder inventory.

## docs/UNIX-NAIVE-H2-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Retained Unix Naive H2: source and acceptance boundaries

Prepared November 16, 2026 UTC. This unit implements the single paired Router
Naive H2 path in the existing retained Linux/macOS host. It does not establish
complete mode parity, a green release, physical-device acceptance or deployment.
The binding product scope remains
[REQUIREMENTS-RECONCILED.md](REQUIREMENTS-RECONCILED.md).

## Shipping path

Linux dispatches exact `naive-h2` through the existing owned controller instead
of the legacy connection fallback. The shared retained Unix physical-graph
predicate accepts the already-compiled H2 graph, and both Darwin PF state
parsers retain its exact mode through capture, readback and Off cleanup.

The shared Node compiler and retained native engine continue to own the
original source, node identity, credentials, private trust, selected DNS,
protection, effective MTU and restoration. Off, AES-256-GCM and
AES-256-GCM plus XOR whitening remain separate captured Start Layer choices.
Plain H2 uses its TCP lower; application UDP, including selected Home DNS and
the private MTU benchmark, travels through authenticated UoT-v2 over H2.
Resolver bootstrap remains separately captured by the existing policy.

This change does not admit Naive H3, REALITY, PQ REALITY, XHTTP or other
unproved retained Unix families. It leaves the existing Windows admission
and broader runtime implementation unchanged. Missing controllers, cancelled
captures and malformed policies still refuse before stopping a replacement
connection.

## Required native release acceptance

The new reusable [native workflow](../.github/workflows/unix-naive-h2-host.yml)
is a required dependency of the existing release aggregate. It consumes the
same run's exact-source runtime bundle on Linux AMD64/ARM64 and macOS
Intel/Apple Silicon. No caller input can select a historical source, narrower
case set or substitute workflow. The central artifact audit requires this job
alongside all pre-existing release dependencies, including installed Linux
SOCKS and imported-Shadowsocks execution.

| Boundary | Required execution |
|---|---|
| Linux retained host | 48 cases through the real installed controller, native engine, namespace, TUN and firewall |
| macOS retained host | 36 cases through the real installed controller, sealed native bundle, PF and utun |
| Authentication | Final username/password, trusted certificate, SNI, independently captured outer key and whitening refusals |
| Selected path | Authenticated original-node proof and selected Home DNS, including wrong-node and resolver failures |
| Lifecycle | Original protection, sealed temporary restoration, owned process and policy retirement |
| MTU | Actual original-path UoT datagrams, effective readback, retained Auto/Retest/Cancel and rollback behavior |
| Source and packaging | Current commit, complete runtime receipt, original image hashes, compiled case inventory, unchanged source before and after execution |

The original Linux case inventory and original 69-case macOS phase remain
required and unchanged. The new 48/36 cases execute in a separate bounded
phase; no existing operation or per-case deadline was enlarged. Each target
must emit all original case receipts, and the phase rejects missing,
duplicate, foreign, failed, skipped or raced results. A receipt is written only
after its owned workspace is retired.

The additional Linux component invokes the actual pinned Naive inbound and
Cronet outbound for all three Start Layers, with private node proof, selected
DNS, benchmark hold/release and source cancellation. Its kernel/TUN boundary
is explicitly modeled. The macOS phase uses the installed static native
composition; it does not claim dynamic-library evidence from a Linux fixture.

## Recorded local validation

The local working source was reconciled through `c05d634f`, `1e9b6626` and
`8510d196ab3dfdabefefa0510370064fd93a1f7f`. All newer Linux imported-execution,
installed SOCKS and Android DNS-fixture changes were preserved. The original
36-file `1e9b6626` snapshot and its patch remain recorded. Earlier component
results below retain their actual worktree base; they are not mislabeled as
packaged or exact-current native release results.

| Local observation | Recorded scope |
|---|---|
| Shared policy tests, based on `3f9f68df` | Race-instrumented tests passed 18 Linux/Darwin × Start Layer × protection leaves, exact restoration, rejected Unix families and existing SS/MAX regressions |
| Privileged-tag client, based on `4b14d026` | Full Linux client compilation passed; 145 source/component verdicts covered dispatch, refusal before replacement, captured Darwin policy, 25 private graph/peer guards and the actual compiled 48-case Linux inventory |
| Darwin PF model | The first run exposed missing H2 Off cleanup-state admission; after the narrow parser correction, all three model tests passed, including the 63-case capture matrix |
| Actual native peer, based on `052366b1` | The pinned 1,148-record shared/native overlay plus 381 registered peer records compiled with race instrumentation; image SHA-256 `403a5ea97088d7f226ae568cfe7ff7ff9b8bd35f0e147961cff1fad8d0791c91` |
| Release/source guards, based on `c05d634f` | Eight new runner tests, 28 aggregate tests, seven preserved Linux imported-execution workflow tests, the full artifact/workflow parity audit and structural/concurrency audits passed |
| Full Darwin client, based on `1e9b6626` | Privileged-tag Darwin/AMD64 source crosscompile passed in 132.77 seconds; all 34 captured source inputs remained unchanged. Image SHA-256 `c5cb5430f7cd595d86cd23d3c6a42da22f67387b54cbd1fd3389dba11711a7b8`; this is source/type compilation, not Apple runtime execution |
| Final source reconciliation, based on `8510d196` | Eight Naive runner tests, 29 complete aggregate tests, 18 SOCKS runner tests, both seven-test SOCKS/imported workflow suites, the full artifact/workflow parity audit and structural/concurrency audits passed; no new Go/native result is inferred from this source-only merge |

The full client compilation log has SHA-256
`1b755317f73123aaf83a446223ad8b48822d8441d7c208466b5037a0008b7e83`.
Source/parser tests and modeled policy cases establish their stated boundary;
they do not establish a native encrypted connection.

### Native traffic attempt stopped before traffic

The actual three-layer H2 component was attempted with the compiled native
peer and the verified pinned Cronet library. All three cases failed in
`desktopNodePeerInterface` with
`route ip+net: netlinkrib: operation not permitted` before listener creation.
No TLS, authentication, DNS or benchmark traffic was accepted. The runtime
log has SHA-256
`ab2821d45af343df2b3a865ffe0c06d4968814736c734d7394d85b038fdf902d`.
The required host gate retains real interface discovery; no synthetic
interface or success fallback was added.

Local relay staging first failed because Git VCS status was unavailable. Its
local-only retry disabled Go VCS stamping and independently recorded source
and image hashes. This is not a release-stamped artifact and does not alter
shipping provenance verification.

## Evidence still required

All four new hosted native phases, exact-current publication, real-device
network transitions/leaks/visual checks and private deployment remain pending.
The new gate cannot be considered passed from its source inventory or the
successful local compile alone.

Other retained Unix mode families remain open requirements. Protected dynamic
Direct Tor on Windows/macOS also remains open: the existing non-Linux import
policy and native guardian capability reject that combination. Existing
literal obfs4 protection and captured predecessor paths must be preserved;
this is not a claim that all Tor support is absent. See
`cmd/client/tor_bridge_capabilities.go`, `cmd/client/tor_bridge_import.go` and
`internal/mobiletor/desktop_physical_policy.go` for those distinct boundaries.

## docs/UNIX-REALITY-HOST-EVIDENCE-2026-11-16.md

# Retained Unix REALITY: source and native acceptance

Prepared November 16, 2026 UTC. This unit adds the single paired Router
`reality-vision`, `reality-pq-vision` and `reality-xhttp` paths to the retained
Linux/macOS host. The binding scope remains
[REQUIREMENTS-RECONCILED.md](REQUIREMENTS-RECONCILED.md); other outstanding
mode/graph/platform requirements remain outstanding.

## Shipping integration

The Linux dispatcher routes those exact modes through the existing owned
controller. The shared physical-graph admission and both Darwin PF state
parsers retain their captured mode through startup, readback and cleanup.
The existing Node compiler and native runtime retain the original node,
credentials, private trust, selected DNS, protection, MTU and restoration.
Off, AES-256-GCM and AES-256-GCM plus XOR whitening remain independently
captured Start Layer choices. Logical datagrams stay within the captured
Xray path; the physical policy admits the original TCP lower.

The shared tests derive the actual original Node profile and Linux/Darwin
policy for all three modes, layers and protection choices. They require
selected Home DNS through `proxy`, no substituted physical resolver or UDP
root, exact source ownership, and restoration of effective MTU without
changing the saved configured value or policy. Malformed mode aliases and
unproved families remain closed. See
[shared admission](../internal/mobilemultihop/desktop_node_admission.go) and
[actual policy tests](../internal/mobilemultihop/desktop_unix_reality_test.go).

## Mandatory native release gate

The [required workflow](../.github/workflows/unix-reality-host.yml) consumes the
same run's exact-source runtime bundle on Linux AMD64/ARM64 and macOS
Intel/Apple Silicon. Every target requires all three mode partitions. The
[executor](../deploy/test_unix_reality_host.py) first checks the complete
compiled inventory and 99 required fixture-contract records, then runs its
mandatory mode partition through the original installed controller.

| Target, per architecture | Vision | PQ Vision | XHTTP | Complete inventory |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Linux retained host | 48 | 51 | 54 | 153 |
| macOS retained host | 36 | 39 | 42 | 117 |

These are native case inventories, not a count of completed product
requirements. The new gate retains selected DNS, private node proof,
wrong-key/user/SNI/short-ID refusals, PQ-key and XHTTP-path refusals where
applicable, independently captured outer-layer keys and whitening, original
protection, sealed restoration and joined retirement. Linux exercises actual
MTU lease commit/rollback and effective readback; Darwin additionally exercises
the existing Auto/Retest/Cancel benchmark lifecycle through the new modes.
Existing Linux inventories and the original 69-case macOS phase remain
mandatory and unchanged.

The fixture accepts only the original bounded Xray and wrapper files and
verifies the real peer's source, address, physical lower and counters. Its
fixed private proof/DNS/benchmark routes sit after the authenticated reference
Xray inbound and expose no general Internet route. Native helpers retain and
hash protected copies of the pinned Xray and actual shipping whitening
binary. All workers, listeners, connections, reference processes and captured
owners must retire through the original lifecycle. The complete source,
bundle, image and commit identities are checked again before a receipt is
written; the owned workspace must already have been removed.

The new workflow is required by the release aggregate. It cannot be selected
out by an optional input or a reduced case list. Original per-operation,
per-root and per-case deadlines are unchanged. The independent new phase has
its own bounded job budget.

## Recorded local observations

Each result below retains its actual source boundary. Earlier evidence is not
relabeled as execution of later concurrent source changes.

- At candidate base `2fb8f5f7`, the actual shared Go 1.26.3 race tests passed
  54 Linux/Darwin × mode × layer × protection leaves plus their two roots,
  with zero failed or skipped records. Compile and execution took 45.067
  seconds within the original 120-second caller bound; the Go test bound was
  60 seconds. Log SHA-256:
  `080ebbb0aeb0f9f062b213b60c4d93f2ad251d9141c85287cb0ff17c5f66d0cb`.
- At candidate base `74d44e8c`, 47 installed-Linux runner checks passed. The
  composed candidates at `3a95f180` and `0b474422` passed ten REALITY runner
  checks, eight existing Naive runner checks, 32 aggregate checks and the full
  artifact/workflow parity audit. One initial Naive dependency-omission test
  selected the former first dependency; its test-only anchor was corrected
  to remove that named dependency exactly, retaining the same required
  refusal. The failed initial attempt remains recorded.
- At candidate base `3a95f180`, the complete actual prepared native peer
  compiled with normal race flags in 236.818 seconds within the original
  300-second bound. Image SHA-256:
  `f493c5a4021d4f2a9af764cf3c9597f41929a87b4fba016b321653e87b83bb74`.
  All 5,397 captured source inputs and all 915 pinned Xray inputs remained
  unchanged after the attempts below.
- All eight existing native Router source-capture records passed. Log
  SHA-256:
  `ef79ca654b174c44a1b930e71b9594bf9b7db4b1d06e858d94073e364b8702e4`.

The actual reference relay initially stopped before compilation because Go
could not obtain local Git build-version status. A separate local diagnostic
built both real reference binaries with `-buildvcs=false` and independently
captured their complete source/image identities. The native peer itself used
normal flags. This local diagnostic does not establish release-stamped
reference artifacts; shipping reference builders and release provenance
requirements are unchanged.

The real Vision traffic attempt then refused all three layers at native
interface discovery with `route ip+net: netlinkrib: operation not permitted`,
before listener creation or encrypted proof/DNS/benchmark traffic. Its log
SHA-256 is
`3753beaaa21c79c9ccfddf7e9623b5f465265c262fdfdc6563024b572596b330`.
PQ Vision and XHTTP traffic were not repeated after the same shared environment
failure. No native discovery bypass or synthesized successful traffic was
added. The kernel/TUN boundary in this peer component is explicitly modeled;
installed host acceptance remains the separate required workflow.

## Current composition and remaining evidence

The original 37-file candidate was preserved before composition with
`0b474422`. The newer Android, OpenVPN foundation, bounded public-point
validation and desktop SOCKS final-layer changes were retained. The only
intersecting candidate file was the native input inventory; its independent
additions were composed with a normal three-way merge and all other 36 owned
files retained their exact bytes.

The full current Linux privileged client compile was attempted once with normal
Go 1.26.3 race flags and the original 300-second caller limit. The compiler was
killed after 120.086 seconds, before producing an image or executing tests; the
caller deadline did not expire. Its log SHA-256 is
`9da8f444fae159f3b50d4bcf8f364eba64fb514f44c59d1be56c756cfba4b0e5`.
The observed signal does not establish a specific memory or cgroup cause.
There was no relaxed compiler flag, removed case or repeated full-client retry.

A separate bounded source-contract check then compiled the two complete
original REALITY fixture files, their actual helper, and their actual shared
source closure with normal race instrumentation. All 99 required records
passed (43 substitution, 16 named-credential, 40 peer-receipt records,
including roots). Compilation took 4.275 seconds within its 180-second
component bound; the three roots took 1.117, 1.068 and 1.018 seconds under their
unchanged 30-second Go / 40-second caller bounds. All 632 captured/current
inputs, all 80 compiler-selected source files, the image and six logs were
verified after execution. The image SHA-256 is
`ba5395942aeb4b8a30357b1f0cbf65d6ab3f9409b17d56b1fc975afaa61a0e94`;
run receipt SHA-256 is
`b3618c3c2988803931eef1640b972e271ca9f83c996bdf12cd5b57af22d63cc3`.
This is extraction of the actual source, not a replacement parser or a full
client/native-host compile. The prepared earlier snapshot was retained and
was not relabeled as the current module.

The full current Linux/macOS builds and native gates, exact-SHA packages and
publication, real-device leak/network-transition/visual checks, signing and
private deployment still require their separate evidence. No current green
release or physical acceptance follows from these local component results.

## docs/WINDOWS-CONNECT-WORKER-EVIDENCE-2026-11-15.md

# Windows CONNECT execution worker correction — 2026-11-15

## Scope and cause

The installed Windows CONNECT execution gate added in `4e7d631269060525396750d9930bad451f4cdbc1`
projects the original encrypted server worker onto its Windows counterparty.
The original image capture required Unix executable bits, and the worker reader
required exact `0700` directory and `0600` file modes. Those checks cannot admit
the Windows file mode representation. This prevented the gate from reaching the
encrypted provider rather than identifying a tunnel failure.

The Windows fixture now projects the shipping private-runtime DACL and file
identity checks into that original worker and peer. It captures the Windows
machine directories for `SystemRoot`, `WINDIR` and `PATH`, and retains the original
Cronet loader, absolute library resolution, and compiled library size/hash seal.
The fixture builds and hashes its separate counterparty worker. Its receipt
identifies this Windows permission/environment projection explicitly.

The installed controller, physical alias ownership, original worker IPC and
protocol bodies, and fourteen required network scenarios remain in the gate.
The production Linux server permission checks retain their existing behavior.
The three added fixture/control inputs are included in the central native build
digest and its per-input omission controls.

## Executed local evidence

The repair was independently prepared against `4e7d6312` and integrated after
`c833afd2ed7be3891aa15bef4ca644c142548932`. Focused host and workflow tests were
rerun against the integrated source, including the newer worker stop diagnostics.

| Check | Result and boundary |
|---|---|
| Host runner | 12 roots passed, including the retained 20 alias records / 3 causal controls and 15 cleanup records / 4 causal controls. |
| Workflow contract | 7 roots passed. |
| Combined controls | 26 roots passed in the preparation lane. |
| Native artifact/workflow parity | Passed in the preparation lane. |
| Fresh pinned core and Xray preparation | Module tidy, dependency verification and AWG verification passed. |
| Full Windows AMD64 peer | Crosscompiled successfully with Go 1.26.3. |
| Full Windows AMD64 worker | Crosscompiled successfully with the final native library loader and seal projection. |
| Actual private-path control module | Crosscompiled successfully for Windows AMD64 and ARM64. |

These are local source, runner and compilation results. They do not establish
native Windows DACL, IPHelper, WFP, TUN or real-device acceptance.

## Required Windows execution

Before its existing network cases, the gate now requires ten native private-path
cases: private input, Windows mode representation, public input, NULL DACL,
unknown principal, writable code, permitted public code reads, hard-linked input,
replaced input, and captured machine environment. Three separately compiled
guard-removal controls must fail at the expected original check: code writer
permissions, private input reader permissions, and captured `SystemRoot`.

The actual Windows CI jobs must execute these controls and all fourteen installed
network scenarios. Publication of this correction does not itself establish
that execution, a completed release, or physical-device acceptance.


---

## docs/WINDOWS-IDENTITY-SOURCE-FIXTURE-EVIDENCE.md

# Windows identity source-copy fixture

## Failure and correction

The Windows native contract run [43157301549](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/43157301549), job 130981823334, failed at the source-copy fixture on `53ceb91433025b5940936c4b4aaf3a1e0db0f77d`.
The shipping desktop preparer now invokes the ordered application gate, admission,
and native owner preparers. Those require the actual pinned native checkout.
The source-copy fixture intentionally uses an empty directory and already replaces
its other native prerequisites with explicit component boundaries.

The fixture now includes the three ordered preparers in that same explicit
boundary and requires each actual shipping dispatch to receive the original
resolved directory exactly once. The real shared-source copying loop, byte-level
checks, and incomplete or changed source refusals continue to execute. Production
preparation and its original-core checks are unchanged.

## Recorded checks

- Before the correction, the same source-copy test reproduced the hosted failure:
  `git rev-parse HEAD` exited 128 in the non-Git fixture directory.
- All 24 Windows identity runner checks passed after the correction.
- A causal control removed the three dispatches from the actual preparer function;
  the corrected fixture rejected the missing calls.
- `git diff --check` passed.

These local checks establish the fixture composition and source-copy contract.
They do not establish native Windows socket, token, ALE, or WFP execution; those
remain required in the hosted Windows job for the published commit.

## docs/WINDOWS-IMPORTED-HOP-RECEIPTS-2026-11-16.md

# Windows ordinary imported-hop latency receipts

This unit extends the Windows per-hop viewer's receipt reader to retain every
captured interior public hop in an ordinary Local path. A public hop must still
have its original node, position, source/configuration, session generation,
request, timing method and complete fifty-response receipt. Selected Local or
Server execution labels keep their existing penultimate-only public position.

The change does not create a route or grant a graph permission. The controller
remains responsible for returning an authenticated original capture; native
multiple-import source and the editor/controller adoption have separate units.
It contributes to the original per-hop and external-node requirements without
closing the remaining graph, platform or live acceptance scope.

## Actual verification

The shipping PowerShell source and test/wrapper were executed from private
source commit `1f34b5e36cf1e2af00269acb8be2e4235f12711f`. The original controller
transport module is byte-identical to published parent
`76de49ad7af75b06853e445ffaff885de3d2d303`; all four executed input hashes were
unchanged before and after the run. This publication contains those exact three
changed source/test files and this evidence record.

Command: `python3 deploy/test_windows_ordered_latency.py --shell pwsh --self-test`.
The actual local PowerShell executable was the existing toolchain installation,
with telemetry disabled before launch. The existing 50-second bound for every
PowerShell child was unchanged. The whole run completed in 63.480 seconds,
returned zero and left no process in its owned process group.

| Evidence | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Shipping PowerShell model | 1,228 checks passed, including independent earlier and multiple public positions. |
| Actual loopback HTTP | All ten scenarios passed: success, public, exit, eight-member CONNECT, early cancel, cancel, owner close, partial result, foreign capture and transport failure. |
| Intentional defects | All thirteen source controls were rejected by their required `ORDERED CONTROL` witness. |
| Source and cleanup | All four captured inputs unchanged; runner and owned children joined. |

The controls cover imported position, old three-member-only CONNECT, selected
labels, selected receipt label, mixed inventory, three-member-only inventory,
private-middle timing budget, foreign capture, premature drain, partial fifty
samples, unjoined HTTP, invalidated history and invalidated drain.

The complete output SHA-256 is
`4c9fc31ed3684296d00d9d1a017fcb09a8b619bb9fab6b64944b2b610a09acba`.
The unchanged transport module SHA-256 is
`2c979c81caf5805d515bfa3f73e110cce1a866e305edec981301a8415b0df3e9`.

## Acceptance boundary

This run exercised PowerShell source/model behavior and real local HTTP on a
Linux host. Its authenticated-controller responses were explicit fixtures;
they do not prove a tunnel. WPF controls execute only in the existing Windows
workflow, which continues to run this gate under both PowerShell 7 and Windows
PowerShell 5.1. Exact-SHA Windows builds, actual widgets, installed networking,
physical devices and the full release retain their separate evidence gates.

## docs/evidence/windows-xray-bootstrap-2026-11-25/README.md

# Windows Xray bootstrap, November 25, 2026

Native Windows amd64 and arm64 passed the original nine Xray traffic cases within the unchanged 90-second limit on commit `526760a3e56f2e3370d1649622e1ca7b64e30b08`. Both jobs also passed the startup-records check, the three reference-detector controls and the wrong-credential root. The elapsed times below come from the Go test output in each job log. [GitHub Actions run 45723168446, attempt 1](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/45723168446)

| Windows architecture | Native job log | Xray traffic | Wrong credential | Original artifact | Extracted receipt |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
| amd64 | [139586277375](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/45723168446/job/139586277375) | 26.36 s | 35.77 s | [15073703660](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/45723168446/artifacts/15073703660) | [JSON](amd64/windows-startup-amd64-bootstrap-baselines.json) |
| arm64 | [139586277419](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/45723168446/job/139586277419) | 25.30 s | 34.25 s | [15073273553](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/45723168446/artifacts/15073273553) | [JSON](arm64/windows-startup-arm64-bootstrap-baselines.json) |

Each traffic run exercised `reality-vision`, `reality-pq-vision` and `reality-xhttp` with each of the three Start Layer settings `off`, `aes-256-gcm` and `aes-256-gcm+xor-whitening`. These jobs exercised encrypted reference-peer traffic with the bootstrap fixture's modeled permit and tunnel. Kernel WFP enforcement has its own native gate. Whole-release qualification requires the complete gate set on the release commit. [Exact-SHA release evidence](../../EXACT-SHA-RELEASE-EVIDENCE.md)

Each downloaded ZIP matched GitHub's reported byte count and SHA-256. Its single 4,006-byte JSON receipt is preserved with the original Windows line ending.

## 4. Retained transformation facts: incomplete, not executable restore payloads

### 4.1 New CONNECT native peer template

mobile/desktop/node_imported_connect_xray_test.go.tmpl was derived from the published mobile/desktop/node_imported_proxy_xray_test.go.tmpl. The recorded extraction began at the function named desktopImportedProxyXraySource and ended before the comment beginning "The callback counts requests only after". That body was renamed and adapted for real HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT, then combined with new header/setup/cases and a physical-owner support test.

The final template was formatted with gofmt. The complete transformation script, appended Go body, and final formatted file bytes are not retained here.

Recorded behavior:
- Both HTTP and HTTPS CONNECT use the actual native HTTP outbound and captured original credentials.
- HTTPS uses the exact captured SNI and trust choice; wrong name and wrong trust require actual typed certificate causes and independently observed TLS server rejection.
- The captured paired REALITY final is compiled by the validated shared imported-entry path. It is not recast as an ordinary imported external final.
- The compiler-only plan in the source loader is closed after validating its nine-string receipt.
- Each per-case compiled plan has registered cleanup.
- The entry stream and final source/credentials remain distinct; final proof/token and selected DNS have independent counters.
- The generic native HTTP outbound has no Close method; the fixture therefore requires lower.empty before registry close after final-engine cleanup.
- Retired owners must not admit another physical dial or authenticated entry stream, including an existing connection that could otherwise bypass a physical-dial-only witness.
- A replacement final proves its own traffic before and after original-owner retirement.
- Existing physical interface/Netlink inventory guards remain intact.

### 4.2 Separate selected-DNS native fixture

mobile/desktop/node_imported_connect_xray_dns_test.go.tmpl was derived from the published mobile/applexray/udp443_traffic_test.go.tmpl. The recorded extraction copied constants/resolver helpers up to the nativeUDP443Physical declaration, renamed them to the desktopCONNECTXrayDNS family, omitted the duplicated Home resolver by starting the resolver-kind loop at one, delegated certificate generation to the optional-SAN helper, and used desktopNodeXrayDNSRoute.

It then appended captured-source, actual pinned-transport, DNS exchange, audit, owner/cancellation, and DoT probe-admission cases. Final Go template formatting used gofmt. The complete appended Go body and final bytes are not retained in this document.

Recorded behavior:
- Both CONNECT protocols carry actual selected DoT, DoH, and DoH3 through the captured REALITY owner.
- Real DNS wire requests and validated responses are required, not merely a QUIC handshake or UDP echo.
- Exact selected server, port, name, trust, path, flow, and owner are preserved.
- Original explicit xtls-rprx-vision-udp443 is captured before compiling DoH3 cases. The ordinary Vision capture is not rewritten after compilation.
- Ordinary Vision first proves Home UDP53 and then actually refuses UDP443.
- Wrong-owner controls first prove the original owner and then remove only the captured live proxy owner, without changing selected DNS bytes.
- Wrong SNI/trust controls require typed QUIC certificate causes, not a generic timeout or refusal.
- Only exact pinned DoT reuse probes ID1/A and ID2/AAAA are separately admitted; application query ID0 remains separately required.
- Invalid probe shapes, foreign IDs, other transports, and unexpected resolver traffic remain rejected/countable.
- Inflight HTTP3 cancellation reaches and joins the original request.
- TCP-only physical entry ownership and drain-before-fixture-teardown remain required.

## docs/testing/client-measurement-clock.md

# Checked client DNS and RTT measurement clocks

This source unit adopts the existing checked `hopmeasure.StartInterval` clock
in four client measurement paths:

| Path | Preserved measurement meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Selected DNS OS-resolver proof | One bounded resolver question after verification of the captured selected DNS runtime |
| Quick node latency and Fastest | Actual direct TCP handshakes, with discovery outside the measured samples |
| Active private-path latency | Complete HTTP response RTT, with the paired node identity proved for every sample |
| Durable node statistics | Separate disconnected direct TCP benchmark, with at least 50 and at most 200 samples |

Windows uses the checked native performance counter; other platforms retain
the shared monotonic clock. The existing request deadlines, pacing, drain and
cancellation completion clocks, wall-clock receipt dates, selected resolvers,
HTTP credentials and labels retain their original roles.

Each sample captures its own interval before measured network work. A start or
read failure, zero/backward interval, or nonfinite result cannot earn a score.
Error-returning paths preserve the original clock cause; the DNS proof and
HTTP handler retain their existing reason/message response shapes. A cancelled
caller cannot adopt a clock readback. DNS verifies the original registered
runtime again after readback. Durable publication still requires the original
tracker, session, profile, physical network, selection and mutation admission.
A late timing failure returns before persistence and leaves saved statistics
unchanged; a failed network sample retains its original incomplete/disqualified
meaning.

The durable benchmark previously used `math.Max(0.001, elapsed)` after
microsecond truncation. That invented minimum is removed. Ordinary results
retain three-decimal display rounding; when that rounding would erase a real
positive interval, the original measured value is retained. A stable incremental
mean prevents repeated addition from placing a constant tiny interval below
its observed minimum and incorrectly disqualifying a valid Fastest result.
The Fastest completeness and failed-sample rules are unchanged.

## Executable evidence

Go 1.26.3 on Linux produced **212 passing race verdicts across 45 test roots**
for the focused client selection. The selection covers the new clock tests and
existing private-node identity, DNS ownership, cancellation, Fastest, telemetry
and profile-statistics contracts:

```sh
go test -race -count=1 ./cmd/client \
  -run '^Test(Client(RTT|DNSClock|DurableLatencyClock|ClockUses)|PrivateLatency|ProfileLatency|Fastest|DNSProof|SelectedDNSLookup|NativeMultihopDNSRuntimeSnapshot|ConnectionTelemetry|ConnectionLive)' \
  -skip '^Test(ProfileLatencyTerminalSaveOwnsCancellationAndProfile|ProfileLatencyPartialActualTCPPreservesSavedBenchmark)$' \
  -timeout=180s
```

The new tests run real TCP handshakes and paired HTTP exchanges with deterministic
per-call clock readings. The selected-DNS tests retain a real registered private
runtime snapshot and a bounded resolver callback. Positive nanosecond and
submillisecond values, ordinary display precision, initialization/read failures,
zero/negative/nonfinite readings, cancellation during readback, and replacement
of the registered DNS runtime are exercised. The actual durable handler performs
50 measured handshakes plus separate discovery; a fault on sample 25 cannot
salvage earlier values, overwrite saved statistics, or start further samples.
Replacing the process tracker before publication also prevents persistence.

A disposable source overlay reinstated the original 0.001 ms floor in the
actual durable sample loop. The tiny-interval handler test then failed because
all persisted/distributed values became 0.001 instead of the supplied actual
0.000001 ms. No repository test or source was modified by this control.

The two roots excluded above were attempted first without exclusions. This
container cannot read the required physical network metadata. The terminal-save
root refused all six scenarios before its first measured TCP connection; the
partial-TCP fixture consequently waited for a listener it never reached.
An overlay of the original `extras.go`, `telemetry.go` and `dns_proof.go`
reproduced both failures exactly. The tests remain unchanged and required in
the normal matching-host suite; these are local execution limits, not passes.

The shipping `cmd/client` executable cross-compiled for Windows AMD64 and ARM64.
`deploy/test_desktop_native_dependencies.py` passed all 21 tests, including the
actual builder source inventory and link/dependency boundary. `git diff --check`
passed. The cross-builds do not establish Windows counter execution or package
release acceptance.

## Remaining scope

This unit covers the four paths above. Other routed-candidate and throughput
timers, including `via_entry_candidates.go` and `multihop_execution.go`, require
their own adoption and contract verification. Matching-host execution, exact-SHA
packages, physical selected-path measurements and network-transition acceptance
remain separate requirements.

## docs/testing/darwin-renewal-fixture-capture.md

# Darwin grouped-renewal fixture capture

The November 9 source `83c0ae617858e331bbb7bc1fd3385120938d45c6`
failed the macOS Intel native ownership gate in
[run 42583227125, job 129046739068](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/42583227125/job/129046739068).
`TestDesktopDarwinGuardianKernelSOCKSAssociations` failed both IPv4 and IPv6
`group-renewal` and subsequent `renewal-authority` checks. The recorded first
loss was an expired child receipt while its next operation was applying;
its acknowledged parent was still valid. The error was observed while the
manual fixture captured a group, before that group's PF renewal call.

The fixture acquired each peer operation with blocking `Lock`, retaining
already-acquired parent operations while waiting for later children. It also
bypassed the broker's one-group `renewing` owner. This allowed the manual
capture to interfere with the monitor's existing renewal transaction.
Production capture already uses a reserved group owner and nonblocking peer
admission. The repair applies those ownership rules to the native fixture.

The fixture now reserves the original broker renewal slot, acquires all peer
operations without blocking, and releases every partial peer and the slot
before a bounded cancellation-aware admission wait. It reads each original
receipt again after acquisition. Only a complete fresh group reaches the
existing native renewal operation; native execution is not retried.

No production PF code, five-second readback age, seven-second permit cap,
three-second control budget, socket identity check, or receipt timestamp is
changed. The original native PF assertions and parent-derived expiry checks
remain mandatory.

Local validation executes the exact platform-independent capture helper and
its focused test bodies extracted from the Darwin test source, using the real
broker/peer types with modeled native policy. Six race cases cover immediate
capture, a held child, capture after that child joins, an existing monitor
transaction, caller cancellation, and a stale child. The complete group
preserves receipts and releases operations and the renewal slot. Two deliberate
source mutations that retain a partial parent lock or borrow the monitor slot
fail at their corresponding ownership assertions.

The existing Darwin runner now requires the parent and all six capture-case
verdicts. Its sixteen SOCKS boundary/runner tests pass locally. A broader
45-test runner attempt passed 44 tests and was blocked only by the environment's
refusal to create Unix sockets in the separate original-channel lifetime test.
These are fixture and runner results, not native Darwin acceptance. The new
source still needs its complete Intel and ARM64 hosted kernel runs; this repair
does not claim to prove that every prior expiry had the same cause.
