## docs/testing/desktop-imported-native-protection.md

# Protected imported desktop connection

## Retained Windows parent and Speed Lab

Protected imported entry-to-paired-final connections now use a typed retained
parent and the corresponding native child. The parent captures all seven
private constructor inputs, authenticates the exact child and journal owner,
and keeps the original constructor alive through joined retirement. Protected
captures cannot silently fall back to the legacy helper. Partial startup is
published to the cleanup owner before entering the child, so cancellation and
failure retain responsibility for the entered work.

Daily connection and temporary Speed Lab execution use that same composition.
Speed Lab prepares the successor before suspending the predecessor, proves the
separate entry and final identities plus selected DNS, and restores the captured
execution choice and latest live MTU. An A-to-B-to-A restoration cannot borrow a
replacement process, constructor, connection operation or private workspace.
The parent also checks recorded cancellation intent before its asynchronous
cancellation callback is delivered.

The Auto/Retest eligibility and status paths replay the original sealed
constructor and use its merged MTU profile. This preserves restrictive proxy
entry Fixed/Jumbo policy instead of considering only the final node. A packet
entry with an independent fixed MTU still permits a compatible shared Auto
policy. Changed envelopes, derived graphs, entry policy or final credentials
cannot authorize the readback. These rules supplement the existing original
operation, network generation, rollback and cancellation guards.

This publication wires the retained Windows composition. The retained Linux
adapter is a separate implementation under validation; macOS retained imported
succession also remains separate. Existing generic Unix imported execution is
not evidence for those retained adapters.

## Source and admission

The Windows native child accepts imported-entry to paired-final graphs through
the explicit `--imported-source-sha256` family and private
`imported-source.json` envelope. This source flag is mutually exclusive with the
ordinary Node source flag. The child authenticates the inherited controller and
original journal pipe before reading either private constructor. It replays the
seven captured imported inputs, derives `DesktopImportedPolicy`, and compares
the exact derived source, config, restored MTU and staged graph bytes. It does
not retry another policy parser on failure.

The shared physical boundary accepts only the sealed concrete Node or imported
policy. The imported constructor remains distinct and live through physical
retirement. Final profile identity still binds journal admission; separate
entry and final identities, credentials and modes remain in the imported
capture. The existing Windows baseline, predecessor, durable commit and
retirement machinery retains the exact policy pointer and source capture.

## Actual native graph

Box preparation compares complete parsed shipping options with the independently
derived graph before its network service can construct an OS source. Imported
graphs own the captured TUN and two separate loopback proof listeners:
`multihop-entry-proof` routes to `entry-wg`, and `multihop-proof` routes to the
final `proxy` selector. Final selected DNS remains on the final path. Entry and
final LAN, IPv6 and MTU restrictions remain part of the immutable capture.

An imported SOCKS5 entry carrying datagrams uses the distinct
`routervpn-desktop-imported-socks5` outbound. Only its captured numeric TCP
control enters the static physical destination set. The existing native SOCKS
client authenticates a separate control connection for each operation before
the broker can grant that operation's negotiated UDP relay. A sibling, a later
native root or the ordinary Node family cannot borrow that relay permission.
Original per-operation close and mixed-failure handling are retained.

The imported entry and its actual Node lower carry the same caller context
through outbound, owner, manager and broker observations. A cancelled observer
returns while the original mutex remains held; it cannot leave a waiter that
later publishes a socket capability or cancel the healthy connection owner.
The native gate retains causal controls that restore each old ambient wait.

## MTU and retirement

Physical MTU replacement carries one original `mobilemtu` operation through the
owner lock, manager lookup, native readback, grant retirement and new binding.
An already-entered native transaction may finalize its returned rollback using
only the original remaining cleanup allowance. It cannot renew that allowance
from the connection lifetime. Repeating adoption reads the exact already
attached returned reader without rebinding. Native or policy failures cannot be
hidden by a cancellation retry.

Uncertain grant retirement revokes the original lifetime immediately through
`CancelAdmission`. Joined Box teardown retains the actual source and any entered
OS operation until its outcome is known. A still-existing TUN alone does not
establish a usable protected path.

## Executable coverage and limits

`deploy/test_desktop_node_physical.py` requires the imported command, native
factory, Box and SOCKS verdicts on Windows, including the individual altered
source and ownership cases. The command tests reject changed private entry or
final inputs, rehashed staged graphs, missing or mismatched restored MTU,
ordinary source records, conflicting flags and missing authenticated journals.
The Box tests construct real native managers and both actual proof listeners,
while replacing only the privileged TUN and OS socket source. The SOCKS tests
use real authenticated control and relay sockets with explicitly modeled
per-socket OS grants.

The cross-platform MTU tests exercise the actual native replacement manager,
including exact rollback, held locks, expired original cleanup and failure
after an entered grant retirement. Existing ordinary Node, external SOCKS,
DNS, admission and retirement suites remain required.

### Recorded executions

The controller race run at local validation snapshot
`7060aae83d6d15d135d6f510af5447ba7b7b4e51` passed 59 imported-parent verdicts
across 11 roots and 105 imported-MTU verdicts across 19 roots, with no skips in
either group. The MTU group includes the twelve new merged-policy verdicts.
Its compiled binary SHA256 is
`f4f96077b610bee2ff4af6bbf1581704f6ff64ef655cab98947c73337456617d`.
Other selected groups in that run are not folded into those counts: the Tor
prelaunch cancellation-cause failure was repaired and separately rerun before
the Tor publication.

Three compiled controller fault controls independently restored legacy-helper
fallback, lost the derived constructor identity, or retired the original
constructor before its child. Each compiled successfully and failed its
intended executable assertion while the unchanged baseline passed. Five native
fault controls similarly restored ambient waits in the lower dialer, owner,
manager, imported SOCKS dispatch and entry observation; each failed the intended
caller-cancellation assertion.

At `f89a1caf387308ce6026e54a32ac1ac97fbf0553`, the application controller and
retained-process test packages compiled for Windows AMD64 and ARM64. At the
separate native snapshot `9bd205ef104362496cceb5e927668a89ea4e0d73`, the actual
shipping source producer regenerated the physical adapter, Box and command
packages; all three compiled for Windows AMD64 with the native feature tags.
The compiled command test binary SHA256 is
`8efe9dae95fa90c6749cd7fbb220b51113ea157c48d1a042da8c7285990af14e`.
Prepared source identities were checked before and after compilation. These
cross-compiles did not execute Windows WFP operations.

Before publication, all 56 changed application/native source and test-template
files were byte-compared with the integrated `f89a1caf` snapshot. The publication
also preserves newer upstream ordinary-Stop DNS retirement and Naive H2
transport changes. Their presence is not relabeled as part of the older native
execution; exact-head hosted builds remain required. Source gates retain the
complete blocking 15-minute Go suite and require the imported parent, merged
MTU and native child cases rather than replacing existing platform controls.
The combined publication tree passed all 34 native shipping-audit methods,
24 Windows node-runner methods and 22 Windows release-contract methods.

Earlier local native executions preserved their real Netlink-denied failures.
Passing modeled source and socket tests do not turn those physical startup
failures into connectivity success. The OpenVPN DNS fixture startup/join repair
is separately published in `053e611114ce9f8e9e0dd430f3c49986b40d0f00`.

These composition tests do not assert physical Windows WFP acceptance, successful
entry public-IP or paired-final node proof, successful selected-DNS proof,
off-LAN interoperability, private deployment or device acceptance. The parent
must separately complete its original path proofs before reporting Connected.

## docs/testing/desktop-imported-policy.md

# Protected desktop imported policy

`DesktopImportedPolicy` is a separate sealed adaptation of the original
`NewImportedEntry` constructor. It retains the complete private imported entry
and independently paired final. It does not synthesize a `NodeProfile` or an
ordinary external final policy. The current native target is Windows.

The private launch envelope replays all seven exact original inputs. The
derived source binds the original source/configuration/metadata/envelope
digests, session, target, both identities and modes, both Start Layers, final
protection policy, combined IPv6/LAN constraints, physical root identities and
any captured TUN MTU restoration value. Rehashing edited output cannot replace
rederivation from those inputs.

The final owns application DNS. Its selected resolver remains routed through
`proxy`; only actual captured physical bootstrap dependencies acquire the
native lower detour. The entry public-path proof has its own loopback listener
on 1098 routed to `entry-wg`. The paired-final proof has its own loopback
listener on 1099 routed to `proxy`. Neither listener grants a successful proof.

Only original physical roots receive the native lower detour. When a direct
SOCKS5 entry needs a negotiated UDP relay, the derived graph names a distinct
retained imported SOCKS type. The static root contains only its exact TCP
control tuple. A TCP-carried REALITY final does not invent a UDP relay. An
authenticated per-operation native association remains required for actual
dynamic UDP admission.

The shared `DesktopPhysicalPolicy` interface is limited to the independently
sealed node/imported constructors. `ReadDesktopPhysicalPolicy` checks concrete
types, including rejecting a caller-defined interface wrapper. The native
readiness record still describes only fresh selected-DNS exchange and TUN
readback. The parent owes the separate imported-entry public-path and paired
final proofs before reporting Connected.

## Executable shared verification

The focused race run on the original seven-input replay plus this adaptation
passed 16 root tests and 507 individual/root verdicts, with no failures or
skips. It ran with Go 1.26.3:

```sh
go test -race -json ./internal/mobilemultihop \
  -run '^(TestImportedLaunchInput|TestDesktopImportedPolicy|TestDesktopNode(Policy|Ready|Read)|TestImportedDesktopPhysical)' \
  -count=1 -timeout=120s
```

Coverage includes nine original entry wire kinds across independent Off/AES/
AES+XOR layers and a paired REALITY/Vision final, plus SS, Hysteria2, WG and both
AWG finals. The full adapted graph is compared against the original after
removing only the exact declared native adaptation. Separate cases exercise
final selected encrypted DNS, entry/final device policy, captured MTU restore,
distinct replay/restoration owners, returned-copy isolation, missing authority,
altered and rehashed proof/configuration/source, closed or network-invalidated
originals, and retained existing Node policy/readiness contracts.

This shared verification does not establish that a native child has installed
WFP policy, authenticated its journal parent, executed both encrypted hops,
restored a physical device connection or passed a release build. Those are
separate native composition and acceptance boundaries.

## docs/testing/desktop-measurement-observer-context.md

# Desktop measurement observer context

Public-IP and DNS Retest observation, and the current/temporary Speed Lab
identity observers, retain the original request context through tracker and
application mutex admission. They do not start a replacement session tracker.
Speed Lab also captures the tracker pointer so an identical session ID in a
replacement tracker cannot authorize an old measurement.

The observer and terminal validator share the same immutable profile/session
binding. Multihop graph generation, both profile tokens, runtime command,
physical network, Tor source/predecessor, and native readiness checks remain
required. Context-capable network, Tor, external, Linux-path, and owned-Node
observers receive the original caller. A legacy callback is not invoked as a
fallback when a contextual native capability is missing.

Cancellation stops measurement requests and joins the observer. It does not
retire a healthy VPN. Terminal adoption acquires tracker/application locks with
the same caller and rejects cancellation or identity replacement before it
authorizes a write.

## Explicit synchronous boundaries

`beginNodeBoundOperation` retains its general protection/recovery transaction
contract. An already-entered transaction may outlive request cancellation and
must finish synchronously. The measurement rejects adoption after that join if
the request was canceled. This is not a hard cancellation deadline for general
protection recovery.

Private asset and native record reads remain bounded regular-file operations.
Mutex admission is cancellable, but an OS read already entered is joined until
return. Already-admitted profile persistence also retains its existing joined
transaction and rollback semantics. No file operation or persistence worker is
detached to manufacture a prompt cancellation result.

The contextless compatibility observer APIs retain those older boundaries.
Fastest and external comparison dependency adoption are a separate increment;
this change does not claim all desktop callbacks are contextual.

## Executable evidence

`deploy/test_desktop_mtu_client_context.py` requires the exact measurement test
roots and leaves alongside the existing MTU/network/process/protection
inventory. Missing, duplicate, skipped, failed, or foreign verdicts fail the
gate. The measurement controls cover held application/tracker/workspace gates,
the original Tor/host caller and deadline, exact tracker replacement, observer
join, and cancellation before/during the joined adoption transaction.

These are source and composed controller tests. They do not establish physical
traffic, device acceptance, or completion of the remaining platform graphs.

## docs/testing/go-source-contract-budget.md

# Complete Go source-contract package budget

The three workflows that run the complete `go test ./...` inventory use the same finite fifteen-minute **per-package** test budget as the existing complete controller gates. This is a CI aggregate allowance; production timeouts, individual test deadlines, negative controls, test filters and package selection are unchanged.

## Observed cause

Two hosted runs of exact commit `587ebb69b7ebbdfbeec061ff8e8d86831d51a45e` distinguish cumulative suite duration from a stalled operation:

| Hosted evidence | Command and result |
|---|---|
| [Recovered requirements source-contract job](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/42548734993/job/128939412931) | `go test ./...` reached Go's default ten-minute package alarm; `cmd/client` ended at **600.036 seconds**. The currently running Tor test root was only **two seconds** old and its current leaf was **zero seconds** old. There was no preceding assertion failure. |
| [Release gap audit job](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/42548734897/job/128939417139) | `go test ./cmd/client ./internal/common -timeout=15m` completed the whole controller package in **670.490 seconds**. It reported the separate **0.51-second** external-MTU cancellation assertion failure rather than a package timeout. This is evidence of suite completion and duration, not a passing package claim. |

Both runs checked out the same SHA and used the same Ubuntu 24.04 image (`20261101.360.1`) and actual Go 1.26.3 toolchain. Both reported empty `GOFLAGS` and `GOEXPERIMENT`; neither invocation enabled race instrumentation, build tags, test filtering or skipping. The release-gap setup action requested an older toolchain series, but its captured Go environment confirms the actual compiler was 1.26.3.

Earlier full-suite evidence at `13fb0b75` already measured **534.447 seconds** for `cmd/client`. Subsequent imported-MTU, caller-lifetime and PQ test matrices increased the complete inventory. The current timeout's goroutine snapshot is insufficient to attribute failure to its last displayed Tor/Netlink operation; the same-source completed controller run directly demonstrates that the normal suite can exceed 600 seconds.

## Bounded correction

Commit `0ab1606cf663c7faf215e2a804a76ed652f76322` had already established `-timeout=15m` for `.github/workflows/multihop-contract.yml` and `.github/workflows/release-gap-audit.yml`, based on earlier full passes and aggregate alarms in different freshly started tests. This correction applies that same finite package allowance to the three remaining complete-inventory commands:

- `.github/workflows/requirements-closure.yml`
- `.github/workflows/release-candidate.yml`
- `.github/workflows/client-apps-ci.yml`

Each command remains `go test ./...`, with only `-timeout=15m` appended. Every package, test and negative case still runs. A runtime deadlock or individual operation timeout remains a failure. The independently reported external-MTU assertion requires its own cause-specific correction.

## Validation and limits

The workflow structure audit and existing authoritative release-gate contracts verify the edited wiring and continued complete controller coverage. A new local full-suite replay is not used as substitute evidence: this sandbox's Netlink restrictions differ from the hosted Linux execution, and the exact-source hosted 670.490-second completion already provides the relevant aggregate timing. The next exact-head hosted run must establish a passing complete suite after its independent assertion fixes.

## MTU and shared multihop follow-up

At `83c0ae617858e331bbb7bc1fd3385120938d45c6`, the [MTU Unix job](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/42583227226/job/129046744193) passed its targeted original-caller MTU gate, then ran the entire controller with `go test ./cmd/client -count=1`. The package exhausted the implicit ten-minute budget at **600.034 seconds**. The displayed `TestDesktopTorPlainSpeedLabRestoresProvedExecutionAndSource` root was only **four seconds** old and its `saved-router/router` leaf was **one second** old. Together with the completed 670.490-second controller run above, this supports applying the same explicit `-timeout=15m` allowance to this complete command. It does not establish that every controller assertion passes. The MTU workflow's mandatory `deploy/test_mtu_throughput_budget.py` contract rejects omission, filtering, skipping, shortened or unbounded budgets, cached runs, conditional execution and ignored failure. Original operation deadlines and the Windows MTU selector remain unchanged.

The [Android shared multihop job](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/42583227061/job/129046737967) at the same SHA completed its first six race-enabled phases in **140.380, 16.086, 18.798, 55.396, 58.259 and 66.511 seconds**. Its remaining **222-root** group then exhausted the **180-second** package budget, ending at **180.086 seconds**. At the alarm, `TestImportedNaiveH3IndependentLayers` was only **one second** old and its current Hysteria2 layer-pair leaf was **zero seconds** old. A focused local race run of that root completed successfully in **3.026 seconds**; the snapshot does not establish a hung transport.

`deploy/test_mobile_multihop_groups.py` now divides that growing remainder into desktop execution, imported transports and the continuing catch-all. The discovered inventory of **536 roots** remains assigned exactly once: the first six groups retain **50, 18, 12, 14, 29 and 191** roots; the new groups contain **94, 73 and 55** roots. Explicit exclusions keep desktop predecessor tests in the original node phase and imported-entry tests in the original external phase. Future Test, Example and Fuzz roots still enter a phase or the catch-all. Every phase retains `-race -count=1 -timeout=180s`, its 240-second subprocess limit, checked exit status and an exact selector generated from the complete current compiler inventory. Android and native-Xray workflows already invoke this shared runner, so no additional workflow or source checkout is introduced.

All three affected groups passed locally with the unchanged race and 180-second bounds: desktop **128.374 seconds**, imported **117.641 seconds**, and the catch-all **15.992 seconds**. Their combined **262.007 seconds** explains why one 180-second aggregate allowance was insufficient; these local durations are not asserted to reproduce hosted timings exactly.

All five partition self-tests and four MTU budget contract tests passed. Partition controls retain omitted, duplicated, unknown, empty and malformed inventory rejection and add explicit split-boundary and future-name controls. The MTU budget contract uses command and gate mutations to ensure that its complete package execution cannot silently become filtered or nonblocking. Hosted reruns remain required to establish the final result under native privileges; a local Tor replay encountered the sandbox's physical-interface Netlink restriction and is not claimed as a successful runtime validation.

## docs/testing/imported-mailbox-duration.md

# Imported mailbox completion and measured DNS duration

The native imported mailbox carries two distinct operations: an empty operation
selects the original Box DNS proof, while `openvpn-observe` reads the retained
OpenVPN packet connection. A current observation may finish within one clock
tick. Failed or pre-cancelled requests may also complete without elapsed time.
Those results can acknowledge completion of their exact request; they do not
provide a successful DNS latency measurement.

## Original failure and repair

On source [`8be50dbf`](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/commit/8be50dbfbd16173980ce672aa91dfba2cee65cc7), the Windows AMD64
native job `129011912997`
reported successful native OpenVPN observations as failed with an actual zero
`milliseconds` value. Its native proof error was nil. The producer applied the
positive DNS duration rule to the observation operation, and the client also
rejected zero-duration completion receipts.

The producer now permits zero-duration `openvpn-observe` success. The client
permits finite, nonnegative, bounded durations for observations and unsuccessful
completion. Passing DNS still requires a strictly positive interval. Negative,
nonfinite, oversized, foreign, stale, or unconsumed results remain invalid. All
original request, source, TUN, reader, generation, OpenVPN receipt, heartbeat,
deadline, cancellation, and join checks remain in force.

The Windows ARM64 uncached-DNS failure showed a new upstream query but omitted
the proof error and measured duration. The fixture now reports both. Its prior
log alone does not prove that its failure had the same cause as the AMD64
observation failure.

## Executable coverage

`TestRouterVPNImportedDNSOperationDuration` uses the actual native mailbox and
retained-owner constructors inside a Go `synctest` bubble. It requires exactly
zero elapsed time for an observation, immediate DNS success/error callbacks,
and pre-cancelled DNS/observation requests. Only the current observation passes;
zero-time DNS fails and cancellation completes without borrowing DNS or packet
evidence. Each case requires its original request and cancellation entry to drain.

`TestDesktopImportedDNSOperationDuration` publishes a real private request and
exercises the actual client decoder, result reader, and cleanup path. Its 20
cases distinguish DNS from observation for positive and zero measurements,
failed/cancelled zero completion, negative/nonfinite/oversized values, and
changed reader/operation identity. Invalid evidence uses an explicit modeled
native close acknowledgement to finish cleanup; it never gains passing or
joined-result authority.

The mandatory native inventory retains its original 13 verdicts and adds six
zero-tick verdicts. The client inventory retains every original case and adds
21 duration verdicts. Missing, duplicate, failed, skipped, or foreign verdicts
remain rejected. The existing OpenVPN inventory is unchanged.

The bounded Linux validation passed 55 client race verdicts across 10 roots
(package 1.863 seconds), and 59 native mailbox/OpenVPN race verdicts across eight
roots (package 1.765 seconds), with no failure or skip. Replacing only the
client production reader with its original source rejected exactly five new
zero-completion leaves. Replacing only the native producer with its original
source rejected the zero-tick observation leaf. The other duration controls
continued to pass; neither control failed by timeout, panic, or race. Four
source/inventory contract tests also passed.

The native component run compiled the actual prepared `cmd/sing-box` package
with explicit overlays for this unit's four templates. Original mailbox,
OpenVPN, and strategy inputs were byte-compared before execution, and the
prepared tree and overlay inputs were hash-checked afterward. This local run
omitted the unrelated native-PQ build tag because the Rosenpass static archive
was unavailable. It is not a completed full-runtime bundle or Windows runtime
acceptance result.

One execution of the original uncached-DNS and OpenVPN TCP/UDP native cases
failed at `create netlink socket: operation not permitted`, before their actual
resolver assertions. Those three cases remain mandatory in hosted native CI;
the local boundary was neither skipped nor bypassed.

## Remaining Windows DNS timing boundary

Go 1.26.3's Windows AMD64 and ARM64 `runtime.nanotime1` read the shared interrupt
clock, and `time.Since` uses that monotonic clock. The mailbox converts elapsed
nanoseconds directly to floating-point milliseconds; zero is not an integer
millisecond truncation. A sufficiently fast real local or Home DNS exchange can
therefore still finish within one tick and be refused by the positive DNS rule.
The selected-DNS measurement in `node_box_dns.go.tmpl` has the same boundary.

DNS-only fixtures wait for a real positive response interval within their
original bounded context. They do not clamp, fabricate, or relax a measured RTT.
This makes those fixtures suitable for proving resolver and ownership behavior;
it does **not** repair real fast Windows DNS measurement.

A separate DNS elapsed-time integration can reuse the existing
`internal/hopmeasure` platform clock, which already uses Windows
[QueryPerformanceCounter](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/profileapi/nf-profileapi-queryperformancecounter)
and its
[boot-stable frequency](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/profileapi/nf-profileapi-queryperformancefrequency)
for that exact measurement bracket, while retaining the existing context and
deadline clocks. That work requires checked conversion, failure and zero-time
controls, and actual fast-DNS execution on both Windows architectures. It is not
implemented by this operation-specific repair.

## docs/testing/imported-mtu-socket-binding.md

# Imported desktop MTU socket binding

The imported desktop MTU adapter must bind its own TCP transfer and UDP probe
sockets to the captured running TUN. The physical interface that carries the
outer encrypted connection is a different owner. A selected interface name or a
cached MTU does not authorize a measurement.

`bindDesktopImportedMTUSocket` requires the original context, an exact active
lease validator, native reader, interface index/name and effective MTU. It
checks that owner before, inside and after the retained raw-descriptor callback.
The production interface readback requires the same running non-loopback TUN.
Cancellation or a changed owner invalidates the socket; there is no ordinary
network fallback.

The OS adapter verifies the descriptor's real type and address family before
setting and reading back its interface. Linux uses `SO_BINDTOIFINDEX` plus the
bound name. Darwin uses `IP_BOUND_IF` / `IPV6_BOUND_IF`. Windows uses
`IP_UNICAST_IF` / `IPV6_UNICAST_IF` and obtains the unbound socket's actual
family/type/protocol through `SO_PROTOCOL_INFOW`; IPv4 preserves the native
set/get byte-order contract. Other targets explicitly reject this adapter and
retain their separate qualified platform requirement.

## Executable checks

`TestDesktopImportedMTUKernelSocketBinding` opens actual TCP/UDP IPv4/IPv6
sockets and checks original binding, changed type/family, unqualified transport,
invalid interface and cancellation. `TestDesktopImportedMTUSocketRequiresOriginalLease`
rejects missing/cancelled callers, absent/replaced lease validators, missing
reader and unqualified network before kernel entry. The Windows native contract
requires both roots on AMD64 and ARM64 under its existing 180-second per-group
limit. Its gate test rejects omission and keeps each selected native test in
exactly one invocation. macOS/Linux full controller suites also select both.

The Linux test reads its real loopback index from the kernel IPv6 table to
exercise the actual socket options without requiring a complete interface
inventory. This is a test of kernel socket binding, not a substitute for the
production TUN readback or an end-to-end physical VPN. The production adapter
still refuses loopback and requires actual native interface inventory.

The actual desktop controller package passes all 32 socket/owner race verdicts
on the integrated continuation source. A verbatim-source projection also passes
those cases and cross-compiles for Windows/Darwin on AMD64/ARM64. Those
cross-compiles are not native execution.
The shared command adapter, authenticated path probes, candidate selection,
rollback and Auto/Retest/Cancel dispatch require their separate integration
checks; these socket functions alone do not complete imported MTU support.

## docs/testing/imported-naive-h2-literal-home.md

# Captured literal Naive H2 over CONNECT with Home UDP

This source change admits selected Home UDP DNS for an imported HTTP CONNECT or
HTTPS CONNECT entry followed by an original literal-address Naive H2 final with
its final Start Layer Off. All three independently captured entry Start Layers
remain supported. A single explicitly selected literal UDP resolver also meets
this rule. The selected resolver is never silently replaced.

## Native behavior and ownership

The pinned sing-box core is
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`. Its prepared Naive outbound passes the
original server address and separate TLS server name to pinned Cronet.
`wrapDNSResolverForServerRedirect` answers matching A/AAAA TLS identity requests
from that exact literal address. Other names still invoke the original resolver.
H2 connects over the authenticated CONNECT stream; application UDP uses the
final node's existing UDP-over-TCP v2 connection.

`internal/mobilemultihop/imported_naive_h2.go` admits this case after existing
strict original transport and selected DNS validation. The final must be literal,
unlayered, and retain its exact credentials, TLS identity and resolver. The
application DNS closure must contain one literal UDP resolver. Hostname bootstrap
closures, DoH3, changed/missing resolvers, foreign lower owners and layered-final
UDP bootstrap retain their rejection.

The executable resolver tree remains unchanged except for the existing owned
entry detour. `imported_entry_desktop.go` and `desktop_egress.go` record the
validated literal H2 bootstrap as a retained dependency without granting it an
unused lower UDP socket. Generic egress callers keep their original behavior.
Application DNS remains final-owned. Apple producer admission checks the same
narrow prerequisites; Android uses the shared compiler.

## Verification

| Check | Result and boundary |
| --- | --- |
| Shared `TestImportedNaiveH[23]` roots, Go race | Passed in 38.504 seconds, including existing DNS and independent-layer controls. |
| New `TestImportedNaiveH2CONNECTLiteralHomeUDP`, Go race | Passed in 1.356 seconds. Two CONNECT types × three entry layers retain exact source/DNS/bootstrap. Protected Windows policy derives without changing original graph objects. Physical roots are TCP only; bootstrap has no physical authority. Hostname, missing/wrong resolver, foreign owner and changed captured graph reject. |
| Actual pinned native H2/H3 and CONNECT literal roots, Go race | All **77 required verdicts** passed: original H2 24, original H3 24, new CONNECT 29. Counts include root verdicts and exact leaves, not device coverage percentages. |
| New native CONNECT leaves | HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT × IPv4/IPv6 original final literals × seven cases: Home success, wrong final password, wrong TLS name, wrong certificate, forced hostname query, wrong CONNECT credentials and wrong CONNECT route. Success completes real authenticated TCP, UDP-over-TCP and final-owned Home DNS, with zero lower UDP and zero bootstrap exchanges/lookups. |
| Native causal control | A Go overlay disabling only pinned Cronet's literal redirect makes the Home case fail: name resolution failure, two bootstrap exchanges, zero lower TCP/final peer connection. Real source remains unchanged. |
| Shared source causal controls | Separate overlays restoring old admission or eager bootstrap traversal each fail the Home test at the intended boundary. |
| Android producer | Both CONNECT phases pass 1,249 checks each and all six existing causal controls. Each phase retains its nine original independent-layer graphs. |
| Apple producer | Actual Swift/shared-Go validation passes 81 graphs and 4,327 checks, retaining existing hostname/bootstrap refusals. |
| Actual desktop Home producer | New retained-selection/launch test passes Go race in 1.177 seconds and exports both original CONNECT graphs with final-owned Home UDP, retained lower descriptor and TCP-only physical grants. |
| Actual exported Home graphs → pinned Libbox | All six Java/Swift/desktop graphs passed native `CheckConfig` under race: seven exact verdicts, 1.146 seconds total. Separate changed-lower-owner and invalid-native-type graph controls fail at the ownership check and actual native parser respectively. |
| Persistent contracts | Workflow nine tests, imported H2 runner ten tests, native literal policy ten tests passed. |

The original 81-graph TCP inventories remain intact. Separate optional producer
export directories hold exactly two Home graphs per Java, Swift and desktop
producer. The existing imported H2 workflow supplies all three directories to a
mandatory separate native parser root; missing roles, missing/extra files,
symlinks, empty/nonobject files, native failure and changed bytes fail the gate.
The reader checks exact CONNECT axes, selected UDP/53 ownership and the retained
bootstrap before calling actual pinned Libbox `CheckConfig` on all six graphs.
Source receipts include the producers, new desktop test, parser template, runner,
workflow and workflow contract. The runner requires seven exact native verdicts.

The new 28-leaf native CONNECT root is registered beside the existing literal
root in `deploy/test_desktop_node_physical.py` and in the imported H2 loopback
inventory. Contract checks preserve every original verdict and reject missing,
duplicate, skipped and failed verdicts. No operation timeout, failure status,
DNS substitution or native lower-UDP guard is relaxed.

## Evidence limits and remaining requirements

The native witness executes on Linux amd64 with the actual pinned core,
checksum-verified Cronet library, authenticated local CONNECT/Naive peers and
declared mapping of captured literal addresses to owned local peers. Focused
native validation copies current changed readers/compiler files into the prepared
pinned tree with the builder's import rewrite; it does not assert a whole current
bundle identity or replace source-pinned CI. Producer/graph and protected-policy
checks do not prove physical host enforcement, signing, deployment or READY.

Final AES/AES+XOR with UDP bootstrap and hostname-dependent UDP bootstrap over
stream-only CONNECT remain unsupported. Imported Server/Compare, additional
selected-hop composition and remaining platform host integrations remain separate
requirements. The original all-device/native scope is unchanged.

## docs/testing/imported-naive-h2.md

# Imported entries with a paired Naive H2 final

The existing `naive-h2` mode is available as a paired Local final through a
captured imported entry. Shared Go, Android, Apple and desktop preparation use
the same source-bound compiler and native Naive transport. Daily connection and
temporary Speed Lab selection retain the original final, entry credentials,
selected DNS, protection, MTU and independent Start Layers. This adds no logical
mode and changes no default resolver or execution score.

## Captured graph

The separate inventory has nine wire variants: SOCKS5, Shadowsocks 2022,
Hysteria2, HTTP CONNECT, HTTPS CONNECT, WireGuard, AmneziaWG, OpenVPN TCP and
OpenVPN UDP. Each has three entry and three final Start Layer choices: Off,
AES-256-GCM, and AES-256-GCM with XOR whitening. Each independent producer must
supply all 81 original graphs; four producers require 324 native parses.

The paired final retains authenticated HTTP/2, verified TLS, the original server
address and port, username/password, certificate/SNI policy and version-2 UDP
over streams. H3, insecure TLS, missing credentials, a different transport,
unowned detours and missing private assets cannot inherit H2 authority. A
Shadowsocks 2022 predecessor must retain the exact cipher's valid key sizes and
identity-key chain; malformed encodings cannot enter through this new final.

HTTP/HTTPS CONNECT receives a distinct `naive-h2-stream` capability. It is not
an SS UoT service declaration or an Xray/VLESS capability. The original seven
compiler inputs remain sealed with their source fingerprint and compiled graph.
Changing a selected profile, source asset, API/token identity or layer cannot
reuse a captured graph or proof. An imported entry never receives the paired
final's private node identity.

## DNS and transport ownership

Application DNS uses the final Naive path. A selected TCP resolver uses an
authenticated stream; a selected UDP resolver uses Naive's version-2 datagram
stream. The separate resolver closure for the final's lower
transport retains the original selected endpoint, transport, port, TLS name,
path and query settings. Only the compiler's owned detour/tag references change.
An independently captured final Start Layer remains the lower resolver owner;
without that layer, its detour is the original imported entry.

The current CONNECT admission requires an explicitly captured TCP-compatible
lower DNS policy. The 81-graph inventories use explicit Home TCP. The compiler
does not convert the default Home UDP policy to TCP or replace it with a public
resolver. Whether an otherwise unused lower UDP resolver can safely remain
configured for an entirely literal H2 endpoint is a separate runtime question;
the current refusal is not evidence that this remaining compatibility
requirement is complete. UDP-capable imported predecessors retain their wider
selected DNS transports.

Native H2 transport must not issue an unrelated UDP probe through its lower
owner. IPv6 literals with a separate TLS name require the captured-literal
resolver policy described in the native Naive transport documentation. A saved
DNS selection, a parser pass or a zero-query literal test does not establish
that application DNS traversed the selected final. The native peer gate must
observe transport bootstrap and selected application DNS separately.

The connection still requires public-path, paired-node identity and selected
DNS proofs from the same current owner before Connected. Cancellation, network
change, teardown, stale measurements and source substitution cannot install a
result into a replacement connection. Temporary restoration retains its
already captured execution choice; it does not rerun Compare.

## Evidence and execution boundaries

The shared compiler's frozen source passed 614 verdicts across eight roots in
two final invocations. One original full invocation completed its graph
assertions but failed because its requested export directory had not been
created; rerunning that unchanged export root in an empty owned directory
passed all 82 verdicts and wrote its 81 original receipts. This is not described
as one successful full invocation.

The actual Java/shared-compiler producer passed 11,234 assertions and six
causal source mutations, including the service admission boundary. The actual
Swift/shared-compiler producer passed 4,329 checks. Each produced its own 81
graphs and certificate material. Android and shared files are nine-string
source/receipt records; Apple and desktop files are original graph objects.
Cross-producer certificate bytes need not be identical.

On base `e2b21c875195df3a75065f27230ffa1774d977a3` plus this change, the desktop
race run passed 176 verdicts across six roots, with no failure or skip, in
39.284 seconds. It covers all 81 graph captures, changed authority, the explicit
CONNECT DNS boundary, both daily and temporary catalogs, and the protected
native parent's retained ordinary and H2 dispatch. That parent fixture models
its kernel/child boundary; it is not actual WFP or installed-device acceptance.

After rebasing onto `1e2ba67ac12bebc10d83fc174db8aee26f267597`, including the
new public Stop ownership rules, the same six-root desktop selection passed
all 176 race verdicts again without failures or skips and regenerated all 81
graphs. This final run took 126.038 seconds including compilation. The original
44 feature files survived the rebase byte-for-byte; the status/evidence text
was updated afterward to record the completed checks.

The shared and mobile executions retain their original source identities.
Their source files survived subsequent integration byte-for-byte; this
comparison is distinct from executing the whole new commit on those hosts.
Native parser, encrypted peer, complete package and device evidence must retain
their own exact source/executable receipts.

### Actual native checks

On the frozen H2 integration based on `f23dea2f`, Go 1.26.3 and pinned core
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`, the real Libbox parser accepted
**324 original graphs**: 81 each from shared Go, Android, Apple and desktop.
All 328 root/leaf verdicts passed without skips. The same compiled reader
rejected five graph substitutions (entry kind, each independent layer, OpenVPN
wire protocol and CONNECT TLS) and four missing/changed/linked native-library
seals at the intended assertions, without panic or race reports.

The preserved parser also accepted all 81 desktop graphs regenerated after the
`1e2ba67a` integration: 82 verdicts, no failures or skips. Dependency inspection
confirmed that the intervening OpenVPN test templates are outside both native
images' compiled package graphs; the changed desktop owner was covered by the
fresh desktop race run. This is a bounded dependency/readback comparison, not a
claim that a new complete native image was rebuilt after that integration.

The actual native protocol image also passed all **24 H2 literal-address
verdicts**, including IPv4/IPv6, trailing-dot names, exact lower TCP ownership,
wrong credentials and wrong trust. Its SHA-256 was
`0bf1f6172472dc6e33c65cada1a69cd34c915abe2fef7e4558141866f7d7a1b5`;
the parser image was
`8e886c929c5eef52d5e21ff538aebdbad6d142cbe99c96fdc0ad788809a06684`.
Final source and library readback remained unchanged. The combined image
compiled the entire 657-leaf imported peer matrix, but its original first
SOCKS/off/off/success witness failed at
`route ip+net: netlinkrib: operation not permitted` before peer execution.
That failure is preserved; compilation and protocol loopback do not establish
the still-required imported physical-peer pass.

After integration, 36 runner/workflow/package/aggregate contract tests passed.
The existing six-host orchestration root and retained Linux DNS package gate
also passed (six tests, 182.624 seconds). Those tests model subprocess and host
boundaries; they do not represent six actual operating-system executions.

## Mandatory native and release gates

`imported-naive-h2-native.yml` requires the actual Apple exports, all nine
Android producer phases and desktop exports before the four native parser
inventories. Parser, encrypted loopback and imported physical-peer stages run
separately with finite, unchanged per-operation deadlines. Missing, renamed,
linked, duplicate, skipped, failed or changed evidence cannot satisfy a stage.

The focused workflow keeps the original ordinary, PQ and XHTTP jobs and adds
H2 after them. Release aggregation separately requires the real H2 workflow.
The executable aggregate checker rejects its removal, substitution, duplicate
job, conditional execution or ignored failure.

Every matching Windows, macOS and Linux x64/ARM64 completed or reused native
bundle must execute the H2 desktop producer, require its exact 82 verdicts and
81 unchanged graph files, then run that completed bundle's `check -c` against
each file. Original bytes and the complete bundle receipt are checked again
afterward. The prior 26 ordinary, 108 packet, 108 OpenVPN, 81 PQ and 81 XHTTP
inventories remain mandatory. Source/orchestration tests model subprocess
boundaries; actual completed-bundle acceptance is a matching-host build gate.

This extension does not close Naive H3, imported Server/Compare, additional-hop
composition, remaining Unix protected-host integration, signing, private
deployment or physical/off-LAN acceptance. A native interface/Netlink denial
remains a failed physical attempt; no simulated route or optional gate turns
it into acceptance.

## docs/testing/imported-naive-h3.md

# Imported entries with a paired Naive H3 final

This source unit adds the existing paired Naive H3 final to the Local imported
entry graph. It preserves the existing twenty runtime profiles and sixteen
logical choices. It does not change the default SMART AUTO, IPv6, Home AdGuard,
Start Layer, or AUTO requirement settings.

## Captured path

The shared constructor, Android daily/Speed Lab path, Apple selection and
PacketTunnel path, and desktop daily/Speed Lab preparation retain these seven
concrete imported wire variants:

| Imported entry | H3 carrier |
| --- | --- |
| SOCKS5 | Authenticated UDP association owned by the original TCP control |
| Shadowsocks 2022 | Original authenticated datagrams |
| Hysteria2 | Original authenticated QUIC path |
| WireGuard | Original captured peer, keys and packet endpoint |
| AmneziaWG | Original captured peer, keys, parameters and packet endpoint |
| OpenVPN TCP | Original native OpenVPN packet endpoint over its TCP wire |
| OpenVPN UDP | Original native OpenVPN packet endpoint over its UDP wire |

Each entry and final independently keeps Off, AES-256-GCM, or AES-256-GCM plus
XOR whitening. The seven wire variants and nine layer pairs form 63 graphs per
producer. XOR remains an obfuscation stage with authenticated encryption.

HTTP CONNECT and HTTPS CONNECT cannot carry this required lower QUIC path.
They are refused before H3 capture; adding a false UDP capability or changing
the final to H2 does not make the requested H3 path supported. Their existing
H2 support and its explicit compatible resolver policy remain available.

The final retains its exact native `naive` transport, `quic: true`, UoT-v2,
credentials, TLS identity and trust. Entry credentials never substitute for a
paired final's identity or private proof token. The original entry and final
Start Layers remain separate owners in the sealed graph.

## DNS, lifecycle and desktop protection

Home AdGuard UDP is retained. Selected DNS queries use the final's `proxy`
route. The separate bootstrap resolver uses the original entry or the final's
captured Start Layer, as appropriate. No public resolver is substituted to make
the graph compile or connect. Native graph controls reject H2 substitution,
selected TCP substitution, selected DNS through the entry, and bootstrap DNS
through the final it is supposed to resolve. The desktop fixture retains its
own captured `10.88.0.53:53`; the shared and mobile fixtures retain their
independent `192.168.50.133:53`. Reader expectations come from those fixed
producer contracts. A graph cannot authorize its own substituted resolver.

The original captured source, generation, protection, IPv6/LAN policy, physical
entry tuples and MTU restoration survive preparation and replay. The path does
not gain Server/Compare authority from an imported public entry. Wrong source,
credentials, final transport, UoT version, TLS policy or detour are refused.

The desktop producer has two distinct outputs for the same 63 captured paths:

- The portable output is the original sealed graph for the shared native parser.
- The protected output is the actual Windows launch projection containing the
  original `DesktopImportedPolicy` and its inert owned SOCKS constructor.

Every matching completed or reused desktop bundle checks all 63 portable
graphs with its own executable. A matching Windows bundle additionally checks
all 63 protected projections. Parsing does not activate a BFE owner, open an
association, or prove protected traffic; the separate Windows companion owns
that execution boundary. The Windows-only constructor guard is preserved.

## Recorded validation

The following results belong to their recorded source snapshots. Current-SHA
native builds, publication and physical-device acceptance remain separate.

| Boundary | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Shared compiler and ownership | 627 race verdicts across ten roots; 63 original captures; no failures or skips |
| Android H3 producer | 63 captures, 9,175 assertions and six causal refusal controls |
| Apple H3 producer | 63 graphs and 3,974 checks; surrounding saved-selector and startup suites retained |
| Desktop capture, dispatch and restoration | 188 race verdicts across eight roots, including the existing H2 producer; no failures or skips; 63 portable and 63 protected exports |
| Matching package, workflow and aggregate contracts | 76 tests passed with the original ordinary, packet, OpenVPN, PQ, XHTTP and H2 inventories retained |
| Native parser | All 252 original graphs accepted by the pinned Libbox reader; all fourteen deliberate faults rejected at their required boundaries |
| Native encrypted loopback and association ownership | 37 race verdicts passed: 24 H3 literal/credential/trust/DNS verdicts and thirteen actual SOCKS control/relay/layer/cancellation verdicts |
| Windows companion | AMD64 PE cross-compilation passed with all six required roots and the child-only peer symbol; execution remains required on Windows |
| Windows peer buffer fixture | Actual pinned Shadowsocks 2022 UDP witness passed both DNS and ordinary-datagram leaves; original zero-headroom fixture failed both at the real authenticated writer |
| Matching native build orchestration | One modeled test exercised all six host targets and passed in 132.915 seconds; this is not native compiler or WFP execution |
| Runner boundaries | Eleven corrected native-reader contracts, ten Windows companion contracts and fourteen workflow contracts passed |

The final desktop run used base `8be50dbfbd16173980ce672aa91dfba2cee65cc7`
plus the integrated H3 source. Its captured Go/configuration source bytes were
unchanged during the run, which took 157.673 seconds including compilation.
An earlier new fixture inherited IPv6 Off from the ordinary external-entry
fixture while expecting On at both ends. That assertion correctly failed;
only the new H3 fixture was corrected to select On for both captured profiles.

The native protocol run used a fresh preparation from the frozen integration
on parent `cd08371bdf17a9a598c8581e2498fd5f920e43aa`. Its source-input digest was
`7902aa16edf77218fb6eb2274f8863e962af323fd4dae4d0e461094e32fce92c`, and its
prepared-input digest was
`1d470f375b4d12c8d94810e426bc03d77a3c2c2ad91de20cd1638625f1e01160`.
The unchanged 11,915,768-byte native library had SHA-256
`23109c55b08829bb1a68682a61a64852b1bbf243753b153cbd8f6e4ea2c05294`.

The first parser attempt accepted the shared, Android and Apple inventories
but rejected every desktop graph because its test reader incorrectly required
the other producers' Home address. The correction changes the test reader and
runner; the original desktop graph and production DNS policy stay intact.
The corrected reader accepted all four original inventories, and a new control
substituting both desktop resolver rows was rejected. All 7,400 selected
protocol compiler inputs remained byte-identical, so the 37-verdict protocol
receipt remains tied to its original image; it is not a second protocol run.

The Windows AMD64 diagnostic used the original WFP test-scope overlay and
produced a 107,947,520-byte PE image with SHA-256
`d61455d0a1a3e0dd675a8b604ace655fc9913cfad039355b6f0a763aa7c8c40f`.
This Linux cross-compilation used CGO disabled and did not include the native
PQ tag. Symbol inspection is not executable inventory discovery, BFE/WFP
execution, ARM64 compilation or physical-device acceptance.

## Required native and release gates

The separate `imported-naive-h3-native.yml` workflow exports actual Android,
Apple and desktop producers and requires the pinned native reader, protocol
loopback and imported peer inventories. It is a mandatory authoritative release
dependency. Its inventory is additional to the existing H2 and other imported
families; a smaller or renamed inventory cannot replace them.

The reader checks all 252 graphs from four producers, five renamed-axis controls,
four native-library seal controls, and five QUIC/DNS-ownership controls. The
imported peer inventory requires 529 leaves, including each layer pair and
source, credential, route, DNS, cancellation and replacement controls. A separate
actual SOCKS association component exercises live control/relay ownership and
all three entry-layer settings without claiming a physical interface proof.

The unchanged physical-owner witness could not capture its interface locally:
the environment denied Netlink access. The physical peer inventory compiled,
but that attempted witness did not establish protected traffic. The first
scratch invocation also omitted its required original source seal and stopped
at source preflight; it is retained separately from the subsequent Netlink
failure. Neither attempt is counted as a passing physical test.

Windows AMD64 and ARM64 must each execute all 88 companion verdicts on their
matching host. Package gates must also use the actual shipping executable and
its original build environment. These mandatory hosted checks, the full
physical peer inventory and exact-SHA release publication remain outstanding
at source publication.

## Remaining requirements

This unit does not complete imported Server/Compare execution, additional-hop
composition, a Home UDP path through stream-only CONNECT/H2 lower transports,
or remaining Unix protected host integration. Current release publication,
physical selected-path/leak/reconnect/network-transition checks, off-LAN
interoperability, signing/distribution, private deployment and ASUS acceptance
still require their own exact-source observations.

## docs/testing/imported-native-mtu.md

# Imported native entry to paired-final MTU

## Shipping path and ownership

The desktop Auto and Retest dispatch recognizes the retained imported native
entry and paired Router VPN final before the older external-node optimizer.
The shared private-node MTU controller now reaches that exact native TUN through
a dedicated desktop owner. It does not use the old unauthenticated UDP script.
The connection keeps its original graph, private final credentials, selected
DNS/device policy, native child, source, physical-network identity and TUN reader.
Imported OpenVPN additionally keeps its original authenticated remote,
generation and packet capacity.

The adapter owns the native begin/set/commit/rollback protocol and its one-shot
unconfirmed-commit revert. The same retained owner excludes ordinary selected-DNS
and OpenVPN observation mailboxes while the MTU lease is active. Measurement
sockets are bound to the observed native interface using the platform-specific
socket controls and exact readback. Only the captured paired private API and
its authenticated MTU sink are admitted. A partial dial returned with an error
is closed before it can enter retained socket ownership.

The existing shared controller selects at most five MTU candidates within the
captured graph’s per-hop envelope. Each accepted candidate requires six valid
authenticated packet-size replies and real 256 KiB upload/download transfers.
Private final-node proof is repeated through the candidate path. Auto may use
one historical hint only if its full path, network and profile fingerprints
match and it is at most 24 hours old; that hint is measured again. Retest bypasses
the hint. The stored measurement does not claim an outer underlay PMTU.

Public Cancel cancels the original operation. Failed or cancelled work retains
its exact native cleanup authority and cannot mutate a replacement connection.
A native commit that completes after cancellation uses the original revert
capability. The retained shared controller then restores the complete original
configuration and verifies the returned rollback reader, so another Retest does
not silently construct a fresh controller to hide stale state. Profile history
rollback changes only the exact measurement fields written by that operation;
newer credentials or another result are retained. Historical writes recheck the
original caller at the final atomic rename admission. Cleanup can release a
removed old workspace only after the exact native owner already recorded both
its closed state and verified child retirement; that release does not report a
measurement or restored interface.

Fixed policy remains the captured compiler policy and is not silently replaced
with Auto. Retest requires Auto without Jumbo. An unavailable private probe,
missing native owner, stale reader or changed physical path remains an explicit
failure; it cannot become a fabricated measurement.

## Executed host verification

The final base-feature check used the race-instrumented Go 1.26.3 Linux AMD64
controller binary from snapshot
`0de31ff7103622fa999da523061581623dece47c`. All 16 base-feature roots passed:
90 root/subtest verdicts, with no failure or skip within that subset. The two
socket roots account for 32 verdicts, and the 14 adapter, persistence and
lifecycle roots account for 58. This includes the final-rename caller check,
checked retired-workspace release, corrected native heartbeat fixture and the
causal long-transfer heartbeat control. The log is
`controller-protected-final-mtu-20261109.jsonl`.

The same combined selector also executed the separate protected Windows
derived-source integration follow-up. Its two leaves and parent failed, so the
complete combined group was **90 passing / 3 failing verdicts**, not a fully
passing run. That dependent integration is not part of the accepted base-feature
result; its concrete fixture diagnosis and pending verification are recorded
below.

### Earlier checkpoints

The initial integrated Go 1.26.3 Linux AMD64 controller build used commit
`9f02b5baf2340fa9e797e84632c0732c9126b850`. That assembled test snapshot included
host adapter checkpoint `76cd8e3a05f25716f84e24d8ec9d0fea15880a86`, native record and
OpenVPN handoff publication `a8704c9599bbd4bed81125c82e228fdce817c987`, the shared
finalization rollback contract and the published platform socket helpers.
The race-instrumented selector `^TestDesktopImportedMTU` passed 13 roots and
79 total root/subtest verdicts, with no failure or skip. Of those, 11 new host
roots account for 47 verdicts; the two kernel socket roots account for the other
32 verdicts. The actual run log is
`controller-combined-imported-20261109.jsonl`.

The host tests execute the actual private file mailbox, shared private-node
controller, host finalizer, cache transaction and public Cancel function. Their
TUN readers, physical-network validator and socket boundary are explicit
fixtures. The private test peer exchanges actual HTTP bytes and authenticates
actual MTU datagrams; it does not stand in for a deployed router or real VPN
interface. The socket tests separately exercise real TCP4/TCP6/UDP4/UDP6 kernel
binding/readback and rejection controls using local loopback metadata. They do
not authorize loopback as a production TUN.

The passing host cases cover:

- Commit, rollback and cancellation after native commit for packet and OpenVPN
  captures, including one-shot revert and ordinary-mailbox exclusion.
- Rejected foreign operations, readers, session/path/MTU identities and probe
  destinations; exact cleanup cannot start another measurement.
- Changed OpenVPN remote, generation, capacity, path and retirement; a numeric
  MTU match cannot attest to a current original reader.
- Real private proof, authenticated datagrams and bidirectional transfers;
  wrong-node proof and blind UDP echoes fail, while cancellation joins the
  private HTTP transfer and restores the original state.
- Auto historical-hint remeasurement, forced Retest bypass, bounded age and
  exact path/profile/network matching; durable rollback preserves newer state.
- A second Retest after cancelled finalization on the same retained controller,
  and a new admission that cannot borrow a preceding operation’s commit bit.
- Missing native owner rejection and closure of a partially opened failed dial.

## Subsequent integrated check and fixture correction

The subsequent snapshot `532b185bae35397c4f67f9b7fb23c1d2cd2aed48` included the
final-rename and retained-retirement follow-ups. Its imported MTU selector
produced 84 passing and 3 failing verdicts, including the failed parent. The two
failed leaves were `SharedPrivateControllerAndFinalization/cached-auto` and
`SharedPrivateControllerAndFinalization/cancel-at-commit`. Both new follow-up
roots passed, but the whole selector did not pass. Its log is
`controller-mtu-final-20261109.jsonl`.

The retained native fixture published its status on commands only. It did not
refresh the heartbeat while actual HTTP body transfer was in progress, unlike
the shipping native loop's 250 ms heartbeat. On the slower integrated run the
host correctly rejected a record older than two seconds. The follow-up fixture
opt-in uses the same protocol implementation and refreshes only its retained
current record between commands. Existing negative cases keep the heartbeat
disabled so they cannot overwrite intentionally changed source, path, reader
or remote observations. A new causal case holds an actual private HTTP body
beyond the original two-second record lifetime: the refreshed fixture must
complete, while the unrefreshed fixture must fail without a measured or
restored claim. Production freshness limits and work/cleanup budgets are
unchanged.

The Windows contract workflow and authoritative release workflow now select
the entire `^TestDesktopImportedMTU` group on AMD64 and ARM64. The focused
workflow contract passed 19 Python tests, including negative mutations that
remove the group or restrict it to the older socket-only cases. This is a
workflow selection result; it does not establish a Windows build result.

## Protected Windows integration follow-up

A protected Windows imported launch runs a derived physical-policy graph.
The dependent integration validates the actual child file against that typed
policy while separately retaining the original imported envelope, entry/final
captures and native host. The original graph cannot replace the derived graph
for MTU planning, and a matching derived graph cannot excuse a changed original
envelope.

In the combined snapshot above,
`TestDesktopImportedMTUProtectedDerivedSourceCapture/shadowsocks` and
`.../wireguard` both failed the retained-process check. The modeled native
process's `Wait` returned immediately while the modeled child was still alive.
The shipping `desktopNodeProcessDone` correctly interprets a returned process
wait as completion, so a subsequent capture rejected that child. The fixture
repair makes `Wait` block until its retained completion channel closes or its
original caller cancels; `Close` closes that channel exactly once after writing
the original retirement receipts. No production process or source guard is
weakened. This follow-up still requires its corrected combined execution result
before it is accepted.

## Native prerequisite and acceptance limits

See `imported-openvpn-mtu-handoff.md` for the prepared native core results:
8 race-instrumented roots / 55 verdicts, including 3 new handoff roots / 14
verdicts; the heartbeat projection also passed its 15 positive verdicts and all
five causal deadline/freshness controls. The broader native physical run reached
the environment’s denied Netlink constructor and did not establish physical
provider acceptance. Those native gates remain required.

The base-feature heartbeat, final-write and retirement cases passed in the
exact snapshot recorded above. The dependent protected derived-source capture
is tracked separately. These records are source/test evidence only: actual
supported-platform TUN traffic, private-server MTU/throughput, network changes,
packaged builds and device acceptance remain separate verification steps.

## docs/testing/imported-openvpn-dns-fixture.md

# Imported OpenVPN selected-DNS fixture

## Failure and correction

The Linux native application workflow for `f23dea2f6d1650bac010cd569ee27392651b539d`
reached the real OpenVPN connected observation, then failed the first selected
DNS mailbox operation for both TCP and UDP. The failed run is
[42564425756](https://github.com/Eabusham2/router-vpn/actions/runs/42564425756),
jobs `128987785015` and `128987784976`.

The fixture graph had no TUN inbound. Its separate MTU fixture therefore did
not satisfy `RouterVPNStrategyDNSProofContext`, which requires exactly one
owned tagged TUN in the same Box before it queries the captured DNS route.
The fixture now registers its explicit platform TUN in that Box. The MTU
owner reads and claims that exact manager entry. Its modeled interface
replacement also updates the real manager, and the test checks that rollback
leaves the packet observer and DNS proof attached to the same registered
reader.

The actual mutual-TLS/data endpoint, native DNS router, uncached TCP DNS
exchange, original caller, mailbox publication, connected generation and
reconnect refusal remain production code. The public endpoint maps to a
loopback test provider. OS TUN creation and interface replacement remain
explicit test boundaries; this is not physical TUN acceptance.

The DNS peer now validates the bounded proof question and returns an A record
only to an A question. AAAA connection-reuse probes receive a matching empty
answer. Positive / NXDOMAIN / positive transitions must still each advance
the real upstream query counter. A failed proof includes its native error and
query count in the test diagnostic.

## Validation on November 9, 2026

The isolated prepared core used pinned source
`1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`. The two original test templates and
the production DNS proof template matched the publication parent byte for
byte before the correction was supplied through a Go source overlay.

On Linux AMD64, Go 1.26.3:

- The corrected native command package compiled with the real OpenVPN remote
  test provider and race instrumentation.
- **41 passing verdicts across five roots** covered the original constructor,
  tuple, caller, mailbox and cancellation/join cases. The final focused run
  passed in 27.546 seconds, including compilation; no cases failed or skipped.
- The ten existing desktop imported OpenVPN packaging contracts passed in
  35.227 seconds, including exact native test inventory and prepared-template
  registration.
- Both the unchanged and corrected native connected cases stopped earlier
  at `create netlink socket: operation not permitted` in this workspace. The
  ordinary native DNS and real provider bind-collision tests hit the same
  environment boundary. The focused package run therefore **did not pass**,
  and local execution did not reproduce or close the later hosted DNS failure.
- An initial attempt with the complete CI tag set could not link the absent
  `libroutervpn_rosenpass` archive. The bounded OpenVPN execution above omitted
  only the unrelated `routervpn_native_pq` tag. Shipping workflow tags and
  native PQ requirements were not changed.

The exact-commit hosted Linux native jobs remain the acceptance gate for
the complete native tag set and actual provider / selected-DNS / MTU handoff
sequence. A local compiler result or the five passing roots cannot substitute
for those jobs.

## docs/testing/imported-openvpn-mtu-handoff.md

# Imported OpenVPN node-MTU reader handoff

The imported OpenVPN observer continues to pin its original authenticated remote,
remote generation, packet capacity, outbound identity, physical-path guard and
native owner. Ordinary DNS and measurement proof still require an inactive MTU
lease. The node-MTU command loop has a separate admission for its own captured
lease and may adopt only the reader returned by that owner’s native MTU
transaction. It validates the original lease, source, path, previous reader,
actual replacement reader, interface and packet tuple before the handoff.

A cancelled native change may return a new reader for the restored original
MTU. That reader is retained only under the transaction’s original cleanup
allowance. It cannot become a successful measured candidate, and cancellation
of the original native lifetime cannot authorize the handoff. Explicit rollback,
one-shot unconfirmed-commit revert and checked lease expiry use the same original
identity. The host/native node-MTU record now carries the exact comparable
OpenVPN packet observation; its next heartbeat reobserves that tuple after the
actual network-policy receipt. A saved pre-change observation is insufficient.

## Executed verification

On Go 1.26.3, Linux AMD64, November 9, 2026, the actual isolated native core was
prepared with the repository’s relay and external preparation functions. The
pinned core was `1ac1a339cb1223e9c70eae14c44411c75033c02d`. Preparation retained the
original inbound source guard and produced external source digest
`8044aaf4d17a630abe94203a65500e014a0c1319d93fa70ed3ce2cdce41d10bd`.

The compiled, race-instrumented prepared `cmd/sing-box` passed eight test roots
and 55 total root/subtest verdicts with this selection:

```sh
go test -race -mod=readonly -ldflags=-checklinkname=0 \
  -tags=with_wireguard,with_gvisor,with_quic,with_utls,with_openvpn,with_naive_outbound,with_purego,routervpn_openvpn_remote_test \
  ./cmd/sing-box \
  -run '^(TestRouterVPNNodeMTUImportedOpenVPN|TestRouterVPNImportedOpenVPN(Constructor|OriginalTuple|CallerContext|Mailbox|CancellationJoins))' \
  -count=1 -timeout=120s
```

The three new roots contain 14 root/subtest verdicts. They cover commit,
rollback, expiry, one-shot revert, a cancelled change returning its exact
rollback reader, and rejected foreign lease/reader/remote generation/capacity/
endpoint/path/lifetime. The existing observer tests retain their original
constructor, tuple, mailbox, deadline and joined-cancellation controls.

`deploy/test_desktop_imported_openvpn_runtime.py` passed all ten preparation,
source and native-test registration checks. The heartbeat projection executes
the extracted shipping loop and observers with explicit file, native and kernel
boundaries. Its checks distinguish the 250 ms periodic refresh from the
post-network observation inside the existing two-second heartbeat allowance;
neither phase can renew the other’s caller or publish a missing packet tuple.
Its two roots / 15 total root/subtest verdicts passed, and all five causal
controls rejected their deliberately broken deadline/freshness variants.

## Unproved physical acceptance

The broader prepared native selection compiled and recorded 31 passing roots /
134 passing root/subtest verdicts before physical cases. Native Box/heartbeat
cases reached `create netlink socket: operation not permitted`; startup cases
therefore did not enter their intended native-start wait. The actual OpenVPN TCP
provider case reached the same denied constructor and was interrupted while its
physical fixture cleanup was blocked. This run is not a passing full native
suite. The mandatory physical-provider tests and their guards remain enabled.

This change is a dependency of the imported Auto/Retest adapter. It does not by
itself enable that public control or establish physical networking, device,
release or deployment acceptance.
